# DRY gate: top 4 plugin options

Design study for a per-change "is the added/changed code DRY" gate built on the PR #52
deterministic-plugin contract (`IDeterministicEvaluationPlugin`, frozen inputs, receipts),
targeting repos like nucleoid/engram-csharp (issue #238). Produced 2026-10-28 from an
8-design judge panel (3 lenses each), adversarial refutation of the top 5, and a
completeness critique. Scores below are the panel mean out of 10.

Owner cost preference: deterministic, then decision model (Jev or configurable), then cheap LLM.
"Luna/Terra" were not identified; treated as Haiku 5.5 class placeholders.

Layout: the main body (this section through "Gaps the panel left open") is the synthesis and
the top-4 recommendation. Appendix A holds all eight designs verbatim with their judge
verdicts and refutations, Appendix B the completeness critique, Appendix C the scout fact
packs (contract, clone tools, Engram, Jev, LLM judging), and Appendix D the method.

## Shared prerequisite (every option needs it, build once)

The current contract gives a plugin only `ExpectedRepository.BaseCommit/HeadCommit` SHAs and a
head-tree path list. No base blobs, no hunks. Frozen-tree scope reading, materialization and
input-set identity are hard-gated to `AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` in `GateRunner`
(lines ~115, 181/861, 260, 273, 285, 296, 381, 499, 521, 565, 645, 785).

Needed (about 1 week, highest review risk, shared by all four options):

- `IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin` capability interface replacing the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks.
- `RepositoryEvaluationScope` additions: `BaseTreeId`, `ChangedPaths`, `AddedLineRanges` (from
  `git diff -U0 -z --no-renames --no-ext-diff --no-textconv base head`), `MaterializedBaseRoot`,
  `BaseObjectIds`, diff identity folded into `InputSetIdentity`.
- Base-blob materialization reusing `ReadAndMaterializeBatchedAsync` / `VerifyGitBlobIdentity`.
- Note: an externally registered plugin's raw PASS is downgraded to INCOMPLETE (no legacy-v1
  authority), so in advisory mode FAIL is the only discriminating outcome. That is enough for a
  "fail until DRY" worker retry loop, but the loop contract (retry cap, human override recorded in
  the receipt) is undefined today.

## Design rules every option must follow (from the refutations)

1. No patch-authored exemption. Generated/test/scope classification comes from the protected
   base only (`.delivery-forge/dry-*.json` via `ProtectedGitPolicyResolver`, csproj
   `IsTestProject` at base). `*.g.cs` names, `<auto-generated>` headers, `[GeneratedCode]`,
   `*Tests*` paths, `jscpd:ignore`, `// dry-intentional` comments added in the patch are
   suppression violations (INCOMPLETE), never exemptions. Every design in the panel was refuted
   on exactly this point.
2. Verdict by net clone-mass delta between base and head, not per-pair cliffs. Otherwise a
   partial refactor (extract from 3 of 5 siblings) fails its own gate, and the issue-238 DRY-up
   PRs would be blocked.
3. Fingerprint over an identifier-blind token stream (all identifiers -> ID, literals -> kind).
   Use per-unit positional alpha-renaming only when verifying a candidate pair, never as the
   winnowing stream (positional placeholders are context dependent; refuted in 3 designs).
4. FAIL requires a concrete finding: both sides as file:line, the existing symbol to reuse or the
   extraction target. Anything uncertain, truncated, over budget, or unavailable is INCOMPLETE.
5. Calibrate on a held-out set, not only the 41 jscpd "Watched" pairs: replay the last 60-200
   merged PRs, add hard negatives (per-VCS providers, DTO/record families, DI wiring, per-table
   SQL Stores) and an adversarial set (rename, split, pad, reorder, loop-to-LINQ).

## Option 1: Deterministic clone-mass delta ratchet (jscpd + DryUpScan reuse)

Panel 6.0 (contract-fit 7, accuracy 5, cost-ops 6). Tier: deterministic. Cost $0.

Mechanism:
- Materialize `head/`, `base/`, and `head-norm/` (line-preserving Roslyn-lexed copy: every
  identifier -> `ID`, literal -> `LIT`, comments dropped, newlines kept so line N stays line N).
- Run the already-pinned jscpd@4.2.5 (`detectClones`, mild, minLines 6, minTokens 50, in-memory
  store, no blame, no config discovery) over `head/` and `head-norm/` via the same reviewed Node
  binary as anti-slop. Canonical sorted JSON on stdout, fragments dropped.
- Keep clone pairs touching an added line range. Re-derive each clone as the maximal
  exactly-equal token run (jscpd ranges start mid-line, so whole-line keys break; refuted),
  key by SHA-256 of that run, union-find into clone classes.
- Ratchet: count occurrences of each class in head (Hc) and base (Bc) with Rabin-Karp over the
  token streams; charge the patch only for Hc > Bc. Aggregate into net duplicated-token mass.
- FAIL rules aligned with `Engram.DryUpScan` (`ThresholdEvaluator`, `PathFilters`): new
  type-1 class >= 10 lines/70 tokens; new sibling class across >= 3 files at 6/50; renamed
  (norm) class >= 15/120; aggregate creep >= 30 added lines across >= 2 new classes (blocks
  splitting a copy into sub-floor pieces); any new class >= 40 lines. Sub-threshold new
  classes are WATCH in the artifact, mirroring the weekly "Watched, not filed" list.
- Exemptions: content-addressed clone-class keys or path-family globs in protected-base
  `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json`; keys expire when code changes.

Fit: `ExpectedTool = node`, `DetectInputs` = in-scope `*.cs` at head, snapshot binds bundle
closure + thresholds + base allowlist oid, verdict computed in `AppendBoundArtifacts` (runs
before `Normalize`), sidecar `dry-binding.json`.

Catches type-1 fully, type-2 via the normalized pass; type-3 (gapped) mostly missed (jscpd has
no gap tolerance); type-4 never. Latency 35-90 s on a 150k LOC tree (tree-bound, base index
cacheable by tree id). Effort about 2.5-3.5 engineer-weeks including the shared prerequisite.

Why it is first: reuses tooling engram-csharp already pins and reviews, so gate and weekly
sweep cannot drift; a gate PASS means the sweep will not later file the same code.

## Option 2 (novel): Conditional-compression redundancy gate

Panel 6.5, highest (contract-fit 5, accuracy 6.5, cost-ops 8). Tier: deterministic, not a clone
detector and not a model. Cost $0. This was prototyped and measured on engram-csharp @ adf2abc
(scripts in `C:/Temp/claude/ncd/`: exp1-3, replay, ratchet, trained; python-zstandard 0.25.0).

Idea: approximate conditional description length K(hunk | donor) with zstd using each head file
as a raw-content prefix dictionary (`ZSTD_dct_rawContent`). Per added unit (contiguous added run,
>= 8 normalized lines, comments stripped):
- `E1 = 1 - |zstd(U | best donor)| / |zstd(U)|`: share of the unit already said by one place.
- `EK` over the concatenated top-8 donors; `conc = E1/EK` is donor concentration. Copy-paste is
  explained by one donor (conc near 1); idiom/boilerplate is explained piecemeal by many.
- NEW_DUPLICATE when E1 >= 0.80 and conc >= 0.90 and not carried; WATCH at 0.70-0.80.
- Donor localization by sliding window gives "src/X.cs:120-147 explained 94% by src/Y.cs:88-115".

Measured: 38/41 Watched pairs caught; 0/60 using/header blocks flagged; 3.3% of random
11-28 line windows flagged (most were real undetected near-duplicates); replay of the last 60
merges: 5 PRs would FAIL (identical 52-line block added to 3 bulk indexers; Slack/Teams sinks
born as clones; webhook buffering copied in Program.cs; a 14-line lockstep edit) with one false
positive (interface/implementation signature mirror, fixed by a declaration-mirror rule).
About 4-8 s per patch. A zstd dictionary trained on the base tree was tested and rejected (no
discrimination: clones 0.48 vs random 0.46 vs headers 0.57).

Fixes required from refutation:
- The carry/ratchet test compared against the base version of the unit's own file, which also
  carries an in-file copy whose original stays in place. Carry only when the unit is explained
  by lines the patch deleted (move/reflow), or do a head-based move test.
- Apply the floor to token count, not lines (line padding evasion).
- Blind to full identifier renames (0/41) and type-4. Pair it with Option 1's normalized pass,
  or treat it as the tier-1 pre-filter, never the sole FAIL authority.

Strengths unique to this option: language-agnostic core (new profile for SQL strings, YAML,
bicep, Svelte, TS; everything else in the panel is C#-only), zero dependencies beyond libzstd
1.5.7 vendored by SHA, and every receipt number is an integer byte length a third party can
recompute. Effort about 7-10 dev-days plus the shared prerequisite.

## Option 3: Deterministic miner + configurable decision model (Jev default)

Panel 5.5 (contract-fit 5.5, accuracy 6, cost-ops 5). Tier: decision model.

Mechanism:
- Candidate pairs from Option 1/2 (type-1/2) plus Roslyn winnowing (type-3, Jaccard >= 0.70)
  plus optional Engram recall for type-4: `search_symbols` on de-camel-cased name stems and
  `search_code` on signature + identifier bag, each hit resolved to the frozen base tree and
  re-scored locally (never trust Engram text or score; Engram indexes the default branch, which
  is the base in the normal PR case). Clear type-1 copies FAIL without any model.
- Only the ambiguous band (cap 40 pairs, overflow = INCOMPLETE) goes to `IDryDecisionProvider`:

```csharp
public interface IDryDecisionProvider
{
    string ProviderId { get; }
    string PinnedModelId { get; }
    Task<DryPairDecision> DecideAsync(DryDecisionState state, DryQuestionSet questions, CancellationToken ct);
}
```

- State: comment-stripped fragments, paths, clone-class size, sibling paths, precomputed line
  counts (Jev cannot count), similarity bucket. Nothing patch-authored (no rationale comments,
  no hot-path markers unless read from base).
- One `POST https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone` per pair, questions evaluated in parallel on
  the same state: Noul `same_behaviour`; Choice `disposition` in
  {call-existing, parameterise-existing, extract-helper, intentional-variation, boilerplate,
  not-duplicate} sent twice with reversed option order (documented first-option bias); Score
  `extractability` rubric 0-4; Noul `deliberate_variation`.
- The trusted plugin recomputes the verdict from raw probabilities (PAIR-FAIL needs
  same_behaviour >= 0.85, both orders agree on a share disposition at >= 0.70, extractability
  >= level 3 of 4, deliberate_variation <= 0.20). Unsure -> INCOMPLETE. Provider error,
  resolved model != pin, or overflow -> INCOMPLETE; a concrete FAIL still stands.
- Memoized host-owned decision cache keyed by sha256(configuration identity || canonical state
  || question-set hash): reruns replay the stored response, so "same inputs, same outcome"
  holds by memoization and the receipt says so.
- Swappable providers: Microsoft-Decision-1 (same primitives, Foundry), self-hosted Perplexity
  pplx-decider (open weights, the only one that can be made reproducible), Claude Haiku 5.5
  with a json_schema enum emulating Noul/Choice/Score.

Jev facts (docs.typesafe.ai, 2026-10-28): jev-1.14.0 (2026-10-14), 64k tokens/request, text
only, $0.042 per M input, output free, 70-500 ms vendor latency, no seed/temperature,
determinism undocumented, aliases `jev-latest`/`jev-preview` must be rejected, no .NET SDK
(OpenAPI at api.typesafe.ai/openapi.json), early access, no documented SLA, deprecation or
data-residency policy. Sending proprietary HoT code to a startup API is a policy decision.
arXiv 2609.29769: decision models and flash LLMs are wrong in the same places; a Jev -> Haiku
cascade gains at most ~2.7 points, so Haiku is an outage fallback, not an accuracy booster.

Cost about 1-3 cents per run, 8-30 s wall clock. Effort about 3 weeks.

## Option 4: Cheap LLM judge (Haiku 5.5, Sonnet 5.5 configurable)

Panel 5.3 (contract-fit 5, accuracy 5, cost-ops 6). Tier: cheap LLM.

Mechanism:
- Pinned launcher builds a canonical evidence pack from git objects: touched members (Roslyn,
  >= 6 lines / 50 tokens), top-k (5, max 8) look-alikes from a blind-hash + winnowing index of
  the base tree (cached by base tree id) plus Engram `search_code`/`search_symbols`, every
  candidate re-read from base blobs, plus `.delivery-forge/dry-policy.md` (repo DRY conventions
  in prose) from the protected base. `packHash` is the real "what was judged" identity.
- Deterministic fast paths first: blind-hash equal >= 25 lines FAIL; zero candidates PASS;
  allowlisted pair fingerprint EXEMPT. Only gray units reach the model.
- 3 stateless `/v1/messages` calls per unit on `claude-haiku-5-5` (fixed snapshot id, retirement
  not before 2027-10-07), `output_config.format = json_schema` with verdict enum, cited candidate
  enum over c1..c8 or none, `load_bearing_reason` enum, `patch_lines`, `suggested_target`;
  candidates wrapped as untrusted data; static prefix prompt-cached.
- Citation verifier (pure function): a DUPLICATE vote counts only if the cited candidate exists
  in the pack, resolves to the bound base blob, and passes a similarity floor. The model cannot
  invent a symbol, only point at a wrong supplied one, which the verifier catches.
- FAIL only on 3/3 verified-citation DUPLICATE votes; CLEAN on 3/3 non-duplicate; anything else
  (UNSURE, split, refusal, max_tokens, 429/529 after bounded retries) is INCOMPLETE.
- Verdict cache keyed by sha256(packHash || unit || configuration identity).

Important API facts (platform.claude.com, 2026-10-28): API version 2026-10-21 removes
temperature/top_p/top_k (400 if sent), so temperature 0 does not exist; reproducibility is by
memoization. Structured outputs guarantee structure, not content. Haiku 5.5 $0.10/$0.50 per M
(<= 100k prompts), cache reads $0.01; Sonnet 5.5 $2/$10. Published evidence: prompt wording
moves clone F1 far more than temperature; GPT-4-class recall on type-4 is poor and high-similarity
non-clones get over-flagged; no published Claude or C# clone evaluation exists, so a local eval
set is mandatory.

Cost about $0.02 per run on Haiku (about $0.5 on Sonnet), 15-25 s warm, 45-70 s cold base
index. Effort about 3-4 weeks.

## Recommended composition

1. Build the shared prerequisite once.
2. Deterministic stage = Option 2 (4-8 s, language-agnostic, catches verbatim and near-verbatim)
   plus Option 1's normalized pass (type-2 renames, sweep-aligned thresholds). Net-mass delta
   accounting, protected-base exemptions only. This alone would have failed 5 of the last 60
   engram-csharp merges and catches every issue-238 shape except a fully renamed copy and
   semantic rewrites.
3. Ambiguous band (type-3 winnowed 0.70-1.0, Engram type-4 hits, boilerplate-shaped exact
   matches) -> Option 3 behind `IDryDecisionProvider`.
4. Option 4 only as the provider-outage fallback or where Engram semantic recall is wanted.
   Unresolved pairs are INCOMPLETE, not majority-voted.

Before building a base index from scratch, check Engram's precompiled `clone_report` artifact
(`get_artifact(repo, "clone_report", "all")`); it may already hold the base-side clone classes.

## Runners-up and novel ideas not in the top 4

- Refactor-feasibility probe (panel 5.0): FAIL is a proof, not an opinion. The host anti-unifies
  the pair (Plotkin, <= 3 holes), rewrites both sites to a helper in a scratch tree, builds and
  runs the referencing tests; green means PROVEN_REMOVABLE and the unifying diff is the fix.
  Tests going red means behavioral divergence, so the pair is cleared. Cost 1.5-7 min per run.
- Behavioral fingerprinting (critic): for pure-ish new helpers (static, primitive/string/
  collection params), run new and candidate on fixed-seed generated inputs in a sandbox;
  identical output vectors = semantic duplicate. Catches rename, loop-to-LINQ and rewrite
  evasions that beat every textual matcher. Narrow scope (small helpers), which is where agents
  most often reinvent existing code.
- Lockstep-hunk detector (critic): compare the patch's own hunks to each other (identifier-blind)
  and flag the same edit applied to N sibling files; mine git log co-change history for a
  "shotgun-surgery debt" prior. Diff-sized input, very cheap, catches intra-patch duplication.
- Proof-of-search ledger: a host-owned Engram MCP proxy records what the agent searched and was
  shown; a new symbol that is a near-duplicate of a symbol the agent was shown becomes
  SEEN_AND_DUPLICATED in the receipt.

## Gaps the panel left open

- Fix-loop contract: retry cap, convergence, human override recorded in the receipt.
- Machine-readable finding schema for the agent (existing symbol to call, extraction target,
  clone class members) versus how much oracle to expose to a gaming agent.
- Cross-repo reinvention of HouseOfTravelLibrary / SerilogExtensions / BCL helpers: only
  Engram-backed options (`search_group`) can see it.
- TFVC repos: base for a shelveset, protected-base config without a protected git ref.
- Test-code policy beyond path exclusion; generated code via a base-protected generator registry.
- Two concurrent PRs each adding the same helper both pass, then duplicate on merge.
- Cost and latency figures above are estimates except Option 2's measured prototype.

# Appendix A: all eight designs in full

Ranked by panel mean. Each design was written by an independent proposer (Claude Opus 5.5, high effort) given the same fact packs; judged by three Claude Sonnet 5.5 lenses (contract-fit, accuracy, cost-ops); the top five were then attacked by two Sonnet 5.5 refuters (feasibility; gaming and false positives). Text is the agents' own output, reproduced verbatim as data.

## A.novel-information-theoretic: Conditional-compression provenance gate (dry-ncd): donor-concentrated redundancy ratchet

Panel mean 6.5 (contract-fit 5, accuracy 6.5, cost-ops 8). Tier: novel.

**One line.** For each added hunk, compress it with zstd once against every head-tree file used as a raw prefix dictionary. If one specific donor explains at least 80% of the hunk's information, and that donor accounts for at least 90% of what the top 8 donors together explain, and the hunk did not already exist in base, the gate FAILs. Calibrated on engram-csharp: 38/41 watched clones caught, 0/60 using-headers, 3.3% of random windows.

### Mechanism

MEASURED, not hypothetical. Prototype scripts are in C:/Temp/claude/ncd/ (exp1.py, exp2.py, exp3.py, exp3nc.py, replay.py, replay_nc.py, ratchet.py, trained.py). They ran on engram-csharp @ adf2abc (the commit the 2026-10-24 Watched list was computed on) with python-zstandard 0.25.0.

Core idea: approximate the conditional description length K(hunk | X) by the length of the zstd output for the hunk with X loaded as a raw-content prefix dictionary. Then ask two questions. How much of the hunk is already said by a single other place in the code (specific redundancy, which suggests copy-paste)? And how much is only said by the language or idiom spread across many files (generic redundancy, which is boilerplate)?

1. UNITS. Run git diff -U0 --no-renames --diff-filter=AM base head over the profile scope (C# profile: src/**/*.cs; tests, generated code and obj are excluded). Each contiguous added run in head is a unit; runs separated by 3 lines or fewer are merged. Normalize: trim, collapse whitespace, strip // and /// comments, drop blank lines. A unit must have at least 8 normalized lines, otherwise it is recorded as BELOW_FLOOR and not judged.

2. REFERENCE = HEAD minus the unit, not base. Building from head means a moved block whose source was deleted has no donor, and duplication inside the patch is still caught, because the sibling added unit stays in the reference. Every in-scope head file is normalized and loaded once as its own raw-content dictionary (ZSTD_dct_rawContent). The unit's own file is rebuilt with the unit excised.

3. SCORES. Compression is zstd level 19 (btultra2), windowLog 25, nbWorkers 0, with no checksum, content size or dict id in the frame.
   - L0 = |zstd(U)|.
   - Lf = |zstd(U with dict f)| for every file f, including own-file-minus-U.
   - donor = argmin Lf.
   - E1 = 1 - Lmin/L0: the share of U's information explained by the single best donor.
   - EK = 1 - |zstd(U with dict = concat of the top-8 donors)|/L0.
   - conc = min(1, E1/EK): donor concentration. A value near 1 means one place explains it (copy-paste). Lower means it is explained piecemeal by many files (idiom).

4. RATCHET. Eown_base = 1 - |zstd(U with dict = base version of U's own file)|/L0. If Eown_base >= 0.80, the content already existed in that file in base (a move, reflow, or diff artifact), so the unit is CARRIED and cannot FAIL. The gate only fails redundancy that the patch created. Shotgun edits applied in lockstep to siblings still fail, because the new text is not in base: #974 gave Eown_base 0.51.

5. VERDICT PER UNIT.
   - NEW_DUPLICATE: E1 >= 0.80, conc >= 0.90, at least 8 lines, not CARRIED, not covered by a protected declaration.
   - WATCH: 0.70 <= E1 < 0.80 with conc >= 0.90. Recorded in the artifact only, mirroring the weekly "Watched, not filed" list.
   - otherwise CLEAN.

6. DONOR LOCALIZATION, for actionable messages only. Within the donor file, slide a window of U's line count and take the argmin of |zstd(U with dict = window)|, giving a finding such as "src/X.cs:120-147 explained 94% by src/Y.cs:88-115".

7. GATE.
   - Any NEW_DUPLICATE -> FAIL.
   - All units scored and none NEW_DUPLICATE -> PASS (raw).
   - Any unit unscorable (decode error, cap exceeded, timeout, count mismatch) -> INCOMPLETE.
   - Zero in-scope units -> NOT_APPLICABLE only if policy allows it, otherwise INCOMPLETE (runner semantics).

CALIBRATION (comment-stripped normalizer, E1 >= 0.80, conc >= 0.90, at least 8 lines):
- Watched list: 41 pairs; the last site was excised from the reference and scored against the rest. 38/41 caught (93%). Missed: TypeScriptJavaScriptSourceScanner:19-36 (E1 0.705), CommunityDetectionService:527-538, and one more just under the bar.
- 300 random 11-28-line production windows (own text excised): 10/300 (3.3%). On inspection most are real near-duplicates that jscpd's 10-line/70-token bar missed: per-VCS provider bodies, NexusContractExtractor regex branches, WorkItemRefreshConfigurationExtensions vs PrefixedSectionBinder (a sibling of a watched pair), SQL upsert blocks. One is load-bearing data: the Languages.cs language table.
- 60 file-header/using blocks: 0/60.
- Threshold sweep, (watched caught / random windows flagged): E1 0.75 -> 39/41 and 15/300; 0.82 -> 36/41 and 10/300; 0.85 -> 30/41 and 7/300; 0.88 -> 25/41 and 4/300.

REPLAY of the 60 most recent first-parent merges into engram-csharp main:
- 41 PRs had units of at least 8 lines; 160 units in total.
- 11 units in 7 PRs were flagged. The ratchet cleared 2 of those PRs (#565 GraphNormalizer, #747 Program.cs; content pre-existed in the own file, Eown_base 0.90 and 0.81).
- The remaining 5 PRs (12%) FAIL:
  - QA F-05/06/07/11: an identical 52-line block added to the Bitbucket, GitHub and GitLab bulk indexers (E1 0.96-0.97).
  - #974: a 14-line lockstep edit in GitBulkIndexer and TfvcBulkIndexer.
  - #600: SlackAlertSink and TeamsAlertSink born as clones; this later became a watched pair.
  - #743: webhook body-buffering copied within Program.cs.
  - #967: ISymbolStore interface signatures explained by SymbolStore. This is a FALSE POSITIVE, handled by the declaration-mirror filter below.
- Before comment stripping, #575 (thin-subclass doc comments and constructor forwarding) and #576 were also flagged. Comment stripping removed both.

SEED IDEA TESTED AND REJECTED: a zstd dictionary trained on the base tree (ZDICT, 112 KB) does not discriminate. Explained fraction, median: watched clones 0.48, random code 0.46, using-headers 0.57 (higher than the clones). Training keeps frequent segments, i.e. boilerplate, and throws away the one-off copies that matter. A raw-content prefix per donor file is required.

### Plugin contract fit

New class Adapters/DryNcdEvaluationPlugin : IDeterministicEvaluationPlugin with:
- Id "deliveryforge.dry-ncd", Version "1.0.0", ExpectedTool "dry-ncd".
- CompatibilityAdapter = EvidenceAdapterKind.External, so it must be explicitly selected via EvaluationPluginSelection("deliveryforge.dry-ncd", Enabled: true, Inputs).

DetectInputs(candidateInputs, ignoredInputs) returns the language-profile matches (src/**/*.cs minus test/generated globs), ordinal-sorted. This feeds the runner's expected==requested input check.

ValidateBeforeExecution(request):
- Requires Capability.Version == "1.0.0" and FormatVersion == "dry-ncd-json-v1" (same pattern as anti-slop's 1.78.0 / oxlint-json-v1 pin).
- Requires Invocation.InheritEnvironment == false, with the environment allow-list TMP/TEMP/SystemRoot/WINDIR only. No network and no secrets are needed.
- Requires Arguments to equal the canonical vector built by DryNcdRequestFactory: --base <sha> --head <sha> --root <frozen> --profile <path> --format dry-ncd-json-v1.
- Otherwise returns NormalizedEvidence(Incomplete).

CaptureBeforeAsync(request, ct) builds the snapshot State = DryNcdSnapshot holding:
- The SHA-256 of the tool exe and its bundled native libzstd.
- The profile bytes: thresholds 0.80/0.90/0.70, floor 8, top-K 8, level 19, wlog 25, normalizer rules.
- The bytes of .delivery-forge/dry-ncd.json read from the PROTECTED BASE via ProtectedGitPolicyResolver (git show <base>:path), never from head.
- The host-computed change-set manifest identity.
It compares the closure hash to a hard-coded ReviewedDryNcdClosureSha256 and the configuration identity to request.ConfigurationIdentity (mismatch -> InvalidOperationException -> runner maps it).

Identity getters:
- GetConfigurationIdentity(request, snapshot) = SHA-256 over profile + declarations + closure + environment keys. Returns "unavailable" without a snapshot, as anti-slop does.
- GetRuntimeClosureIdentity(request, snapshot) = tool directory closure hash.
- GetExecutableIdentity(request, snapshot) = the snapshot hash of Invocation.FileName (the dry-ncd exe), satisfying ValidateCapabilityBinding's absolute-path + bytes-hash rule (GateRunner.cs ~742-806).

Execution is the host ICommandExecutor running the exe. There is no plugin execute hook, and none is needed: the tool is a pure function of the frozen bytes.

Normalize(request, CommandResult, snapshot):
1. Parse stdout JSON: {schemaVersion, tool, zstdVersion, unitsTotal, unitsScored, unitsBelowFloor, unitsCarried, unitsDeclared, findings[{path, start, end, donor, donorStart, donorEnd, e1, conc}], watch[...], diagnostics[]}.
2. INCOMPLETE if: the JSON is unparsable; any diagnostic is present; unitsScored + BelowFloor + Carried + Declared != unitsTotal from the host manifest; any finding path is outside the frozen inputs; a score falls outside [-0.5, 1.0]; or the exit code is not 0/1.
3. findings > 0 and exit 1 -> new NormalizedEvidence(GateOutcome.Fail, reason listing file:line <- donor:line, Fixture: false, ProductionCapable: true, Limitations).
4. Clean with exit 0 -> NormalizedEvidence.Pass(...).

ValidateAfterExecution re-checks that the closure hash is unchanged.

AppendBoundArtifacts writes dry-ncd-report.json (the full per-unit E1/EK/conc/donor table plus WATCH list) and dry-ncd-binding.json (base/head/tree, plugin ids, config/closure/input-set identities, declarations hash). Each is written with FileMode.CreateNew and recorded as name -> "sha256:<hex>" in artifactHashes. Stdout stays a compact summary under the runner's capture cap.

CONTRACT GAPS (must be fixed; I am not hand-waving them):
(a) Frozen-tree scope is hard-gated to anti-slop at GateRunner.cs:861 (`if (plugin is not Adapters.AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin)`), with the same type test at :115/:260/:273/:285/:296/:381/:785 and Id string checks at :499/:521/:565/:645. These must be replaced by a capability interface, e.g. IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin { bool RequiresFrozenTree; bool RequiresBaseBlobs; long CaptureLimit; }, plus a generic sidecar hook.
(b) RepositoryEvaluationScope is head-only and has no diff. The fix is a new IRepositoryChangeSetReader.ReadChangeSetAsync(base, head, timeout, ct) -> RepositoryChangeSet(ChangedUnits[(Path, HeadStart, HeadEnd, BaseBlobOid?, HeadBlobOid)], ChangeSetIdentity). It would be implemented in GitRepositoryIdentityReader by reusing its existing git diff --name-only and cat-file --batch path. The host then materializes head blobs to <root>/head/ and changed files' base blobs to <root>/base/, plus inputs/changes.json, all hash-verified by VerifyMaterializedInputs. InputSetIdentity becomes the hash of the head-tree identity plus the base commit plus the change-set identity.
(c) PASS cannot be authoritative today. HasTrustedLegacyV1Authority means an externally registered plugin's PASS surfaces as final INCOMPLETE ("advisory") while FAIL passes through. As a blocking gate it is therefore useful immediately for FAIL; making PASS authoritative needs a TrustedGatePluginRegistry.BuiltIns binding or the future-v2 authority.

Registration: TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistration(GateId "dry", plugin, ExpectedToolVersion "1.0.0", ExpectedFormatVersion "dry-ncd-json-v1", ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity "sha256:<reviewed>"). This is host code only, never supplied by the candidate repo.

### Determinism and receipts

What is pinned:
- The dry-ncd self-contained single-file .NET 10 exe, plus the bundled native libzstd 1.5.7. libzstd output can change between zstd versions; within one version and parameter set, single-threaded (nbWorkers=0) output is deterministic. The closure hash is the reviewed constant.
- Compression parameters, as explicit ZSTD_c_* values rather than "level 19 defaults", to survive library default drift: compressionLevel 19, windowLog 25, nbWorkers 0, contentSizeFlag 0, checksumFlag 0, dictIDFlag 0.
- Normalizer profile bytes: regexes, comment rules, scope globs, floor 8, top-K 8, thresholds 0.80/0.90/0.70.
- Donor iteration is ordinal-sorted by path, and argmin ties break by path, so results do not depend on enumeration order.

What is hashed:
- ConfigurationIdentity = SHA-256(profile bytes || protected-base .delivery-forge/dry-ncd.json bytes || closure hash || environment allow-list).
- RuntimeClosureIdentity = SHA-256 of exe + libzstd bytes.
- InputSetIdentity = SHA-256 over sorted (path \0 headOid \0 mode) of every in-scope head file, plus base commit, plus ChangeSetIdentity (sorted path/start/end/baseOid/headOid of every unit).
- ExecutableIdentity = sha256 of the exe bytes.

Reruns: identical frozen bytes plus identical closure produce byte-identical compressed lengths, hence identical scores and outcome. The receipt's dry-ncd-report.json carries L0/Lf/LK integers, so any consumer can recompute E1 and conc and re-run the pinned tool to verify. No model, network, clock or randomness is involved; it is the only option in the set whose replay equality is provable rather than memoized.

What becomes INCOMPLETE:
- The runner already maps tool timeout or cancel, capture overflow, non-quiescent process, source change mid-run, and frozen-input re-hash mismatch.
- The plugin adds: closure or config mismatch with the reviewed pins; unit-count reconciliation failure; a unit whose head or base blob cannot be decoded as UTF-8 text; per-file cap exceeded (proposed 2 MiB normalized per donor file, 64 MiB total reference, 4,000 donor files, 400 units per patch; beyond these the result is INCOMPLETE, never sampled); exit codes other than 0/1; any tool diagnostic.
- Declaration-file problems: unparsable; an entry with a blanket glob (for example src/** or a directory without a donor glob); a retired entry that matches no head path.
- Any in-head suppression marker such as "// dry-ncd: ignore". The candidate cannot exempt itself, mirroring anti-slop's SuppressionViolations.

Cross-implementation caveat: calibration was run with python-zstandard 0.25.0. The .NET tool must reproduce the calibration numbers within ±0.01 on a golden fixture before it is trusted. ZstdSharp (managed port) is not guaranteed byte-identical to native, so prefer P/Invoke to the pinned native lib.

### Engram usage

Deliberately NOT in the verdict path. Engram indexes the default branch, so an unmerged head is invisible to it, and its ranking is not a pinned, byte-reproducible function, so it cannot feed a receipt that claims replay equality.

Uses outside the verdict:
(1) Calibration and corpus growth, offline: get_artifact(repo, "clone_report", "all") and the weekly DryUpScan output supply more labeled pairs to extend the golden fixture beyond the 41 watched pairs. Reviewer-dismissed pairs become negative fixtures.
(2) Declaration authoring: detect_communities plus find_related_code on the donor and unit files help a human decide whether a sibling family (the 5 VCS bulk indexers, the 6 HTTP extractors) is deliberately per-VCS before adding a protected-base declaration.
(3) Remediation hints, posted after the gate as advisory text, never hashed into the outcome: for each FAIL finding, get_file_outline(repo, donorPath) and search_symbols resolve the donor span to a symbol (e.g. "HostedGitBulkIndexer.IndexCommitsAsync"), and change_impact(repo, [unitPath, donorPath], view="review") lists callers and convention exemplars. The PR comment can then say "reuse/extract X" instead of only file:line.
(4) Optional donor prefilter for very large repos (>4,000 files): search_code is not acceptable here (it is non-deterministic). A local deterministic 8-gram shingle index is used instead. So Engram is not used even there.

### Load-bearing duplication handling

There are no inline suppressions and no candidate-controlled switches. Load-bearing duplication is absorbed by seven measured or structural mechanisms, plus one reviewed declaration channel.

(1) Donor concentration (conc >= 0.90). Idiom and boilerplate are explained piecemeal by many files, not by one, so they fail the concentration test. Measured: using/header blocks 0/60 flagged (median conc 0.85, E1 max 0.81). Attribute/DI/constructor-forwarding shapes behave the same way.

(2) Comment stripping. Copied doc comments on deliberately parallel sibling classes were the #575 false positive; stripping comments removed it, and removed #576.

(3) Size floor of 8 normalized lines. One-liners, guard clauses and short property blocks are never judged.

(4) Ratchet. CARRIED units (content already in the own file in base: moves, reflows, diff artifacts) cannot FAIL. Editing inside an existing watched duplicate therefore does not block unrelated work. Only newly created redundancy fails.

(5) Declaration-mirror filter, a deterministic C# profile rule. In a unit whose file declares an interface or abstract type, signature-only member lines (ending in ';' with no body) are dropped before scoring when the donor is a type implementing it. This fixes the #967 ISymbolStore <- SymbolStore false positive. Equivalent rules apply to partial-class pairs and generated *.g.cs files, which are out of scope.

(6) Data tables. If more than 70% of a unit's normalized lines are initializer or entry lines (e.g. `["x"] = "y",`, `Key = value,`, enum members), the profile's DATA shape rule demotes NEW_DUPLICATE to WATCH. This covers Languages.cs-style registries. It is a profile rule, hashed into ConfigurationIdentity.

(7) Protected-base declarations in .delivery-forge/dry-ncd.json, read from BASE via ProtectedGitPolicyResolver, never from head. Format: entries {unitGlob, donorGlob, reason, issue, family}. Example: {"unitGlob": "src/Engram.Core/Indexing/*BulkIndexer.cs", "donorGlob": "src/Engram.Core/Indexing/*BulkIndexer.cs", "reason": "per-VCS paging semantics differ by provider", "issue": "#238"}. A matching unit gets verdict LOAD_BEARING_DECLARED. The finding, entry hash and reason are written into the receipt artifact; the unit is not silently dropped.

Why this is not a bypass:
(a) A declaration only takes effect after it has merged through a reviewed PR. A patch cannot declare its own exemption, because head-side edits to the file are ignored for that run.
(b) Blanket globs (no donor glob, or a directory-wide unit glob) are rejected and the result is INCOMPLETE.
(c) Entries matching no head path are retired and the result is INCOMPLETE, the same rule as anti-slop's RetiredIgnoredFiles.
(d) The count of declared units is reconciled in the totals.

Hot-path duplication kept for performance falls under the same declaration channel, with reason "perf" and a benchmark link.

### Cost and latency

$0 per run; no API or model is involved.

Measured prototype (Python + native libzstd, Windows laptop, engram-csharp src/ at about 646 files / 5 MB normalized):
- Building per-file dictionaries for the whole tree: about 1.6 s.
- Scoring one unit against all 646 donors, plus the top-8 concat: about 0.09 s.
- Full 60-PR replay, including git archive per PR: 48-60 s, i.e. under 1 s per PR on average.

Estimated for a typical 300-line C# patch (8-20 units):
- About 2 s dictionary build, plus 1-2 s scoring, plus 1-3 s donor localization for findings only, plus git materialization.
- About 4-8 s wall-clock under the gate's single deadline.

Memory was not measured. Production should use one ZSTD_CCtx with ZSTD_createCDict_advanced per donor built lazily and in parallel (deterministic, because each length is independent), or ZSTD_CCtx_refPrefix per donor. If more than 4,000 files, apply a deterministic shingle prefilter to the top 64 donors plus the own file.

For comparison, jscpd over src/ in the weekly job is minutes-scale. This gate is faster because it only scores added units.

### Precision and recall expectation

Type-1 exact and whitespace-only clones: about 100%. All watched pairs that are byte-similar score E1 0.85-0.95.

Type-1 with cosmetic edits (literal or name swaps such as "slack"/"teams"): caught. The 41 jscpd-found watched pairs give 38/41 (93%) at 0.80.

Type-3 gapped clones were tested by deleting 15% of lines and inserting noise in 10%. E1 median 0.77 and 75th percentile 0.83, so roughly 40-50% are caught. About 75% are caught at the WATCH level, which is recorded but does not fail.

Type-2 consistent identifier renaming:
- Partial (30% of identifiers renamed): E1 median 0.67, mostly missed.
- Full rename: 0/41. The byte channel is blind to renaming.
- An identifier/literal-blinded "skeleton" second channel was tested. At E1 >= 0.85 and conc >= 0.95 it catches 7/41 fully-renamed clones with 2/300 random false flags; at 0.82 it catches 14/41 with 3/300. That is too weak to FAIL on, so it is proposed only as a WATCH signal.

Type-4 semantic clones: 0%. Out of reach by construction.

Over-flagging:
- 3.3% of random 11-28-line windows are flagged, but most are genuine undetected near-duplicates rather than false positives.
- Real false-positive classes seen: interface/implementation signature mirrors (fixed by rule 5), declarative data tables (rule 6), and deliberately parallel sibling families (declarations).
- On the 60-PR replay, after the ratchet, 5 of 41 PRs FAIL; 1 of the 5 is a false positive (#967), which is fixed by the mirror filter.

Strictness relative to the owner's weekly policy: the weekly sweep files only clones that appear in at least 3 sibling files or are at least 80 LOC, and only watches 2-site clones of 11-28 lines. This gate as specified FAILS new 2-site clones of 8 or more lines (e.g. Slack/Teams). Matching the weekly policy instead, FAIL only when E1 >= 0.85 and either the unit is at least 20 lines or at least 2 distinct donor files have E1 >= 0.80, would drop recall on the watched set to about 25-30/41 and leave the rest as WATCH.

### Failure modes

- Type-2 renamed and type-4 semantic duplicates pass silently; the byte channel scores fully-renamed clones at E1 about 0.2. Mitigation: skeleton channel as WATCH only, or cascade WATCH units to the tier-2/3 plugins.
- Copy-then-edit gaming: a developer who perturbs about 30% of tokens drops E1 below 0.80. This is the same weakness every token detector has; the WATCH band at 0.70 records it in the receipt for reviewers.
- Declarative or data registries and sibling families deliberately kept per-VCS will FAIL until a protected-base declaration is merged. That is first-run friction: expect about 5-10 declarations for engram-csharp (bulk indexers, VCS providers, HTTP extractors, Languages.cs).
- zstd version or parameter drift changes scores. Mitigated by pinning the closure hash and explicit ZSTD_c_* parameters, plus a golden-fixture test (±0.01). A managed port (ZstdSharp) may not be byte-identical to native libzstd.
- conc can exceed 1 slightly, because smaller dictionaries give cheaper offset codes. It is clamped to 1. In donor-ranking ties a smaller file can win over the true donor; this is harmless to the verdict but can mislocate the message.
- Very small units (8-10 lines) have a larger zstd fixed overhead share, so E1 is noisier. The floor is set at 8 deliberately; raising it to 10 trades recall for stability.
- Per-file dictionaries miss duplication whose donor material is split across two files (a unit stitched from A and B): E1 drops while EK stays high. Low conc reads it as idiom, so it passes.
- Diff-based units: whitespace-only reflow or a reorder shows as added lines. The ratchet catches it via Eown_base, but a cross-file move with simultaneous edits can look like new redundancy if the source copy was kept.
- The contract work is the real risk: generalizing the 12 anti-slop type and Id checks in GateRunner, and adding a change-set reader with base-blob materialization, touches security-sensitive frozen-input code.
- Until a built-in authority binding exists, the gate's PASS surfaces as final INCOMPLETE (advisory) under GateRunner.cs:396-403. Only FAIL is decisive.

### Implementation sketch

Pins:
- libzstd 1.5.7 native (win-x64 and linux-x64 builds vendored with SHA-256), called via P/Invoke: ZSTD_createCCtx, ZSTD_CCtx_setParameter, ZSTD_createCDict_advanced with ZSTD_dct_rawContent, ZSTD_compress2 / ZSTD_compress_usingCDict.
- .NET 10 SDK, self-contained single-file publish of the tool.
- No Node and no network.

Tool, tools/DeliveryForge.DryNcd/, about 900 LOC:
- Program.cs: argument parsing for --base, --head, --root, --profile, --format; --version prints {"schemaVersion":"1","toolVersion":"1.0.0","formatVersion":"dry-ncd-json-v1","zstd":"1.5.7"} for CapabilityDetector.
- LanguageProfile.cs: JSON profile covering globs, comment rules, floor, thresholds, mirror and data-shape rules.
- Normalizer.cs.
- ZstdNative.cs: P/Invoke wrapper.
- ConditionalCompressor.cs: L0/Lf/LK.
- DonorRanker.cs.
- Ratchet.cs: uses <root>/base blobs.
- DonorLocator.cs: sliding window.
- DeclarationMatcher.cs.
- ReportWriter.cs: compact stdout plus the full report file path in the frozen output.

Evidence component, components/evidence/DeliveryForge.Evidence/:
- Adapters/DryNcdEvaluationPlugin.cs
- Adapters/DryNcdRequestFactory.cs: canonical args and environment allow-list.
- Adapters/DryNcdReportNormalizer.cs
- DryNcdDeclarations.cs: protected-base parser plus blanket/retired validation.
- IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin.cs: capability interface replacing the anti-slop type checks.
- IRepositoryChangeSetReader.cs, RepositoryChangeSet.cs, ChangedUnit.cs: one type per file per house style.
- GitRepositoryIdentityReader: add ReadChangeSetAsync (git diff -U0 -z --no-renames base head plus base blob materialization into <root>/base, reusing ReadAndMaterializeBatchedAsync and VerifyGitBlobIdentity).
- GateRunner: generalize lines 115/260/273/285/296/381/499/521/565/645/785/861 to the interface; add a generic binding-sidecar path.
- AdvisoryDryNcdComposition.cs, modelled on AdvisoryAntiSlopComposition.
- TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistration for gate "dry".

Calibration harness, tools/DeliveryForge.DryNcd.Calibrate/: replays the 41 watched pairs, 300 random windows, 60 headers and the last N merges for any repo path, and emits threshold sweep tables. Port the prototype scripts in C:/Temp/claude/ncd/.

Tests:
- Golden fixture with frozen unit and donor byte snippets and expected L0/Lf integers, guarding against zstd drift.
- Normalizer and profile rules.
- Ratchet carried/new cases.
- Declaration blanket/retired/head-edit-ignored cases.
- Count reconciliation leading to INCOMPLETE.
- Runner integration with the StubEvaluationPlugin pattern.

Effort, about 7-10 dev-days:
- Tool: 2-3 days.
- Contract generalization and change-set reader: 3-4 days; highest review risk.
- Plugin, normalizer, declarations and tests: 2 days.
- Calibration harness and engram-csharp declaration seeding: 1 day.

### Novelty claim

It is not a clone detector: there is no token matching, AST hashing, suffix structure or pair enumeration, as in jscpd, CPD or dupFinder. It is not a model either: no Jev, LLM or embedding is involved. It measures information.

1. Conditional description length via raw-content prefix dictionaries, i.e. how much of this hunk the repository already says.

2. Donor concentration (E1/EK), which separates "said once, somewhere specific" (copy-paste) from "said everywhere" (language idiom). Raw NCD or a dictionary cannot make that distinction, and it is the property that drives 0/60 using-headers while keeping 38/41 clones.

3. The reference is head minus the unit, plus an information-level base ratchet (Eown_base) instead of clone-pair diffing. Moves, reflows and pre-existing duplication pass, while new shotgun edits fail.

4. Language-agnostic core. Only a small normalizer profile is language-aware; the same tool works on TS, Python, SQL or YAML with a new profile.

5. Every number in the receipt is an integer byte length that a third party can recompute with the pinned closure, which makes this the most replayable DRY evidence possible.

It also tested and rejected the seeded "trained zstd dictionary" variant (no discrimination: clones 0.48 vs random 0.46 vs headers 0.57), so the recommendation is evidence-backed rather than speculative.

### Open questions

- Policy strictness: should the per-change gate FAIL on new 2-site clones of 8 or more lines (as calibrated: Slack/Teams would block), or match the weekly #238 bar (at least 3 sibling files or at least 80 LOC, with 2-site clones as WATCH)? The thresholds differ (0.80 vs 0.85 plus a donor-count leg).
- Is generalizing GateRunner's anti-slop-only frozen-tree path (IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin plus IRepositoryChangeSetReader with base-blob materialization) acceptable as part of this work, or should an interim advisory-only version read git objects directly (weaker frozen-input guarantees)?
- Should WATCH-band units (E1 0.70-0.80) cascade to a tier-2 decision-model or tier-3 LLM plugin for a FAIL/PASS judgement, given those tiers' nondeterminism and receipt cost, or stay record-only?
- Should the type-2 skeleton channel ship as WATCH-only from day one, or wait for evidence that renamed copy-paste actually occurs in engram-csharp patches? None was seen in the 60-PR replay.
- Who owns .delivery-forge/dry-ncd.json declarations in engram-csharp? An initial seed is needed for the bulk indexers, VCS providers, HTTP extractor families and the Languages.cs table, otherwise the first runs will fail on known-deliberate families.
- Must calibration be reproduced on linux-x64 CI with the native libzstd 1.5.7 build to confirm byte-identical lengths versus the Windows run? Determinism across OS/CPU for single-threaded zstd at fixed version is expected but not verified here.
- Should tests/ be in scope? The calibration and replay covered src/ only, mirroring the DryUpScan production-only rule.

### Judge verdicts

**contract-fit (5/10).** Plugin surface maps well: the interface has DetectInputs, ValidateBeforeExecution, CaptureBeforeAsync, Normalize, AppendBoundArtifacts, and the three identity getters, and the proposal uses each correctly. I confirmed those signatures in DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs. I confirmed 8 AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin references in GateRunner.cs, so the frozen-tree hard-gating gap is real. The design is deterministic (pinned native libzstd, explicit ZSTD_c_* params, ordinal tie-breaks, integer lengths in the receipt), and most uncertain paths become INCOMPLETE (count reconciliation, caps, diagnostics, closure mismatch). Weaknesses: (1) It is not drop-in. It needs a new frozen-tree capability interface, a change-set reader, and base-blob materialization, so about half the work is security-sensitive runner changes, not a plugin. (2) PASS is non-authoritative today, so only FAIL counts until a built-in authority binding exists. (3) Cross-platform byte reproducibility of zstd is asserted, not verified (Windows vs linux-x64, python-zstandard vs P/Invoke), yet the closure hash and the golden fixture are the trust anchors. (4) The protected-base declaration file is a candidate-influenced channel. It is read from base via ProtectedGitPolicyResolver, but anyone who merges a declaration can exempt a family. The blanket-glob and retired-entry rules are good but are heuristics for what counts as blanket. (5) Head-minus-unit reference and the ratchet make the verdict depend on the full head tree, so InputSetIdentity must cover every in-scope head file. This is specified, but it is large. (6) The Windows-only PATH, the 'tool is a pure function of frozen bytes' claim and the reconciliation of units against a host manifest require the host to compute units identically to the tool (duplicated normalizer/diff logic). A mismatch gives INCOMPLETE, not PASS, so it fails safe. (7) The unit normalizer reads comments and strips them, so a candidate can shape units, for example by splitting hunks or padding with changed lines, to dilute E1 or the merge-3-lines rule. That is gameable, though the proposal admits the token-perturbation weakness.

Strongest weakness: The design requires generalizing GateRunner's anti-slop-only frozen-tree and authority paths plus a new change-set and base-blob materializer, and PASS stays advisory (final INCOMPLETE). The plugin is not a clean fit with the current contract. Its replay-equality claim also rests on unverified cross-OS zstd byte-identity.

Suggested fix: Ship the contract generalization first as its own reviewed change (IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin plus IRepositoryChangeSetReader), with tests proving anti-slop is unchanged. Bind the plugin as a TrustedGatePluginRegistry built-in so PASS is authoritative. Verify the golden zstd fixture on both win-x64 and linux-x64 in CI, and make the closure identity per-platform. Have the host, not the tool, compute and hash the unit manifest from one shared normalizer. Reject any declaration entry whose unitGlob or donorGlob matches more than N files, with N set explicitly in the hashed profile. Treat declaration edits as requiring CODEOWNER approval so a self-exempting patch cannot also merge the exemption.

**accuracy (6.5/10).** Strongest evidence-backed design on precision and recall. It was measured on the real repo: 38/41 watched clones caught, 0/60 using-header blocks flagged, 3.3% of random windows flagged (mostly real near-duplicates), and a 60-PR replay where 1 of 5 failing PRs was a false positive (a rule now covers it). Donor concentration (conc) plus the base ratchet are a smart split between copy-paste and idiom. It should catch the issue-238 shapes that are byte-similar: identical 52-line wrappers across the bulk indexers (E1 0.96-0.97), the Slack/Teams clone, and function-for-function duplicated helpers. Boilerplate (usings, DI, records, constructor forwarding) is largely absorbed by comment stripping, the size floor and conc.

Weaknesses under this lens:
- Recall on the named shape "copy-pasted helper manually renamed" is near zero (full rename 0/41). The skeleton channel is WATCH-only.
- The 8-line normalized floor means short byte-identical wrapper methods (likely under 8 lines) are never judged. That is a likely issue-238 shape.
- The 85%-identical mechanical-difference shape sits right at the 0.80 threshold. Type-3 gapped clones are only caught about 40-50% of the time.
- It is easy to game. About 30% token perturbation, a split method or reordered statements drop E1 below 0.80, and the 0.70-0.80 WATCH band does not block.
- Calibration is biased and circular. The watched list was found by jscpd, a byte-level detector, so a byte-level metric scoring well on it is expected. Thresholds were tuned on the same 41 pairs, with no held-out set.
- It judges per-file donors only, so donors stitched from two files are missed.
- False-positive handling depends on 5-10 hand-seeded declarations and on narrow C#-specific rules (mirror filter, 70% data-table rule). Generated code and tests are excluded by scope rather than handled.
- It fails new 2-site 8-line clones, which is stricter than the weekly policy and likely noisy.

Strongest weakness: It is blind to renamed or lightly perturbed copies (full rename 0/41, 30% perturbation misses, 8-line floor), and the calibration is circular. Thresholds were tuned on jscpd-found byte clones with no held-out set, so recall on the "manually renamed helper" shape and on short wrappers is unproven and probably poor.

Suggested fix: Make the identifier/literal-blinded skeleton channel a real signal, with its own thresholds and a donor-count leg, so full-rename copies can fail when blinded E1 and conc are high and the unit is at least 20 lines. Lower the floor (to about 5 lines) for wrapper methods using a method-level unit. Add a hold-out set: tune thresholds on half the watched pairs and the replay, then validate on the rest, plus synthetic renamed, split and reordered mutants. Block on WATCH when 3 or more sibling files share the donor, to match the weekly policy.

**cost-ops (8/10).** Strongest on the owner's cost ordering. It is fully deterministic with no model, network, API keys or Engram freshness dependency. The estimate is $0 and about 4-8 s for a 300-line patch (about 2 s dictionary build plus about 0.09 s per unit against 646 donors). That is credible for a prototype that was actually measured on engram-csharp. Worst-case scaling is also fine: the 400-unit cap is about 36 s, and the caps push anything larger to INCOMPLETE rather than sampling. Cold start is cheap because there is nothing to warm or cache beyond a 1.6 s dictionary build, and results are byte-replayable. The tool is small (about 900 LOC) and the pins are simple: one exe, one libzstd, one profile. The costs are on the implementation side. The 7-10 day estimate is dominated by generalizing GateRunner's anti-slop-only frozen-tree path (12 type and Id checks) and adding a change-set reader with base-blob materialization. That is security-sensitive core work, not a plugin drop-in, and it is required before even an advisory plugin runs through the proper runner. PASS is advisory-only until an authority binding exists, so only FAIL is decisive at first. Operational burden: native libzstd for win-x64 and linux-x64 must be vendored and verified byte-identical across OS and CPU, which has not been done. The .NET port must reproduce the Python calibration within 0.01. Any zstd or normalizer bump invalidates both the closure pin and the calibration. Memory is unmeasured. Day-one friction is the 5-10 protected-base declarations. Calibration comes from a single repo and a single language, so thresholds need re-tuning per repo. Even so, ongoing runtime cost stays at zero.

Strongest weakness: Implementation and maintenance risk sits in the contract generalization (GateRunner frozen-tree and change-set reader) plus unverified native-zstd cross-OS byte-identity. The 7-10 day estimate has a large unknown inside the 3-4 day runner work, and every zstd or normalizer change forces re-pin and re-calibration.

Suggested fix: Ship an interim advisory version first. It would run the tool via the existing ICommandExecutor on a host-materialized head and base checkout, with no GateRunner refactor, to get real receipts cheaply. Do the IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin generalization as a separate reviewed PR. Before the P/Invoke tool is built, verify win-x64 vs linux-x64 length equality on a golden fixture. Measure peak memory. Add a CI job that re-runs calibration automatically on any pin bump. Pre-seed the declaration file from the DryUpScan sibling families to cut first-run friction.

### Adversarial refutation

**Refuted: true (major).** Claim attacked: Step 4 RATCHET: "If Eown_base >= 0.80 the content already existed in that file in base (a move, reflow, or diff artifact), so the unit is CARRIED and cannot FAIL. The gate only fails redundancy that the patch created." This is what is supposed to give the gate its "new duplication only" verdict. The replay claim that #565 and #747 were "content pre-existed (move/reflow)" rests on it.

Evidence:

The ratchet cannot tell a move or reflow from a verbatim intra-file copy, and I checked the design's own replay data.

1. In C:/Temp/claude/ncd/ratchet.py, Eown_base is the unit compressed against the whole base version of the unit's own file. Any block copy-pasted within a file that already contains the original scores high here. Whenever the best donor is the own file, E1 is roughly Eown_base. That means E1 >= 0.80 implies Eown_base >= 0.80 and the unit is CARRIED. The gate therefore cannot FAIL on intra-file duplication of pre-existing code, which is a common DRY violation.

2. The two PRs the ratchet clears are not moves or reflows. I ran `git diff -U0` on both merges in engram-csharp.
- #565 GraphNormalizer: hunk `@@ -250,0 +251,12 @@` is a brand-new 12-line private method `ReadMetadataAttributes`. There are 0 deleted lines, and replay_nc.json records del_=0.
- #747 Program.cs: hunk `@@ -604,0 +605,17 @@` is a new 17-line registry-guard block. There are 0 deleted lines. Its comments say it is the same defense-in-depth guard as the sibling handler (#373/#370).
- The replay shows both with donor "(self)" and base_own 0.90 and 0.81. They are genuine new same-file copy-paste of base content, and the ratchet waves them through.

3. #743 only FAILs because its donor was another unit added in the same PR. Its base_own is 0.63, below the cutoff, and its E1 of 0.87 comes from the PR's own sibling copy. A patch that copies a pre-existing block once inside one file passes. The reported "5 of 41 PRs fail, 12%" depends on this accident.

4. The calibration cannot catch this. The "watched clones" are cross-file pairs, and the 300 random windows were scored with the own text excised, so the ratchet was never exercised on a patch that copies from its own file. The prose "moves/reflows/diff artifacts" describes the intent, but the Eown_base test does not implement it.

5. Smaller feasibility issues, lower severity:
- The design treats ZSTD_createCDict, CCtx_refPrefix and python loadDictionary as interchangeable. In zstd these take different attach and copy paths, selected by pledged source size, so compressed lengths are not guaranteed equal across APIs. The "within 0.01" golden test is the only protection, and replay equality across OS is unverified.
- The contract gaps (a) to (c) are real work. Of the anti-slop type and Id checks in GateRunner, `plugin is not Adapters.AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` at :861 and the lines at :115, :260, :273, :285, :296, :381 and :785 are hard gates. GitRepositoryIdentityReader.cs:97 and :651 also filter inputs by `AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.IsSupportedInput`. PASS stays advisory because of `HasTrustedLegacyV1Authority`, which requires `ReferenceEquals(_authorityRegistry, TrustedGatePluginRegistry.BuiltIns)`. A plugin registered through TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistry can therefore never produce an authoritative PASS. Only FAIL is usable as a blocking gate.

Salvage:

Redefine the ratchet reference. Carry a unit only if it is explained by text the patch removed, not by the whole base own file. Use the normalized deleted-side lines of the whole patch (all files) as the carry dictionary: Edeleted = 1 - |zstd(U with dict=deleted lines)|/L0, carried if >= 0.80. Moves, reflows and diff artifacts delete the text they re-add, so they stay carried. Pure additions copied from pre-existing code, as in #565 and #747, then have Edeleted near 0 and FAIL. Add #565 and #747 as golden fixtures that must be NEW_DUPLICATE. Re-run the 60-PR replay and report the PR fail rate again. Also pin one zstd API path (ZSTD_compress2 with a prefix or CDict and explicit pledged size) and add a cross-OS golden test. Ship the FAIL-only gate first, since PASS is advisory under the current contract.

**Refuted: true (fatal).** Claim attacked: That the gate separates real from load-bearing duplication without a bypass, and that a coding agent under pressure cannot trivially evade it by rename, reorder, split or wrap, or satisfy it with a fake justification.

Evidence:

Taken from the design's own text and numbers. No repo files were needed.

1. The ratchet exempts the most common copy-paste. Step 4 says that if Eown_base >= 0.80 (the unit's content already exists in the unit's OWN file in base), the unit is CARRIED and "cannot FAIL". It is computed against the base own file, not head. So it cannot tell a move from a copy where the original stays in place. Verbatim-copying a method or block into the same file or class has Eown_base near 1.0 and is never judged. Only the donor is scored; its own-file donor is treated as carried. The agent only has to put the duplicate next to its source. The replay "clears" #565 (Eown 0.90) and #747 (0.81) as pre-existing content. Nothing shows these were moves rather than in-file copies. The check that would settle it is whether the source span still exists in head, and the design does not do it.

2. Rename evasion is admitted and unfixed. Full identifier rename scores 0/41, with E1 about 0.2, which is CLEAN rather than WATCH. The skeleton channel catches 7-14/41 and is demoted to WATCH-only. Partial rename of 30% of identifiers gives E1 0.67, which is also missed. The gate also hands the agent an oracle. A FAIL names the donor, the line ranges and E1, and a WATCH band exposes the gradient. Renaming the flagged span and resubmitting is the cheapest way to turn it green, cheaper than extracting a helper. The result is a green receipt on still-duplicated code, which is worse than no gate.

3. Splitting and the floor.
   - The floor is 8 normalized lines, and the normalizer trims, collapses whitespace, strips comments and drops blank lines. It does not join lines. Reflowing a 52-line block into 7 long lines (C# is whitespace-insensitive) makes it BELOW_FLOOR and unjudged.
   - Scattering a clone as sub-floor fragments of 6-7 lines between existing methods, with more than 3 unchanged lines between them, also avoids judgement. Units are per contiguous hunk with no patch-level aggregation.
   - The gate scores the whole hunk, not a sliding window. Padding a copied block with unique filler dilutes E1 below 0.80 (a 52-line clone plus 52 lines of new code gives about 0.5). A brand-new file is one unit, so padding works there too.
   - Stitching from two donors drops E1 and conc together. Failure_modes concedes this reads as idiom and passes.

4. The declaration channel defeats the headline catches. Declarations are file-glob scoped, with no span or content binding. The design expects 5-10 of them, including *BulkIndexer.cs. The headline replay catches (QA F-05/06/07/11 across Bitbucket/GitHub/GitLab bulk indexers, and #974 Git/Tfvc) are in exactly those families. After seeding, a new 52-line copy in a declared family is LOAD_BEARING_DECLARED and passes. The "blanket glob" rule is undefined beyond examples. The "issue" and "reason" fields are free text and unvalidated. The gate scans only src/**/*.cs, so the declaration file itself is not DRY-gated and a rubber-stamped declaration PR is a fake-justification path. For an agent whose patch is a legitimately parallel sibling (the 6th extractor, a new per-table Store, a per-VCS provider), the only exit is a human merge, because head-side declarations are ignored. This is a hard deadlock for that agent, and it creates pressure to evade by rename instead.

5. The false-positive case is circular. Thresholds were tuned on the same 41 jscpd-found byte-similar pairs, with no held-out set. The 3.3% flag rate on random windows is called "mostly real" by inspection, not by an independent label, so FPR is effectively unmeasured. The negative set (60 using-headers) is trivial. There are no hard negatives such as DI registration, DTO and record families, switch mappers or constructor-forwarding. One real false positive (#967) is patched by a hand-written special rule. The profile rules for mirrors and data tables are ad hoc, and each new false-positive class needs another one. A 12% PR FAIL rate on replay includes deliberately per-VCS code.

6. Small units of 8-10 lines (~300 bytes) have noisy E1 because of zstd's fixed overhead. That sits right at the threshold, so scores near 0.80 are unstable. The design admits this.

Net: the design's own measurements show rename, reflow, split, pad, two-donor stitching and in-file copy all evade it. The ratchet bypass in (1) is the most severe, because it needs no effort.

Salvage:

Do not claim non-evadability. Position it as a cheap, deterministic tier-1 pre-filter for lazy verbatim and near-verbatim copies, never as the sole FAIL authority.

Required fixes:
- (a) Replace the base-own-file ratchet with a head-based move test. CARRY only if the matching base span is absent from head (deleted or moved). An in-file copy that leaves its source in head must score.
- (b) Normalize at token level (join lines, strip braces-only lines) and apply the floor to token count, not lines.
- (c) Score a sliding window within each hunk, and aggregate all added lines per patch in file order, so padding and fragment splitting do not dilute E1 or hide below the floor.
- (d) Add a pairwise multi-donor check for stitched copies.
- (e) Make declarations span- and content-hash-bound, with a validated issue link and an expiry, and apply the DRY gate to the declaration file's own PR.
- (f) Do not expose raw E1 or donor details to the agent, or cap the number of retries.
- (g) Route WATCH units, and units flagged by a skeleton channel (identifier/literal-blinded), to a tier-2 or tier-3 semantic judge for FAIL/PASS.
- (h) Build a held-out hard-negative set (parallel DTOs, DI registration, per-VCS providers) and a renamed-copy adversarial set, and report recall and FPR on them before enabling FAIL.


## A.det-ast-structural: dfdry: Roslyn structural clone ratchet (nucleoid.dry-structural)

Panel mean 6.2 (contract-fit 7, accuracy 5.5, cost-ops 6). Tier: deterministic.

**One line.** A pinned, offline .NET single-file tool parses the frozen base and head C# trees with Roslyn (syntax only, no compilation). It normalizes member bodies into token and statement streams and finds type-1, type-2 and gapped type-3 clones that touch the patch hunks. It FAILs only when the patch raises the instance count of a clone class above calibrated thresholds, and exemptions can only come from an allowlist read from the protected base.

### Mechanism

Inputs: frozen base tree, frozen head tree, `git diff -U0 --find-renames=90%` hunks for *.cs, the protected-base allowlist `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json` (read via `git show <base>:<path>`; a copy at the patch head has no authority) and the code-owned config `dfdry.config.json`.

1. SCOPE. Production C# paths only, mirroring engram-csharp PathFilters.
   - Excluded: `*Tests*/` projects, `obj/`, `bin/`, `*.g.cs`, `*.Designer.cs`, `Migrations/`, files with an `<auto-generated>` header, and types marked `[GeneratedCode]`.
   - Changed units are members whose span overlaps an added or modified head hunk line.
   - No changed production units gives NOT_APPLICABLE (with policy allowance).

2. PARSE. `CSharpSyntaxTree.ParseText` with a pinned `LanguageVersion` and a fixed preprocessor symbol set from config (for example `NET10_0`, `RELEASE`, recorded in the receipt).
   - No MSBuild, no restore, no SemanticModel. The result is a pure function of the bytes.
   - Parsed trees and unit tables are cached by git blob oid, so files unchanged between base and head are parsed once.
   - A parse error inside a changed unit makes that unit INCOMPLETE.

3. UNITS. Methods, constructors, accessors, operators, local functions and lambdas with 4 or more statements, plus every maximal run of 4 or more statements inside them. Each unit gets a stable key: `path#Namespace.Type.Member(arity)#ordinal`.

4. NORMALIZATION. Three views are computed per unit, with trivia and comments removed and `this.` qualifiers dropped:
   - (a) L1: the raw token kinds and text (type-1).
   - (b) L2-consistent: identifiers alpha-renamed per unit, so the first distinct identifier becomes `$1` and so on; literals reduced to their kind (STR, NUM, CHAR); `true`, `false`, `null` and keywords kept (type-2 with consistent renaming).
   - (c) Statement shapes: each statement becomes `SyntaxKind` plus L2-blind tokens (all identifiers become ID), hashed with XxHash64 seed 0. The unit becomes a sequence of statement-shape hashes.
   - A characteristic vector is also computed: counts of `SyntaxKind` over the unit subtree, Deckard-style but exact.

5. CANDIDATES. These are exact (no LSH, so no probabilistic misses).
   - (i) Winnowing (MOSS) over L2 token streams with k=25 tokens and window w=20, which guarantees detection of any shared run of 44 or more normalized tokens. A candidate needs 3 or more shared fingerprints.
   - (ii) A brute-force comparison of characteristic vectors, every changed unit against all head units. Roughly 300 x 25k comparisons is about 1 s. A candidate needs normalized L1 vector distance of 0.25 or less, and unit sizes within 1.5x of each other. This catches gapped type-3 cases that share no 44-token run.
   - Intra-patch pairs, where both sides are changed units, are included.

6. VERIFY AND CLASSIFY each candidate pair (U changed, V anywhere):
   - T1: an equal L1 region of 70 or more tokens AND 10 or more non-blank, non-comment lines.
   - T2: an equal L2 region with the same minimums.
   - T3: the statement-shape LCS gives Dice = 2*|LCS| / (|U|+|V|) of 0.80 or more over an aligned region of 6 or more statements AND 70 or more tokens, with no single gap longer than 3 statements, AND token-level winnowed coverage of 0.75 or more.
   - Pairs are grouped into clone classes. The signature is the L2 region hash for T1/T2. For T3 it is the sorted set of member keys joined by the alignment.

7. NOVELTY RATCHET. The point is that only duplication introduced by this patch counts.
   - For each class, count instances in head and in base. Base is obtained by mapping U to its base counterpart through the hunk and rename map, or through the same member key.
   - The class is NEW only if the head count is greater than the base count.
   - Moved code is not new: if U has no base counterpart but some base unit W, deleted or changed in head, has L2-equality or Dice of 0.95 or more with U, and W~V already held in base, it is a move.
   - Editing pre-existing duplication without adding an instance is not new.

8. FILTERS (load-bearing and boilerplate; details in that field). Applied to NEW classes.

9. SEVERITY, using DryUpScan's thresholds as gate rules:
   - FAIL if T1 is 80 or more LOC.
   - FAIL if T1 or T2 covers 90% or more of a whole member and the class has 2 or more instances (the "function in 2 or more places with cosmetic-only differences" rule).
   - FAIL if any T1/T2/T3 class has 3 or more instances in 3 or more distinct files (the sibling rule).
   - FAIL if a T3 or partial T2 region is 25 or more statements.
   - Otherwise the finding is WATCHED: recorded in the artifact, not failing. This mirrors the "Watched, not filed" list.

10. VERDICT.
   - FAIL: any non-exempt NEW finding at FAIL severity. Each finding carries file:line spans for both sides, type, similarity, token count, and an extraction hint naming V's member key when V predates the patch ("reuse/extract from X").
   - PASS: zero such findings AND positive completeness proof. That means every manifest file parsed, units enumerated == units analyzed, no budget exceeded, and exit code 0.
   - Everything else is INCOMPLETE.
   - Tool exit codes: 0 = clean, 1 = findings, 2 or higher = error.
   - Output is stdout JSON `dry-scan-json-v1`.

### Plugin contract fit

Class `DryStructuralEvaluationPlugin : IDeterministicEvaluationPlugin`.
- Id "nucleoid.dry-structural", Version "1.0.0+<dfdry source commit>", ExpectedTool "dfdry".
- CompatibilityAdapter = `EvidenceAdapterKind.External`.
- Registered by host code through `TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistration(GateId:"dry", Plugin, ExpectedToolVersion:"1.0.0", ExpectedFormatVersion:"dry-scan-json-v1", ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity: <reviewed sha256>)` into `TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistry`. Policy only selects it via `EvaluationPluginSelection("nucleoid.dry-structural", true, Inputs)`.

CONTRACT GAP, stated honestly:
- `GateRequest` carries only `RepositoryIdentity(BaseCommit, HeadCommit, TreeId, IsClean)`. There is no diff, no base bytes and no changed paths.
- `ValidateAutomaticScopeAsync`, materialization and the lease are gated by `plugin is not AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` (GateRunner.cs:861). The same type check appears at :115, :260, :273, :285, :296, :381 and :785.

So the plan has two phases.

PHASE 0 (no runner change; advisory):
- A code-owned `DryStructuralRequestFactory` (modelled on `AntiSlopRequestFactory`) uses trusted host git to do the following:
  - run `git diff -U0 -z --find-renames=90% <base> <head> -- *.cs`;
  - run `git ls-tree -r -z` on both trees and `git cat-file --batch`;
  - verify every blob in memory against its git object id;
  - materialize production C# blobs into a host-owned temp root at `base/` and `head/`;
  - write a content-addressed `manifest.json` containing baseCommit, headCommit, treeId, files [{side, path, oid, mode}], canonical hunks [{path, oldStart, oldCount, newStart, newCount}], the allowlist oid and bytes, and the config digest.
- It builds `CommandInvocation(FileName: <abs dfdry path> == Capability.ExecutablePath, Arguments: ["scan","--manifest",<path>,"--format","dry-scan-json-v1"], WorkingDirectory: <materialized root>, Environment: {DOTNET_SYSTEM_GLOBALIZATION_INVARIANT=1, DOTNET_CLI_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT=1, TMP, TEMP, SystemRoot}, InheritEnvironment:false)`.
- Capability comes from `ToolCapabilityDetector` with `CapabilityProbe(Tool:"dfdry", Source: TrustedExplicit, VersionArguments:["--version"], RequiredVersionPrefix:"1.0.0", DocumentedFormatVersion:"dry-scan-json-v1")`. `--version` emits JSON {schemaVersion, toolVersion}, satisfying `ValidateCapabilityBinding`'s executable-hash requirement.

Members:
- `DetectInputs`: candidate .cs paths minus ignored and generated paths, i.e. the whole head production C# corpus, not only changed files, because clones are matched against the whole repo. Inputs above 24,000 argv bytes go through the manifest, as in anti-slop.
- `ValidateBeforeExecution`:
  - `Capability.Version=="1.0.0"` and `FormatVersion=="dry-scan-json-v1"`, else INCOMPLETE;
  - `request.Plugin.Inputs` must equal the manifest head inputs;
  - manifest base/head must equal `request.ExpectedRepository`.
- `CaptureBeforeAsync`:
  - re-read the manifest;
  - re-hash every materialized file to its git blob oid;
  - hash the dfdry executable bytes;
  - compute ConfigurationIdentity and RuntimeClosureIdentity;
  - compare them to the reviewed constants (`ReviewedDfdryLinuxX64ClosureSha256`). A mismatch throws `InvalidOperationException`; since this is not the anti-slop plugin, that maps to ERROR, so prefer returning an INCOMPLETE from `ValidateAfterExecution`, or extend the runner catch to frozen-tree plugins.
  - The snapshot is `EvaluationPluginSnapshot(new DrySnapshot(manifestSha, fileOids, configId, closureId, exeId))`.
- `GetConfigurationIdentity`, `GetRuntimeClosureIdentity` and `GetExecutableIdentity` return the snapshot values. They return "unavailable" when there is no snapshot, as anti-slop does.
- `Normalize` parses stdout (64 MiB cap for non-anti-slop) and requires the following, with any failure giving INCOMPLETE:
  - the schema matches;
  - report base/head equal the request;
  - filesParsed per side equal manifest counts;
  - changedUnitsEnumerated equals changedUnitsAnalyzed;
  - budgetExceeded is false;
  - no parse errors in changed units;
  - the exit code (0 or 1) agrees with failingFindings>0.
  Results:
  - Failing findings give `new NormalizedEvidence(GateOutcome.Fail, reason, false, ProductionCapable:true, limitations)`. If the findings list is truncated, the result is still FAIL, because a concrete finding exists.
  - Clean gives `NormalizedEvidence.Pass(...)`. The runner currently downgrades this to final INCOMPLETE (advisory) with PluginRawOutcome PASS, because external plugins cannot hold legacy-v1 authority (GateRunner.cs:396-403).
  - No changed production units gives `new NormalizedEvidence(GateOutcome.NotApplicable, ..., NotApplicableRationale:"no changed production C# units")`.
- `ValidateAfterExecution`: re-hash the materialized files, because the runner's `VerifyMaterializedInputs` is anti-slop-only. A mismatch gives INCOMPLETE.
- `AppendBoundArtifacts`: write `dry-report.json` (canonical sorted JSON, full findings including WATCHED), `dry-manifest.json` and `dry-binding.json`. The binding file is needed because the runner's `anti-slop-binding.json` sidecar is keyed to anti-slop's plugin id. It holds repository{base, head, tree}, plugin ids and identities, tool{version, format, executableIdentity}, the allowlist oid and the thresholds. Files are written with `FileMode.CreateNew`, and each is recorded as `artifactHashes[name]="sha256:<hex>"`.
- Phase 0 weakness: the factory, not the runner's lease, deletes the materialized root.

PHASE 1 (recommended small runner generalization):
- Introduce `IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin` (marker plus `DetectInputs` semantics) to replace the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks.
- Add `IRepositoryDiffScopeReader.ReadDiffScopeAsync(baseCommit, headCommit, timeout, ct)` returning `RepositoryDiffScope(Head: RepositoryEvaluationScope, BaseMaterializedRoot, BaseObjectIds, ChangedPaths, Hunks)`. `GitRepositoryIdentityReader` already runs `git diff --name-only -z <base> <head>` and reads base blobs; it would add `-U0` hunk parsing and base materialization.
- The runner then owns materialization, `FrozenMaterializationLease` disposal, post-run re-hash, and InputSetIdentity, which is computed over sorted (side, path, oid, mode, ignored) entries for base and head, plus canonical hunks, plus the allowlist oid.

### Determinism and receipts

PINNED:
- dfdry is a self-contained single-file linux-x64 build:
  `dotnet publish -c Release -r linux-x64 --self-contained -p:PublishSingleFile=true -p:Deterministic=true -p:ContinuousIntegrationBuild=true -p:PublishReadyToRun=false`
  - The .NET 10 SDK is fixed in global.json with rollForward `disable`.
  - Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp is pinned to an exact version, for example the 5.0.x line matching the .NET 10 SDK (verify), and System.IO.Hashing to an exact version, both locked via packages.lock.json with RestoreLockedMode.
  - The executable bytes are therefore the whole runtime closure: one file containing the runtime, Roslyn and the algorithm.
- `ReviewedDfdryLinuxX64ClosureSha256` is hard-coded in the plugin, like anti-slop's `ReviewedLinuxX64GnuRuntimeClosureSha256`. A mismatch gives INCOMPLETE.
- `dfdry.config.json` is code-owned and lives in the host bundle, not the repo. It holds thresholds, k/w, LanguageVersion, preprocessor symbols, path filters and boilerplate rules.

HASHED IDENTITIES:
