- ConfigurationIdentity = SHA-256 over exe bytes, config bytes, allowlist blob oid and bytes, manifest bytes, and each env key/value in ordinal order. The env is fully code-owned with no secrets, so it can safely be hashed.
- RuntimeClosureIdentity = SHA-256 of the exe bytes plus the config.
- ExecutableIdentity = `sha256:<exe>`.
- InputSetIdentity = SHA-256 over the sorted list of (side, path, oid, mode, excludedFlag), plus canonical hunks, plus the allowlist oid.
- The receipt's command envelope gets FileName, argv and Environment verbatim. Code never enters argv; only the manifest path does.

DETERMINISM:
- Syntax-only parsing is a pure function of bytes plus LanguageVersion plus symbols.
- Globalization invariant mode is set; there is no culture-dependent formatting.
- Fingerprints use XxHash64 with a fixed seed. Identities use SHA-256.
- Parsing may run in parallel, but units are sorted by (path ordinal, span start) before indexing.
- Every emitted collection is sorted; output does not depend on Dictionary iteration order.
- Candidate search is exact brute force, with no randomized LSH.
- The JSON writer uses fixed property order and no timestamps in the report. Timing lives only in CommandResult.
- RERUN: the same base/head/tree, closure, config and allowlist produce a byte-identical `dry-report.json`. A CI test asserts this across two runs and across a cold versus warm oid cache.

INCOMPLETE when:
- closure, config or exe hash mismatch;
- manifest/request base or head mismatch;
- a materialized blob re-hash mismatch, before or after the run;
- the protected allowlist is malformed or has a schema error;
- a parse error inside a changed unit;
- budgets exceeded: more than 400k units, more than 64 MiB source per side, more than 2,000 changed units, or the time budget (shared gate deadline);
- report counts do not reconcile;
- exit code is not 0 or 1, or the exit code disagrees with the findings;
- stdout is truncated with zero findings;
- the process timed out, was cancelled or did not reach quiescence;
- base is not an ancestor of head (merge-base differs), so the hunks are ambiguous;
- the repo uses SHA-256 object format and the tool was built SHA-1 only, or the reverse;
- clean result: always advisory under current authority rules (final INCOMPLETE, raw PASS).

### Engram usage

The verdict path makes no Engram calls. Engram indexes only the default branch, is networked, and its index state is not a frozen input, so it cannot be part of a reproducible receipt. Engram is used offline and in advisory sidecars only:
- (1) Seeding and calibration. `get_artifact(repo:"engram-csharp", type:"clone_report", key:"all")`, together with the DryUpScan "Watched, not filed" pairs, builds the labelled eval set (known real duplicates versus accepted duplicates). That set tunes the 0.80 Dice and 70-token thresholds.
- (2) Allowlist proposals. `detect_communities` and `search_symbols(kind:"class")` over sibling families (for example the `*BulkIndexer` classes and the tree-sitter HTTP extractors) feed a human-reviewed PR to `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json` on the protected base. Engram output never enters the gate directly; only the reviewed base file does.
- (3) Remediation hints, optional and non-authoritative. After a FAIL, the harness can call `change_impact(repo, changed_files, view:"review")` and `structural_impact(symbol_id of V)` to show the developer the existing helper's callers. This goes in the review comment, not in the receipt.

### Load-bearing duplication handling

The patch cannot exempt itself: there are no in-code suppression comments, and a head-side edit of the allowlist has no authority (same rule as anti-slop-ignore). Five mechanisms instead:

1. NOVELTY RATCHET. Only an increase in a clone class's instance count fails. Existing accepted duplication, moves/renames, and edits inside already-duplicated code never fail.

2. STRUCTURAL BOILERPLATE FILTERS. These are code-owned config, applied before severity. A unit is treated as trivial and dropped when any of these holds:
   - 75% or more of its statements share one statement shape and the run is under 25 statements. This covers DTO mapping `a.X = b.X;`, DI registration chains, and constructor field assignment.
   - It consists only of guard clauses (`ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull`, `if (...) throw`).
   - It is an auto-property, field or attribute list, record/primary-constructor declaration, a using directive, or `Dispose`/`DisposeAsync` pattern code.
   - It is a `LoggerMessage` partial or a `switch` arm table where every arm is a single return of a literal.
   The 70-token / 10-line / 4-statement minimums remove small idioms.

3. HOT-PATH EXEMPTION, anchored to the base. A pair is exempt only if V, as it exists in the BASE tree, already carries runtime-effective hot-path markers:
   - the attributes `[MethodImpl(MethodImplOptions.AggressiveInlining)]` or `[SkipLocalsInit]`;
   - `unsafe`, `stackalloc`, or `Vector128`/`Vector256`/`Vector<T>`;
   - `ref struct` containment.
   U must also carry the same marker. The author cannot fake the base side; adding an attribute to U alone does not exempt.

4. PROTECTED-BASE ALLOWLIST `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json`. Read at the base commit via `ProtectedGitPolicyResolver`-style `git show`. Entries are either:
   - families: a glob set plus a required rationale and owner, for example `src/Engram.Indexing/Vcs/*BulkIndexer.cs` with "per-VCS by design: auth/paging semantics differ"; or
   - clone-class signatures (an L2 hash).
   A NEW class whose every instance falls inside one family is downgraded to WATCHED. A family adding a 6th sibling still fails if the duplicated region is 25 or more statements, unless the entry sets `maxRegionStatements`. Adding or changing entries requires a separate reviewed PR to base, so exemption is a governed decision, not a per-patch escape hatch. Stale entries are reported, not fatal.

5. TIERING. Two-instance T3 clones under 25 statements are WATCHED: recorded with a hash in the artifact but not failing. This keeps borderline near-misses from blocking while preserving the evidence trail.

### Cost and latency

$0 marginal cost, no network. Estimate for engram-csharp (about 150k LOC, about 2.5-3.5k .cs files, about 25k units) on a 4-vCPU CI runner, unmeasured (needs a benchmark spike).

Typical 300-line patch:

| Step | Cold cache | Warm oid cache |
|---|---|---|
| Git materialization (ls-tree x2, cat-file --batch, diff -U0) | 1-2 s | same |
| Single-file startup | ~0.15 s | same |
| Roslyn parse of head corpus (parallel) | 1.5-3 s | 0.2 s (only changed files re-parsed; the cache is a host-owned, content-addressed store outside the repo, keyed by closure identity + oid) |
| Winnowing index | ~0.3 s | same |
| Vector brute force (~30 changed units x 25k) | <0.1 s | same |
| Alignment | ms | ms |
| Base novelty pass (base versions of changed/deleted files only, plus candidate V files reused by oid) | <0.5 s | same |
| Total wall time | ~4-8 s | ~2-3 s |

Peak RSS is about 0.8-1.5 GB holding the syntax trees. Discarding trees after unit extraction keeps it under 500 MB.

Build effort and CI cost are one-off: a publish of about 70 MB single-file.

### Precision and recall expectation

CATCHES:
- Type-1, exact or whitespace/comment-different, above 70 tokens and 10 lines: about 100% recall.
- Type-2 with consistent renaming, including literal-value changes: high recall via L2. Blind or inconsistent renames are caught via statement-shape LCS when the structure matches.
- Type-3 gapped (inserted, deleted or modified statements; 80% statement-shape Dice or more; gaps of 3 statements or fewer): good recall for copy-paste-then-edit, which is the dominant issue-238 pattern (sibling indexers, extractors, per-table Store methods).
- Intra-patch duplication: new code copied twice within the PR.

MISSES:
- Type-4 semantic clones: a LINQ pipeline versus a loop, different API calls with the same intent, async versus sync variants.
- Duplication below the thresholds.
- Duplicated logic split across differently shaped helpers.
- Cross-language duplication: C# versus SQL, TS or bicep.
- Duplicated constants and magic strings, since literals are normalized away.
- Duplication against code outside the repo (other HoT repos).
- Duplicated test code (excluded by default).
- Code hidden behind preprocessor symbols not in the configured set.

OVER-FLAGS (expected false-FAIL sources, to calibrate):
- Interface-mandated sibling implementations not yet in the allowlist.
- Methods differing only in a long SQL or route string literal. Literal normalization makes them L2-equal; this is arguably a genuine "parameterize" smell, but some are deliberate.
- Enum or switch dispatch tables with real logic per arm.
- Option or validator classes with parallel shapes.
- Short structurally identical bodies in different domains that cross the 70-token bar (for example retry or try-catch-log wrappers). Arguably real DRY opportunities, but contentious.

Expected precision on FAIL-severity findings after calibration on the clone_report and watched set: about 85-90%. Recall on type-1 to type-3 at or above the thresholds: about 90%. Both are guesses that require measurement on 50-100 replayed merged engram-csharp PRs.

### Failure modes

- Contract gap: Phase 0 relies on a code-owned request factory for base/head materialization and cleanup. The runner's frozen-scope, lease and re-hash machinery stays anti-slop-only (GateRunner.cs:861) until the IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin generalization lands.
- CaptureBeforeAsync exceptions for a non-anti-slop plugin map to ERROR rather than INCOMPLETE (GateRunner.cs:255-265). Pin and closure mismatches must be surfaced via ValidateBeforeExecution/ValidateAfterExecution, or the runner catch must be generalized.
- Final PASS is never authoritative today: external plugins lack legacy-v1 authority, so a clean result surfaces as final INCOMPLETE with raw PASS (GateRunner.cs:396-403). Only FAIL is a hard outcome. Policy must treat raw PASS plus advisory INCOMPLETE as non-blocking, or the gate blocks every PR.
- Gaming: an author can split a copied block into two helpers just under the 70-token / 10-line minimums, or reorder statements to drop Dice below 0.80. The ratchet does not stop deliberate evasion; human review still owns that.
- Hot-path exemption abuse is limited: it requires the BASE counterpart V to already carry the marker. A brand-new duplicated hot path in both U and a new V passes as intra-patch only if both carry markers. That is a known hole, so flag intra-patch hot-path pairs as WATCHED, not exempt.
- Novelty mis-mapping: heavy refactors that rename a member and change its body below the 0.95 move threshold look like a new instance. This can produce a false FAIL when code moved and was edited in the same patch.
- Literal normalization turns parallel per-table Store methods that differ only in SQL text into T2 clones. That is correct per issue 238, but may block intentionally separate queries until an allowlist entry lands.
- Preprocessor-dependent code (#if DEBUG / NET8_0) parsed with one fixed symbol set hides or invents duplicates in conditional regions.
- Memory/time budget on very large repos or mega-patches (codegen dumps): budget exceeded gives INCOMPLETE, which blocks if the gate is required. That is intended, but noisy for vendored-code imports unless the protected base excludes them.
- Allowlist governance drift: families broaden over time into a de facto bypass. Mitigation: stale and unused entry reporting, required owner and rationale, and a maxRegionStatements cap.
- Same-UID host cannot make the installed dfdry closure immutable, so there is no post-snapshot mutation detection beyond re-hashing exe bytes after execution (same limitation as anti-slop, docs/quality-gates.md:50).
- Threshold drift versus the weekly jscpd sweep: different tokenizers mean a clone the gate passes may later be filed by the sweep, or the reverse. Calibration must align both or retire jscpd's per-patch role.

### Implementation sketch

NEW TOOL: `tools/DeliveryForge.DryScan/` (net10.0 console, one type per file).
- `Program.cs`: `scan` / `--version` verbs.
- `DryScanManifest.cs`: manifest + file entry + hunk records, combined with a one-line justification as compositionally bound DTOs.
- `ManifestVerifier.cs`: git blob hashing.
- `ProductionPathFilter.cs`
- `CSharpUnitExtractor.cs`: Roslyn `CSharpSyntaxWalker` over members and statement runs.
- `TokenNormalizer.cs`: the L1, L2-consistent and statement-shape views.
- `WinnowingIndex.cs`: k=25, w=20, XxHash64 seed 0.
- `CharacteristicVector.cs`: SyntaxKind counts and distance.
- `StatementAligner.cs`: LCS/Dice with a max-gap constraint.
- `CloneClassBuilder.cs`
- `NoveltyRatchet.cs`: base/head instance counts and move detection.
- `BoilerplateFilter.cs`
- `HotPathExemption.cs`
- `DryAllowList.cs`: schema plus glob/signature matching.
- `SeverityClassifier.cs`
- `DryScanReportWriter.cs`: canonical JSON `dry-scan-json-v1`.
- `OidUnitCache.cs`

Pins:
- global.json SDK 10.0.x with rollForward `disable`.
- Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp exact version (5.0.x; verify against the pinned SDK).
- System.IO.Hashing exact version.
- packages.lock.json with RestoreLockedMode=true.
- Deterministic single-file publish for linux-x64, plus win-x64 for local advisory runs, with a separate reviewed closure hash per RID.
- Optional later: tree-sitter (native lib + C# bindings) for TS/Go, pinned as part of the same single-file closure. Defer; not needed for engram-csharp.

DELIVERY-FORGE (`components/evidence/DeliveryForge.Evidence/Adapters/`):
- `DryStructuralEvaluationPlugin.cs`
- `DryStructuralRequestFactory.cs`: git diff -U0 / ls-tree / cat-file, materialization, manifest.
- `DryScanReport.cs` plus `DryScanReportNormalizer.cs`: count reconciliation into NormalizedEvidence.
- `DrySnapshot.cs`
- `AdvisoryDryComposition.cs`: mirrors `AdvisoryAntiSlopComposition`, with a sample CLI.
- Registration: one `TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistration` with the reviewed closure.

Phase 1 runner work:
- `IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin.cs`, `IRepositoryDiffScopeReader.cs`, `RepositoryDiffScope.cs`.
- Replace the roughly 10 `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks in GateRunner.cs.
- Extend `GitRepositoryIdentityReader` with hunks and base materialization.

Tests:
- Golden fixture repos with labelled T1/T2/T3 positives, boilerplate negatives, move/rename, an edit inside pre-existing duplication, an intra-patch clone, a hot-path pair, and allowlist family behaviour.
- Byte-identical rerun test and cold/warm cache equivalence.
- An INCOMPLETE matrix: tampered blob, closure mismatch, parse error, budget, truncated stdout.
- Engram-csharp replay harness: the last 100 merged PRs plus the watched pairs, reporting precision and recall.

Effort, roughly 4-6 dev-weeks:
- Tool: about 2,000-2,500 LOC, 2-3 weeks.
- Plugin and factory: about 800 LOC, 1 week.
- Runner generalization: about 300 LOC plus tests, about 1 week.
- Calibration and allowlist seeding on engram-csharp: about 1 week.

### Novelty claim

This is the owner's option 1 made concrete. What it adds beyond "run a deterministic clone detector":
- (a) A per-patch NOVELTY RATCHET. It compares clone-class instance counts between the frozen base and head trees, with move detection. The gate fails only on duplication the patch introduces, never on legacy duplication or relocations. Neither jscpd 4.2.5 nor CPD nor dupFinder can do this, and the weekly sweep cannot either.
- (b) Load-bearing exemptions that cannot be bypassed from the patch. A protected-base allowlist of sibling families and clone signatures, with the same authority model as anti-slop-ignore, plus a hot-path exemption that requires the BASE counterpart to already carry runtime-effective markers.
- (c) WATCHED versus FAIL severity tiering that encodes DryUpScan's filing thresholds as gate rules, so near-miss clones are receipted but non-blocking.
- (d) Exact brute-force candidate generation (no LSH), plus oid-keyed parse caching. This makes the result byte-reproducible and fast enough to be a blocking gate.
- (e) Each FAIL names the pre-existing symbol to reuse or extract.

### Open questions

- Will the owner accept the Phase 1 GateRunner generalization (IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin, IRepositoryDiffScopeReader with hunks plus base materialization), or must Phase 0 ship first with factory-owned materialization?
- Should a clean DRY result ever become authoritative PASS? Today external plugins cannot mint legacy-v1 authority, so only FAIL is a hard signal.
- Are test projects in scope? DryUpScan is production-only; test-helper duplication is often deliberate.
- Should literal-only differences (SQL/route text) count as type-2 duplication, given issue 238's per-table Store targets? Or should they be a separate 'parameterizable' severity?
- What fixed preprocessor symbol set should be used, and should multi-target projects be parsed once per target framework?
- Allowlist governance: who owns `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json` in the protected base, and is a required rationale/owner/expiry enough to stop it becoming a bypass?
- Exact Roslyn package pin compatible with the .NET 10 SDK on the runner. Verify that a deterministic single-file publish yields a stable hash across build agents.
- Can the engram-csharp clone_report artifact plus the ~40 watched pairs and replayed merged PRs give enough labelled data to calibrate the 0.80 Dice / 70-token / 25-statement thresholds?
- Should this gate complement the weekly jscpd sweep (keep both), or replace its per-patch role? Do their thresholds need to be unified?
- Is tree-sitter coverage for non-C# languages needed now, or is C#-only acceptable for engram-csharp?

### Judge verdicts

**contract-fit (7/10).** Strong fit overall. The tool is a single pinned file, so the runtime closure is the executable bytes, and a hard-coded reviewed hash makes that identity reproducible. Config, executable and input-set identities are all defined over frozen bytes. The ratchet and allowlist are anchored to the protected base, not the patch head, so the candidate cannot self-exempt. Uncertainty paths (count reconciliation, truncation, parse errors, budgets, exit-code mismatch, tampered blobs) all become INCOMPLETE. The proposal also states its contract gaps honestly: GateRequest carries no diff, the anti-slop-only type checks in GateRunner, and CaptureBefore exceptions mapping to ERROR. The mapping onto the plugin members is mostly clean, and the verdict path makes no Engram calls. I did not open the contract files or GateRunner.cs myself, so the line-number claims about it are unchecked. Deductions: (1) Phase 0 puts materialization, the base allowlist read and cleanup in a factory outside the runner lease, so the frozen-input guarantee rests on trusted-but-unreviewed host code and a same-UID TOCTOU window. (2) The parse cache is a hidden input not covered by the receipt identities, so poisoning or staleness is possible. (3) A clean result is only advisory today, so the gate is FAIL-only until the runner is generalized. (4) Phase 0 pushes closure mismatch into ValidateBefore/After workarounds instead of the natural CaptureBefore path. (5) NOT_APPLICABLE rests on a vaguely specified policy allowance. (6) The merge-base/ancestor check and SHA-256 object-format handling are listed as INCOMPLETE conditions without saying where they are enforced. (7) Evasion by splitting code under the thresholds is a verdict-influence path the author controls, though that is a recall limit and not a receipt bypass.

Strongest weakness: Phase 0 depends on a code-owned request factory doing materialization, base-allowlist reading and cleanup outside the runner's frozen-scope, lease and re-hash machinery. The verdict's input integrity then relies on unreviewed host code, and the oid-keyed parse cache sits outside the receipt identities.

Suggested fix: Make Phase 1 a prerequisite, or at least ship the IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin and diff-scope reader first. The runner should then own materialization, the lease, post-run re-hash and InputSetIdentity, and should map CaptureBefore mismatches to INCOMPLETE. Include the allowlist oid, the config digest and a cache-disabled or cache-closure-keyed attestation in the identities. Prove with a test that the cold-cache and warm-cache reports are byte-identical and that a poisoned cache entry yields INCOMPLETE.

**accuracy (5.5/10).** Strong on type-1/2 recall and on avoiding false-FAIL via a base-anchored novelty ratchet, protected allowlist and tiering. Winnowing (k=25, w=20) plus exact vector brute force gives credible coverage for byte-identical wrappers across the 5 bulk indexers, per-table Stores and a renamed copy of a helper (L2 alpha-renaming handles consistent renames). Weaknesses on the issue-238 shapes and precision: (1) The 85%-identical mechanical-difference methods are the hard case. T3 needs Dice>=0.80 with gaps <=3 AND winnowed token coverage >=0.75; with mechanical differences in 15% of statements (often scattered, e.g. type names, API calls differing per VCS) coverage can fall below 0.75 or gaps interrupt the run. Literal-to-kind normalization helps but identifier-to-$N renaming is per-unit, so different type/API names break L2 equality and push everything onto the statement-shape LCS, which is blind to what changed. (2) The 70-token/10-line floor and 4-statement unit minimum mean the roughly 40 known 11-28 line sub-threshold clones mostly land in WATCHED, not FAIL, because FAIL requires >=80 LOC T1, >=90% whole-member coverage, >=3 instances in 3 files, or >=25 statements. So the new-sibling case (a 2nd or 3rd copy of an 11-28 line helper) often only passes as WATCHED; effective recall of blocking behaviour for the dominant issue shape is much lower than the claimed 90%. (3) The novelty ratchet's 'head count > base count' rule on a sibling family works for a 6th indexer but is exactly what the family allowlist then downgrades, and the allowlist example (*BulkIndexer.cs 'per-VCS by design') would silence the very duplication issue 238 targets; recall depends heavily on governance. (4) Move/rename mis-mapping can false-FAIL on refactors (acknowledged). (5) Gameability: splitting a method into helpers below 70 tokens/10 lines, statement reordering, extracting each half into separate methods, or converting loop/LINQ all evade; the intra-patch case is not meaningfully protected. Statement-shape blindness to identifiers also creates false matches (different callee, same shape) which the thresholds only partly mitigate. (6) Boilerplate filters are sensible (75% same-shape, guard-only, records, generated excluded), but test arrange blocks are excluded only via path, and DI registration chains, switch tables with real arms, and try/catch/log wrappers crossing 70 tokens remain predicted false-FAIL sources; precision 85-90% is an unmeasured guess. (7) Literal normalization makes per-table SQL Stores T2, which will likely FAIL legitimate separate queries until allowlisted. Overall: solid deterministic backbone, honest about type-4 misses, but the severity gates are tuned to filing thresholds rather than per-change blocking, leaving real recall gaps on short and semi-identical sibling code while still carrying notable false-positive and evasion risk.

Strongest weakness: FAIL severity requires large or 3-file clones (>=80 LOC, >=25 statements, >=90% whole-member, or 3 instances in 3 files), so the typical issue-238 shape (11-28 line helper copied a 2nd/3rd time, or 85%-similar methods with scattered mechanical differences) lands in WATCHED and passes, while T3 coverage >=0.75 plus per-unit identifier alpha-renaming erodes recall on exactly the near-identical sibling methods.

Suggested fix: Make severity gate-oriented instead of sweep-oriented: FAIL when a patch adds a NEW instance (U in changed unit, V pre-existing) of a T1/T2 class or T3 Dice>=0.85 at >=10 lines/70 tokens, regardless of file count, with the family allowlist only able to downgrade after a per-family maxRegionStatements. Improve T3 recall by aligning on call-target and type-name-aware statement shapes (keep external callee names, abstract only local identifiers) and by adding parameterization-aware matching (diff of two bodies = set of consistent substitutions, so 'same skeleton, N substitutions' is flagged as extractable). Add anti-evasion via clone detection over unit pairs after inlining-by-extraction (match method bodies against concatenated sibling helpers) and replay-calibrate on the ~40 watched pairs and last 100 PRs before enabling blocking.

**cost-ops (6/10).** Runtime cost is excellent: deterministic, zero marginal cost, no network or keys, and the verdict path does not depend on Engram index freshness. The 4-8 s cold and 2-3 s warm estimates are plausible, and it is the owner's preferred tier. Held back by an optimistic 4-6 week estimate that omits the replay and calibration harness, a high pin burden (large single-file build per RID, closure hash re-approval on every Roslyn or algorithm change, unverified cross-agent hash stability), an unspecified cache size and eviction policy, unmeasured latency and memory figures, and the fact that a clean result is never authoritative PASS under current runner rules, so only FAIL blocks until Phase 1 lands.

Strongest weakness: Implementation and maintenance cost is underestimated and unmeasured: the estimate omits calibration tooling, the cost table is a guess, and every Roslyn or algorithm change forces a re-pinned, re-reviewed closure hash with unverified reproducibility across build agents.

Suggested fix: Run a 2-3 day benchmark spike first (minimal Roslyn parse plus winnowing, timed cold and warm on real engram-csharp head). Ship v1 as advisory with only type-1/type-2 winnowing plus the novelty ratchet, defer type-3 vector and LCS work until replay data justifies it, specify cache limits and eviction, verify build-agent hash reproducibility early, and budget the replay and calibration harness explicitly.

### Adversarial refutation

**Refuted: true (major).** Claim attacked: That the Phase 0 pipeline (per-unit L2 alpha-renamed winnowing plus whole-unit characteristic vectors) runs under the unmodified delivery-forge runner and yields FAIL on the issue-238 clone shapes: 11-28 line copy-pasted blocks and near-identical sibling methods.

Evidence:

Two independent failures.

(A) Contract: Phase 0 sets CommandInvocation.WorkingDirectory to the materialized root. GateRunner.RunAsync calls ValidateRequest(request) first (GateRunner.cs:74, unguarded). ValidateRequest (:701-706) throws ArgumentException ("not the parent-frozen host root") unless Path.GetFullPath(Invocation.WorkingDirectory) equals _trustedWorkingDirectory. ValidateCapabilityBinding (:760-766) likewise requires Capability.ProbeWorkingDirectory to equal the trusted root. Only the anti-slop path swaps in the materialized root, and it does so after validation (:179-183), from the scope reader's result. So the plan as written throws before any gate runs. Secondary: in Phase 0 the receipt's InputSetIdentity comes from Bind -> ComputeInputSetIdentity(paths only) (DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs). The sha256 over (side, path, oid, mode, hunks, allowlist) the design promises exists only in Phase 1, because ValidateAutomaticScopeAsync returns early for non-anti-slop plugins (:861).

(B) Algorithm: the clone shape that issue 238 and jscpd actually report is a 10-30 line region inside larger, otherwise different methods. The design alpha-renames identifiers per unit, so the first distinct identifier becomes $1 and so on, and winnows that L2 stream. A shared sub-block gets different $n numbering whenever the code before it in either host unit introduces or reorders identifiers. The L2 fingerprints then differ, and T1/T2 region verification, which also uses L2, never sees the pair. Per-unit renaming only preserves matches when the clone starts at the unit start, i.e. whole-method clones. The fallback does not recover it either: characteristic vectors are computed over the whole unit subtree, so a 15-line clone inside an 80-line method is dominated by the unrelated code (the 0.25 L1 distance and 1.5x size gate both fail), and the statement-shape LCS is only run on candidates that already passed one of these two filters.

Winnowing arithmetic is also inconsistent. The guarantee for k=25, w=20 is detection of a shared run of 44 or more tokens, but it only guarantees about 1 shared fingerprint there. A 70-token run has 46 k-grams, 27 windows, and a guaranteed minimum of ceil(27/20)=2 distinct fingerprints. The candidate rule requires 3 or more, so a threshold-sized (70 token) T1 clone can legitimately fail to become a candidate.

Even when a pair is found, the typical shape lands in WATCHED. Per the severity rules, FAIL needs 80 or more LOC, or 25 or more statements, or 3 instances in 3 files, or at least 90% of a whole member. Most of the 11-28 line pairs on the watched list therefore pass. Together these mean the mechanism does not produce the advertised FAIL verdict on the dominant issue-238 shapes.

Salvage:

(1) Phase 0: keep Invocation.WorkingDirectory equal to the trusted host root and pass the absolute manifest path in argv (the tool does not need to run from the materialized root). Alternatively go straight to the Phase 1 runner change that sets the materialized root after ValidateRequest, as anti-slop does. Document that InputSetIdentity is paths-only until Phase 1, and carry oids, hunks and the allowlist oid in ConfigurationIdentity. (2) Winnow an identifier-blind stream (identifiers to ID, literals to kind) rather than the per-unit alpha-renamed one, with k and w chosen so that the 3-fingerprint rule is guaranteed at the token minimum (e.g. require 1-2 shared fingerprints, then verify). Apply consistent alpha-renaming only inside the aligned candidate region during verification, never over the whole unit. Compute the characteristic-vector fallback per statement run or sliding window of statements, not per whole unit. (3) Re-tier severity so that a new (not legacy) 2-instance T1/T2 clone at or above the jscpd minimums (10 lines / 70 tokens) is FAIL. Keep the 3-in-3-files, 80-LOC and 25-statement rules for the legacy and allowlisted families.

**Refuted: true (fatal).** Claim attacked: That the gate separates real duplication from load-bearing or boilerplate duplication with no bypass, and that a pressured coding agent cannot cheaply evade it (rename, reorder, split, wrap) or satisfy it with a fake justification. Specifically the scope exclusions, the boilerplate filter, the novelty ratchet's "move" rule, the hot-path exemption, the path-glob allowlist families, and the FAIL-severity thresholds.

Evidence:

The design closes the "head-side allowlist edit" and "in-code suppression comment" holes. Several other exits are patch-controlled and cost nothing.

1. Scope exclusions are decided by content the patch writes.
 - Excluded: `[GeneratedCode]` types, files with an `<auto-generated>` header, `*.g.cs`, `*.Designer.cs`, and `*Tests*/` paths.
 - An agent can paste `[GeneratedCode("x","1")]` onto a class, or add the header, or name the directory `FooTests`. The duplicated unit then never becomes a changed production unit, and the result is NOT_APPLICABLE or a clean pass.
 - This is the same patch-authored-suppression class of bug the design claims to have eliminated.
 - The attribute is not checked against any protected-base record.

2. The fixed preprocessor set hides compiled code.
 - Only `NET10_0` and `RELEASE` are parsed. Code the agent wraps in `#if NET8_0_OR_GREATER` (or `#if !RELEASE`, or any symbol outside the set) is invisible to the analysis but compiles in the real build.
 - The design lists this only as a "miss", but it is a one-line, deterministic bypass for an adversarial author.

3. The unit minimum is counted in statements, which can be gamed.
 - Units need 4 or more statements, and the T3 region needs 6 or more.
 - Collapsing a copied loop body into one fluent LINQ expression, a big object initializer, or an expression-bodied member gives 1 statement. The "statement-shape LCS" and unit enumeration never see it.
 - Splitting a copied block into helpers of 3 statements or fewer, each under 70 tokens, defeats every minimum. The extracted helpers are themselves clones of each other that nothing ever evaluates.

4. T3 is defeated by cheap padding.
 - Dice = 2*LCS / (|U|+|V|), with a maximum gap of 3 statements.
 - Inserting 2 junk statements (`var t = a;`, `_ = x;`) per 2 original statements gives Dice of about 0.67, under 0.80, with gaps of 2.
 - Reordering independent statements breaks the LCS outright.
 - Replacing `foreach` with `for`, or `if/else` with a ternary, changes SyntaxKind and the shape hash.
 - Per-unit alpha renaming helps only the renaming case; the structural rewrites above are untouched.
 - The `0.75` token-coverage conjunct drops with the same padding.

5. The boilerplate filter is a laundering device.
 - A unit is dropped when 75% or more of its statements share one shape and the run is under 25 statements.
 - An agent pads the copied method with 9 repeated `x.A = y.A;` or `_log.X(...)` statements around 3 real logic statements. The whole unit is classified as DTO mapping and filtered before severity.
 - The filter is computed over the whole unit, not over the shared region, so the copied logic is exempt.

6. The "move" exemption in the novelty ratchet is a copy launderer.
 - A new unit U is treated as a move if some base unit W, "deleted or changed in head", has L2-equality or Dice of 0.95 or more with U, and W~V already held in base.
 - In the issue-238 scenario at least two siblings V1 and V2 already exist. The agent copies V1 to U and edits one token in V1 (a rename, a log line).
 - W=V1 is now "changed", U equals base V1 and V1~V2 held in base, so U is classified as a move and never counted.
 - The text says "deleted or changed" and never requires that no head instance of W remains.
 - The ratchet also has no defined class identity across base and head. The T3 signature is the sorted member keys, so adding or renaming one member yields a new signature with base count 0. A patch that bug-fixes one sibling of a pre-existing 3-file clone can then show up as a NEW class with 3 files and FAIL. That is a false-positive source on legacy duplication the patch merely touches.

7. The hot-path exemption's runtime effectiveness is unverifiable in a syntax-only parser.
 - Markers are `[MethodImpl(AggressiveInlining)]`, `[SkipLocalsInit]`, `unsafe`, `stackalloc` and `Vector<T>`.
 - If any base counterpart V is already marked (common in this repo), the agent copies V and keeps or adds the attribute. `[SkipLocalsInit]` is inert without `unsafe`, and it is free to add.
 - The stated hole (new U and new V, both marked, only WATCHED) means writing the same marked code twice in one patch passes.

8. Allowlist families are path-glob matched, and the patch controls paths.
 - A family such as `src/Engram.Indexing/Vcs/*BulkIndexer.cs` downgrades every NEW class whose instances all fall in the family.
 - The agent just names its new file `XyzBulkIndexer.cs` in that directory. Anything under 25 statements passes, and `maxRegionStatements` lets an entry remove even that cap.
 - Rationale and owner are free text and unvalidated. There is no expiry, and stale entries are non-fatal.

9. Allowlist governance creates a deadlock and an approval-pressure loop.
 - The allowlist is read only from the protected base. A legitimate sibling therefore cannot land until a separate allowlist PR merges first.
 - That pushes people toward broad globs and rubber-stamp entries.
 - If the harness lets the agent open that PR, or lets the "base" be a branch the agent controls, the protection is moot. The design never states who may merge the allowlist PR or how the base is pinned to protected history.

10. The FAIL thresholds leave the target case unenforced, and the evasions are easy.
 - FAIL needs T1 of 80 or more LOC, or 90% or more of a whole member with 2 or more instances, or 3 instances in 3 distinct files, or a region of 25 or more statements. Everything else is only WATCHED.
 - The typical issue-238 case is an 11-28 line helper copied once or twice, or an 85%-similar method. It is WATCHED and passes.
 - The cheapest evasions are adding 2 lines so whole-member coverage drops under 90%, copying into only 2 files, or putting the second copy in the same file as the first.
 - The "fails until DRY" requirement is therefore not met. This is a large-clone gate, not a DRY gate. The judges' accuracy weakness is correct, and it doubles as the gaming surface.

11. Type-4 clones are an explicit miss, and the design misattributes this to cost. A loop-to-LINQ rewrite, or an async copy of a sync method, defeats the gate by construction. That is the dominant way an LLM agent "fixes" a flagged clone without extracting anything.

12. Minor: the oid-keyed parse cache sits outside the receipt identities (a judges' note), and Phase 0 factory materialization has no lease or re-hash guarantee.

Net: with the design as written, an agent needs zero justification and at most a few seconds of mechanical edits to pass (items 1, 2, 5, 6, 8), and even naive copy-paste mostly lands in WATCHED (item 10). Meanwhile structural false FAILs occur on touched legacy clones (item 6) and on deliberate per-table sibling Stores, which pushes owners toward blanket allowlist globs.

Salvage:

Keep the deterministic Roslyn ratchet as the cheap first stage and treat its clean result as advisory only. Fix these specific items.

(a) Drop all in-code and in-path exclusions that the patch can author.
 - Exclude generated or test code only by protected-base path globs or `.editorconfig generated_code`, read from base.
 - Treat `[GeneratedCode]`, the `<auto-generated>` header and `*Tests*` naming as a signal to flag when newly added by the patch, not as an exemption.

(b) Parse every `#if` branch, or union the branches, so no compiled code is hidden.

(c) Measure unit and region minimums in tokens and non-blank lines, not statements. Collapse fluent expressions to their token count. Also compute clone coverage at the token level with gap tolerance, so padding and loop-to-LINQ rewrites do not drop it below threshold.

(d) Make the novelty ratchet net: head instance count of a class must not exceed base count, minus instances deleted in head. Define class identity by L2 hash (or region hash), not by member keys. A "move" requires that W no longer exists in head, not merely that it was changed.

(e) Run the boilerplate filter only on the shared clone region, and require the matching shape run to be contiguous and not padded. Count the filter's use in the receipt and cap how much of a unit it can exempt.

(f) For the hot-path exemption, require markers on both sides in BASE for U's own base counterpart, or drop it. Use an allowlist signature instead.

(g) Make allowlist family entries bind to a clone signature or an exact file set, not a glob that new files can match. Require a mandatory expiry, a CODEOWNERS-enforced review, and a `maxRegionStatements` ceiling the entry cannot raise. State how the base is pinned to protected history.

(h) Lower the FAIL tier for newly introduced 2-instance T1/T2 clones to about 10 lines and 70 tokens, using the allowlist as the relief valve. Otherwise stop calling the gate "DRY".

(i) Add a bounded cheap-judge stage for the type-3/4 and "is this boilerplate or load-bearing" decisions. Feed it the deterministic candidate pairs plus the base-side justification, never the author's own justification, and treat its verdict as INCOMPLETE-or-FAIL, never as an exemption. This closes the semantic-rewrite and padding evasions, which deterministic matching alone cannot.


## A.det-token-clone: jscpd Patch-Ratchet DRY Gate (plugin id "jscpd.dry-patch")

Panel mean 6 (contract-fit 7, accuracy 5, cost-ops 6). Tier: deterministic.

**One line.** Pinned jscpd 4.2.5 runs over the whole frozen head C# tree twice: once on raw text and once on an identifier/literal-normalized copy that keeps line numbers. Host code keeps only clones that touch the patch's added lines. It groups them into clone classes keyed by token hash, and fails only when a class has more occurrences in head than in base (NEW clone mass) above sweep-aligned thresholds. Exceptions come from a content-addressed allowlist read from the protected base, never from the patch.

### Mechanism

Inputs: base commit B and head commit H (request.ExpectedRepository), both frozen. Everything below is a pure function of the git objects, the pinned bundle and the protected-base config.

1. Frozen diff scope (host-trusted, in the generalized GitRepositoryIdentityReader):
   a. `git merge-base --is-ancestor B H`. If false, INCOMPLETE.
   b. `git -c core.quotepath=off diff -z -U0 --no-renames --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --no-color --diff-algorithm=histogram B H -- "*.cs"`. Parse the `@@ -a,b +c,d @@` headers into AddedLineRanges[path] (head-side 1-based ranges; d=0 means a pure deletion, so no range). Binary or unparsable hunks give INCOMPLETE.
   c. Production scope comes from the protected-base config `.delivery-forge/dry-gate.json`, read with `git show B:<path>` the same way anti-slop-ignore is read. Include `src/**/*.cs`. Exclude `**/obj/**`, `**/bin/**`, `*.g.cs`, `*.Designer.cs`, `**/Migrations/*.cs`, test projects, and files whose first 5 lines carry `<auto-generated`. The excludes are evaluated the same way on B and H.
   d. Materialize three host-owned roots under the existing 512 MiB, 64 MiB and 16 MiB ceilings: `head/` (raw head blobs of in-scope files, hash-verified), `base/` (raw base blobs of in-scope files), and `head-norm/`, a line-preserving normalization of every head file. `head-norm/` uses Roslyn's lexer only (Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp `SyntaxFactory.ParseTokens`, version pinned): each IdentifierToken becomes `ID`, each Numeric, String, Char or interpolated literal becomes `LIT`, comments are dropped, and every newline is kept, so line N in head-norm is line N in head.
   e. Suppression scan (like JavaScriptSuppressionScanner):
      - Any `jscpd:ignore-start`, `jscpd:ignore-end` or `<auto-generated` marker inside an added range is a SuppressionViolation, so INCOMPLETE. jscpd honours these markers natively, so they would otherwise be silent bypasses.
      - Pre-existing markers are tolerated only if the same marker exists at base in the same file.
      - A newly added file matching a generated-path exclude is INCOMPLETE unless the protected config marks that directory as generator-owned.
   f. If there are zero added lines in scope, the result is NOT_APPLICABLE with a rationale, but only if the policy's AllowedNotApplicable list contains the gate. Otherwise the runner makes it INCOMPLETE.

2. Execution (the only path the runner allows): Invocation = pinned Node binary, arguments `<bundle>/run-jscpd-frozen.mjs --manifest inputs/manifest.json`, WorkingDirectory = the frozen root, environment cleared to the allow-list SystemRoot, WINDIR, TMP, TEMP, TZ=UTC.
   - The launcher calls jscpd 4.2.5's programmatic `detectClones` twice, over the explicitly listed and ordinally sorted files of `head/` and then `head-norm/`.
   - Options for both passes: format csharp, mode mild, minLines 6, minTokens 50, maxLines 5000, maxSize 2mb, in-memory store, no blame, no gitignore, no config discovery, reporters none, absolute false.
   - The launcher writes one canonical JSON to stdout: `{schema:"dry-jscpd-v1", jscpd:"4.2.5", passes:{raw:[...], norm:[...]}, fileCounts:{raw:n, norm:n}}`. Each clone is `{a:{path,startLine,endLine}, b:{path,startLine,endLine}, tokens}`, sorted. Fragments and dates are dropped, so stdout is small (well under the 64 MiB non-anti-slop cap) and byte-identical on every rerun.
   - Exit codes: 0 means the run finished, anything else means the tool failed.

3. Analysis (host plugin code, in AppendBoundArtifacts, which runs before Normalize and can read stdout.txt from gateDirectory):
   a. Verify that fileCounts equal the manifest counts and every clone path is inside the manifest. Any mismatch is INCOMPLETE.
   b. Keep a clone pair only if at least one side overlaps an AddedLineRange.
   c. For each kept side, compute a clone key: SHA-256 of the Roslyn token stream over whole lines [start, end], using kind plus text for raw and normalized kind for norm.
   d. Union-find all kept pairs into clone classes keyed by token hash. This is independent of the order jscpd reports pairs in. Then add the pre-existing occurrences of the same key by linear Rabin-Karp counting.
   e. Ratchet: count exact occurrences of the class's token sequence in `head/` (Hc) and in `base/` (Bc), using the same lexer. A class is NEW only if Hc > Bc AND at least one occurrence overlaps added lines with at least 50% of its lines added. Editing an existing clone, moving a file, or touching one of the 5 VCS indexers without adding a copy keeps Hc <= Bc, so it never fails.
   f. Boilerplate filter (deterministic, rule-set versioned): drop a class if 80% or more of its tokens fall inside using directives, attribute lists, auto-property or field declarations, `services.Add*` registration statements, or constructor `this.x = x` assignments. Rules are applied to Roslyn trivia-free tokens.
   g. Allowlist: drop a class if its key, or its path-family glob plus a maxTokens bound, appears in `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json` read from base B (never from H).
   h. FAIL rules, any of which fails the gate:
      - R1, new type-1 mass: raw class, NEW, at least 10 lines and 70 tokens. This is the weekly sweep's jscpd floor.
      - R2, sibling proliferation: raw or norm class, NEW, at least 6 lines and 50 tokens, and Hc spans 3 or more distinct files. This is the sweep's sibling rule.
      - R3, renamed type-2: norm class, NEW, at least 15 lines and 120 tokens, with no matching R1 class.
      - R4, aggregate creep: the sum of added lines across NEW classes of at least 6 lines and 50 tokens is 30 or more, spread across 2 or more classes. This blocks splitting one copy into sub-threshold pieces.
      - R5, large block: any NEW class of at least 40 lines, even when it is entirely intra-patch.
   i. NEW classes at or above the floor but under every FAIL rule are recorded as WATCH (the gate's version of "Watched, not filed"); they do not fail.

4. Outcome: at least one FAIL rule hit gives FAIL, with ProductionCapable=true and the class keys plus file:line ranges in Reason. Zero NEW classes above the floor gives PASS. Everything else is INCOMPLETE.

### Plugin contract fit

Class DryClonePatchEvaluationPlugin : IDeterministicEvaluationPlugin.
- Id "jscpd.dry-patch", Version "1.0.0", ExpectedTool "jscpd", CompatibilityAdapter = EvidenceAdapterKind.External. It must be explicitly selected through EvaluationPluginSelection, because External has no default and Resolve throws otherwise.
- Registered by trusted host code through TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistration(GateId "dry", Plugin, ExpectedToolVersion "4.2.5", ExpectedFormatVersion "dry-jscpd-v1", ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity <reviewed sha256>).

DetectInputs(candidateInputs, ignoredInputs): returns every candidate matching the protected-base scope globs (all in-scope head *.cs, not only changed files, because the whole tree is the comparison corpus), sorted ordinally and minus ignoredInputs. The runner then requires NormalizeInputs(DetectInputs) == NormalizeInputs(request.Plugin.Inputs), otherwise INCOMPLETE. That check only happens once the runner's frozen-scope branch applies to this plugin (contract gap 1 below).

ValidateBeforeExecution:
- ERROR if Capability.Version != "4.2.5" or FormatVersion != "dry-jscpd-v1".
- ERROR if the Invocation is not the canonical one: Node path, launcher path, manifest arg, cleared environment, no StandardInput.
- INCOMPLETE if the thresholds file is missing from the protected base.

CaptureBeforeAsync(request, ct): computes a DrySnapshot and returns it as EvaluationPluginSnapshot(State: DrySnapshot). DrySnapshot is a mutable holder with:
- RuntimeClosureIdentity: SHA-256 over package.json, package-lock.json (jscpd 4.2.5 and its lockfile-pinned @jscpd/* closure), run-jscpd-frozen.mjs, node_modules/jscpd, node_modules/@jscpd/*, the Node bytes, and the Roslyn assembly version plus the normalizer rule-set id. It is checked against hard-coded reviewed constants exactly like ValidateReviewedPinsAsync; a mismatch throws InvalidOperationException, which becomes INCOMPLETE.
- ConfigurationIdentity: SHA-256 over the closure identity, the dry-gate.json bytes at B, the dry-allow.json bytes at B, the manifest bytes, the allow-listed environment, and the threshold record.
- ExecutableIdentity: hash of the Node bytes.
- Scope handles: the head/base/head-norm roots and AddedLineRanges.

GetConfigurationIdentity, GetRuntimeClosureIdentity and GetExecutableIdentity return the snapshot values, or "unavailable" when there is no snapshot, mirroring anti-slop.

Execution: the host ICommandExecutor runs Node and the launcher. There is no in-process tool call.

AppendBoundArtifacts(request, snapshot, gateDirectory, hashes):
- Reads stdout.txt (already written at runner step 11) and runs the deterministic analysis from mechanism step 3.
- Writes with FileMode.CreateNew: dry-hunks.json (canonical AddedLineRanges + DiffIdentity), dry-classes.json (every NEW, WATCH, allowlisted and boilerplate-dropped class, with key, Hc, Bc, ranges and rule hits), and dry-binding.json (an anti-slop-binding.json equivalent: gate, base/head/tree, plugin id/version, the identities, tool name/version/format/executable identity, thresholds id, finalAuthority). Each file is recorded as name -> "sha256:<hex>".
- Stores the verdict in DrySnapshot.Analysis.
- Must catch ALL exceptions internally and set Analysis = Failed(reason). The runner catches only IOException and UnauthorizedAccessException, so any other exception would escape the gate.

Normalize(request, execution, snapshot) maps, in order:
- TimedOut, Cancelled, null exit code, or exit code != 0: INCOMPLETE.
- Analysis Failed, or a count or manifest mismatch: INCOMPLETE.
- Any FAIL rule hit: new NormalizedEvidence(GateOutcome.Fail, reason, false, true, limitations).
- No NEW class above the floor: NormalizedEvidence.Pass(reason). This sets ProductionCapable=false, and the runner downgrades it to advisory INCOMPLETE with PluginRawOutcome PASS.
- Zero added lines: new NormalizedEvidence(GateOutcome.NotApplicable, ..., NotApplicableRationale: "no in-scope added C# lines").

ValidateAfterExecution: re-checks that the snapshot closure hash still matches the reviewed constant. Like anti-slop, there is no stronger post-snapshot immutability guarantee.

Contract gaps that require host changes; the plugin cannot do these alone:
1. GateRunner gates frozen-scope validation, materialization, capture limits and capability timing on `plugin is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` (GateRunner.cs lines 115, 260, 273, 285, 296, 381, 785, 861, plus the sidecar code around 499-645). Replace these with a host-owned marker interface, e.g. IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin { FrozenScopeRequirements Requirements }, carrying the extension filter, materializeBase, includeHunks, the suppression scanner and the capture limit. Only trusted host plugins implement it.
2. RepositoryEvaluationScope has no base or diff fields. Add BaseCommit, BaseTreeId, ChangedPaths, AddedLineRanges, BaseMaterializedRoot, NormalizedRoot and DiffIdentity. Extend IRepositoryEvaluationScopeReader.ReadEvaluationScopeAsync with the requirements argument.
3. InputSetIdentity currently covers only the head tree entries, and the runner overwrites the plugin's value with it. It must also fold in BaseTreeId and DiffIdentity, otherwise two patches with the same head and different bases share an identity.
4. The plugin needs a way to see the scope, either a GateRequest.FrozenScope property or an overload CaptureBeforeAsync(request, scope, ct).

### Determinism and receipts

Pinned:
- jscpd 4.2.5 and its full npm lockfile closure, installed in a reviewed host bundle (never from the candidate repo).
- The Node binary bytes. Reuse anti-slop's reviewed Node and add a second reviewed closure constant, e.g. ReviewedDryJscpdRuntimeClosureSha256.
- The run-jscpd-frozen.mjs bytes.
- The Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp package version and a normalizer rule-set id ("cs-lex-norm-v1").
- A boilerplate rule-set id ("dry-boilerplate-v1").
- The threshold record, versioned as "dry-thresholds-v1": floor 6 lines/50 tokens; R1 10/70; R2 6/50 across 3 or more files; R3 15/120 normalized; R4 30 aggregate added lines across 2 or more classes; R5 40 lines; added-line overlap at least 50%.
- Policy files dry-gate.json and dry-allow.json, read only from the protected base B. The patch cannot change its own thresholds or allowlist.

Hashed identities:
- RuntimeClosureIdentity = closure files plus Node bytes plus normalizer/assembly ids.
- ConfigurationIdentity = closure identity, policy bytes at B, manifest bytes, environment allow-list and thresholds.
- InputSetIdentity (extended) = SHA-256(head frozen identity as today || BaseTreeId || DiffIdentity), where DiffIdentity = SHA-256 of canonical sorted (path, startLine, count) added ranges plus the git diff flags string.
- ExecutableIdentity = Node bytes.
- Artifacts stdout.txt, dry-hunks.json, dry-classes.json and dry-binding.json are recorded in ArtifactHashes.

Reproduction:
- The launcher passes an explicit, ordinally sorted file list, has no config discovery, blame, timestamps or absolute paths, and sorts its output. The environment is cleared with TZ=UTC. jscpd's Rabin-Karp is deterministic for a fixed file order and fixed bytes, so stdout.txt is byte-identical on rerun.
- Classes are keyed by token hash through union-find, so the verdict does not depend on the pair order jscpd happens to report.
- Base occurrence counting is exact Roslyn token-sequence matching, with no heuristics.
- A rerun on the same (B, H, bundle, policy) reproduces stdout.txt, dry-classes.json and the outcome bit for bit, and a mismatch is itself evidence of tampering.
- No network, no Engram, no clock.

Becomes INCOMPLETE:
- B is not an ancestor of H.
- Binary, unparsable or CR-only line-ending hunks.
- A suppression marker added in the patch, or a newly added generated-path file.
- Over the frozen materialization ceilings.
- Closure hash != reviewed constant.
- Timeout, cancel, non-zero exit, stdout over 64 MiB, non-quiescent process.
- jscpd file counts != manifest, or a clone path outside the manifest.
- An analysis exception.
- A materialized-input re-hash mismatch.
- A PASS without positive proof: counts reconcile and both passes ran.

Authority: under current code, an externally registered plugin's PASS surfaces as final INCOMPLETE (advisory) with PluginRawOutcome=PASS, while FAIL passes through as FAIL. That is enough for "fails until DRY" to block, but PASS cannot serve as positive authority until a built-in binding is added to TrustedGatePluginRegistry.BuiltIns or v2 authority ships.

### Engram usage

Engram is deliberately kept OUT of the verdict path. It indexes the default branch, not the frozen base B, its index generation is not content-addressed in a receipt, and it needs network and OAuth. Any Engram-derived input would break byte reproducibility.

Offline use for calibration and policy authoring:
- get_artifact(repo="engram-csharp", type="clone_report", key="all") plus the DryUpScan "Watched, not filed" list (~40 pairs of 11-28 lines) seed dry-allow.json key entries and the labelled evaluation set.
- get_file_outline / search_symbols on the sibling families (5 VCS bulk indexers, 6 tree-sitter HTTP extractors, per-table Stores) help write path-family allowlist globs with maxTokens bounds and rationales.
- find_related_code co-change data identifies families that are intentionally parallel.

Post-verdict remediation, outside the receipt (delivery-forge planner or review comment): for each FAIL class, search_symbols / search_code on the pre-existing occurrence's enclosing method name suggests the existing helper to reuse. change_impact(repo, changed_files, view="review") supplies convention exemplars. These hints are advisory text, never inputs to the outcome.

If Engram later offers index snapshots pinned to a commit with a content hash, a fuzzy candidate pass could be added as a separate advisory gate, not inside this one.

### Load-bearing duplication handling

Layers, none of which can be granted from inside the patch:

1. Ratchet. Pre-existing duplication never fails: the head occurrence count Hc must exceed the base count Bc. Editing one of the 5 VCS indexers, reformatting a cloned block, or moving files does not raise Hc. Only adding a new copy does.

2. Floor plus the deterministic boilerplate classifier. Classes that are 80% or more using directives, attributes, field or auto-property declarations, DI registrations or constructor assignment are dropped, and anything under 6 lines/50 tokens is never considered. This removes the C# boilerplate that Sonar is known to flag falsely.

3. Protected-base allowlist `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json`, read via `git show B:` exactly like anti-slop-ignore. Entries come in two kinds:
   - Exact clone-class keys (token SHA-256) with a rationale and an owner. If the code changes, the key no longer matches and the entry silently stops applying, which forces re-review.
   - Path-family entries, e.g. `{"family":"src/**/Vcs/*BulkIndexer.cs","maxClassTokens":400,"rationale":"per-VCS API shapes intentionally parallel; perf hot path"}`. These allow NEW occurrences only within that family and up to that size.

   The allowlist is policy, not a bypass:
   - It must be merged to the base in its own reviewed change, CODEOWNERS on .delivery-forge/.
   - The patch under evaluation cannot add or edit an entry that applies to itself.
   - Its bytes are in ConfigurationIdentity.
   - Every allowlisted class is listed in dry-classes.json and dry-binding.json, so receipts show exactly what was excused.

4. Inline jscpd:ignore or auto-generated markers added by the patch produce INCOMPLETE, not PASS, so they cannot be used to dodge the gate.

5. Thresholds are aligned with the existing weekly sweep (10 lines/70 tokens, 3 or more siblings), so the gate does not fail on things the sweep deliberately treats as "watched". Sub-threshold new clones are reported as WATCH without failing.

### Cost and latency

$0 marginal cost. CPU only, no API calls.

For engram-csharp (~150k LOC under src/) the cost is dominated by tree size, not patch size, so a 300-line patch costs about the same as a 10-line one. These are estimates, not measurements; the weekly jscpd run in engram-csharp would give a real number:
- Frozen materialization of head, base and the normalized head: ~3-8 s (git cat-file --batch, ~2x 6 MB plus Roslyn lexing ~1-2 s).
- jscpd raw pass: ~15-40 s.
- jscpd normalized pass: ~15-40 s. Same process; the passes could run concurrently on separate workers.
- Union-find plus exact base counting: under 2 s.

Typical total: ~35-90 s wall clock, with peak memory around 1-2 GB for the Node process (jscpd memory store). This fits a single finite gate deadline, e.g. 300 s, beyond which the result is INCOMPLETE.

Possible later optimization, keeping the same verdict: cache the base-side token index keyed by BaseTreeId plus the normalizer id, since the base is stable across PR iterations.

### Precision and recall expectation

Type-1 (exact, whitespace or comment differences): high recall at or above 6 lines/50 tokens. Fails at 10 lines/70 tokens, or 6/50 when the class spans 3 or more files. Precision is high after the ratchet and boilerplate filter.

Type-2 (renamed identifiers or literals): caught by the line-preserving normalized pass at 15 lines/120 tokens. jscpd 4.x mild mode alone would miss these, because it keeps identifiers. Precision is lower: normalizing every identifier, including type names, makes structurally similar but semantically different code look alike, e.g. two EF entity configs or two Refit interfaces. That is why the bar is higher and why path-family allowlists exist. Expect some over-flags on mapper/DTO conversion methods and switch-to-enum mappings.

Type-3 (gapped): mostly missed. jscpd has no gap tolerance, so one inserted or changed line splits a 20-line clone into two 9-line fragments under R1. R4 (30 or more aggregate added lines in NEW classes of at least 6 lines across 2 or more classes) and R2 (3 or more siblings at 6 lines) recover part of this. Copy-then-tweak with several scattered edits evades the gate.

Type-4 (semantic, e.g. LINQ vs loop, a different algorithm with the same behaviour): not caught at all.

Intra-patch duplication (two new copies): caught, because Bc=0 and Hc=2.

Duplicates of code that only existed in other repos: not caught; the gate is single-repo.

Missed case: new code that should have called an existing helper but is written differently. Over-flag case: the first legitimate copy in a new sibling family that is not yet allowlisted. That one needs an allowlist PR first, which is a deliberate speed bump.

### Failure modes

- Contract generalization risk: the 10+ `plugin is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks in GateRunner must become a marker interface. A missed site silently gives the DRY plugin no frozen scope, a 1 MiB vs 64 MiB capture-limit mismatch, or the wrong capability-binding timing.
- AppendBoundArtifacts runs before Normalize and the runner only catches IOException/UnauthorizedAccessException there. If the analysis does not catch every exception internally, a bug escapes as an uncaught gate failure rather than INCOMPLETE.
- InputSetIdentity collision: if the runner's head-only identity is not extended with BaseTreeId and DiffIdentity, receipts for different bases with the same head look identical.
- jscpd extents and the Roslyn token range drift: jscpd's Prism tokenizer and Roslyn disagree on token boundaries (verbatim/raw strings, interpolation, preprocessor lines), so a class key computed over whole lines may not match across occurrences. That under-counts Hc or Bc. Mitigation: key on Roslyn tokens of whole lines only, and if Hc < 2 for a class jscpd reported, mark INCOMPLETE rather than PASS.
- Base-count blind spot: if a pre-existing clone at base was partially edited and re-cloned, Bc can be 1 while Hc is 2. That is correctly NEW, but authors will see it as 'I only edited existing code'. Needs clear messages listing both occurrences.
- Allowlist governance becomes the real policy surface: overly broad path-family globs (e.g. src/**) effectively disable the gate. Lint dry-allow.json and cap family maxClassTokens.
- Normalized-pass false positives on structurally templated C# (EF IEntityTypeConfiguration, Refit interfaces, MediatR handlers, record DTO mapping) cause friction and pressure to raise thresholds.
- Evasion via small mutations: renaming plus reordering statements, or inserting a no-op line every 8 lines, defeats a non-gapped detector. A deterministic gate cannot close this; it is the documented type-3/type-4 gap.
- Line-ending edge cases: CR-only files, or files where git's \n line count differs from jscpd's, cause a hunk/clone line misalignment. Detect a count mismatch and return INCOMPLETE.
- Performance cliff: very large generated-but-not-excluded files or a huge vendored directory blow memory or the deadline (INCOMPLETE). Scope globs must exclude them from the protected base.
- Authority: PASS is advisory INCOMPLETE under current legacy-v1 rules, so a policy that requires a final PASS will never merge until a built-in binding or v2 authority exists. FAIL blocking works today.
- jscpd 4.2.5 is now a legacy line, with v5 a Rust rewrite. Security fixes may require a closure re-review and re-baselining of thresholds, since clone extents change.

### Implementation sketch

External pins:
- npm jscpd@4.2.5 with its exact lockfile closure of @jscpd/core, finder and tokenizer 4.2.5 and transitive dependencies. Installed into a host-owned bundle `tools/dry-jscpd-bundle/` containing package.json, package-lock.json and run-jscpd-frozen.mjs (~150 LOC: manifest read, two detectClones calls, canonical sorted JSON to stdout).
- The same reviewed Node binary as anti-slop.
- Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp pinned via central package management.
- Reviewed closure SHA-256 constants per platform, like ReviewedLinuxX64GnuRuntimeClosureSha256.

Host changes in components/evidence/DeliveryForge.Evidence (one type per file):
- IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin.cs and FrozenScopeRequirements.cs: marker interface; replace the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` sites in GateRunner.cs. About 2 days, the highest-risk part, with regression tests on anti-slop.
- RepositoryEvaluationScope additions: BaseCommit, BaseTreeId, ChangedPaths, AddedLineRanges, BaseMaterializedRoot, NormalizedRoot, DiffIdentity. Add LineRange.cs.
- GitRepositoryIdentityReader: ancestor check, `diff -U0` hunk parser (UnifiedDiffHunkParser.cs), base and normalized materialization, extended InputSetIdentity, verification of the extra roots. About 3 days.
- CSharpCloneSuppressionScanner.cs: jscpd:ignore and auto-generated markers vs base.
- CSharpLexicalNormalizer.cs: Roslyn ParseTokens, line-preserving ID/LIT substitution.

Plugin and analysis (under Adapters/ and Dry/):
- DryClonePatchEvaluationPlugin.cs, DrySnapshot.cs, DryCloneRequestFactory.cs (canonical invocation plus a content-addressed manifest, mirroring AntiSlopRequestFactory), DryThresholds.cs (versioned record), DryGatePolicy.cs and DryAllowList.cs (protected-base readers via ProtectedGitPolicyResolver), JscpdCanonicalReportParser.cs, CloneClassBuilder.cs (union-find over token-hash keys), TokenSequenceOccurrenceCounter.cs (Rabin-Karp over the Roslyn token streams of head/ and base/), BoilerplateClassifier.cs, DryVerdictEvaluator.cs (R1-R5 plus WATCH), DryBindingArtifactWriter.cs. About 4-5 days.
- AdvisoryDryComposition.cs, mirroring AdvisoryAntiSlopComposition. About 1 day.

Tests: fixture git repos covering pre-existing clone touched (PASS), new 6th sibling (FAIL R2), renamed copy (FAIL R3), copy split by edits (R4), added jscpd:ignore (INCOMPLETE), allowlist added in the same patch (ignored, so FAIL), non-ancestor base (INCOMPLETE), and a byte-identical rerun golden test. About 2-3 days.

Calibration: replay the last ~200 merged engram-csharp PRs (base to merge commit) and record FAIL/WATCH rates. Confirm the commits that introduced the known watched clones would have hit WATCH or FAIL as expected. Seed dry-allow.json for the VCS indexer, tree-sitter extractor and Store families. About 2-3 days.

Total: about 2.5-3.5 engineer-weeks.

### Novelty claim

The owner's list only orders cost tiers (deterministic, decision model, cheap LLM). This design adds four concrete mechanics on top of a stock clone tool:
- A base-vs-head occurrence-count ratchet keyed on Roslyn token hashes, independent of the order jscpd reports pairs in. It fails only on NEW clone mass, so the ~40 watched clones and the intentionally parallel sibling families never block unrelated edits. jscpd 4.2.5 has no baseline mode, and v5's --baseline-from-ref would mean a major-version tool swap plus a second full scan.
- A line-preserving lexical normalization shadow tree that turns jscpd 4.x, which keeps identifiers, into a type-2 detector without changing tools. Because line N stays line N, hunk scoping still works.
- Anti-bypass rules specific to clone detection: jscpd's own ignore markers and auto-generated headers added in the patch are treated as suppression violations (INCOMPLETE), and generated-path excludes cannot be self-granted.
- Thresholds and the allowlist come only from the protected base, and allowlist entries are content-addressed clone-class keys that expire automatically when the code changes. Load-bearing duplication is therefore handled as reviewed policy rather than a bypass.

It also identifies and specifies the contract changes PR #52 does not yet support: base/diff fields on RepositoryEvaluationScope, a frozen-tree marker interface replacing the anti-slop type checks, a diff-aware InputSetIdentity, and verdict computation in AppendBoundArtifacts because it runs before Normalize.

### Open questions

- Is it acceptable to generalize GateRunner's anti-slop-only frozen-scope path into a host-owned IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin marker, or should the DRY gate get a separate runner path? It touches about 10 type-check sites.
- Should RequestFactory compute the base as request.ExpectedRepository.BaseCommit (the protected base) or as merge-base(protected base, head)? They differ when the PR branch is behind main.
- Must PASS become authoritative (a built-in binding in TrustedGatePluginRegistry.BuiltIns), or is FAIL-blocks / PASS-advisory enough for the first rollout?
- Should the normalized type-2 pass normalize all identifiers, or keep type names (e.g. PascalCase identifiers followed by `<` or `.`) to cut false positives? This needs calibration on engram-csharp history.
- Who owns `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json` in each repo (CODEOWNERS), and should path-family entries require a maxClassTokens cap and a linked issue?
- Should test projects be in scope? The weekly sweep is production-only, but copy-paste test setup is also a DRY concern; possibly a separate gate id with looser thresholds.
- Is jscpd 4.2.5 an acceptable long-term pin given the v5 Rust rewrite, or should the plugin be written so the detector can be swapped (e.g. PMD CPD 7.x with --ignore-usings) behind the same canonical report schema?
- Measure the actual jscpd wall-clock time and memory on engram-csharp src/ (~150k LOC) for both passes before fixing the gate deadline.
- Should WATCH-level new clones feed back into the weekly DryUpScan 'Watched, not filed' list automatically (from receipts), unifying the sweep and the gate?

### Judge verdicts

**contract-fit (7/10).** I checked the contract claims against the code. The interface has DetectInputs, CaptureBeforeAsync, ValidateAfterExecution, Normalize and AppendBoundArtifacts. GateRunner.cs has exactly the cited `plugin is [not] Adapters.AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` sites at lines 115, 260, 273, 285, 296, 381, 785 and 861. AppendBoundArtifacts runs at line 331, before Normalize at 357, and catches only IOException and UnauthorizedAccessException. The proposal states these gaps itself and mostly maps cleanly onto the plugin contract.

Strengths:
- Identities are well defined and reproducible: closure hash, config bytes read from the protected base B, manifest, canonical sorted output and an explicit file list.
- Uncertain paths become INCOMPLETE: non-ancestor base, count mismatch, suppression marker added, timeout, closure mismatch.
- Engram is kept out of the verdict path.
- Policy and allowlist are read from B, not H.
- PASS is non-authoritative under legacy-v1 rules, and the proposal says so honestly.

Weaknesses:
- It is not a drop-in plugin. It needs a marker-interface refactor of about 10 runner sites, new base and diff fields on the scope, and an extended InputSetIdentity. The runner overwrites the plugin's identity, so a missed site silently degrades the gate.
- The verdict is computed inside AppendBoundArtifacts, which is meant to bind artifacts. This relies on mutable snapshot state, and the runner does not catch general exceptions there.
- Reproducibility rests on two tokenizers (jscpd Prism and Roslyn) that the proposal admits can disagree. Hc < 2 on a class jscpd reported is handled as INCOMPLETE, but that will produce frequent INCOMPLETE noise.
- Candidate influence is reduced but not eliminated. Code can be placed in excluded globs, or in a file shape that matches an exclude. Type-3 and type-4 evasion are open. Base B trust is unresolved: protected base versus merge-base is an open question, and a branch behind main may see stale policy.
- A PASS that cannot be authoritative limits how useful the receipt is for "fail until DRY".

Strongest weakness: The plugin cannot work without a risky runner and scope refactor (the AntiSlop-specific branches, the missing base and diff fields, and an InputSetIdentity the runner overwrites). Its verdict logic also sits in AppendBoundArtifacts, whose exception handling and ordering are not designed for it, and its reproducibility depends on two tokenizers that must agree.

Suggested fix: Specify the host contract change as a first-class deliverable before the plugin. Add a frozen-tree marker interface with a diff-aware scope that carries base, diff identity and added ranges, and a Normalize overload that receives the analysis result. Compute the analysis in a Normalize-time step that catches all exceptions and returns INCOMPLETE, with AppendBoundArtifacts only writing artifacts. Pin base to the protected base commit, require the merge-base to equal it (otherwise INCOMPLETE), and replace jscpd/Roslyn key matching with a single tokenizer (Roslyn only) so every clone is verified under one definition.

**accuracy (5/10).** Strong on exact copies, weak on near copies and small ones. Ratchet (Hc > Bc), the protected-base allowlist and the 6-line floor work well against false failures on pre-existing sibling families and edits. Checked against the issue-238 shapes: (1) Byte-identical wrappers across 5 files: caught only when the wrapper is at least 6 lines and 50 tokens. Many real wrapper methods are 3-5 lines and fall under the floor, so recall is limited there. When they do clear the floor, R2 (3 or more files) fires. (2) Methods about 85% identical with mechanical differences: this is type-3 and jscpd has no gap tolerance. One changed or inserted line splits a 20-line clone into two roughly 9-line fragments under R1's 10-line floor. The design admits copy-then-tweak evades it. R4 (30 aggregate added lines) helps only for larger patches. The normalized pass rescues only differences that are identifier or literal changes. (3) Private static helpers duplicated function-for-function: caught when at least 10 lines or 70 tokens, which is typical. Short helpers of 6-9 lines in 1-2 files are missed. (4) Helpers copy-pasted then renamed: caught by the normalized pass only at 15 lines and 120 tokens. Renamed helpers of 10-14 lines, the most common size, are missed. Precision: the ratchet, floor, boilerplate filter (80% usings, attributes, fields, DI, ctor assignments) and test-project exclusion should avoid most idiomatic-boilerplate failures. Normalizing all identifiers including type names will over-flag per-table Stores, EF configs, Refit interfaces and DTO mappers. Those are structurally templated, and the design concedes friction. The first legitimate new sibling needs an allowlist PR before it can pass. Gameability is high: reordering statements, inserting a no-op line every 8 lines, splitting a method and semantic rewrites (LINQ vs loop) all defeat it. Only variable renaming is defeated.

Strongest weakness: Recall on near-miss duplication (85% identical methods, gapped copies, short 6-14 line renamed helpers) is poor. jscpd has no gap tolerance and the type-2 threshold is 15 lines and 120 tokens, so copy-then-tweak evades the gate. That is the most common real-world copy-paste pattern and an easy bypass.

Suggested fix: Add a gap-tolerant type-3 pass. Compute a deterministic line-level or token-level similarity (for example, normalized token-shingle Jaccard or an edit-distance alignment between each added method and same-family or repo-wide methods, at a threshold of about 0.8). Run it at method granularity using Roslyn method boundaries rather than jscpd extents. Merge adjacent or near-adjacent jscpd fragments between the same two files (gap of 3 lines or fewer) into one clone before applying the R1 and R3 thresholds. Lower R3 to about 10 lines when the class spans 2 or more files. Keep type names in normalization to cut false positives on templated Stores and configs.

**cost-ops (6/10).** Cost-order fit is ideal: tier 1, $0 marginal, no network, no API keys, no Engram in the verdict path, so no model availability or index freshness risk. Reproducible and cacheable (base token index keyed by BaseTreeId). Operability is the weak side. (1) The 35-90s estimate is unmeasured and likely optimistic: two full-tree jscpd passes over ~150k LOC in Node with an in-memory store at 1-2 GB, plus Roslyn lexing of head, base and head-norm, and Rabin-Karp counting. A 300-line patch pays a full-repo cost every iteration, which is a latency tax on every PR push, and cold CI runners would pay Node bundle and Roslyn startup too. Early-exit on zero in-scope added lines is only a partial mitigation. Parallelising the two passes costs more memory. (2) Implementation effort is the real cost. The proposal's own total of 2.5-3.5 engineer-weeks is for an advisory plugin, and it is dominated by a high-risk refactor of about 10 `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` sites in GateRunner, new scope fields, a diff parser, and an InputSetIdentity change. That touches the exemplar plugin and needs regression tests. It is deterministic in the owner's sense but not cheap to build. (3) Maintenance and pinning burden is heavy: reviewed closure SHA-256 per platform for jscpd, Node, Roslyn and launcher; Roslyn normalizer and boilerplate rule-set versions; a legacy jscpd 4.x line with a v5 Rust rewrite looming, which would force threshold re-baselining. (4) Ongoing human operations: allowlist governance (CODEOWNERS PRs before the first legitimate sibling), a 200-PR calibration replay, and friction from type-2 normalized false positives that will push pressure on thresholds. (5) The Roslyn-versus-jscpd token-boundary drift is an operability hazard. It will surface as INCOMPLETE noise, and INCOMPLETE blocks merges. (6) PASS is advisory-only until authority work ships, so it gives no positive merge value yet. Overall it is the cheapest per run, but latency and build and maintenance cost are understated, so I score it workable with real fixes.

Strongest weakness: Full-tree double jscpd pass on every patch regardless of size, with unmeasured 35-90s and 1-2 GB estimates, atop a roughly 3-week, high-risk GateRunner and scope-contract refactor plus a heavy pin/rule-set/allowlist maintenance surface.

Suggested fix: Measure real jscpd and Roslyn timings on engram-csharp before committing to the design. Cut per-run cost with a cached base token index keyed by BaseTreeId and a cheap pre-filter: build a Roslyn token-hash index of added windows and only run jscpd, or skip it entirely, when no added window hash collides with a head or base index. Run the normalized pass only if the raw pass or the pre-filter finds candidates. Ship a phase 1 advisory plugin without the type-2 shadow tree, R3 and R4, and without the full GateRunner generalization. Use a narrow second path in the runner first, then generalize after the first version works. Keep the detector behind a swappable canonical report schema, since the jscpd 4.x pin carries a v5 migration risk.

### Adversarial refutation

**Refuted: true (major).** Claim attacked: Mechanism steps 3c-3e: the ratchet. A jscpd clone side is keyed by SHA-256 of the Roslyn token stream over whole lines [start,end]. Hc/Bc are exact occurrence counts of that sequence in head/ and base/. A class is NEW only if Hc > Bc. Reproducibility is claimed to be independent of what jscpd reports. This is meant to give FAIL on a new copy and PASS on an edit to an existing clone.

Evidence:

I installed jscpd@4.2.5 and ran it on small C# fixtures. The fixtures were 20-line copy-pasted methods with only the method name changed (RunA/RunB/RunC), run in mild mode with minLines 6 and minTokens 50.
