1. Clone extents start mid-line, not on a line boundary. The reported ranges were A.cs 5:32-24:2, B.cs 5:32-24:2 and C.cs 5:32-24:2. Line 5 is `public async Task<int> RunA(...)`, and the clone starts after the divergent identifier. Expanding to whole lines per step 3c pulls in the divergent prefix (`RunA` vs `RunB` vs `RunC`). The same clone therefore gets a different key on every occurrence. A renamed method signature on the first line of a copy-pasted block is the most common copy-paste shape. For these classes, exact whole-line sequence counting gives Hc=1 and Bc=0. Union-find over pairs links the occurrences, but the class has no single key whose count over head/ and base/ is meaningful.
   - Per the design's own mitigation ("Hc < 2 for a class jscpd reported -> INCOMPLETE"), the plain raw-pass copy-paste yields INCOMPLETE, not FAIL.
   - The R1 tier aligned with the weekly sweep (10 lines/70 tokens) is effectively unreachable for it.
   - Only the normalized pass can rescue the verdict, because ID substitution equalizes the boundary line. That pass has the higher bar (R3: 15 lines/120 tokens). So 10-14 line copies, or renamed-signature copies not in the normalized tier, are INCOMPLETE or missed.
   - The same keying breaks the "editing an existing clone keeps Hc <= Bc" guarantee. A touched signature line changes the whole-line key, so Bc counts 0 for it.
2. Extents are not even stable per pair for the same code. A.cs vs C.cs came out 5:33 with 64 tokens, while B.cs vs C.cs came out 5:32 with 66 tokens. Whole-line expansion hides some of this, but it confirms that jscpd's boundaries are an unreliable input to an exact-match key.
3. jscpd reports a spanning subset of pairs, not the complete graph. With three identical files it reported (B,C) and (A,C) and never (A,B). With the new file A0 added (s3 fixture), it reported only (A0,Z) and (A,Z). Union-find tolerates this. But the Hc/Bc counts must come from the host's own scan, and that scan depends on the key problem above.
4. Launcher feasibility: `import {detectClones} from 'jscpd'` fails on Node 24 with ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND (`colors/safe`). A CJS require is needed. The `store:'memory'` option also printed "store name memory not installed". Both are fixable but absent from the design, and the pinned-closure review has to cover them.
5. Contract side (confirmed from GateRunner.cs and EvidenceModels.cs): the plugin cannot get base materialization or the diff without the host refactor the design itself lists. The AntiSlop-only branches are at lines 115, 260, 273, 285, 296, 381, 785 and 861. RepositoryEvaluationScope has no base or diff fields. AppendBoundArtifacts runs before Normalize and the runner catches only IO/UnauthorizedAccess exceptions there. These are acknowledged gaps rather than refutations. The runtime and cost figures remain unmeasured.

Salvage:

Do not key or count on whole lines. After jscpd reports a pair, the host re-derives the canonical clone itself:
- Map both sides to Roslyn token indices.
- Extend or trim to the maximal exactly-equal token run, on raw tokens for the raw pass and on normalized kinds for the norm pass.
- Compute the key over that common run only, so all occurrences share one key.
- Run Rabin-Karp counting for Hc and Bc over that run.

Alternative with no key matching at all: run the same pinned jscpd on the frozen base tree and compare per-class occurrence counts, accepting the doubled cost.

Also:
- Validate the plan on fixtures before committing to it: renamed-signature copy (must FAIL, not INCOMPLETE), an edit to one of two existing clones (must PASS), and a file move.
- Replace the ESM import with CJS require in the launcher.
- Make the INCOMPLETE-on-Hc<2 path report as "key mismatch" so it cannot silently become the main verdict.

**Refuted: true (major).** Claim attacked: That jscpd.dry-patch separates real duplication from load-bearing or boilerplate duplication, without a bypass, and that an agent under pressure to pass cannot cheaply evade it or satisfy it with a fake justification.

Evidence:

The design is a textual-identity detector with a public, deterministic oracle. It also adds exact-match ratchet rules and patch-controlled scoping, and these create bypasses and false positives beyond the type-3/4 gap the design already admits.

GAMING (cheap evasion)
1. Mutate-to-pass. Copy a block and apply one cosmetic edit every ~5 lines:
   - `x == null` to `x is null`;
   - `var` to an explicit type (a keyword, so it survives the ID normalization);
   - reorder two independent statements;
   - insert a `var _ = 0;` line.
   jscpd has no gap tolerance, so each edit splits the clone into fragments under the 6-line/50-token floor (norm: 15/120). The gate then returns PASS.
   The failure Reason lists exact class keys and file:line ranges, and the gate is free, offline and byte-deterministic. An agent can binary-search the minimal mutation in a few reruns. "Fails until DRY" becomes "fails until textually different", and the code it leaves behind is obfuscated duplication.
2. Touch-the-original. Because the verdict is Hc > Bc over exact token sequences, an agent can add the new copy and also tweak one token in the original, or in the copy. The head sequence then has no matching multiplicity.
3. Padding. The 50%-added-lines rule can be diluted by placing the copy beside pre-existing identical lines such as braces and neighbouring members. The class then falls under 50% added and is not NEW.
4. Per-patch creep.
   - 9-line pairs (under the 10-line R1 floor) are WATCH forever, until a third file appears.
   - R4 caps each patch at 29 added lines of sub-threshold classes.
   - The base moves every PR, so there is no cumulative accounting. Dozens of 9-line clones can land over many PRs.
5. Existing ignore region. Only markers inside ADDED ranges are flagged. If a base file already contains a `jscpd:ignore-start/end` pair, the patch can add duplicate code inside that region. jscpd skips it natively, no marker is added, no violation fires, and the result is PASS. The design has no region-aware check.
6. Path-controlled scope. Excludes (test projects, Migrations, obj/bin, generated paths) and the family globs are all evaluated on paths the patch chooses.
   - A new `Foo.Tests` project, or a `Migrations/` folder, holding production helper copies may escape scope. The test-project rule is unspecified, and "generator-owned" is only partly covered.
   - Naming a new file `src/**/Vcs/NewBulkIndexer.cs` earns the family allowance. It permits NEW occurrences up to 400 tokens with no per-patch cap on the number of classes. The design does not say whether ALL occurrences or just ONE must match the family.

FAKE JUSTIFICATION
7. The allowlist is keyed on token hashes, which are opaque to reviewers. The rationale is free text, never verified. dry-classes.json hands the agent the exact key. An agent can open a tiny, config-looking stacked PR1 (`.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json` with the precomputed hash of the code it is about to write), then land PR2 containing the duplicate. The design's "cannot self-grant" holds only inside a single patch. In an agent-driven pipeline with human rubber-stamping, CODEOWNERS review becomes the justification surface, and the gate becomes a stream of allowlist PRs.

FALSE POSITIVES
8. Consistent bulk edits to existing sibling families. Changing a parameter or API across the 5 VCS indexers or 6 extractors, or a formatter or rename pass, rewrites a 6+-line sequence in N files.
   - The new sequence has Hc=N, Bc=0, and 100% of its lines are added.
   - Result: R2 FAIL on code that was already parallel, which is the case the ratchet claims never to fail.
   - A content-hash allowlist entry cannot be created in the same PR, so authors fall back to coarse path-family globs. That pushes policy toward the broad globs the design itself lists as a failure mode.
9. Whole-line keying versus jscpd's mid-line token extents. This is already listed as a failure mode. Its mitigation ("Hc<2 means INCOMPLETE") yields frequent spurious INCOMPLETE on exactly the common edits. That is an oracle for agents (reformat until it flips) and friction for humans. Files above maxLines 5000 or maxSize 2mb are similarly INCOMPLETE.
10. Normalized pass. Collapsing every identifier makes EF configs, Refit interfaces, mappers and DI wiring look identical. The boilerplate filter only drops classes that are at least 80% boilerplate tokens.

Net: the design reliably catches only accidental verbatim paste. It does not stop deliberate or LLM-assisted evasion, and it has unacknowledged holes (5, 6, 7, 8). A gate that returns PASS on mutated duplicates gives false assurance of DRY.

Salvage:

Keep the jscpd ratchet only as a cheap tier-1 filter whose output is FAIL or "no verbatim duplication found". Never let PASS stand for "DRY". Route every non-FAIL to a second semantic stage (a configurable decision model or a cheap LLM) that judges the changed hunks against Engram-retrieved similar code. Patch the concrete holes:

(a) Scan the whole head file for jscpd ignore regions, not only added lines. Fail or INCOMPLETE if any ignore region at head contains added lines, or disable marker honouring entirely.
(b) Resolve scope and family globs from protected-base project metadata, not path names. Require ALL occurrences of a class to match a family. Cap NEW occurrences per family per patch, and cap the number of classes.
(c) Treat bulk same-multiplicity rewrites (Hc == Bc modulo a consistent edit) as non-NEW by keying the ratchet on the normalized or sequence-skeleton hash, not the exact raw token hash.
(d) Add gap tolerance, for example fuzzy near-clone matching (SimHash or shingling over statements) with a cumulative per-family counter in an append-only ledger. This closes mutate-to-pass and per-patch creep.
(e) Do not echo exact class keys or thresholds to the author-facing failure message. Echo only file:line ranges, and require allowlist PRs to carry human-readable path plus code excerpt, with a reviewer-visible diff of excused code.
(f) Cap how many allowlist entries one author or one PR chain may add.


## A.hybrid-cascade: dry-cascade (deliveryforge.dry-cascade): staged deterministic -> decision-model -> cheap-LLM DRY gate with memoised verdicts

Panel mean 5.7 (contract-fit 5, accuracy 6, cost-ops 6). Tier: hybrid.

**One line.** One External plugin runs a pinned, self-contained scanner binary. Stage 1 is a Roslyn parse-only clone index over the frozen head tree, restricted to code added or changed by base..head. It returns PASS when there are no candidates and FAIL for new type-1/2 copies at or above the threshold. Only pairs in the ambiguous band go to a pinned decision model (Jev by default, configurable), then optionally to Haiku 5.5 with a 3-vote unanimity rule. Every model verdict is memoised by a content hash of the candidate pair and the stage identity, so reruns replay the same verdicts.

### Mechanism

INPUTS (frozen; needs a contract extension, see plugin_fit): head tree blobs for all tracked *.cs files; base blobs for changed *.cs only; head-side added/changed line ranges from `git diff -U0 -M50% --no-ext-diff --no-textconv <base> <head> -- '*.cs'`; protected-base ledger `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json` read with `git show <base>:path`. The head copy of the ledger has no authority. All of this goes into a content-addressed manifest `inputs/manifest.json`.

STAGE 1 (deterministic, offline, inside the binary `df-dry`):
1. Parse every included file with Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp (exact pinned version embedded in the single-file binary). Parse only: no compilation, restore or semantic model. Fixed CSharpParseOptions (pinned LanguageVersion, no preprocessor symbols, DocumentationMode.None).
   - Any parse diagnostic in a CHANGED file -> INCOMPLETE.
   - A parse diagnostic in an unchanged file -> that file is excluded and counted in the limitations.
2. Fragment extraction:
   - (a) member bodies: methods, ctors, accessors, local functions, lambdas with block bodies;
   - (b) sliding statement windows of 5..N consecutive statements inside a block, so gapped and partial copies are caught.
   - Drop fragments under 6 lines or 40 tokens (the trivial floor).
3. Normalisation per fragment:
   - L0: token kinds and text without trivia or comments (type-1).
   - L1: identifiers alpha-renamed to positional placeholders (consistent within the fragment); literals collapsed to their kind; predefined type keywords kept (type-2).
   - Fingerprint: winnowing over L1 token 5-grams with a window of 4, using a SHA-256-truncated 64-bit hash with no seed.
4. Index: an L0-hash map, an L1-hash map and an inverted fingerprint index over the whole head tree (ordinal path order; parallel parse with a sorted merge, so output order is stable).
5. Candidate pairs: A is a fragment where at least 50% of lines fall in head added/changed ranges. B is any other head fragment that does not overlap A, in the same file or anywhere else, including other added code (intra-patch duplication).
6. Attribution ratchet: drop the pair if the base version of A's file already contained a fragment with the same L1 hash and the base clone class held B's location. That pair was pre-existing; it is recorded as `watched` in the artifact and not attributed.
7. Path class from the code-owned PathFilters, matching engram-csharp's DryUpScan: test/, *.Tests, Migrations, *.g.cs, *.Designer.cs and generated headers are excluded by default. A boilerplate-shape catalog is matched on L1 shape: auto-property blocks, ctor-assign-only, guard-clause runs (ThrowIfNull/ThrowIfNullOrEmpty), DI registration chains, attribute-only controller scaffolding, record/DTO declarations.
8. BAND CLASSIFICATION per pair (thresholds in dry-rules.json, hashed into the configuration identity):
   - CLEAR-FAIL. Not ledger-covered, not boilerplate-shaped, production path, and one of:
     - L0 or L1 equal with at least 15 lines and 100 tokens;
     - L0 or L1 equal with at least 10 lines and 70 tokens (the DryUpScan/jscpd floor), and this patch makes the clone class size at least 3 (the sibling rule);
     - whole member body L1-equal with at least 6 statements ("same function, cosmetic-only differences");
     - L0 equal with at least 80 lines, even if boilerplate-shaped.
   - AMBIGUOUS. One of:
     - L0/L1 equal at 10-14 lines in a class of size 2;
     - L0/L1 equal at or above threshold but boilerplate-shaped;
     - type-3: winnowed containment max(|FA∩FB|/|FA|, |FA∩FB|/|FB|) of at least 0.70 and below 1.0, with both sides at least 10 lines;
     - an optional Engram type-4 recall hit (see engram_usage).
   - CLEAR-PASS: everything else. Containment below 0.70, under the floors, test code, or ledger-covered.
   - Candidate de-duplication: keep the best B per A per clone class (largest containment, then ordinal path), so 20 sibling copies produce one pair plus a class size, not 20 model calls.
   - Hard budget: more than 25 ambiguous pairs -> INCOMPLETE ("candidate budget exceeded; split patch or add ledger entries"). It never silently samples.

STAGE 2 (decision model, ambiguous pairs only; provider configurable as typesafe.jev, microsoft.decision-1 or a self-hosted pplx-decider):
1. One POST https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone per pair with a pinned versioned model id (jev-1.14.0; the aliases jev-latest and jev-preview are rejected at configuration validation).
2. The state is JSON with:
   - comment-stripped L0 text of A and B (comments removed to cut the injection surface; Jev's jaggedness docs warn that embedded instructions steer it);
   - repo-relative paths and path classes;
   - the clone class size and the sibling paths;
   - containment;
   - line counts precomputed, because Jev cannot count.
3. Questions:
   - `verdict`, a Choice with fixed criteria, each with a one-sentence rubric: SHOULD_SHARE, LOAD_BEARING (perf hot path, deliberately per-provider/per-VCS divergence expected, framework-mandated shape), TRIVIAL, DIFFERENT_BEHAVIOUR;
   - `same_intent`, a Noul.
   - To counter first-option bias, the same request is sent twice with the option order reversed, and both answers must agree.
4. Pair rules:
   - FAIL if both orders give SHOULD_SHARE with p >= 0.85 and same_intent >= 0.80.
   - CLEARED if both orders give the same non-SHOULD_SHARE label with p >= 0.85.
   - Otherwise UNRESOLVED.

STAGE 3 (cheap LLM, off by default, enabled per configuration):
1. Unresolved pairs, or all ambiguous pairs if the stage 2 provider returns 401/429/529, a timeout or a transport error, go to claude-haiku-5-5 with:
   - output_config.format json_schema {verdict: enum[SHOULD_SHARE, LOAD_BEARING, TRIVIAL, DIFFERENT_BEHAVIOUR, UNSURE], reason_category: enum, evidence_lines: [{side, line}]};
   - explicit effort `low`;
   - no temperature (removed in API 2026-10-21).
2. N = 3 independent stateless calls with no multi-turn challenge.
3. Pair rules:
   - FAIL only if all 3 say SHOULD_SHARE;
   - CLEARED only if all 3 give non-fail labels other than UNSURE;
   - otherwise UNRESOLVED.
   - stop_reason refusal or max_tokens, or a schema failure -> UNRESOLVED.

AGGREGATION (Normalize):
- INTEGRITY first, giving INCOMPLETE on any of:
  - manifest/report hash mismatch;
  - the report's analyzed file count differs from the frozen inputs;
  - a changed-file parse error;
  - a finding outside the frozen inputs;
  - an unknown rule id;
  - budget exceeded;
  - the report schema version is not `df-dry-json-v1`;
  - an exit code other than 0 or 1.
- Then: any pair FAIL (stage 1, 2 or 3) -> FAIL. ProductionCapable is true only under an exact trusted binding, as for anti-slop. Each finding is a concrete file:line pair, the clone class and the deciding stage. Unresolved pairs are listed as limitations.
- Else any UNRESOLVED -> INCOMPLETE.
- Else zero changed *.cs -> NOT_APPLICABLE, with rationale, where the policy allows it.
- Else PASS: all pairs cleared or no candidates, exit 0.
FAIL ranks above UNRESOLVED because FAIL needs a concrete finding. The gate keeps failing until no attributable pair stays in the FAIL band.

### Plugin contract fit

Registration:
- `DryCascadeEvaluationPlugin : IDeterministicEvaluationPlugin` with Id "deliveryforge.dry-cascade", Version "1.0.0", ExpectedTool "df-dry" and CompatibilityAdapter = EvidenceAdapterKind.External.
- Registered through `TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistration(GateId: "dry", Plugin, ExpectedToolVersion: "1.0.0", ExpectedFormatVersion: "df-dry-json-v1", ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity: <reviewed sha256>)`.
- It must be explicitly selected through `EvaluationPluginSelection("deliveryforge.dry-cascade", Enabled: true, Inputs)`, because Resolve throws for External without a selection.

CONTRACT GAPS THAT MUST BE CLOSED FIRST (the angle is infeasible without them):
- (1) GateRunner.ValidateAutomaticScopeAsync returns early when `plugin is not Adapters.AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` (GateRunner.cs:861). Without a change, this plugin gets no frozen tree, no materialisation and no InputSetIdentity. Replace that check, and the ten other anti-slop special cases (:115, :260, :273, :285, :296, :381, :499, :521, :565, :645, :785), with a new interface. Suggested shape: `IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin { bool IsFrozenInputPath(string path); bool RequiresChangeScope { get; } int MaximumCaptureBytesPerStream { get; } string? BindingArtifactKind { get; } }`.
- (2) RepositoryEvaluationScope (EvidenceModels.cs:212-220) carries head-tree data only. Add `RepositoryChangeScope? Changes` with these fields: ChangedPaths; HeadAddedLineRanges (path -> [start, end] list from `git diff -U0 -M50%`); RenamedFrom; BaseMaterializedRoot plus BaseMaterializedObjectIds for changed *.cs base blobs; ChangeSetIdentity = sha256 of base commit, head commit and the canonical hunk list.
  - GitRepositoryIdentityReader already runs `git diff --name-only -z <base> <head>` (:108-111) and reads base blobs via `git cat-file --batch`. It needs `-U0` hunk parsing, and base-blob materialisation into a second host-owned root, verified with the existing VerifyGitBlobIdentity / VerifyMaterializedInputs path.

MEMBER MAPPING:
- DetectInputs(candidateInputs, ignoredInputs): ordinal-sorted *.cs paths in candidateInputs that are not in ignoredInputs, so the input set is the whole head *.cs tree (comparisons span the repo). The runner then requires requested == expected (:877-880).
- ValidateBeforeExecution(request) returns non-null (a no-execution receipt) for any of:
  - Invocation is not canonical (FileName == Capability.ExecutablePath, Arguments exactly ["scan", "--manifest", "inputs/manifest.json", "--format", "df-dry-json-v1"]);
  - the environment is not exactly the allow-list (SystemRoot, WINDIR, TMP, TEMP, DF_DRY_OUTPUT, DF_DRY_CACHE_ROOT, DF_DRY_SECRETS_REF, DF_DRY_OFFLINE);
  - Capability.Version != "1.0.0" or FormatVersion != "df-dry-json-v1";
  - a cache, output or secrets path lies inside the repository root;
  - the inputs exceed the ceiling (20,000 files).
- CaptureBeforeAsync(request, ct). Inside the runner's deadline-linked token:
  - hash the df-dry binary (executable identity);
  - read and hash dry-rules.json, the boilerplate catalog, prompt templates, Jev criteria, the Anthropic JSON schema and the provider configuration (all shipped beside the binary in a host bundle root, as anti-slop does with bundleRoot);
  - read the protected-base ledger blob oid via ProtectedGitPolicyResolver;
  - assert ChangeSetIdentity equals the manifest's;
  - check that the verdict cache root exists, is outside the repo and is not writable by the candidate checkout;
  - compute the configuration and runtime-closure identities and compare them to the request (mismatch -> InvalidOperationException -> INCOMPLETE).
  - The snapshot state is a sealed record of all those digests plus the output directory path.
- Execution: the host ICommandExecutor runs df-dry with WorkingDirectory = MaterializedRoot. Stages 1-3 all happen inside this process, so the runner's tree-kill and timeout cover model calls (TimedOut -> INCOMPLETE at :381-388). There is no in-process plugin execute hook, so model calls cannot live in Normalize (synchronous; any throw is ERROR) or in CaptureBeforeAsync (it runs before candidates exist).
  - stdout: a bounded summary JSON with counts, per-pair decisions and the sha256 of every file written to DF_DRY_OUTPUT. Typically under 50 KiB, well inside the 64 MiB non-anti-slop capture cap, or 1 MiB if the binding sets a lower cap.
- ValidateAfterExecution(request, snapshot): re-hash the binary, rules and ledger blob; any drift -> INCOMPLETE.
- Normalize(request, CommandResult, snapshot): parse the stdout JSON, reconcile analyzedFiles == frozen input count, changedFiles == ChangeSetIdentity paths, and pair counts per band == the per-stage decision counts. Then apply the aggregation rules in mechanism.
  - Fail and NotApplicable are built with the NormalizedEvidence constructor; Pass uses NormalizedEvidence.Pass.
- AppendBoundArtifacts(request, snapshot, gateDirectory, artifactHashes): copy each file from DF_DRY_OUTPUT whose hash matches the stdout manifest into gateDirectory with FileMode.CreateNew, and record "sha256:<hex>" in artifactHashes. Files:
  - dry-candidates.json (stage 1 pairs, bands, fingerprints);
  - dry-cascade.json (per-pair stage, verdict, cache key, hit/miss);
  - dry-transcripts/<cacheKey>.json (raw request and response bodies, auth headers stripped);
  - dry-binding.json (the anti-slop-binding.json equivalent: repository base/head/tree, ChangeSetIdentity, plugin and tool identities, per-stage identities).
  - A hash mismatch throws IOException. The runner catches only IOException/UnauthorizedAccessException (:331) and maps them to INCOMPLETE, so every other error is wrapped as IOException.
- GetConfigurationIdentity(request, snapshot): the snapshot digest, or the literal "unavailable" without a snapshot (same convention as anti-slop).
- GetRuntimeClosureIdentity(request, snapshot): sha256 over binary bytes, embedded-resource digest, prompt templates, criteria and schema.
- GetExecutableIdentity: the snapshot hash of the binary.
- Authority caveat (honest): under current code an External plugin cannot hold legacy-v1 authority (HasTrustedLegacyV1Authority :955-969). PASS is therefore downgraded to final INCOMPLETE (advisory, :396-403), with PluginRawOutcome = PASS. FAIL passes through unchanged. Today the gate can block, but its PASS is evidence only, not authority.

### Determinism and receipts

PINNED:
- df-dry: a self-contained single-file .NET 10 binary built with Deterministic=true, ContinuousIntegrationBuild=true and PublishSingleFile. Its reviewed sha256 is hard-coded per RID (linux-x64, win-x64), in the same pattern as ReviewedLinuxX64GnuRuntimeClosureSha256. The Roslyn version is embedded and pinned.
- dry-rules.json thresholds and the boilerplate catalog; prompt templates, Jev criteria JSON and the Anthropic json_schema; the provider configuration (provider id, endpoint host allow-list, versioned model id such as jev-1.14.0 or claude-haiku-5-5, Anthropic API version 2026-10-21, effort, vote count, probability thresholds, decision-rule version).
- The protected-base ledger blob oid.

HASHED IDENTITIES IN THE RECEIPT:
- ConfigurationIdentity: rules, catalog, provider configuration, ledger oid, env allow-list keys and non-secret values. DF_DRY_SECRETS_REF contributes its reference name only, never the secret bytes; the secret is read by the binary from a host ACL'd file or OS credential store.
- RuntimeClosureIdentity: binary, embedded resources, prompts, criteria, schemas.
- InputSetIdentity: the frozen head *.cs tree identity from the scope reader.
- ChangeSetIdentity: base, head and canonical hunks, written to dry-binding.json because InputSetIdentity does not cover base or the diff.
- Per-stage identity blocks in dry-binding.json:
  - stage1 {detectorVersion, normalizerVersion, rulesSha};
  - stage2 {provider, requestedModel, resolvedModels[] (from the response `model` field), criteriaSha, thresholds, orderPermutation};
  - stage3 {provider, model, apiVersion, effort, schemaSha, votes};
  - engram {endpoint, repo, indexVersionHashes, retrievalSnapshotSha} when Engram recall is used.

MEMOISATION:
- pairKey = sha256 of canonical JSON {stageId, provider, requestedModel, promptSha/criteriaSha/schemaSha, effort, decisionRuleVersion, orderPermutation, sha256(A L0 text), sha256(B L0 text), A/B repo-relative paths and path classes, cloneClassSize, containment rounded to 2 decimals}.
- The cache is host-owned and content-addressed: DF_DRY_CACHE_ROOT/<key[0..1]>/<key>.json, write-once with CreateNew, outside the repo. An entry holds the raw request bytes sha, the raw response bytes, the parsed verdict, the resolved model and the timestamp.
- Rerun on the same base/head: stage 1 is bit-identical, so the keys are identical, every model verdict is a cache hit, and the receipt is identical except for timestamps and attempt directory.
- DF_DRY_OFFLINE=1 is a verification mode: network calls are forbidden and a cache miss means UNRESOLVED, so the rerun proves replay without new model evidence.
- A cache hit is evidence of what the model said once, not proof it would say it again. The transcript is bound as an artifact so a reviewer can audit it.

BECOMES INCOMPLETE (never PASS):
- Any provider error, 429/529, timeout or refusal; max_tokens; a schema violation; Jev order-swap disagreement; vote disagreement; UNSURE.
- The candidate budget is exceeded.
- A changed file fails to parse.
- Manifest or count reconciliation fails.
- The cache directory is unreadable.
- Configured Engram recall is unavailable.
- Rules or binary drift during the run.
- The runner's own conditions: process not quiescent, capture bound exceeded, TimedOut or cancelled.

LIMITATION (same-UID host, as stated in docs/quality-gates.md:50): the cache and bundle cannot be made immutable against a same-UID attacker. The cache is therefore evidence, and a production deployment must put it on a service-owned path.

### Engram usage

Engram is optional and only ever widens recall or sharpens finding text. It never decides a verdict.

(1) Type-4 recall (opt-in, `engramRecall: true`):
- For each new member in the patch with at least 15 lines that stage 1 did not match, the binary calls search_code(query = member name + parameter types + the top-8 identifier tokens by IDF, repo = the target repo, kind = method, max_results = 5).
- Hits with score >= the configured floor (start at 0.45; the one probe scored 0.31-0.40 on irrelevant results, so this needs calibration) whose symbol exists in the head tree become AMBIGUOUS pairs, using the head-tree body located by symbol path rather than Engram's possibly stale chunk. They can therefore only reach FAIL through stage 2/3 agreement.

(2) Pre-existing clone context: get_artifact(repo, type = clone_report, key = all) seeds the `watched` attribution. It helps the base ratchet label old clones, but the head-tree stage 1 ratchet stays authoritative.

(3) Finding enrichment only: for a FAIL whose B side is an existing member, add structural_impact(symbol_id, max_depth = 1) caller counts to the finding message ("B already has 7 callers; call it instead"). change_impact(repo, changed_files, view = review) convention exemplars can be attached to the artifact for the human.

Determinism and access:
- Engram indexes the default branch and changes over time. Every Engram response is stored as a content-addressed retrieval snapshot keyed by (engram endpoint, repo, list_artifacts version hashes, query). Its sha is recorded, so reruns use the snapshot.
- Headless gates cannot use the developer PKCE sign-in. Engram recall needs a service-principal token through DF_DRY_SECRETS_REF. When it is configured but unreachable, the result is INCOMPLETE rather than a silent recall drop.
- Engram cannot see the unmerged head, so it can only find duplicates of code that already exists on the default branch. Intra-patch duplicates come from stage 1 alone.

### Load-bearing duplication handling

There are four layers, none of them a candidate-controlled bypass.

(1) Structural exclusions in code-owned rules (hashed into ConfigurationIdentity):
- the trivial floor (under 6 lines or 40 tokens);
- test, generated and migration paths, the same filters as engram-csharp DryUpScan PathFilters;
- the boilerplate L1-shape catalog: auto-properties, ctor-assign-only, guard-clause runs, DI registration chains, attribute-only controller scaffolding, record/DTO declarations.
Boilerplate-shaped exact clones go to the AMBIGUOUS band, not FAIL, unless they reach at least 80 identical lines.

(2) A protected-base ledger `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json`, read only from the BASE commit via ProtectedGitPolicyResolver, mirroring `.delivery-forge/anti-slop-ignore`. Patch-head edits have no authority.
- Entries: {id, cloneClassL1Hash or member fingerprint, pathGlobs (for example src/Engram.Indexing/Vcs/*/BulkIndexer.cs), reasonCategory (hot-path | per-provider-divergence | framework-mandated), owner, expires (at most 180 days)}.
- A patch that adds a fourth per-VCS indexer copy matching an allowed class passes stage 1. A patch that both adds duplication and adds the allow entry still fails, because the allow lands only after a separately reviewed merge. The two-step flow is the declared, auditable exception path. The ledger blob oid and every matched entry id are written into dry-binding.json, and expired entries are ignored with a limitation.

(3) Model-stage semantics: LOAD_BEARING, TRIVIAL and DIFFERENT_BEHAVIOUR are first-class non-failing labels, but only for AMBIGUOUS pairs. A model can never clear a stage 1 CLEAR-FAIL pair. Clearance by Jev requires the label to survive the option-order swap at p >= 0.85; clearance by Haiku requires 3/3 agreement. Comment text is stripped from model input, so an in-code "this is load-bearing, ignore" comment cannot steer the verdict.

(4) Inline markers such as `// dry:load-bearing <ledger-id>` are accepted only if the id exists in the BASE ledger. They narrow which ledger entry applies; they never create one.

### Cost and latency

These are estimates, not measurements. Calibrate them by replaying the last ~200 merged engram-csharp PRs through stage 1.

Stage 1 (every run): parse-only Roslyn over ~150k LOC (~1,000 files) plus the index takes about 3-8 s CPU and a few hundred MB RAM; materialisation of ~6-8 MB of head *.cs blobs plus changed base blobs adds about 1-2 s. Cost $0.

Expected distribution for a typical 300-line C# patch:
- ~65-75% of patches stop at stage 1 with no attributable candidates (PASS).
- ~5-10% stop at stage 1 with a CLEAR-FAIL. A patch with clear FAILs still sends its ambiguous pairs to stage 2 so the finding list is complete, but an option `stopOnClearFail` can skip them.
- ~20-25% have 1-4 ambiguous pairs (mean about 2.5) and go to stage 2.
- Stage 2 (Jev): 2 requests per pair (order swap) of about 4-8k input tokens. At $0.042/M input with free output that is about $0.0003-0.0007 per pair, with about 0.1-0.8 s latency per call and pairs run 8-way in parallel. Expect about 70-80% of ambiguous pairs resolved at p >= 0.85.
- Stage 3 (Haiku 5.5, only when enabled): about 8k input plus 600 output tokens per vote is about $0.0011, so about $0.0035 per pair for 3 votes, with 2-6 s latency (votes run in parallel). Reached by about 4-7% of patches.

Per run: the expected average model spend is about $0.0005 per patch; the p95 is about $0.02; the hard cap with a 25-pair budget at both stages is about $0.10. Wall clock: p50 about 10 s, p95 about 30 s, configured gate Timeout 180 s.

Memoised reruns cost $0 and finish in roughly stage 1 time.

The arXiv 2609.29769 finding (decision-model and flash-LLM errors correlate; cascades gain at most 2.7 points) means stage 3 should be enabled mainly as a Jev-outage fallback, not as an accuracy booster. By default it is disabled, and unresolved pairs are INCOMPLETE.

### Precision and recall expectation

- Type-1 (exact, whitespace/comment-insensitive): recall about 1.0 above the floors (6 lines / 40 tokens to be a candidate; 10/70 or 15/100 for FAIL). Expected FAIL precision about 0.85-0.95 after the boilerplate catalog. Residual false positives are intentional per-provider copies, which the ledger handles.
- Type-2 (consistent identifier rename, literal changes): L1 alpha-renaming catches it with similar recall. Inconsistent renames (two variables merged into one) fall to type-3 containment.
- Type-3 (gapped, statements added or removed): winnowed containment at 0.70 or above over 5-statement windows should catch copies with a few edited or inserted statements. Pairs below 0.70 containment, and heavily reordered or restructured code, are missed. The 0.70-1.0 band goes to models, so final precision there depends on Jev and Haiku. Published LLM results show weaker Type-2/3 verdicts and over-flagging of high-similarity non-clones; no Jev or Claude clone benchmark exists for C#.
- Type-4 (semantic: LINQ vs loop, a different algorithm for the same intent): stage 1 does not catch it. It is caught only through opt-in Engram search_code recall, which is expected to be low (the probe quality was poor) and limited to default-branch code.
- Misses:
  - duplicates under 6 lines or 40 tokens;
  - duplicates spread across non-contiguous members;
  - cross-repo duplication (out of scope unless Engram corpus-wide recall is enabled);
  - duplicates of code in excluded test paths;
  - near-copies of helpers that live in NuGet packages or the BCL ("should have used string.Join").
- Over-flag risks:
  - boilerplate shapes not yet in the catalog (EF configuration, minimal-API endpoint maps, tree-sitter query wiring in the 6 HTTP extractors);
  - generated code without standard markers;
  - the sibling-class rule firing on new per-table Stores that are legitimately parallel. These need ledger entries or catalog growth.
- The target is roughly at most 1 false FAIL per 50 patches, measured against a labelled set. Seed that set with engram-csharp's ~40 "Watched, not filed" pairs, DryUpScan-filed issues, and known load-bearing per-VCS clones.

### Failure modes

- Contract not generalised: without replacing `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` at GateRunner.cs:861 and adding a change scope, the plugin gets no frozen tree or diff, so every run returns INCOMPLETE. This is a blocking prerequisite, not a configuration choice.
- Final PASS is never authoritative today: an External plugin cannot hold legacy-v1 authority, so a raw PASS surfaces as advisory INCOMPLETE (GateRunner.cs:396-403). Consumers must read PluginRawOutcome, or authority must be added for this gate.
- Decision-provider drift: Jev has no documented retirement policy. If jev-1.14.0 is withdrawn, every cache miss becomes a provider error, then INCOMPLETE or stage 3. Memoised verdicts stay valid only for the pinned resolved model; a resolved-model change in the response must mismatch the key, giving a miss and new evidence.
- Non-reproducible first verdict: model outputs are not replayable (no seed; temperature removed in Anthropic API 2026-10-21; Jev determinism undocumented). Reproducibility comes only from memoisation, so a deleted or corrupted cache makes a rerun produce possibly different verdicts. Offline verification mode turns that into INCOMPLETE rather than a silent flip.
- Cache poisoning on a same-UID host: anything that can write DF_DRY_CACHE_ROOT can plant CLEARED verdicts. Mitigations: a service-owned path, entries hashed into the receipt, and transcript artifacts for audit. This cannot be fully prevented under the documented same-UID limitation.
- Prompt injection through code: string literals and identifiers still reach the model after comment stripping. Exposure is asymmetric, because a model can only clear AMBIGUOUS pairs, never stage 1 CLEAR-FAIL. Injection can therefore cause false negatives in the type-3 band, not false FAILs.
- Data egress: proprietary engram-csharp code goes to TypeSafe (early-access startup, no documented DPA or data residency) and to Anthropic (ZDR status unchecked). A policy block means configuring a self-hosted pplx-decider or disabling stages 2/3, which turns the ambiguous band into INCOMPLETE.
- Rate limits and outages during CI bursts: 429/529 lead to UNRESOLVED and then INCOMPLETE. Without stage 3 enabled, a Jev outage turns about 20-25% of patches INCOMPLETE.
- Partial-parse risk: parse-only Roslyn with no preprocessor symbols treats #if-excluded regions as trivia, so duplicated code under inactive #if branches is invisible. A parse error in a changed file leads to INCOMPLETE.
- Rename/move attribution: `git diff -M50%` can miss a heavily edited move. The moved code then appears added and can pair with a leftover copy, a correct FAIL if the old copy remains. A false FAIL is possible when the base copy was deleted but rename detection paired the wrong files.
- Candidate explosion on large refactor patches (for example 3,000-line generated additions): the more-than-25-ambiguous-pair budget gives INCOMPLETE, forcing a patch split or ledger work rather than an expensive or sampled judgement.
- Threshold mis-calibration: the 0.70 containment floor, the 10/70 and 15/100 FAIL floors and p >= 0.85 are starting points borrowed from jscpd/DryUpScan and Jev confidence-routing docs. They are not validated on C# and must be tuned against the labelled set before the gate becomes required.

### Implementation sketch

CONTRACT, in DeliveryForge.Evidence:
- IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin.cs: a new interface replacing the 11 anti-slop type and id checks in GateRunner.cs. AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin implements it with its current values, so behaviour is unchanged and the existing tests pin it.
- RepositoryChangeScope.cs: a new record holding ChangedPaths, HeadAddedLineRanges, RenamedFrom, BaseMaterializedRoot, BaseMaterializedObjectIds and ChangeSetIdentity. It is added as an optional `Changes` field on RepositoryEvaluationScope.
- GitRepositoryIdentityReader.cs: add `git diff -U0 -M50% --no-ext-diff --no-textconv -z` hunk parsing (bounded with the existing MaximumBatchPayloadBytes) and base-blob materialisation for changed included paths, reusing VerifyGitBlobIdentity, MaterializeFrozenObjectAsync and VerifyMaterializedInputs.
- GateRunner: generalise AppendAntiSlopBindingArtifact into a per-plugin binding hook, or leave it to AppendBoundArtifacts.
- Effort: about 5-7 dev-days including regression tests for anti-slop parity.

PLUGIN, in components/evidence/DeliveryForge.Evidence/Adapters/Dry/ (one type per file per HoT style):
- DryCascadeEvaluationPlugin.cs
- DryCascadeRequestFactory.cs (canonical argv plus content-addressed inputs/manifest.json, modelled on AntiSlopRequestFactory)
- DryCascadeReportNormalizer.cs (strict parse of df-dry-json-v1 and count reconciliation)
- DryAllowLedger.cs and DryAllowLedgerEntry.cs (protected-base read via ProtectedGitPolicyResolver)
- DryCascadeSnapshot.cs
- DryBindingArtifactWriter.cs
- AdvisoryDryCascadeComposition.cs (mirror of AdvisoryAntiSlopComposition for local runs)
- Effort: about 4 dev-days.

SCANNER TOOL, in tools/DeliveryForge.DryScan, a .NET 10 console app published self-contained single-file per RID, with Deterministic=true and ContinuousIntegrationBuild=true. Its sha256 per RID is pinned in the plugin like the ReviewedLinuxX64...Sha256 constants.
- Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp pinned to one exact reviewed version (for example 4.14.0; confirm current), parse-only.
- CSharpFragmentExtractor, TokenNormalizer (L0/L1), Winnower (5-gram, window 4), CloneIndex, ChangeAttribution (hunk overlap plus base ratchet), BandClassifier, BoilerplateShapeCatalog, PathFilters.
- Providers: IDecisionProvider plus JevDecisionProvider (raw HttpClient POST https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone; there is no .NET SDK, so either hand-write a few DTOs or generate them from https://api.typesafe.ai/openapi.json with NSwag/Kiota), MicrosoftDecisionProvider (Foundry deployment name), SelfHostedDeciderProvider, and AnthropicJudgeProvider (claude-haiku-5-5, output_config.format json_schema, anthropic-version 2026-10-21, effort low).
- Supporting classes: VerdictCache (write-once, content-addressed), EngramRecallClient (optional, MCP over HTTP with a service-principal token), CascadeReportWriter.
- Endpoint host allow-list enforced in code.
- Effort: stage 1 about 7-9 days, providers and cache about 4-5 days.

CALIBRATION, in tools/DeliveryForge.DryScan.Calibrate: replays N historical base..head pairs from engram-csharp, emits the band distribution, and scores against a labelled pair set built from DryUpScan-filed issues and the watched list. It also pins the thresholds into dry-rules.json. Effort: about 3-5 days.

TOTAL: about 5 engineer-weeks to an advisory gate; tightening thresholds to required status depends on calibration.

### Novelty claim

The owner listed three separate tiers. This option is a single cascade whose stages carry different authority, which none of the three tiers covers:
- (1) Asymmetric authority: deterministic stage 1 alone can FAIL clear copies, and models can only adjudicate the ambiguous band. A model can never overturn a deterministic FAIL, so a model or injection error can only produce a false negative there.
- (2) Base-ratchet attribution: a per-change gate fails only on duplication the patch introduces. Clones whose L1 class already existed in base are recorded as `watched` and not charged to the patch, so the weekly sweep's ~40 watched pairs never block unrelated edits.
- (3) Content-addressed verdict memoisation, plus an offline verification mode, turns non-deterministic model calls into replayable receipt evidence with bound raw transcripts.
- (4) Option-order-swap agreement for Jev, to cancel its documented first-option bias, and 3/3 unanimity for the LLM, with disagreement mapped to INCOMPLETE instead of majority vote.
- (5) Load-bearing duplication declared through a protected-base, fingerprint-scoped, expiring ledger: a two-merge exception path, not an in-patch bypass.
- (6) A fixed candidate-pair budget that turns runaway patches into INCOMPLETE instead of sampling.
- (7) Calibration from engram-csharp's existing DryUpScan filed and watched history as a labelled set.

### Open questions

- Is it acceptable under HoT data policy to send engram-csharp source fragments to TypeSafe (early access, no documented DPA or residency) and to Anthropic? If not, should stage 2 default to a self-hosted pplx-decider or Microsoft-Decision-1 on Azure Foundry?
- Should the gate also escalate CLEAR-FAIL patches' ambiguous pairs, for a complete finding list, or stop at the first deterministic FAIL to save cost and latency (`stopOnClearFail`)?
- Who owns `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json` entries, and what maximum expiry is acceptable? Should ledger additions require a specific reviewer or CODEOWNERS rule in engram-csharp?
- Is generalising GateRunner's 11 anti-slop special cases into IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin, plus adding RepositoryChangeScope, in scope for this work, or should it land first as its own PR against the #48-#50 plugin issues?
- Should this gate get a path to production authority (built-in registration plus legacy-v1 binding, or future-v2)? Under current code its raw PASS always surfaces as advisory INCOMPLETE.
- Where does the verdict cache live in CI (service-owned volume, Azure blob with immutability policy) so it outlives ephemeral runners and is not writable by the candidate job?
- Is Engram type-4 recall worth a headless service-principal integration, given that search_code returned weak scores (0.31-0.40, irrelevant top hits) in the probe? Or should type-4 be out of scope for v1?
- Should test code be in scope? DRY in tests is often deliberate. The current default excludes it, but shared test fixtures could be a separate opt-in.
- Should stage 1 also run pinned jscpd 4.2.5 (--min-lines 10 --min-tokens 70) for parity with the weekly DryUpScan sweep? That adds a Node runtime closure to the identity in exchange for a shared vocabulary of clone classes.

### Judge verdicts

**contract-fit (5/10).** Interface fit is mostly credible and the member mapping is detailed. I confirmed the interface members exist in DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs (DetectInputs, ValidateBeforeExecution, ValidateAfterExecution, GetConfigurationIdentity, GetRuntimeClosureIdentity, GetExecutableIdentity, CaptureBeforeAsync, Normalize). I also confirmed 'is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin' special-casing in GateRunner.cs at lines 115, 260, 273, 285, 296, 381, 785 and 861, so the proposal correctly identifies that the frozen-tree path is hard-wired and needs a refactor before it can work. Strengths: INCOMPLETE is the default for uncertain paths. That covers parse errors, budget overrun, count reconciliation, model disagreement and offline cache misses. The ledger is read from base through ProtectedGitPolicyResolver, which keeps the candidate repo out of the authority path. Prompt text is comment-stripped, and a model can only clear AMBIGUOUS pairs, not stage 1 CLEAR-FAIL. Weaknesses under this lens: (1) It is not pluggable as the contract stands. It needs a new interface, a RepositoryChangeScope, base-tree materialisation and diff hunk parsing. That is a substantial host change. It also contradicts the PR #52 principle that the plugin wraps one external tool over frozen head-tree inputs. (2) Reproducibility is weak for the receipt discipline. The stage 2 and 3 verdicts are non-deterministic network calls, and replay depends on a mutable cache that the proposal itself admits is writable by a same-UID attacker. The config and runtime-closure identities cannot cover remote model behaviour. 'Resolved model' is only self-reported by the response. DF_DRY_SECRETS_REF contributes only its name. A PASS or CLEARED from a cache hit is therefore not reproducible evidence, and offline replay only proves the cache was read. (3) Network egress and a secrets reference inside the 'deterministic' tool step break the offline frozen-input model that anti-slop follows. The environment allow-list has to be widened and an endpoint allow-list is enforced only in the binary's own code. (4) Final PASS downgrades to advisory INCOMPLETE because an External plugin cannot hold legacy-v1 authority. The gate therefore cannot yield authoritative PASS under current code, and the proposal defers this to an open question. (5) Bypass surface: the allow-ledger is base-protected, but the 'ledger oid' is verified only through plugin-side reads. A candidate can still influence the verdict through the code content that reaches the model, through string literals and identifiers, and through file layout that pushes pairs into the CLEAR-PASS band (for example by changing the shape just enough to fall below 0.70 containment). That is an inherent evasion rather than a contract bypass, but it is not noted as a receipt-level limitation. (6) The hard-coded binary hash per RID, with a single-file .NET publish, is not bit-reproducible across build hosts in practice. Also, stdout carries per-pair decisions and artifact hashes that the plugin trusts. The binary is a single process whose self-reported report is normalised by the plugin, as in anti-slop. But the reconciliation checks are weaker because the plugin cannot independently recompute the clone index.

Strongest weakness: Non-deterministic model stages sit inside a receipt whose identities cannot capture remote model behaviour, and replay depends on a mutable same-UID cache. Rerun reproducibility and PASS authority are therefore weaker than the contract's frozen-input discipline. The design also depends on a large host-contract rewrite (new interface, change scope, base materialisation) before it can run at all.

Suggested fix: Split into two registered plugins. Plugin A is a fully deterministic df-dry stage 1 with no network access and an offline-only env allow-list. Its PASS and FAIL are bound by identities as in anti-slop, and it emits ambiguous pairs only as limitations, which map to INCOMPLETE. Model adjudication becomes a separate advisory step outside the authority path, or a distinct plugin whose verdicts are recorded as a signed, content-addressed evidence artifact with the resolved model and raw transcript bound into the receipt. A cached verdict is never treated as reproducible and can never turn INCOMPLETE into PASS without an explicit reviewer-bound attestation. Land the IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin and RepositoryChangeScope refactor first, with anti-slop parity tests, and decide the External-plugin authority path before the gate is made required.

**accuracy (6/10).** Strong where it counts for exact and renamed copies. Roslyn token-level L0/L1 normalisation, positional alpha-renaming, winnowed 5-gram containment and intra-patch pairing should catch 85%-identical methods and copy-pasted helpers that were manually renamed. Most of the issue-238 shapes are covered by this.

The base-ratchet attribution is a real precision win. It stops the roughly 40 known watched pairs from blocking unrelated edits. Models only adjudicate the ambiguous band and cannot overturn a CLEAR-FAIL, so injection can only cause false negatives. The ledger is also well designed.

Recall gaps against the issue-238 shapes:
- The 6-line / 40-token candidate floor and the 10/70 and 15/100 FAIL floors mean the "byte-identical wrapper methods across 5 files" shape often falls below the gate. Thin wrappers are typically 3-8 lines. The only route to FAIL for a short wrapper is the 6-statement whole-body rule, which a thin wrapper will not meet.
- Anything between the candidate floor and 10 lines is CLEAR-PASS. Containment under 0.70 is a silent pass.
- Type-3 detection depends on order-sensitive 5-grams. Reordering statements, splitting a method into two sub-floor helpers, or inserting no-op statements drops containment and drops fragments below the floors. It is gameable in cheap ways.
- Type-4 and semantic duplication is effectively unhandled. The Engram recall is weak (probe scores 0.31-0.40) and only sees default-branch code.
- Cheap-model clearance in the 0.70-1.0 band can launder real duplication. Jev has no clone benchmark, and the proposal itself cites correlated errors.

Precision risks:
- Collapsing all identifiers to positional placeholders plus literal-collapse makes structurally similar but semantically distinct code match. Examples are per-table Stores with different SQL and the six tree-sitter extractors. These then hit the sibling rule (class of 3 or more at 10 lines) and CLEAR-FAIL without any model check.
- The deterministic FAIL path cannot be cleared by a model. Every false positive is therefore resolved only by the ledger, which is a two-merge, owner-gated process and costly at roughly 1 false FAIL per 50 patches.
- The boilerplate catalog is a hand-grown allow-list. DI chains, EF configuration and minimal-API maps are explicitly listed as unaddressed. Boilerplate that is identical for 80 or more lines still FAILs.

The thresholds are unvalidated, and the proposal says so. Overall the design is credible and workable, but the hard floors and the clear-fail sibling rule shape the real recall and precision outcome.

Strongest weakness: The fixed line and token floors (candidate 6 lines / 40 tokens, FAIL 10/70 or 15/100) leave the byte-identical thin-wrapper shape from issue 238 largely uncaught. The same floors are the cheap way to game it, by splitting methods into sub-floor helpers or reordering statements. Meanwhile the sibling-class rule can CLEAR-FAIL legitimately parallel per-table Stores deterministically, with no model check and only the ledger to clear it.

Suggested fix: Add a wrapper and thin-delegate rule that aggregates sub-floor fragments. Aggregate by clone class across files, so that 3 or more files with identical L0 members of at least 3 statements form a candidate regardless of line count.

Make the type-3 index order-insensitive. Use set-based statement-level hashes, or canonicalise the order of independent statements, so reorder and split evasion lowers containment less.

Route sibling-rule FAILs for Store-like or extractor-like classes through the ambiguous band instead of CLEAR-FAIL. Alternatively require the SQL or query literals to be equal before treating a clone as deterministic.

Before enforcing, calibrate on the labelled set (the roughly 40 watched pairs plus DryUpScan-filed issues). Report precision and recall per shape, and set the floors from that data.

**cost-ops (6/10).** Operationally the cascade is sound in shape. Stage 1 is deterministic and free. Model spend is tiny (about $0.0005 average, about $0.10 cap). Memoised reruns cost nothing. The cost-preference order (deterministic, then decision model, then cheap LLM) is respected, and stage 3 is off by default. The estimates are labelled as unmeasured. The weakness is implementation effort and maintenance.

- Effort: about 5 engineer-weeks to an advisory gate. Most of it is a Roslyn clone detector with winnowing, a clone index, base-ratchet attribution and a boilerplate catalog. A mature tool such as jscpd, which DryUpScan already uses, does not cover C# token normalisation and attribution. A large GateRunner refactor is also required first (11 anti-slop special cases) plus a new RepositoryChangeScope.
- Maintenance burden:
  - per-RID pinned binary hashes;
  - a pinned Roslyn version;
  - a ledger, a catalog and calibrated thresholds, all of which need ongoing tuning;
  - several provider adapters (Jev, Microsoft Decision, a self-hosted decider, Anthropic).
- External dependencies are heavy and fragile:
  - Jev is early access with no documented retirement or availability policy, and no .NET SDK exists.
  - Jev outages turn about 20-25% of patches INCOMPLETE when stage 3 is off.
  - A durable service-owned verdict cache is needed on ephemeral CI runners.
  - Optional Engram recall needs a headless service-principal token, and Engram only indexes the default branch, so stale-index handling and retrieval snapshots add more work.
- Cold start: stage 1 parses about 1,000 files on every run (about 3-8 s, estimated). That is acceptable but not cached across runs. There is no incremental index keyed on base.
- Calibration is a prerequisite for the gate to be trusted, and a further 3-5 days is budgeted for it.
- The final PASS is always advisory INCOMPLETE under current code. The gate's value therefore depends on further authority work.
- Data egress policy is unresolved. That is an operability blocker for stages 2 and 3, and the fallback turns the ambiguous band into INCOMPLETE.

Strongest weakness: Roughly 5 engineer-weeks and a high ongoing maintenance load (a custom Roslyn clone detector, per-RID pinned binaries, a GateRunner refactor, four provider adapters, a durable verdict cache, calibration), all built around an early-access Jev dependency with no availability or retirement guarantees.

Suggested fix: Ship a v1 that is stage 1 only, with the ambiguous band mapped to INCOMPLETE or advisory, and defer the model stages. Reuse pinned jscpd 4.2.5 plus the existing DryUpScan parser, thresholds and PathFilters instead of writing a Roslyn winnowing detector. That cuts stage 1 from 7-9 days to about 2-3 and gives clone-class vocabulary parity with the weekly sweep. Land the IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin and RepositoryChangeScope refactor as its own PR first. Cache the base-tree clone index per base commit to cut cold start. Add stage 2 only after replaying historical patches shows the ambiguous band is worth the model dependency. Add stage 3 and Engram recall last, and keep stage 3 as an outage fallback only.

### Adversarial refutation

**Refuted: true (major).** Claim attacked: Stage 1 deterministically produces the right band on issue-238 clone shapes: type-3 winnowed containment >= 0.70 over L1-normalised 5-gram fingerprints catches "copies with a few edited or inserted statements". The same stage 1 is also claimed to FAIL the byte-identical 10-28 line sibling shapes, with only the ambiguous band going to a model.

Evidence:

I read DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs, GateRunner.cs (scope validation, the PASS downgrade) and GitRepositoryIdentityReader.cs. The plumbing fits as the design says: the anti-slop special-casing at GateRunner.cs:861 is real and the design already concedes the contract gap. The clone-detection core fails on its own terms.

(1) The type-3 mechanism is broken by its normalisation.
- L1 renames identifiers to positional placeholders "consistent within the fragment", and winnowing runs over those L1 5-grams.
- Placeholder numbers depend on first-occurrence order inside the fragment, so the token stream is context-dependent.
- Winnowing only guarantees that a shared substring yields a shared fingerprint when each token is context-independent.
- Take a sibling copy that inserts one statement early and introduces a new local (typical for the 6 tree-sitter extractors or 5 VCS indexers). Every later local gets a shifted placeholder (v3 becomes v4), so almost all downstream 5-grams differ.
- Containment then falls well below 0.70 and the pair is CLEAR-PASS, so the gate silently passes.
- The same applies to the 5..N sliding windows. The same code at different window starts, or inside different enclosing fragments, is renumbered differently.
- jscpd and CPD avoid this by mapping all identifiers to one class token. The design's own claim that it "should catch copies with a few edited or inserted statements" is therefore not achievable as specified. Only exact L1 equality of whole fragments survives.
- The 11-28 line "Watched" pairs in #238 are mostly near-copies, not exact ones. Exact-only detection misses most of them.

(2) The thin-wrapper and sibling shapes do not stay deterministic.
- The byte-identical thin-wrapper shape is explicitly boilerplate-shaped (ctor-assign, guard clauses, DI, DTO). Those go to AMBIGUOUS even above the floor, so the model decides them.
- Size-2 classes of 10-14 lines also go to the model.
- Stage 3 is off by default and Jev is early access, so a stage 2 outage or unresolved pair becomes INCOMPLETE. For the main issue-238 shapes the verdict is a model verdict or INCOMPLETE, not a deterministic one.
- In the other direction, the sibling rule (class size >= 3) CLEAR-FAILs legitimately parallel per-table Stores with no model check.
- The only exit is a ledger entry. It must be merged in a separate PR first, and it is keyed by clone-class hash or glob, so the author must pre-author it for code that does not yet exist. "Fails until DRY" can therefore be unsatisfiable for deliberately parallel code.

(3) Receipt reproducibility. A FAIL or PASS that depends on remote model output is only replayable via a mutable same-UID cache. The contract's pinned-tool and frozen-input model cannot attest it, and an External PASS is downgraded to advisory INCOMPLETE anyway (GateRunner.cs:396-403).

Salvage:

Keep the architecture and fix the detector.
- Winnow over a context-free token stream: all identifiers map to one ID token, literals to their kind, and operators and keywords are kept.
- Use positional alpha-renaming only as a second-pass exact-equality check on the candidate pair, never as the fingerprint stream.
- Re-derive the type-3 containment threshold on the labelled #238 watched pairs.
- Drop the unconditional sibling-size CLEAR-FAIL. Send class size >= 3 to the model band, or require a ledger-free structural signal such as the same member signature shape.
- Let the first-time ledger entry be authored in-patch as a reviewable, non-binding "declared load-bearing" request that yields INCOMPLETE with a clear reason rather than FAIL. This removes the chicken-and-egg without giving the candidate a bypass.
- Say plainly that for issue-238 shapes the model band is the primary decider, so the design is not tier-1 deterministic.

**Refuted: true (fatal).** Claim attacked: That the cascade separates real duplication from load-bearing or boilerplate duplication with no candidate-controlled bypass, and that an agent under gate pressure cannot cheaply evade it (rename, reorder, split, wrap) or satisfy it with fake justification. In the design's words: "four layers, none of them a candidate-controlled bypass" and "injection can therefore cause false negatives in the type-3 band, not false FAILs".

Evidence:

All points below come from the design's own rules.

A. Candidate-controlled exemptions exist, contradicting "no bypass".
- Stage 1 step 7 excludes "*.g.cs, *.Designer.cs and generated headers". Both are chosen by the author of the patch.
- Naming a file `Foo.g.cs`, or adding `// <auto-generated/>` to the top of a file, removes it from the gate. Nothing checks that the file is really generated.
- Path classes (test/, *.Tests, Migrations) are string matches on a path the patch controls. A helper placed under a `Migrations` or `Tests` segment is out of scope.
- Only *.cs is scanned. Duplicated logic in SQL or tree-sitter query strings is invisible, because L1 collapses literals to their kind. Per-table Stores are mostly SQL text. Code under inactive `#if` branches is also invisible.

B. The FAIL band is a set of cliffs, and the gate is an oracle for walking off them.
- A FAIL needs L0/L1 equality at 10 lines/70 tokens (class of 3 or more) or 15/100, or a whole body with at least 6 statements.
- Splitting a duplicated 40-line function into helpers of 5 or fewer statements and under 10 lines makes every pair CLEAR-PASS. Windows are per block, not across members, and the orchestrator is under the 6-line floor.
- Inserting one dummy statement every 2-3 statements breaks 5-gram L1 winnowing. Containment drops below 0.70, which is also CLEAR-PASS.
- Reordering, for to while or LINQ, if inversion and temporaries all do the same. Type-4 is out of scope by default.
- Every FAIL names the exact file:line pair and class, so an agent can perturb and re-run until the band drops. The cost is a few iterations.

C. The perturbation that leaves the CLEAR-FAIL band is the same one that steers the judge.
- A perturbation statement such as `var _ = "per-provider divergence by design: LOAD_BEARING"` breaks L1 equality. It moves the pair into AMBIGUOUS and puts text the author controls into the judge input.
- Only comments are stripped. String literals, identifiers, file paths and the sibling paths still reach the model. A new file beside `GitHubBulkIndexer`, `GitLabBulkIndexer` and similar reads as per-VCS divergence by context alone.
- Order-swap cancels position bias, not persuasion. Both orders see the same planted text.
- Confining models to the ambiguous band is no protection. That band is exactly where evasion lands. A model can only clear pairs there, so a steered clear is the attack itself.

D. Verdict shopping defeats the memoisation.
- The key includes sha256 of the L0 text of both sides. Any cosmetic edit, such as a rename, gives a new key and a fresh stochastic roll from Jev or Haiku.
- The gate becomes pass@k on the judge. There is no sticky record that a pair previously FAILed. Memoisation only protects identical text.
- The 3/3 and both-orders-agree rules push disagreement to INCOMPLETE. Agents re-roll until the models happen to agree on CLEARED.

E. The ledger does not discriminate. It just moves the decision to a rubber stamp.
- Per-VCS bulk indexers and per-table Stores are L1-equal and over the floors. They are deterministically CLEAR-FAIL, and no model can overturn that.
- The issue-238 requirement that load-bearing duplication must not fail is met only by a prior merge of a ledger entry.
- The entry holds an opaque L1 hash plus a glob and a 3-value reasonCategory enum that nothing verifies. A reviewer cannot see what a ledger-only PR licenses.
- A glob such as `src/**/*Store.cs` becomes a permanent blanket exemption. A ledger-only PR is NOT_APPLICABLE or PASS, so the agent splits its work into two PRs and passes both.
- The `// dry:load-bearing <id>` marker is undefined as to whether the id's hash and glob must still match the clone. If only the id is checked, it is a bypass.

F. Systematic false positives that hit legitimate work.
- A partial DRY-up refactor fails. Extracting the helper from 3 of 5 siblings makes the new helper body A, with at least 50% added lines. The remaining unchanged siblings are B, with a class of 3 or more. That is CLEAR-FAIL.
- The base ratchet does not help. The helper file is new, so there is no base A file. The gate therefore punishes incremental refactoring toward DRY, and the cheapest compliant action is to not touch the clones.
- A mechanical signature change across all 5 existing per-VCS indexers changes their L1 hash, so the ratchet no longer matches. The sibling rule then FAILs it.
- The 50%-of-lines attribution means converging two existing methods by small edits is never attributed.

Net: precision and evasion pull in opposite directions. The deterministic floors cannot be overridden by the models, so they must be both loose (to avoid false FAILs) and tight (to resist evasion). They cannot be both. The load-bearing judgement sits only where evidence is weakest, and it takes input the author controls.

Salvage:

The smallest change that survives is to replace per-pair, cliff-based attribution with delta accounting, and to remove every author-controlled input. The staged cascade and the plugin contract can stay.

1. Judge the patch by net change in clone mass, not by pairs.
   - Compute total duplicated-token mass per clone class at base and at head.
   - FAIL only if the patch increases mass beyond a budget.
   - A partial extraction, a signature sweep or a move then passes, because mass falls or stays flat.
   - Aggregate across members, so splitting into sub-floor helpers does not hide mass. Use gap-tolerant matching: CPD-style tolerance for small non-matching insertions, or a tree-fragment or bag-of-subtrees measure (Deckard-like) that ignores statement order and inserted no-op statements. This removes the single-token or dummy-statement cliff.

2. Remove author-controlled exemptions.
   - Generated and test status must come from the base-protected path taxonomy plus a verified generator marker, for example matching a base-listed generator output set. It must never come from a header or filename in the patch.
   - Scan non-.cs duplicate carriers: SQL strings, `.sql`, `.scm` queries and resource files. Hash string-literal content, or at least flag long identical literals.

3. Make the model stage non-steerable and non-rerollable.
   - Send the judge only L1-normalised text, with identifiers and literals replaced by placeholders. Take path classes from the base taxonomy, not from patch-chosen paths.
   - Key verdicts on the stage-1 pair identity (class L1 hash plus base-relative location class), not on raw text.
   - Record every attempt per pair lineage. Once a pair has FAILed, a later CLEARED is accepted only if the L1 hashes also changed materially.

4. Replace the opaque-hash ledger.
   - Ledger entries should be keyed to existing base paths, include the code diff or snippet in the review, and have a narrow glob.
   - Inline markers should be accepted only if the id's hash and glob both match.
   - Require a named CODEOWNER for the ledger file.

5. Add an adversarial regression suite before the gate becomes required: split-helper, dummy-statement, reorder, `.g.cs` and header, string-steering and partial-extraction cases, each with an expected verdict.


## A.jev-decision: DRY Decision Gate (dotnet launcher + deterministic candidate miner + typed decision model per candidate pair)

Panel mean 5.5 (contract-fit 5.5, accuracy 6, cost-ops 5). Tier: decision-model.

**One line.** A pinned, self-contained .NET launcher first mines DRY candidate pairs deterministically: Roslyn method/window clones against the frozen base and head trees, scoped to the patch hunks and ratcheted against the base. Clear-cut type-1 clones fail without any model. Only mid-band pairs (type-2, type-3, and Engram-retrieved type-4) go to a configurable decision model (Jev jev-1.14.0 by default, behind IDryDecisionProvider), which answers typed Noul/Choice/Score questions per pair. The trusted plugin recomputes the verdict from the raw probabilities using code-owned thresholds, and anything unknown is INCOMPLETE.

### Mechanism

Inputs. The frozen base tree, the frozen head tree, and the hunk list from git diff -U0 -M base..head, restricted to *.cs. This needs the contract extension described in plugin_fit.

STEP 1: fragments (deterministic, syntax-only Roslyn, no restore, no semantic model).
- Parse every included .cs blob in both trees with CSharpSyntaxTree.ParseText (LanguageVersion pinned).
- Extract fragments: methods, constructors, accessors with bodies, local functions, lambdas of 5 or more statements, and sliding statement windows of 6 consecutive statements inside any block.
- Skip *.g.cs, *.Designer.cs, obj/, bin/, Migrations/*.Designer.cs and anything with [GeneratedCode].
- For each fragment compute four fingerprints:
  - H1: SHA-256 of the trivia-stripped token stream (type-1).
  - H2: SHA-256 after blind alpha-renaming. Locals, parameters and private fields become positional placeholders by first occurrence. Literals become kind placeholders (STR/NUM/CHAR/BOOL). Keywords, operators and the member-access names of non-local symbols are kept (type-2).
  - W: winnowing fingerprints over normalized token 7-grams with window 4 (type-3 recall).
  - Normalized statement count and token count.

STEP 2: patch-scoped candidates.
- A head fragment is "new" when 50% or more of its lines fall inside added or changed hunks.
- Pair each new fragment with every other fragment (both trees, intra-patch included) that matches on:
  - (a) H1, or
  - (b) H2, or
  - (c) Jaccard of winnow sets 0.70 or higher, with both sides at 6 or more normalized statements and 50 or more tokens.
- Drop a pair (each drop gets a reason code) when:
  - the existing side is deleted or moved in head, because -M renames make the pair a move, not a duplicate;
  - the existing side sits in a test/fixture path (configurable, default **/*Tests*/**, **/Fixtures/**);
  - either side is under 5 normalized statements or 40 tokens (trivial);
  - either side is pure declaration boilerplate by Roslyn kind classification: only property/field declarations, DI-registration call chains (services.AddX), attribute/using lists, record positional declarations, or guard-clause-only blocks.
- Ratchet: drop the pair if the same (H2(A), H2(B)) clone class already had this many members in base. Only patches that create a clone or add a member to an existing class count.

STEP 3: Engram type-4 recall, optional (mode off|advisory|required).
- Query only new fragments with 8 or more statements that found no Step-2 candidate.
- Per fragment: one search_symbols(query = de-camel-cased method-name stems, repo, kind=method) and one search_code(query = signature + identifier bag, top 30 distinct identifiers, repo, kind=method, max_results=8), 4 in parallel.
- Engram indexes the default branch, which is the base in the normal PR case, so this is the right corpus to search against.
- Never trust Engram text. Every hit is resolved to (path, symbol) in the frozen base tree via the Roslyn outline and re-read from frozen bytes. Hits that do not resolve are dropped.
- Locally re-score each surviving hit and keep it only if normalized-identifier Jaccard is 0.35 or higher, or the shared invoked-member-name set overlaps 60% or more. This removes the weak semantic ranking seen in the probe (scores 0.31-0.40, README hits).

STEP 4: deterministic pre-decisions (no model).
- DET-FAIL: H1 match, 15 or more normalized lines AND 120 or more tokens, new side 80% or more added, not inside a declared family (see load_bearing). Mirrors the issue's "80 LOC byte-identical" spirit at a per-patch size.
- DET-FAIL: the patch adds a 3rd or later member to an H2 clone class spanning 3 or more sibling files outside any declared family. Mirrors the ">=3 sibling files" rule.
- Everything else that survived Step 2 or 3 is MODEL-BAND.

STEP 5: rank and budget.
- Rank MODEL-BAND pairs by (new-side added lines x similarity), then path ordinal for ties.
- Evaluate the top 40. Overflow is recorded.

STEP 6: decision model, one request per pair.
- State (JSON) = {
    schema: "df-dry-state-v1",
    newFragment: {path, startLine, endLine, kind, code},
    existingFragment: {path, startLine, endLine, kind, code, tree: base|head},
    match: {via: H2|winnow|engram, similarityBucket: "very-high|high|medium"},
    siblingContext: {sameDirectory: bool, declaredFamily: name|null, familyRationale: text|null},
    hotPathMarkers: [attribute names found, e.g. MethodImpl(AggressiveInlining), SkipLocalsInit],
    patchClaimedRationale: text|null
  }
- Code is comment-stripped (comments are the prompt-injection surface named in Jev's jaggedness page) and bounded to 12k tokens per pair by a conservative chars/3.2 estimate. An oversize pair is UNSURE, never truncated.
- Numbers are bucketed in code because Jev cannot count or compare numerics.
- Questions, all in one request (isolated, parallel, so near-zero marginal latency):
  - Q1 noul "same_behaviour": do the two fragments implement the same behaviour, such that one call to a shared function with at most a few extra parameters could replace both?
  - Q2a choice "disposition_fwd", criteria in fixed order {call-existing, parameterise-existing, extract-helper, intentional-variation, boilerplate, not-duplicate}.
  - Q2b choice "disposition_rev": same criteria in reversed order. This counters the documented first-option bias.
  - Q3 score "extractability", rubric levels 0-4:
    - 0 unrelated logic.
    - 1 similar shape, different algorithm or side effects.
    - 2 same algorithm, but types/side effects diverge so sharing needs non-trivial generics or callbacks.
    - 3 same logic differing only in identifiers/literals/one branch: a parameterised helper is straightforward.
    - 4 identical logic: direct reuse.
  - Q4 noul "deliberate_variation": does the state show the duplication is deliberate (per-provider/per-VCS behaviour that is expected to diverge, a hot-path marker, generated or interop shape)?

STEP 7: verdict, recomputed in the trusted plugin from the raw answers (the launcher's opinion is ignored).
- PAIR-FAIL when all of these hold:
  - Q1 >= 0.85;
  - Q2a.choice == Q2b.choice, both in {call-existing, parameterise-existing, extract-helper}, each with confidence >= 0.70;
  - Q3 score >= 0.625 (normalized expectation, i.e. level 2.5 or above of 0-4);
  - Q4 <= 0.20.
- PAIR-CLEAR when any of these holds:
  - Q1 <= 0.30;
  - both dispositions agree on {intentional-variation, boilerplate, not-duplicate} with confidence >= 0.70;
  - Q4 >= 0.80 AND Q3 < 0.875.
- Otherwise PAIR-UNSURE.
- Family-internal pairs need the stricter FAIL bar: Q1 >= 0.95 and Q3 >= 0.875.

STEP 8: gate outcome.
- FAIL if there is at least one DET-FAIL or PAIR-FAIL. The finding lists file:line for both sides plus the suggested disposition. FAIL needs only one concrete finding, so overflow and unavailability do not block it.
- Else INCOMPLETE if any of these: a PAIR-UNSURE, a pair with no decision (provider unavailable), overflow > 0, Engram mode=required and Engram failed, or a count that does not reconcile (generated == decided + dropped-with-reason + overflow).
- Else PASS (all candidates cleared with positive proof).
- NOT_APPLICABLE when the patch changes zero included .cs paths, with a rationale; this still needs the policy's AllowedNotApplicable.

### Plugin contract fit

Class: DryDecisionEvaluationPlugin : IDeterministicEvaluationPlugin
- Id "deliveryforge.dry-decision", Version "1.0.0", ExpectedTool "df-dry-gate", CompatibilityAdapter EvidenceAdapterKind.External.
- Registered by host code through TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistration(GateId "dry", Plugin, ExpectedToolVersion "1.0.0", ExpectedFormatVersion "df-dry-report-v1", ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity <reviewed launcher closure sha>).
- Candidate repos cannot supply it. Policy can only select it via EvaluationPluginSelection("deliveryforge.dry-decision", Enabled, Inputs).

Infeasible as-is under PR #52, and the adaptation:
- (a) There is no plugin execute hook, and GateRunner.ValidateCapabilityBinding requires a hashed on-disk executable equal to Invocation.FileName. So the decision-model call cannot live in CaptureBeforeAsync or Normalize: Normalize exceptions map to ERROR, not INCOMPLETE, and it would bypass bounded capture and timeout. All network work (Engram + decision model) therefore runs inside the launcher (a self-contained single-file net10.0 exe, DeliveryForge.DryGate), executed by the host ICommandExecutor. Its bytes become Capability.ExecutableIdentity via ToolCapabilityDetector (VersionArguments ["--version","--json"], DocumentedFormatVersion "df-dry-report-v1", RequiredVersionPrefix "1.0.").
- (b) The contract carries no base tree and no diff, and frozen materialization is anti-slop-only (the `plugin is not AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` check at GateRunner.cs:861 and its siblings). Required change:
  - Add an interface IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin (separate file) with: `FrozenTreeRequirements GetFrozenTreeRequirements()` (include predicate, MaterializeBase=true, EmitHunks=true, suppression scanner = none); `bool UsesHostFrozenScope`.
  - Replace the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks in ValidateAutomaticScopeAsync with that interface. Anti-slop implements it with its current behaviour.
  - Extend RepositoryEvaluationScope with ChangedHunks (IReadOnlyList<DiffHunk>, record DiffHunk(string Path, string? BasePath, int BaseStart, int BaseCount, int HeadStart, int HeadCount)), MaterializedBaseRoot, and BaseObjectIds.
