  - GitRepositoryIdentityReader adds `git diff -U0 -M --no-color --no-ext-diff -z <base> <head> -- *.cs`, then materializes base blobs (it already reads them via cat-file --batch) under MaterializedRoot/base and head blobs under MaterializedRoot/head, writing hunks.json to MaterializedRoot/meta.
  - VerifyMaterializedInputs re-hashes both trees after execution.

Member mapping:
- DetectInputs(candidateInputs, ignoredInputs): returns tracked paths matching **/*.cs under configured roots (default src/**), minus ignored and generated globs, sorted ordinally. The runner's expected==requested equality check then binds Plugin.Inputs.
- ValidateBeforeExecution(request) returns Incomplete when any of these holds:
  - Capability.Version != "1.0.0" or FormatVersion != "df-dry-report-v1";
  - the host-registered decision config (not the repo) names an alias model (jev-latest / jev-preview);
  - the provider id is unregistered;
  - the credential reference file is missing or not host-owned;
  - Invocation.InheritEnvironment is true or the environment holds keys outside the allow-list {SystemRoot, WINDIR, TMP, TEMP, DOTNET_gcServer};
  - Invocation.Arguments differ from the canonical arguments built by DryDecisionRequestFactory.
- ValidateAuthorityWithoutExecution: null (no legacy authority).
- CaptureBeforeAsync: computes and freezes into the snapshot (record DryGateSnapshot):
  - launcher sha256;
  - config-bundle file digests (thresholds.v1.json, questions.v1.json, rubric text, state-schema, families file oid from protected base via ProtectedGitPolicyResolver `git show <base>:.delivery-forge/dry-families.json`);
  - provider id, endpoint scheme+host, pinned model id, engram mode + URL;
  - ConfigurationIdentity (below). Compares it to request.ConfigurationIdentity unless that is "unavailable"; a mismatch throws InvalidOperationException, which the runner maps to INCOMPLETE once the runner extension treats IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin like anti-slop at GateRunner.cs:255-265.
  - Re-runs ValidateReviewedPins-style checks: launcher bytes and question/rubric files must equal hard-coded reviewed sha256 constants in the plugin, otherwise INCOMPLETE.
- Execution: Invocation = (launcherPath, ["evaluate","--layout","frozen-v1","--config","<host bundle>/dry-gate.config.json","--credential-file","<host-owned path>","--report","stdout"], WorkingDirectory replaced by MaterializedRoot, Environment = allow-list, InheritEnvironment=false, StandardInput=null).
  - The launcher writes one canonical JSON report to stdout, self-capped at 8 MiB under the 64 MiB non-anti-slop cap.
  - Report content: candidates with fingerprints and drop codes, Engram query/response digests and resolved hits, per-pair state sha256, request sha256, verbatim response JSON, resolved model, usage, latency, error class.
  - Exit codes: 0 = report complete; 3 = report written with partial decisions; anything else = tool failure.
- Normalize(request, execution, snapshot), pure:
  - Parse the stdout JSON. Unparsable, wrong schema, or exit not in {0,3}: INCOMPLETE.
  - Reconcile counts.
  - Assert every candidate path is in Plugin.Inputs or the base tree.
  - Assert every decision.resolvedModel == the pinned model id.
  - Recompute every PAIR verdict with DryPairVerdictPolicy using the snapshot's thresholds, and re-derive the DET-FAIL rules from the reported fingerprints.
  - FAIL: new NormalizedEvidence(GateOutcome.Fail, reason listing pairs, false, ProductionCapable: true, limitations). Uses the constructor because there is no Fail factory.
  - PASS: NormalizedEvidence.Pass(...).
  - Otherwise NormalizedEvidence.Incomplete(reason, limitations...).
  - Always add the limitation "Remote decision model weights are not content-addressed; verdict reproducibility is by memoized response, not regeneration."
- ValidateAfterExecution: re-hashes the launcher (same as GetExecutableIdentity), returns Incomplete if it changed.
- AppendBoundArtifacts(request, snapshot, gateDirectory, artifactHashes): writes, with FileMode.CreateNew, and records sha256 for:
  - dry-binding.json (an analogue of anti-slop-binding.json, because the runner's sidecar is hard-keyed to dmmulroy.anti-slop): schemaVersion, gate, repository base/head/tree, plugin id/version, configurationIdentity, runtimeClosureIdentity, inputSetIdentity, provider, pinned model, thresholds, question-set sha, rubric sha, engram mode, families-file oid;
  - dry-questions.v1.json (exact question bytes sent).
  - The full transcript is already bound as stdout.txt by the runner.
  - Only IOException/UnauthorizedAccessException may escape; all serialization is done in the snapshot so nothing else can throw here.
- GetConfigurationIdentity(request, snapshot): snapshot null gives "unavailable". Otherwise sha256 over canonical lines: plugin id/version; launcher sha; normalizer params (k=7, w=4, window=6, Jaccard 0.70, minimum sizes); DET thresholds; pair thresholds; question-set bytes sha; rubric bytes sha; state-schema version; provider id; endpoint host; pinned model id; long-context header=false; per-pair token bound; candidate budget 40; engram mode + URL + tool params; test/generated globs; families-file blob oid; env allow-list keys and values; credential *reference path only*. The credential value is never hashed, so the identity is secret-independent.
- GetRuntimeClosureIdentity(request, snapshot): sha256 of the launcher single-file bytes. Roslyn and the provider client are embedded, so one file is the whole local closure. Compared against ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity.
- GetExecutableIdentity: snapshot hash of the launcher.
- Input-set identity: scope.InputSetIdentity extended for this plugin to hash head entries (path\0oid\0mode\0ignored) + base entries + canonical hunk list + base commit. A PASS is therefore bound to the exact diff, not just the head tree.
- Final authority: under current GateRunner.cs:396-403 an externally registered plugin's PASS is downgraded to INCOMPLETE (advisory), with PluginRawOutcome=PASS. FAIL passes through unchanged. So "fails until DRY" works today. Clean PASS becomes authoritative only if a future v2 authority path is enabled.

### Determinism and receipts

What is pinned:
- launcher bytes (sha256 = ExecutableIdentity = RuntimeClosureIdentity), which embed Roslyn Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp (exact version pinned in the csproj, e.g. 4.14.0; syntax-only, LanguageVersion explicit) and the provider client;
- normalizer constants, thresholds, question JSON and rubric bytes (reviewed sha256 constants in the plugin);
- the decision model by versioned id only: jev-1.14.0. Aliases are rejected at config load, and the response's resolved `model` must equal the pin per pair, else INCOMPLETE;
- the provider endpoint host;
- the Engram URL and tool parameters;
- the families file, by protected-base blob oid.

Identities hashed:
- ConfigurationIdentity: everything above except the secret.
- InputSetIdentity: head + base C# entries + hunk list + base commit.
- Per-pair decisionKey = sha256(ConfigurationIdentity || canonical state bytes || question-set sha).

Reproduction:
- Candidate generation is fully deterministic given the frozen trees.
- Engram is not, because its ranking and index generation drift. Its raw responses are therefore recorded in the report (stdout.txt), and every snippet is re-read from frozen bytes.
- Jev has no seed or temperature knob and identical-request determinism is undocumented, so the launcher keeps a host-owned, append-only decision cache outside the repo (e.g. %ProgramData%/delivery-forge/dry-decisions/<decisionKey>.json, storing the verbatim response plus its sha). A rerun on identical inputs and config replays the stored response instead of re-asking. "Same inputs give the same outcome" therefore holds by memoization, and the receipt says so in Limitations.
- An offline `df-dry-gate replay --report stdout.txt` recomputes the verdict from bound artifacts alone, so auditors never need the API.

What becomes INCOMPLETE:
- provider 401/413/415/422/429/529/5xx after one bounded retry (429/529 only, within the gate deadline), transport error, or timeout;
- 400 max_tokens_exceeded;
- resolved model != pin;
- any PAIR-UNSURE;
- candidate overflow beyond 40;
- Engram failure when mode=required;
- report counts not reconciling;
- schema violation, or exit code not 0/3;
- config/closure/reviewed-pin mismatch;
- frozen re-hash mismatch;
- runner-level timeout/cancel/non-quiescent/capture overflow.

Never PASS on any of these. A concrete DET-FAIL or PAIR-FAIL still yields FAIL even when other pairs are undecided.

### Engram usage

Engram is used for recall only, never for verdict text.
1) Type-4 candidate retrieval for new fragments of 8 or more statements that have no syntactic candidate:
   - search_symbols(query = method-name stems, repo, kind=method), using the 'weighted' rows above the floor;
   - search_code(query = signature + identifier bag, repo, kind=method, max_results=8).
   Every hit is resolved against the FROZEN base tree via a Roslyn outline at the same path and symbol name, and re-scored locally (identifier Jaccard 0.35 or higher, or invoked-member overlap 60% or higher). Unresolvable hits are dropped with reason code ENGRAM_UNRESOLVED.
2) get_artifact(repo, "clone_report", "all") is fetched once per run as a cross-check. It tells whether a base clone class is already known (feeds the ratchet's "already existed" test when the Roslyn base scan and the Engram report disagree; disagreement is recorded, not decisive).
3) Optional sibling-family hinting via find_related_code(repo, filePath of the existing side, maxHops=1). Strong co-change between the two files is put into state.siblingContext as a categorical 'co-evolves' flag. This helps Q4 recognise per-VCS sibling families, but it can never on its own clear a pair.

Engram indexes the default branch, which matches the base in the normal PR flow. When the index commit differs from the base (or cannot be determined), the report marks engramIndexStale and the results still only propose candidates. Mode is configurable: off | advisory (failures recorded, no effect on outcome) | required (failure gives INCOMPLETE). CI needs a service identity token for the Entra-protected MCP endpoint, passed by host-owned credential file like the model key.

### Load-bearing duplication handling

There are four layers, and none of them is a repo-controlled bypass.

(1) Deterministic exclusions with recorded reason codes, never silent:
- tests/fixtures (configurable globs, host-owned);
- generated code;
- trivial fragments (under 5 statements or 40 tokens);
- pure declaration boilerplate by Roslyn syntax-kind classification (property/field-only types, DI registration chains, attribute/using blocks, positional records, guard-only blocks);
- moves/renames (the existing side was deleted or moved in head).

(2) Ratchet: duplication that already existed in base (the same H2 clone class with the same member count) is not charged to the patch. Editing inside an old clone does not fail. Adding a new member does.

(3) Declared families: .delivery-forge/dry-families.json is read ONLY from the protected base via ProtectedGitPolicyResolver (`git show <base>:...`), never from the head. Example: {name:"vcs-bulk-indexers", globs:["src/**/Indexing/*BulkIndexer.cs"], rationale:"per-VCS API shapes intentionally diverge"}. Effects:
- pairs inside one family skip the DET-FAIL rules and go to the model with familyRationale in state;
- they need the stricter FAIL bar (Q1 >= 0.95 AND extractability >= 0.875, i.e. near-identical logic).
So a byte-identical helper copied into the 6th indexer still fails, while divergent per-VCS logic clears via Q4 >= 0.80. A patch cannot widen a family for itself: family edits take effect only after they merge to base, under normal review.

(4) In-patch rationale: a `// dry-intentional: <reason>` comment on the new fragment is NOT a suppression. It is passed to the model as patchClaimedRationale (comments are otherwise stripped), and the pair's best possible outcome is capped at PAIR-UNSURE, giving INCOMPLETE that is visible to the reviewer. It can turn a FAIL into a reviewed INCOMPLETE, but never into a PASS. The claim is listed in the receipt, and the human or policy disposition is recorded there too.

Hot-path markers (MethodImpl AggressiveInlining, SkipLocalsInit, Span-heavy unsafe blocks) are surfaced as categorical state for Q4 rather than auto-cleared.

### Cost and latency

Assumes a typical 300-line C# patch against a ~150k LOC repo (cost and latency estimated, not measured).

Pipeline stages:
- Base+head materialization via git cat-file --batch: about 2-5 s (roughly 6-10 MB of C#).
- Roslyn syntax-only parse + fingerprinting of both trees: about 4-8 s. Head fingerprints could be cached by blob oid in a host cache, bringing this to under 2 s warm.
- Hunk-scoped candidate join: under 1 s.
- Engram, about 3-10 new fragments x 2 queries at 4-way parallel: about 2-6 s.

Model band:
- Typically 5-25 pairs after pre-filters and DET decisions.
- Jev cost: each request carries state of about 3-6k tokens and 5 isolated questions. Assuming input is billed per question-evaluation, that is about 25k tokens x $0.042/M, about $0.001 per pair, so roughly $0.005-$0.03 per run (the 40-pair cap bounds it at about $0.05).
- Jev latency: 70-500 ms per request (vendor numbers; third party measured 0.84 s end-to-end), 4-way parallel, so about 2-6 s.

Totals:
- Wall clock: about 12-30 s cold, 8-15 s warm.
- Money: about 1-3 cents per run.
- Swapping in Haiku 5.5 through the provider abstraction would be about $0.0025 per pair (about 10x the cost) and multi-second latency.
- A self-hosted Perplexity Decider costs only GPU time.

### Precision and recall expectation

By clone type:
- Type-1 (exact): recall about 1.0 for fragments of 5 or more statements via H1. Precision is high, because DET-FAIL needs 15 or more lines / 120 or more tokens and not boilerplate. Smaller exact clones go to the model.
- Type-2 (renamed identifiers/literals): recall high via H2 alpha-renaming. That is better than jscpd 4.2.5 and PMD CPD for C#, neither of which normalizes identifiers. The model decides whether parameterisation is warranted. Over-flag risk: structurally identical but semantically unrelated small methods, e.g. two mappers with the same shape. Q1 and the rubric are meant to clear those.
- Type-3 (gapped): moderate recall via 7-gram winnowing at Jaccard 0.70 or above. Clones with large insertions, or under 6 statements, are missed.
- Type-4 (semantic): low recall. It depends on Engram retrieval quality, which the probe showed is weak, plus the local re-score. Published LLM type-4 recall is about 0.07-0.23 on BCB, and there is no Jev code-clone benchmark. Treat it as a bonus, not a guarantee.

Over-flag sources:
- high-similarity non-clones, which the literature shows models label as clones (mitigated by requiring dual-order Choice agreement and Q4 <= 0.20);
- C# boilerplate the classifier misses (e.g. controller action scaffolding).

Under-flag sources:
- clones split across multiple smaller methods;
- duplication of logic that exists only in another repo (single-repo scope);
- Engram stale index.

Before the gate is made required, the expectation must be validated on a labelled set built from engram-csharp:
- issue #238 filed groups as positives;
- the ~40 "Watched, not filed" pairs, labelled individually;
- per-VCS indexer and tree-sitter extractor siblings as load-bearing negatives;
- synthetic boilerplate negatives.

Target: PAIR-FAIL precision of 0.95 or better, with thresholds re-tuned on that set. Re-baseline on any model or question change.

### Failure modes

- Decision provider unavailable (401/429/529/timeout/egress blocked) on every run: the gate is stuck INCOMPLETE. Only deterministic DET-FAILs still bite; required-gate users see no PASS.
- Jev early-access withdrawal or retirement of jev-1.14.0 with no documented deprecation policy: pinned id stops resolving, so permanent INCOMPLETE until a re-pin plus re-calibration ships as a new reviewed config.
- Remote weights silently change behind a versioned id: undetectable by hash. Mitigated only by the memoization cache and periodic eval-set regression runs.
- Prompt injection through identifiers or string literals in the candidate code (comments are stripped, literals are not): could push Q4 toward deliberate_variation and clear a real duplicate. The bound transcript makes it auditable but not preventable.
- Position bias or low confidence makes many pairs PAIR-UNSURE: an INCOMPLETE flood that trains people to ignore the gate. Needs threshold tuning and unsure-rate telemetry.
- Over-flagging structurally identical but unrelated small methods (DTO mappers, visitor overrides): false FAILs that block merges. Requires boilerplate-classifier tuning per repo.
- Engram index stale or ranking poor: type-4 recall collapses silently in advisory mode. Recorded as engramIndexStale, but the outcome is unaffected.
- Data governance: sending proprietary HoT C# to a startup API with no documented data residency or SLA may be disallowed. The self-hosted Decider provider is then the only option.
- Contract extension (IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin, base materialization, hunks in RepositoryEvaluationScope) is not accepted: the plugin cannot see the base or the diff and is infeasible as designed.
- Decision cache poisoning or corruption on a same-UID host: a replayed wrong verdict. Mitigated by storing the response sha alongside the entry and re-verifying it, but the same-UID limitation (as for anti-slop) remains.
- External plugin PASS is downgraded to INCOMPLETE by GateRunner.cs:396-403. Users may misread an advisory INCOMPLETE as a gate failure unless the UI surfaces PluginRawOutcome.
- Large refactor patches (thousands of new fragments): candidate overflow beyond 40, so INCOMPLETE unless a FAIL is found. The budget must be tuned or the patch split.

### Implementation sketch

Contract changes in components/evidence/DeliveryForge.Evidence (one type per file, per HoT style):
- IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin.cs and FrozenTreeRequirements.cs (new).
- DiffHunk.cs (new record).
- RepositoryEvaluationScope: add ChangedHunks, MaterializedBaseRoot, BaseObjectIds.
- GitRepositoryIdentityReader: base-tree materialization under root/base, head under root/head, `git diff -U0 -M -z` hunk parsing to meta/hunks.json, input-set identity v2 (adds base entries, hunks, base commit), and re-hash of both trees.
- GateRunner: replace the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks (:115, :260, :273, :285, :296, :381, :499, :521, :565, :645, :861) with the interface or a capability flag. Anti-slop implements it with unchanged behaviour.

Plugin in Adapters/:
- DryDecisionEvaluationPlugin.cs
- DryDecisionRequestFactory.cs (canonical Invocation/args/env allow-list)
- DryGateSnapshot.cs
- DryGateReport.cs and DryGateReportParser.cs
- DryPairVerdictPolicy.cs and DryThresholds.cs (code-owned, with reviewed sha constants)
- DryFamiliesPolicy.cs (protected-base read)
- AdvisoryDryComposition.cs, a sibling of AdvisoryAntiSlopComposition, using a TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistry registration.

Launcher, a new project tools/DeliveryForge.DryGate (net10.0, PublishSingleFile, SelfContained, win-x64 and linux-x64, deterministic build, ContinuousIntegrationBuild=true):
- RoslynFragmentExtractor.cs, FragmentNormalizer.cs (H1/H2), WinnowingFingerprinter.cs, BoilerplateClassifier.cs, CloneCandidateJoiner.cs, HunkOverlapFilter.cs, BaseRatchet.cs;
- EngramCandidateRetriever.cs (MCP streamable HTTP client);
- IDryDecisionProvider.cs, DryDecisionState.cs, DryQuestionSet.cs (embedded questions.v1.json and rubric.v1.json), DryPairDecision.cs;
- TypeSafeSystemOneProvider.cs (POST https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone, client generated from https://api.typesafe.ai/openapi.json or hand-rolled HttpClient, since there is no .NET SDK);
- optional providers: MicrosoftDecisionFoundryProvider.cs, PerplexityDeciderLocalProvider.cs (self-hosted, weights sha in config identity), ClaudeStructuredOutputProvider.cs (claude-haiku-5-5 with a json_schema enum emulating Noul/Choice/Score buckets; no temperature param);
- DecisionCache.cs, ReportWriter.cs, ReplayCommand.cs.

Interface shape:
```csharp
public interface IDryDecisionProvider
{
    string ProviderId { get; }
    string PinnedModelId { get; }
    Task<DryPairDecision> DecideAsync(DryDecisionState state, DryQuestionSet questions, CancellationToken ct);
}
```
DryPairDecision carries ResolvedModel, the raw response bytes and sha, per-question probabilities, usage, and ErrorClass.

Pins:
- jev-1.14.0;
- Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp (exact version in Directory.Packages.props, e.g. 4.14.0);
- the launcher's own version 1.0.0 with format df-dry-report-v1.
No Node or jscpd closure is needed.

Tests:
- plugin tests with a stub launcher fixture (the StubEvaluationPlugin pattern);
- golden reports covering FAIL, PASS, each INCOMPLETE path, and model-id mismatch;
- launcher unit tests over synthetic C# clones of types 1, 2 and 3;
- an eval harness over the engram-csharp labelled set.

Effort: about 3 weeks for 1 developer.
- Contract generalization + base/hunk scope: 4-5 d.
- Launcher candidate miner: 5 d.
- Provider + cache + Engram client: 3 d.
- Plugin/normalize/binding + tests: 3-4 d.
- Eval set and threshold calibration: 2-3 d.

### Novelty claim

Beyond the owner's "use a decision model like Jev", this design adds the following.
(1) A split between the model's opinion and its authority. The model only returns raw typed probabilities; the trusted plugin recomputes every verdict with code-owned, hash-pinned thresholds. The external launcher cannot mint a PASS or FAIL label, which matches delivery-forge's "receipts are evidence not authority" rule.
(2) Dual-order Choice questions in the same request to neutralise Jev's documented first-option bias. They are free because questions are isolated and parallel, and disagreement is mapped to UNSURE.
(3) A base-ratchet over alpha-renamed clone classes. Only clones created or extended by the patch count, and moves via -M are excluded, so the per-change gate is complementary to the weekly repo-wide sweep instead of re-litigating its "Watched, not filed" backlog.
(4) Engram as a recall-only oracle. Every Engram hit is re-resolved and re-scored against frozen base bytes, so index staleness and weak ranking can only lose candidates, never fabricate evidence.
(5) Memoized-decision determinism. A decision cache keyed by (ConfigurationIdentity, canonical state, question-set sha), plus an offline replay command, gives "same inputs give the same outcome" for a non-deterministic remote model.
(6) Protected-base family declarations with a stricter (not waived) FAIL bar, plus patch-claimed rationale that can downgrade FAIL to INCOMPLETE but never reach PASS. Load-bearing duplication is handled without any suppression bypass.
(7) Deterministic DET-FAIL short-circuits, so the model is only paid for the ambiguous middle band.

### Open questions

- Will the owner accept the contract extension (IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin, base materialization, ChangedHunks in RepositoryEvaluationScope, and replacing the ~11 `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks in GateRunner)? Without it there is no base or diff, and the design is infeasible.
- Is sending HoT proprietary C# to TypeSafe (no documented data residency, SLA or deprecation policy, early access) allowed? If not, should the default provider be the self-hosted Perplexity Decider (pinned weights, which can enter the runtime closure) or Microsoft-Decision-1 on the HoT Azure estate?
- Is PAIR-UNSURE giving INCOMPLETE acceptable, or should policy allow an unsureBudget (e.g. up to 2 UNSURE pairs still PASS-advisory)? This trades rigour against gate fatigue.
- Is input billing per question-evaluation or per request (affects the cost estimate by up to 5x)? Are org rate limits in the TypeSafe console sufficient for 4-way parallel CI runs?
- Should Engram default to advisory or required? CI needs a non-interactive Entra service identity for the MCP endpoint, which does not exist today (the current setup is user PKCE).
- Is a host-owned decision cache acceptable as the reproducibility mechanism given the same-UID host limitation already documented for anti-slop?
- Should the gate ever be required, given that an externally registered plugin's PASS is downgraded to INCOMPLETE by GateRunner.cs:396-403? Or should it stay a FAIL-only advisory blocker until v2 authority exists?
- Should calibration thresholds (0.85/0.70/0.625/0.20) be per-repo config, set via the protected base, or fixed in code? Per-repo needs a labelled set per repo.

### Judge verdicts

**contract-fit (5.5/10).** Member mapping onto IDeterministicEvaluationPlugin is careful, and I spot-checked the contract: GateRunner does gate on the anti-slop plugin type at the cited lines, and PluginRawOutcome plus the advisory PASS downgrade exist. The plugin recomputes verdicts from raw probabilities with code-owned thresholds, nearly every uncertain path maps to INCOMPLETE, and families come from the protected base. Weaknesses: identities do not determine the outcome, because the model is remote and unseeded, and a mutable host decision cache and Engram drift sit outside every identity. Candidate literals and identifiers can steer the model (prompt injection), so a real duplicate could be cleared. The contract needs a large extension (base tree, hunks, about 11 anti-slop checks replaced), and the real logic hides in an opaque launcher. Exit code 3 handling is unverified against the runner.

Strongest weakness: The verdict is not a pure function of the bound identities: remote unseeded model, unbound mutable decision cache, and Engram index drift all feed the result, and candidate code can steer the model.

Suggested fix: Bind memoized decisions and response shas into the receipt and let Normalize verify only from them. Record the Engram index commit and treat drift as INCOMPLETE. Send only literal-masked normalized code to the model. Land the base-tree and hunk contract extension as a separate precursor PR, and confirm exit-code-3 handling in GateRunner.

**accuracy (6/10).** Strong on type-1 and type-2 recall: H1 and H2 alpha-rename hashing beats jscpd, which does not normalize identifiers. That covers byte-identical copies and manually renamed helpers when the fragment is 5 or more statements. Precision is well protected. Boilerplate (records, DI chains, generated code, tests) is excluded by deterministic reason codes. The base ratchet, declared families, dual-order Choice and a conservative AND of Q1/Q2a/Q2b/Q3/Q4 for FAIL all push toward few false FAILs. Real weaknesses on issue-238 shapes: (1) Byte-identical wrapper methods across 5 files are typically short (3-4 statements, under 40 tokens), so they fall to the "trivial" drop. That drop is exactly the cheapest, most common issue-238 shape, and the DET-FAIL rule needs 15+ lines and 120+ tokens. (2) The 85%-identical methods with mechanical differences are likely in sibling families (the 5 VCS bulk indexers), where the FAIL bar is raised to Q1 >= 0.95 and Q3 >= 0.875 and DET-FAIL is skipped. Those pairs will probably clear or land UNSURE, so the design may miss the main target. (3) Type-3 recall depends on 0.70 Jaccard over 7-grams, which is fragile at 85% similarity when the differences are scattered every few tokens. (4) Type-4 recall is admittedly low. Gameability is moderate. Reordering statements, splitting a method into sub-5-statement pieces, adding noise statements and varying member-access names all defeat H2 and winnowing. A model injection via identifiers or literals is acknowledged but unmitigated. Unsure-heavy bands risk an INCOMPLETE flood, and no labelled-set numbers exist yet, so the precision and recall are expectations rather than evidence.

Strongest weakness: The minimum size floor (5 statements / 40 tokens) and the stricter family bar together exclude or soften the two most common issue-238 shapes: short byte-identical wrapper methods, and 85%-identical sibling-family methods with mechanical differences. Both are the primary recall targets.

Suggested fix: Add a deterministic cumulative rule. Count identical or alpha-identical small fragments (3+ statements) across N or more distinct files as one clone class. Fail when the patch adds the Nth member, with N = 3 mirroring the sibling-files rule, even below the size floor. Inside declared families, keep DET-FAIL for H2 classes and use a similarity-gated model path (Q3 >= 0.75 with type-2/3 evidence) instead of the near-identical bar. Make the winnow Jaccard threshold and window size configurable and calibrate them on the issue-238 filed groups. Add an order-insensitive statement-multiset fingerprint to resist reorder and split gaming, and sanitize string literals and identifiers in model state.

**cost-ops (5/10).** Cost/latency per run is good: deterministic mining keeps model calls to about 5-25 pairs, roughly 1-3 cents and 12-30 s cold, and DET-FAIL short-circuits honour the owner's cost order. Operability is weaker, in four ways. (1) Effort is about 3 weeks, and it is not an advisory plugin so much as a contract refactor: IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin, base-tree and hunk materialization, replacing about 11 `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks in GateRunner, and input-set identity v2. That is a large blast radius on the exemplar plugin. (2) It also builds a Roslyn miner, an MCP client against an Entra-protected endpoint, a provider layer, a decision cache and a replay command. (3) External dependencies stack up: Jev is early access with no SLA or deprecation policy, and a retired jev-1.14.0 means permanent INCOMPLETE. The gate needs an API key, network egress and CI Entra service identity for Engram that does not exist today, and stale Engram indexes plus data-governance questions add more. (4) Pinning and maintenance are heavy: the launcher is a self-contained single-file build per OS, which means bumping the reviewed sha constants on every Roslyn or provider change, re-calibrating thresholds on any model or question change, and running an eval harness. Further operability risks: the host-owned decision cache is stateful and per-machine, which hurts CI runner reproducibility and cold-start. PAIR-UNSURE yielding INCOMPLETE risks a flood that trains people to ignore the gate. Because an external plugin's PASS is downgraded to INCOMPLETE, the gate is effectively FAIL-only. The cost estimate rests on unmeasured assumptions about Jev billing, which could be 5x off. The 40-pair budget overflow turns large refactor patches INCOMPLETE.

Strongest weakness: Implementation and maintenance burden is out of proportion to an advisory FAIL-only gate. It needs about 3 weeks, a deep GateRunner and scope contract refactor, a bespoke Roslyn miner, an MCP client and a stateful decision cache, and it depends on an early-access vendor model with no SLA that can strand the gate in permanent INCOMPLETE.

Suggested fix: Ship a deterministic-only v1 first. That means the Roslyn H1/H2 miner with ratchet and declared families, a smaller contract extension (base tree plus hunks only), and DET-FAIL as the only FAIL source. Treat the mid-band as INCOMPLETE or a reviewer report. Add the decision model behind IDryDecisionProvider as an optional second phase, once an eval set shows the deterministic band is insufficient. Drop the host decision cache by keying the receipt on the stored response only. Make Engram advisory-only until a CI service identity exists. Measure Jev billing and latency on a real 300-line patch before committing to the cost story.

### Adversarial refutation

**Refuted: true (major).** Claim attacked: That the miner + typed-decision pipeline yields FAIL on the issue-238 clone shapes (sibling-family files with cosmetic or mechanical differences, and byte-identical wrappers) while clearing load-bearing duplication.

Evidence:

Four problems, ordered by how much they matter. I checked the PR #52 contract files. I did not run the design's thresholds against real engram-csharp sources.

1. The family bar is internally contradictory with the rubric. The design's own Q3 rubric says level 3 means "same logic differing only in identifiers/literals/one branch". That is exactly the issue-238 "cosmetic-only differences" shape. Level 3 normalizes to 0.75. A family-internal FAIL needs Q1>=0.95 AND Q3>=0.875, which is level 3.5 or above of 0-4, so only near-identical code qualifies. PAIR-CLEAR also fires when Q4>=0.80 AND Q3<0.875, and a per-VCS family is the case Q4 is designed to flag. So a 6th indexer or extractor that copies a sibling with cosmetic edits is expected to score Q3 about 0.75 and Q4 high, which gives PAIR-CLEAR. The Step 4 DET-FAIL rule for 3 or more sibling files is explicitly skipped for declared families. In effect the declared-family exemption disables, for the 5 VCS bulk indexers and 6 tree-sitter extractors, the rule that issue #238 was written around. Only byte-identical copies still fail.

2. Type-3 recall is far weaker than "moderate". Step 2(c) needs Jaccard >= 0.70 over 7-gram winnow sets.
   - If a fraction d of tokens is scattered-different, a 7-gram survives with p=(1-d)^7, and Jaccard = p/(2-p).
   - Jaccard >= 0.70 needs p >= 0.82, which means d <= about 2.8%.
   - At d=5%, Jaccard is about 0.54. At d=10% it is about 0.31.
   - H2 keeps member-access names of non-local symbols, so sibling methods that differ in type names, field names and DTO members (GitHub vs Azure DevOps shapes) diverge on well over 3% of tokens. Syntax-only Roslyn also has no semantic model, so the "local vs field vs type" split in alpha-renaming is a heuristic. A copied method that uses `_http` in one file and `_client` in another breaks H2 equality.
   - Those pairs never become candidates, so no model is consulted and the gate PASSes. This is a pre-model recall hole that the thresholds the design says are to be tuned later cannot fix without a different matcher.

3. The size floor drops the other primary shape. Pairs under 5 normalized statements or 40 tokens are dropped, and pairs with 6 or more statements and 50 or more tokens are required for winnowing. Short byte-identical wrapper methods are issue-238's second shape. They never reach DET-FAIL, because that needs 15 or more lines and 120 or more tokens, and they cannot be matched at all if they fall below the floor.

4. "Fails until DRY" is only advisory under the real contract. GateRunner.cs:429-434 forces ProductionCapable=false for any externally registered plugin, because HasTrustedLegacyV1Authority returns false. docs/quality-gates.md:40-44 states that a receipt FAIL "remains advisory evidence rather than production authority", and that AuthoritativeEvaluationHost throws NotSupportedException. The FAIL outcome does pass through, but nothing in the repository consumes it as blocking.

Two points where the design's contract claims are accurate or generous. The protected base commit is already held by GitRepositoryIdentityReader, so base materialization is a smaller change than the design implies. The runner's expected-versus-requested input equality check does work as the design says.

Verdict: on the issue-238 shapes the mechanism is expected to PASS or CLEAR the main targets, not FAIL them, and the pipeline reaches that outcome through its own thresholds.

Salvage:

Keep the architecture: a deterministic miner, a typed per-pair decision with trusted-side verdict recomputation, and the FAIL-only advisory framing. Change four things.
(a) Make family membership lower the DET-FAIL bar for H2-exact clones instead of waiving it, for example still FAIL when the patch adds a new member to an H2 class that already has 3 or more sibling files.
(b) Align the family FAIL threshold with the rubric. Use Q3>=0.75 (level 3, "differs only in identifiers/literals/one branch") plus Q1>=0.90, and drop the "Q4>=0.80 AND Q3<0.875 clears" rule for pairs whose H2 or LCS similarity is already very high.
(c) Replace the 7-gram Jaccard with a gap-tolerant matcher: LCS or Smith-Waterman alignment over normalized statement or token sequences, reporting coverage (aligned tokens over the shorter side), or a statement-level shingle with 3-gram and containment thresholds. Tune the threshold on the labelled engram-csharp set before going further.
(d) Lower the size floor for byte-identical (H1) wrappers to 3 statements, and route those through a deterministic rule that requires the existing side to be non-test and non-boilerplate, instead of dropping them.
State plainly that the FAIL is advisory-only until the authority path exists.

**Refuted: true (major).** Claim attacked: That load-bearing duplication is separated from real duplication via four layers, none of which is a repo-controlled bypass, and that a coding agent under pressure cannot trivially evade the gate or satisfy it with a fake justification.

Evidence:

I checked GateRunner.cs around lines 385-410. A plugin PASS is downgraded to INCOMPLETE unless the policy authority, required-gate binding and legacy-v1 authority all hold. The design itself concedes this for an externally registered plugin. So a clean, DRY patch and a patch that merely avoids FAIL both end as INCOMPLETE, with PluginRawOutcome=PASS as the only difference. FAIL is therefore the gate's only discriminating outcome. Every attack below only needs to turn a FAIL into a non-FAIL.

1. The "dry-intentional" comment is a bypass, contrary to the design's own claim.
- The design says the comment caps the pair at PAIR-UNSURE, which gives INCOMPLETE and "can turn a FAIL into a reviewed INCOMPLETE".
- But INCOMPLETE is exactly what every clean patch gets, and also what any provider outage or overflow gives.
- The comment lives in the candidate's own head tree. One line of text therefore turns FAIL into the same result as a DRY patch.
- Nothing distinguishes "reviewed INCOMPLETE" from "clean INCOMPLETE" in the gate outcome. Visibility in the receipt is not a gate.

2. Family globs are a path-based bypass.
- The families file is read from the protected base, but the new file's path is chosen by the candidate.
- Dropping a duplicate into a file named like `src/**/Indexing/*BulkIndexer.cs` skips DET-FAIL. It also moves the FAIL bar to Q1>=0.95 and Q3>=0.875, which is the soft bar.
- The 85%-similar sibling-family case, which the judges already named as the primary recall target, then passes by construction.
- Any broad glob declared once for a legitimate reason becomes a permanent exemption zone.

3. Evasion by rename, reorder, split or wrap is cheap, and the design admits some of it.
- The size floors are 5 statements / 40 tokens, and winnow candidates need 6 or more statements per side. Chopping a duplicated method into helpers of 4 statements or fewer evades all of them. The design lists "clones split across multiple smaller methods" as an under-flag source.
- Winnowing uses k=7, w=4, Jaccard>=0.70. Inserting a dead local or no-op statement every few tokens drops Jaccard below 0.70. Positional alpha-renaming makes extra temps shift placeholders.
- H2 keeps member-access names of non-local symbols. A one-line forwarding wrapper (`Alias()` calling `Real()`) changes those names and breaks H2.
- foreach to for, LINQ to loop, and reordering independent statements shift the token stream. Statement-level moves are not normalized.
- Engram type-4 recall is described as low and runs only for fragments with 8 or more statements and no syntactic candidate.

4. Hot-path and deliberate-variation signals are agent-writable.
- hotPathMarkers (MethodImpl(AggressiveInlining), SkipLocalsInit) feed Q4.
- The CLEAR rule is Q4>=0.80 AND Q3<0.875. A level-3 same-logic clone scores 0.75, so it clears if the agent adds one attribute to the copy.
- Comments are stripped, but identifiers and string literals are not. A string constant or identifier such as `ProviderSpecificDivergentPath` carries a natural-language steer into the Q4 prompt.
- patchClaimedRationale is a further explicit free-text channel. The design admits injection is "auditable but not preventable".

5. The gate is a retry oracle against a non-deterministic model.
- The finding returns file:line for both sides plus a suggested disposition. That is a gradient for the agent.
- FAIL needs a conjunction: Q1>=0.85, Q2a==Q2b in the extract set, each at 0.70 or more, Q3>=0.625, Q4<=0.20. Any single wobble gives UNSURE, which is not FAIL.
- Jev has no seed. The memoization cache keys on exact state, so a cosmetic one-token edit creates a new key and a fresh sample.
- Re-rolling until one answer wobbles is cheap at 1-3 cents per run. The cache stabilizes identical replays only, not agent retries.

6. False-positive and deadlock path.
- H2 turns literals into placeholders. Per-table Stores differing only in SQL strings and table names are H2-identical, which is a stated load-bearing shape in engram-csharp.
- DET-FAIL rule 2 hard-fails the third-plus member of an H2 class spanning 3 or more sibling files outside a declared family. The model is never consulted and the in-patch rationale does not apply.
- Declaring the family needs a prior merged PR, since it is read from base only. A legitimate new sibling therefore has no in-PR escape.
- The unresolved escape is a human override of a FAIL. Pressure for that override pushes toward over-broad family globs, which feeds attack 2.

7. Ratchet and baseline drift.
- Whatever gets merged under INCOMPLETE, through the attacks above or human acceptance, becomes the base clone class and is free forever after.
- The "moved/deleted existing side" drop reason adds another exemption. That one depends on how head-side pairs are retained, so I flag it as unverified rather than proven.

Net: the design's strongest stated property, "no suppression bypass", is false. A fake justification, a path choice or a one-line attribute can each convert FAIL into the same outcome a clean patch gets. And the syntactic miner is evadable by the splitting and padding tricks that agents optimise for.

Salvage:

Smallest surviving changes:
1. Make `dry-intentional` a non-escape. It must never lower FAIL to INCOMPLETE. A claimed rationale can only be recorded, and the FAIL stands until a family or allowlist entry lands in the protected base, or a recorded human override is applied outside the patch.
2. Remove patchClaimedRationale and hotPathMarkers from the model state, or compute them from the protected base. Strip or normalize string literals and identifiers inside the compared fragments. Never let the head tree's own text lower the FAIL bar.
3. Bind family exemptions to base-existing paths. A new file matching a family glob must have its first pair judged at the normal bar, and family-internal pairs keep the DET-FAIL byte-identical rule.
4. Close the cheap evasions deterministically. Fold fragments of 4 statements or fewer into their callers before fingerprinting, treat dead stores and forwarding wrappers as no-ops in normalization, and lower the winnow window.
5. Cap the retry oracle. Return only an aggregate FAIL reason to the agent, key the decision cache by the pair's normalized H2 pair rather than the exact state, and rate-limit re-runs per head lineage.
6. Add a human-override path for a DET-FAIL, and state in docs that a clean PASS cannot be distinguished from a bypassed FAIL while PASS is downgraded to INCOMPLETE.
Fallback: ship the deterministic-only core (the DET-FAIL rules, a ratchet, and the exclusions) as the actual gate, and keep the decision model as non-authoritative annotation.


## A.engram-semantic: Engram-Recall / Base-Verified Clone Gate ("delivery-forge.dry-recall")

Panel mean 5.3 (contract-fit 6, accuracy 5, cost-ops 5). Tier: deterministic.

**One line.** Engram only nominates candidate duplicates (it is treated as an untrusted, non-reproducible recall oracle). A pinned local Roslyn tool re-reads every candidate's bytes from the protected base commit, checks them against Engram's per-symbol contentHash, and scores new-vs-existing pairs with deterministic metrics (Greedy String Tiling plus a subtree-hash bag). The verdict is a pure function of (base, head, frozen candidate manifest, config).

### Mechanism

Two phases. Engram is network-bound and non-reproducible, so it stays out of the gate's execution. Only its frozen output goes in.

PHASE A: harvest (host code, before GateRunner; the only step that talks to Engram)
A1. `git diff -U0 -z --find-renames=90% B H -- '*.cs'` gives the changed .cs paths and the added line ranges. Roslyn (Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp, exact pin) parses each head blob, read by `git cat-file --batch` at H. It enumerates "units": methods, constructors, local functions, accessors and lambdas with block bodies of 3 or more statements. A unit is NEW/CHANGED when at least 5 of its lines, or at least 30% of its lines, fall in added hunks. Long methods (more than 40 statements) also yield sliding 8-statement windows, so a block copied into a big method is still seen.
A2. Each unit is normalized. Locals, parameters and fields are alpha-renamed per unit (v1, v2, ...). String and number literals become STR/NUM. Keywords, operators, type names and invoked member names are kept, because external API calls carry the meaning. Trivia and comments are dropped. A unit counts as non-trivial only with at least 70 normalized tokens AND at least 10 physical lines. These match the weekly jscpd sweep's --min-tokens 70 / --min-lines 10, so gate and sweep agree.
A3. Three deterministic queries per non-trivial unit (query builder is code-owned and versioned, e.g. qb-1):
- Q1 `search_code(query, repo=R, kind="method", language="csharp", max_results=20)`. The query is the camelCase-split method name, the top 12 identifiers by IDF, and the invoked member names. The IDF table is computed locally from the base tree and cached by base tree id.
- Q2 `search_symbols(query=<method name stem>, repo=R, kind="method", max_results=10)`. This targets sibling-family copies such as *BulkIndexer.IndexAsync.
- Q3 `search_text(query=<rarest literal or member chain of 12+ chars>, repo=R, max_results=20)`, for up to 2 literals. A hit maps back to its enclosing unit locally.
- Optional cross-repo Q4: `search_group(query, repos=<configured shared libs, e.g. HouseOfTravelLibrary, SerilogExtensions>)`.
Candidates are the union, deduped by (repo, file, line, endLine) and ordered by (query priority, engram score desc, symbolId ordinal). The cap is K=25 per unit.
A4. Every request is recorded in canonical JSON with the raw response bytes, the MCP initialize serverInfo {name, version}, and the sha256 of the tools/list schemas used. These go into a content-addressed manifest, `dry-candidates.<sha256>.json`, bound to B, H, the diff identity (sha256 of `git diff --raw -z B H`) and the query-builder version. Any transport error, 401, timeout or truncated flag is written into the manifest as an error entry; it is never dropped.

PHASE B: gate executable (`dryscan`, run by GateRunner; offline, no secrets)
B1. Re-derive the same units from B/H with `git cat-file --batch` against the exact SHAs. Object reads by commit SHA are content-addressed, so they are frozen by construction. Then confirm that the manifest's unit list equals the derived list.
B2. Verify each candidate at the protected base:
- Read `B:<file>`, take lines [line..endLine] split on '\n', join with '\n', and sha256 the result. That must equal Engram's `contentHash`, which engram-csharp computes exactly this way (SymbolHelpers.ComputeContentHash over string.Join("\n", lines) in AstExtractor.cs:126).
- On a mismatch, search B's file for a Roslyn unit whose line-slice hash equals contentHash; a match means the unit moved and is relocated.
- Otherwise the candidate is STALE and dropped.
- Also exclude SELF (the candidate overlaps lines this patch modifies in the same file, i.e. the old version of the unit) and MOVED (the patch deletes the candidate's lines, so it is a move, not a copy).
B3. Gap and type-1/2 backstop. A local normalized-hash index of every base unit (Roslyn over B, cached by tree id, about 5-10 s for 150k LOC) catches exact and renamed copies that Engram missed or has not indexed yet. Intra-patch: all new units are also compared pairwise.
B4. Pair scores, all deterministic:
- S_gst = Greedy String Tiling coverage of the new unit's normalized tokens by the candidate (min tile 12 tokens), asymmetric containment, computed in both directions with the max kept.
- S_ast = Dice over the bag of Merkle hashes of normalized syntax subtrees with at least 4 nodes.
- S_api = Jaccard of the invoked-external-member sets (only when both sides have at least 5 distinct calls).
B5. Pair bands:
- CONFIRMED: normalized-hash equality, OR (S_gst >= 0.80 AND S_ast >= 0.80), OR the third-copy rule from #238 (S_gst >= 0.70 against at least 2 distinct existing files, i.e. the patch creates the 3rd sibling).
- SUSPECT: S_gst >= 0.60 OR S_ast >= 0.70 OR S_api >= 0.80 (a type-4 hint).
- CLEAR: everything else.
B6. Apply the load-bearing filters (see that field). Emit `dryscan-json-v1` on stdout with full counts and findings: new file:line, existing file:line@B, scores, band, query provenance. Exit 0 when clean, 1 when there are findings, anything else on error.

Gate verdict (plugin Normalize):
- FAIL: any unwaived CONFIRMED.
- INCOMPLETE: any unwaived SUSPECT, any manifest error, a count-reconciliation failure, or a stale ratio above 20%.
- PASS (raw): otherwise.
- NOT_APPLICABLE: no non-trivial changed units (only when policy allows it).

Engram scores are recorded but never compared to a threshold.

### Plugin contract fit

New class `DryRecallEvaluationPlugin : IDeterministicEvaluationPlugin`:
- Id "delivery-forge.dry-recall"; Version "1.0.0+qb-1"; ExpectedTool "dryscan"; CompatibilityAdapter = EvidenceAdapterKind.External (as anti-slop does), so it must be explicitly selected through EvaluationPluginSelection.
- Registered by trusted host code via `new TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistry([new TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistration("dry", plugin, ExpectedToolVersion: "1.0.0", ExpectedFormatVersion: "dryscan-json-v1", ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity: <sha>)])`. It is never in TrustedGatePluginRegistry.BuiltIns, so it has no legacy-v1 authority.

Members:
- DetectInputs(candidateInputs, ignoredInputs): ordinal-sorted candidate paths ending ".cs" (case-insensitive), not ignored, excluding `*.g.cs`, `*.Designer.cs` and `**/Migrations/**`. SelectApplicable enforces the subset rule.
- ExpectedTool / capability: CapabilityProbe Tool="dryscan", Source=TrustedExplicit, VersionArguments ["--version"], output {"schemaVersion":"dryscan-json-v1","toolVersion":"1.0.0"}, RequiredVersionPrefix "1.0.0", DocumentedFormatVersion "dryscan-json-v1". dryscan is published self-contained single-file, so Capability.ExecutableIdentity (sha256 of the file) covers Roslyn as well; a framework-dependent `dotnet dryscan.dll` would hash only the dotnet host.
- ValidateBeforeExecution(request): require the canonical invocation and return a non-null Incomplete otherwise (giving a no-execution receipt).
  - FileName == Capability.ExecutablePath.
  - Arguments exactly [scan, --base, B, --head, H, --repo, <root>, --manifest, <abs path>, --git, <abs git>, --format, dryscan-json-v1].
  - B/H equal request.ExpectedRepository.BaseCommit/HeadCommit.
  - Manifest file name hash equals its bytes' sha256.
  - InheritEnvironment=false with an env allow-list {SystemRoot, WINDIR, TMP, TEMP}: no Engram token, the gate is offline.
- CaptureBeforeAsync(request, ct) returns EvaluationPluginSnapshot(State = DrySnapshot{ManifestSha256, Manifest (bounded 16 MiB), DiffIdentity, AllowFileBlobOid (git rev-parse B:.delivery-forge/dry-allow or "absent"), GitExecutableSha256, ConfigurationIdentity, RuntimeClosureIdentity}). It throws InvalidOperationException (which becomes INCOMPLETE) when manifest.base/head/diffIdentity do not match the request.
- GetConfigurationIdentity(request, snapshot) / GetRuntimeClosureIdentity(request, snapshot) / GetExecutableIdentity(request, snapshot): return the snapshot values, or the literal "unavailable" when snapshot is null (the anti-slop pattern at AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs:204-216).
- Normalize(request, CommandResult, snapshot): parse stdout (keep it under 1 MiB; detail lives in artifacts). Then:
  - Reconcile, else INCOMPLETE: report.base/head/diffIdentity/manifestSha == snapshot; unitsAnalyzed == manifest units; harvested == verified + relocated + stale + self + moved + crossRepoUnverified; no manifest error entries; stale/harvested <= 0.20; exit code in {0,1}, consistent with the findings.
  - FAIL: `new NormalizedEvidence(GateOutcome.Fail, reason, false, true, limitations)`.
  - PASS: `NormalizedEvidence.Pass(...)`.
  - NOT_APPLICABLE: constructor with a rationale.
- ValidateAfterExecution: re-hash the manifest file; a mismatch is INCOMPLETE.
- AppendBoundArtifacts(request, snapshot, gateDirectory, artifactHashes):
  - Writes `dry-candidates.json` (a manifest copy), `dry-report.json` (full findings) and `dry-binding.json`. The runner's sidecar is hard-keyed to 'dmmulroy.anti-slop' at GateRunner.cs:499, so this plugin needs its own.
  - All files use FileMode.CreateNew, with name -> "sha256:<hex>" recorded.
  - Every non-IO exception must be wrapped as IOException, because the runner catches only IO/Unauthorized there.

Configuration identity = sha256 over: dryscan bytes, git bytes, thresholds constants (code-owned, versioned with Version), query-builder version, AllowFileBlobOid@B, cross-repo config, env allow-list key/values.

Runtime closure identity = dryscan bytes + git bytes (no secret ever enters an identity).

CONTRACT GAPS, stated honestly:
(1) RepositoryEvaluationScope has no base tree or diff, and ValidateAutomaticScopeAsync is hard-gated to AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin (GateRunner.cs:861). v1 therefore needs NO runner change: dryscan reads immutable objects by SHA itself, and the runner's before/after clean-identity check still catches tree mutation. Input-set identity then falls back to the legacy sha256 of Plugin.Inputs. Phase 2 should replace the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks (:115, :260, :273, :285, :296, :381, :499, :785, :861) with a capability interface such as `IFrozenDiffScopePlugin`, plus a `RepositoryDiffEvaluationScope` (ChangedPaths, AddedLineRanges, BaseObjectIds, HeadObjectIds, DiffIdentity), so the runner mints the input-set identity over base+head+diff.
(2) Under current code, a raw PASS from any non-built-in plugin is downgraded to final INCOMPLETE (advisory) at GateRunner.cs:396-403, while FAIL passes through. That still satisfies "fails until DRY" in advisory mode, but this gate cannot be authoritative until v2 authority exists.

### Determinism and receipts

PINNED:
- The dryscan single-file binary hash, which includes Roslyn at an exact package version.
- The git binary hash.
- Threshold constants and query-builder version, folded into plugin Version.
- The protected allow-file blob oid at B.
- Engram serverInfo version and the sha256 of the tools/list schemas used, recorded in the manifest and bound artifact.

HASHED IDENTITIES:
- ConfigurationIdentity: the config set above.
- RuntimeClosureIdentity: dryscan + git bytes.
- InputSetIdentity, v1: the legacy hash of the selected .cs paths, plus `dry-binding.json`, which carries sha256(B, H, DiffIdentity, ManifestSha256).
- InputSetIdentity, phase 2: runner-minted over the same tuple.

HOW RERUNS REPRODUCE: scoring never uses Engram bytes or Engram scores. Every candidate's bytes come from `B:<file>` and are verified against Engram's contentHash. The verdict is therefore a pure function of (B, H, manifest, config). Re-running the gate with the same manifest gives a byte-identical report. Re-harvesting after a reindex can change only the candidate SET (recall), never the score of a candidate found both times. That shows up as a new ManifestSha256 and so a distinguishable receipt.

Index state binding: MCP exposes no indexed-commit. engram-csharp has LatestIndexedCommit internally (HealthStore.cs:62) but no MCP tool returns it, and per-symbol `indexedAtVersion` came back null in a probe. So index state is bound indirectly:
- The exact request/response transcript is kept, which records what the index answered.
- Per-candidate contentHash verification against B proves each used candidate was current at the protected base.
- The stale ratio is recorded.
If Engram later exposes the indexed commit, the harvester also records it and requires `git merge-base --is-ancestor <indexed> B`. Files in diff(indexed..B) are then rescanned locally as gap-fill.

BECOMES INCOMPLETE:
- Any Engram error, timeout, 401/429 or truncated response in the manifest.
- A manifest whose B/H/diff do not match the request.
- A manifest whose bytes changed after capture.
- Stale candidates above 20%.
- Unit-count mismatch.
- Any unwaived SUSPECT pair.
- dryscan timeout or bound exceeded, or an exit code outside {0,1}.
- A parse failure of a changed .cs file (Roslyn diagnostics that make the unit tree unreliable).
- Base or head blobs above the size caps (reuse the 64 MiB per-object cap).
- Cross-repo enforcement enabled while the mirror commit is unpinned.

### Engram usage

Roles:
- Engram is a RECALL ORACLE only.
- Tools used: `search_code(query, repo, kind="method", language="csharp", max_results=20)` for structural+semantic nomination, `search_symbols(name stem, repo, kind="method", max_results=10)` for sibling families, and `search_text(rare literal/member chain, repo, max_results=20)` for copy-paste fingerprints.
- Optional `search_group` / corpus-wide `search_code` for cross-repo.
- `get_file_outline` is a fallback to map search_text hits to symbols when local Roslyn mapping fails.
- `get_symbol` is deliberately NOT relied on for bytes. A probe (HOTBrain-v2, IBlobStore) returned `"source": ""` even though the metadata (line, endLine, byteOffset, byteLength, contentHash) was present. Bytes come from git at B instead.

What makes this work: Engram symbol rows carry `contentHash` = sha256(UTF-8 of lines[line-1..endLine-1] joined by '\n'), per engram-csharp SymbolHelpers.ComputeContentHash + AstExtractor.cs:126. That gives a cheap, exact local currency check for every candidate.

Default-branch-only indexing:
(a) The patch head is never queried as indexed content; intra-patch duplicates are found locally.
(b) Base B may be newer or older than the index. Candidates are verified or relocated at B by contentHash, and stale ones are dropped and counted.
(c) Code that reached B after the last index is invisible to Engram. The local normalized-hash backstop over all base units still catches type-1/2 copies of it. Type-3 copies of that window are missed until reindex; this is recorded as a limitation (index lag unknown via MCP).

Cross-repo:
- Configured repo list (e.g. HouseOfTravelLibrary, SerilogExtensions, ContentOrchestration.Contracts).
- Hits are verified against a host-provided local mirror fetched at a pinned commit/tag recorded in the config identity.
- With a pinned mirror, CONFIRMED cross-repo pairs FAIL with the message "use package X helper Y".
- Without one, hits are reported as advisory limitations and never FAIL. This is a declared scope boundary, not an uncertain claim.

Auth:
- The harvester needs a headless Entra credential. The current setup is interactive PKCE with client ce5d95e9-..., so CI needs a confidential client or service principal (open question).
- The token lives only in the harvester process and is never in the gate env, receipts or identities.

Corpus coverage finding: engram-csharp is NOT in the HoT Engram instance (`search_symbols(repo="engram-csharp")` returned "Repository not found", and it is absent from list_repos). The target repo must be registered in whichever Engram instance the gate points at. Neither `clone_report` nor any other artifact exists for it.

### Load-bearing duplication handling

None of these is a bypass the candidate patch can apply to itself.
1. Size floor: units under 70 normalized tokens or 10 lines are never units. Pure delegation bodies (2 statements or fewer), constructor-assign-only bodies, auto-property and record DTO members are skipped as trivial.
2. Built-in path classes:
   - Generated code (`*.g.cs`, `*.Designer.cs`, `Migrations/**`) is excluded.
   - Test code (`tests/**`, `*Tests.cs`) is by default excluded as a TARGET of FAIL. It still serves as a candidate source, so a production copy of a test helper is not flagged. All of this is configurable in the code-owned config, not repo policy.
3. Protected-base allow file `.delivery-forge/dry-allow` (same authority model as `.delivery-forge/anti-slop-ignore`): only the version at B is read, and patch-head edits are ignored and reported as a limitation. Entries are families: {id, globs[], reason, owner, optional expires}. Pairs whose two sides fall in the same family are CLEAR-by-policy and listed in the receipt with the entry's blob oid. Examples:
   - `vcs-bulk-indexers`: src/**/Vcs/*BulkIndexer.cs
   - `ts-http-extractors`: src/**/Http/*TreeSitter*Extractor.cs
   - `per-table-stores`: src/**/Database/*Store.cs
   - `hot-path`: one entry per perf-critical file.
   Adding a deliberately duplicated new file is a two-step change: merge the allow entry first (human-reviewed, visible in base history), then the code. The gate stays FAIL until then; that friction is intentional.
4. Base-side marker: a candidate unit at B whose leading trivia contains `// dry: template <family-id>` (with a matching allow family) is a declared template, so copies of it are CLEAR. The marker counts only on the BASE side. A marker added by the patch on the new unit has no authority.
5. Moves (patch deletes the candidate) and self-edits (candidate overlaps lines the patch modifies) are excluded mechanically.
6. SUSPECT pairs give INCOMPLETE, never FAIL. The author resolves this by refactoring or by a base allow entry; a gate never silently passes a borderline pair.
7. Watched-but-sub-threshold existing pairs (#238's ~40 pairs of 11-28 lines) are pre-existing and both sides are at B, so they never trigger. Only pairs with at least one NEW side are evaluated, which makes this a ratchet, not a repo-wide re-litigation.

### Cost and latency

Rough estimate for a typical 300-line C# patch, unmeasured:
- Units: about 8-15 non-trivial NEW/CHANGED units.
- Harvest: 3-5 Engram calls per unit, about 40-70 calls. At concurrency 4 and roughly 0.2-0.8 s per call, about 5-15 s.
- Gate:
  - git diff + cat-file: under 1 s.
  - Roslyn parse of changed files plus about 50-150 candidate files: 1-3 s.
  - Base-tree normalized-hash backstop: 5-10 s cold over about 150k LOC, under 1 s warm (cache keyed by base tree id, stored host-side outside the repo).
  - Scoring: 15 units x 25 candidates x GST at about 300 tokens is O(n^2) per pair, under 0.5 s in total.
- End to end: about 10-30 s cold, 6-15 s warm.
- Dollar cost: effectively $0 marginal (self-hosted Engram; query embeddings server-side at fractions of a cent per run). No LLM tokens.

### Precision and recall expectation

These are expectations to calibrate against engram-csharp's labeled pairs (DryUpScan filed issues = positives; Watched-not-filed and known boilerplate = negatives).

Type-1 (exact): precision ~0.95+ after trivial and family filters. Recall about 1.0 for units at or above the floor, independent of Engram, through the local normalized-hash backstop and the intra-patch comparison.

Type-2 (renamed identifiers/literals): same as type-1. Alpha-renaming plus literal classing collapses them. The risk is over-flagging structurally identical but semantically distinct code: mapping methods, Refit/EF config, switch tables. Mitigation is the family allow entries plus kept API member names; a kept API name differing (e.g. GitHubClient vs AzureDevOpsClient calls) lowers S_gst below 0.80.

Type-3 (gapped): caught when Engram nominates the candidate and GST/AST meet 0.80/0.80, with containment covering "copied block plus extra lines". Recall is bounded by Engram search quality. A probe of search_code on Nexus returned weak, off-target top hits (scores 0.31-0.40). Expect perhaps 50-75% type-3 recall, mostly from Q2 (name-stem) and Q3 (rare-literal) rather than semantic search, and only for code indexed at or before B. Precision is high at 0.80/0.80; the 0.60-0.80 band goes to INCOMPLETE rather than FAIL.

Type-4 (semantic, different structure): deterministic metrics cannot confirm it. At best the S_api >= 0.80 hint marks a SUSPECT, which gives INCOMPLETE. Most type-4 is missed, honestly.

Over-flag risks:
- Boilerplate controllers or DI registration not covered by families.
- Tests, if the test exclusion is turned off.
- A SUSPECT band set too wide, causing INCOMPLETE fatigue.

Under-flag risks:
- Copies from non-symbol code (top-level statements, scripts).
- Code merged after the last reindex (type-3 only).
- Copies from unregistered repos.

### Failure modes

- Target repo not indexed in the gate's Engram instance (true today for engram-csharp in the HoT instance). Every Q1-Q3 call errors, so the gate is INCOMPLETE; the type-1/2 backstop alone could be offered as a degraded mode, but only if explicitly configured and recorded.
- Engram index lags B by an unknown amount (no MCP field exposes LatestIndexedCommit). Type-3 copies of recently merged code are missed silently, mitigated only by recording index lag as a limitation.
- Massive refactor or rename patch makes many candidates STALE (above 20%), so INCOMPLETE. The author must reindex or rerun after the base updates.
- search_code ranking drift across reindexes or embedding-model changes means a different top-25. Some pairs found in run 1 are absent in run 2: a recall difference, visible as a different ManifestSha256, not a score difference.
- get_symbol returns empty source (observed in probe). The design does not depend on it, but any fallback that did would be wrong.
- contentHash recipe changes in a future Engram version (e.g. CRLF normalization). Every candidate becomes STALE and the gate goes INCOMPLETE. The harvester must record the Engram version and dryscan must pin the recipe per version.
- Headless auth: the interactive PKCE-only Engram client cannot run in CI, so the harvester cannot produce a manifest and the gate is INCOMPLETE.
- Threshold miscalibration: SUSPECT band too wide leads to chronic INCOMPLETE; too narrow lets real type-3 clones pass. Needs labeled eval before turning on enforcement.
- Allow-file churn: teams pre-register broad families (`src/**`), effectively disabling the gate. Mitigate with a code-owned cap on glob breadth and receipt listing of every allow-based exemption.
- Roslyn parse errors on preview C# syntax (.NET 10 / C# 14 features) if the Roslyn pin lags. Unreliable units mean INCOMPLETE.
- PASS never becomes authoritative under current GateRunner (external plugin PASS is downgraded to INCOMPLETE advisory). Consumers must read PluginRawOutcome, or the gate only meaningfully blocks via FAIL.
- Prompt-injection is irrelevant (no model decides), but Engram responses are untrusted. A poisoned or wrong response can only nominate a candidate whose bytes are then verified at B, so it cannot fabricate a FAIL on code that does not exist at B.

### Implementation sketch

delivery-forge (one type per file):
- `components/evidence/DeliveryForge.Evidence/Adapters/DryRecallEvaluationPlugin.cs` (plugin)
- `Adapters/DrySnapshot.cs` (private nested is fine)
- `Adapters/DryRequestFactory.cs`: canonical invocation, the AntiSlopRequestFactory analogue
- `Adapters/DryCandidateManifest.cs` + `DryCandidateManifestEntry.cs`: compositionally bound DTOs, may share a file
- `Adapters/DryCandidateManifestReader.cs`: bounded, canonical JSON, sha256 name check
- `DryCandidateHarvester.cs`: host-side Engram MCP client over streamable HTTP, deterministic query builder, canonical request/response capture; uses the official ModelContextProtocol C# SDK pinned to an exact version at implementation time
- `DryQueryBuilder.cs`
- `AdvisoryDryComposition.cs`: mirrors AdvisoryAntiSlopComposition: harvest, SelectApplicable, request factory, GateRunner.RunAsync
- Tests: DryRecallEvaluationPluginTests (normalize, reconcile, INCOMPLETE paths), DryCandidateManifestTests

New tool project `tools/DeliveryForge.DryScan/`, published `-r linux-x64 --self-contained -p:PublishSingleFile=true -p:Deterministic=true -p:ContinuousIntegrationBuild=true`, with the SDK pinned in global.json:
- Program.cs
- GitObjectReader.cs: `git diff -U0 -z`, `git cat-file --batch`
- UnitExtractor.cs: Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp, exact pin matching the repo's LangVersion, e.g. 4.14.0, or 5.0.x for C# 14
- TokenNormalizer.cs
- GreedyStringTiler.cs (min tile 12)
- SubtreeHashBag.cs
- CandidateVerifier.cs: contentHash recipe v1 + relocation
- BaseUnitHashIndex.cs: host cache keyed by tree id
- AllowPolicy.cs: reads `B:.delivery-forge/dry-allow`
- PairClassifier.cs
- ReportWriter.cs: dryscan-json-v1

Phase 2 runner change: introduce `IFrozenDiffScopePlugin` + `RepositoryDiffEvaluationScope`, and replace the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks with capability interfaces so the runner mints input-set identity over base+head+diff.

Calibration: harness that replays engram-csharp history. Take DryUpScan-filed clone groups as positives and Watched-not-filed pairs plus known boilerplate as negatives, synthesize patches by reverting one side, and tune 0.80/0.80/0.60/0.70.

Effort: about 2.5-3.5 engineer-weeks.
- dryscan tool: ~1 wk.
- Plugin, manifest and composition: ~1 wk.
- Harvester with headless auth: 3-4 d.
- Calibration and docs (quality-gates.md section): 3-5 d.
- Phase-2 runner generalization: +1 wk.

### Novelty claim

Not in the owner's three-item list:
1. Engram as an UNTRUSTED recall oracle whose output is frozen into a content-addressed manifest. The network/non-deterministic step happens before the gate, so the gate executable stays offline and hermetic while still getting semantic recall.
2. Per-candidate currency proof using Engram's own `contentHash` re-derived from the protected base commit. This turns Engram's default-branch-only, unknown-lag index into verifiable evidence: stale, relocated and moved candidates are classified mechanically, and scores are reproducible across reindexes because Engram bytes and scores never enter the decision.
3. Ratchet semantics (only pairs with a NEW side count) plus the #238 third-copy escalation.
4. A move/self-edit exclusion derived from the diff.
5. Load-bearing duplication declared through protected-base families and base-side template markers that the patch cannot self-grant, reusing the anti-slop protected-ignore authority model instead of an in-patch suppression attribute.

### Open questions

- Which Engram instance does the gate target? engram-csharp is not registered in the HoT instance (Repository not found); does nucleoid run its own, and how does a headless CI principal authenticate (confidential client vs current PKCE-only public client)?
- Can Engram expose LatestIndexedCommit (already in HealthStore) via an MCP tool or initialize metadata, so index lag becomes a hard precondition instead of a limitation? Also, why is per-symbol indexedAtVersion null?
- Should SUSPECT pairs be INCOMPLETE (blocking if the gate is required) or recorded as a PASS limitation? The repo's rule says uncertain means INCOMPLETE, but chronic INCOMPLETE may erode trust; this could be where a Jev/Haiku adjudicator plugs in later.
- Exact thresholds (0.80/0.80 confirm, 0.60/0.70 suspect, 70 tokens/10 lines floor) need a labeled calibration run on engram-csharp history before enforcement; who owns the labels?
- Is the contentHash recipe (sha256 of '\n'-joined line slice, CR retained) a stable public contract of Engram, or must dryscan version-gate it per Engram serverInfo version?
- Are tests excluded as FAIL targets by default, or held to a looser threshold?
- Cross-repo enforcement: which shared libraries, at which pinned commits/tags, and is a FAIL that says 'use package X' acceptable when adopting X needs a package reference change?
- Phase-2 runner generalization (IFrozenDiffScopePlugin) is needed for a runner-minted base+head+diff input-set identity; acceptable scope for the PR that adds this plugin, or a follow-up?
- Should the harvester's manifest be produced by a separately privileged principal (like the future v2 signer) so the patch process cannot craft a candidate-poor manifest to dodge FAIL? Today a host could pass an empty-but-well-formed manifest; reconciliation only proves every unit was queried, not that the answers were genuine.

### Judge verdicts

**contract-fit (6/10).** Interface mapping is mostly sound. The plugin members (DetectInputs, ValidateBeforeExecution, CaptureBeforeAsync, Get*Identity, Normalize, ValidateAfterExecution) exist as claimed. The GateRunner anti-slop special-casing is real: I confirmed `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks at lines 115, 260, 273, 285, 296, 381, 785 and 861. The PASS-to-INCOMPLETE advisory downgrade is real (HasTrustedLegacyV1Authority). The proposal is also honest about both gaps. Receipt discipline is good: uncertain paths (Engram errors, stale ratio, count mismatch, SUSPECT pairs) become INCOMPLETE, and scoring is a pure function of (B, H, manifest, config).

Weaknesses under this lens:
1. The manifest is a trusted input with no authenticity. The candidate-producing process can hand over an empty but well-formed manifest. Reconciliation only proves each unit was queried, not that the answers were genuine. This is the biggest bypass: the verdict depends on recall that nobody attests to. The proposal lists this as an open question, but it undermines the claim that a PASS means anything.
2. Input-set identity in v1 falls back to the legacy hash of Plugin.Inputs. That hash does not bind base, head or diff. Binding lives only in dry-binding.json, an artifact the plugin writes itself. This is weaker than the anti-slop frozen-input discipline (the exact Git tree) that the task context names as the bar.
3. Raw PASS can never be authoritative, since it is downgraded to advisory. Only FAIL is meaningful, so a gate that "fails until DRY" has no trustworthy pass path.
4. The harvester runs outside GateRunner and is not covered by any identity. Engram version and query-builder version are recorded only in the manifest.
5. The "no runner change" claim is optimistic. Phase 2 needs a generalization touching nine special-case sites.
6. The repo-controlled allow file read at B is acceptable as protected base. The base-side `// dry:` marker is also a base-only authority, but it needs careful handling.
7. Reproducibility under a rerun depends on a cached host-side index keyed by tree id, which is not in the receipt identities.

Strongest weakness: The frozen candidate manifest is an unauthenticated, host-produced input that the verdict depends on for recall. A PASS-leaning empty or poor manifest passes reconciliation, and the v1 input-set identity does not bind base, head or diff into a runner-minted identity.

Suggested fix: Have the runner (or a separately privileged signer) mint and verify the manifest, with its sha256 and a harvester identity bound into ConfigurationIdentity. Make the local full-base normalized-hash backstop the sole PASS-determining recall path, so Engram only adds FAIL or SUSPECT findings. Generalize the AntiSlop special-casing through a capability interface before shipping, so the input-set identity covers base, head and diff. Add the host-side base-unit cache key to the receipt.

**accuracy (5/10).** Strengths: alpha-renaming plus literal classing makes renamed copies and the manual-rename helper case near-certain catches. The local base-tree normalized-hash backstop and the intra-patch comparison give type-1/2 recall that does not depend on Engram. GST plus an order-insensitive subtree-hash bag gives some resistance to statement reordering. Ratchet semantics (only pairs with a new side) and the SUSPECT-to-INCOMPLETE band avoid silent passes. Weaknesses on the issue-238 shapes: (1) The 70-token / 10-line floor and the skip of delegation bodies of 2 statements or fewer remove byte-identical wrapper methods, a headline 238 shape, and most duplicated private static helpers. These are typically 3-9 lines, so the gate mirrors jscpd's floor but cannot see them, and the ~40 known 11-28 line pairs are mostly near the floor. (2) Alpha-renaming keeps invoked member and type names. The 85%-identical methods with mechanical differences, such as GitHubClient vs AzureDevOpsClient calls, will probably fall below the 0.80/0.80 CONFIRM line. That outcome is INCOMPLETE, not FAIL. That is the main failure shape the gate exists to catch, and on it the gate mostly reports uncertain. (3) The protected allow families (vcs-bulk-indexers, ts-http-extractors, per-table-stores) match exactly the sibling files #238 targets. Registering them makes the intended catches CLEAR-by-policy and contradicts the third-copy rule. If they are not registered, the gate over-flags per-VCS logic. (4) There is no principled boilerplate model beyond the allow files. DI registration chains of 10+ lines with identical call names will hit normalized-hash equality and be CONFIRMED (FAIL). Switch tables and mapping code over-flag for the same reason. Tests are excluded as targets, which is safe for precision but hides test duplication. Gameability is high. Splitting a method into sub-10-line helpers, or into pieces under the 12-token tile, evades both the unit floor and the GST. The 30% and 5-line added-line rule can be dodged by touching few lines in a copied unit. Adding a trivial statement or changing a few API names pushes a copy under 0.80 into INCOMPLETE rather than FAIL. Type-3 recall is admitted at ~50-75% and Engram-dependent. Type-4 is mostly missed. INCOMPLETE fatigue is likely until thresholds are calibrated.

Strongest weakness: The size floor (70 tokens / 10 lines, delegation bodies skipped) removes the byte-identical wrapper methods and the small duplicated private static helpers that issue 238 targets. Meanwhile the 85%-identical near-misses sit below the 0.80/0.80 FAIL line, so they yield INCOMPLETE instead of FAIL.

Suggested fix: Add a sibling-family rule that works on small units. Group by (method-name stem, signature shape) across sibling files, and FAIL when the patch creates a 3rd or later copy at any size at or above 3 statements. This also resolves the conflict with allow families, because the family list then marks deliberate divergence instead of suppressing detection. Weight the kept API names so near-identical bodies with mechanical differences score as parametric duplicates, for example by abstracting type and member names that differ consistently across the pair, and score the result at S_gst >= 0.85. Make the floor apply to a merged cluster instead of per unit, so split helpers get re-aggregated. Define built-in boilerplate classes for DI registration chains and switch tables. Calibrate thresholds on the known positives and the ~40 watched pairs before enforcing.

**cost-ops (5/10).** Cost and latency are excellent: roughly zero marginal dollars, about 10-30 s cold and 6-15 s warm for a 300-line patch, and no LLM in the loop. That fits the owner's top preference (deterministic). Operability and effort are the weak points.

Effort is about 2.5-3.5 engineer-weeks for a full Roslyn tool (units, normalizer, GST, subtree-hash bag, verifier, allow policy), a harvester, and a plugin. That is large for a first advisory plugin, and a phase-2 runner refactor adds another week. Calibration needs labeled history that does not exist yet.
