Dependencies are heavy for a "deterministic" gate:
- The harvester needs live Engram over the network and a headless Entra credential. Only interactive PKCE exists today, so CI auth is an open question.
- engram-csharp is not indexed in the HoT instance, so as designed every run would be INCOMPLETE until that is fixed.
- Engram indexes only the default branch and exposes no indexed-commit, so freshness is unknown and recall silently degrades.
- The gate is not hermetic end to end, and the manifest has trust and forgery holes (the design's own open question about a crafted empty manifest).

Maintenance is a real burden:
- Pins to track: Roslyn (a version lag on C# 14 means parse errors and INCOMPLETE), the Engram contentHash recipe (a change makes every candidate STALE), tools/list schemas, and the query-builder version. Any of these can silently or loudly break the gate.
- Threshold and family-allow tuning is ongoing. Chronic INCOMPLETE from the SUSPECT band is a probable operability failure.
- Under the current GateRunner a PASS from an external plugin is downgraded, so the gate only blocks via FAIL until v2 authority lands.

The local Roslyn plus normalized-hash backstop is the cheap, valuable core (type-1/2). The Engram-nomination layer adds most of the cost and fragility for uncertain type-3 gain (the design's own probe showed weak search_code hits, with 50-75% recall guessed).

Strongest weakness: Operational fragility and effort: the gate cannot produce a verdict without a live, authenticated, indexed Engram plus a fresh manifest. Today that means a missing repo index, no headless CI auth, and unknown index lag. On top of that comes roughly 3-4 weeks of Roslyn and harvester work, ongoing pin and threshold maintenance, and chronic-INCOMPLETE risk.

Suggested fix: Ship a v0 that is a single offline dryscan with no Engram: Roslyn normalized-hash plus GST over the base tree and the patch itself, cached by base tree id. Make it advisory first, and calibrate on engram-csharp history. Make Engram an optional second-stage recall booster behind a flag. Treat Engram errors as a recorded limitation (degraded mode explicit in config) rather than INCOMPLETE once the local backstop covers type-1/2. Defer the harvester, headless auth, cross-repo support, and runner generalization until the base tool's precision is measured. This cuts effort to about 1-1.5 weeks and removes the network, auth, and index-freshness dependencies from the critical path.

### Adversarial refutation

Not in the top five; not refuted.


## A.cheap-llm-judge: dry-judge: Haiku 5.5 vote over a frozen clone-candidate pack, with enumerated citations

Panel mean 5.3 (contract-fit 5, accuracy 5, cost-ops 6). Tier: cheap-llm.

**One line.** A pinned local launcher (df-dry-judge) builds a canonical evidence pack from base..head using git objects only: the touched C# members, the top-k existing look-alike code from a Roslyn winnowing index of the base tree plus Engram search_code, and the protected-base DRY policy. Claude Haiku 5.5 (configurable to Sonnet 5.5) then judges each touched member 3 times through a static JSON schema. The citation field can only name one of the supplied candidates (c1..c8 or none). The gate FAILs only on a unanimous, citation-verified "should reuse/extract" verdict. Any disagreement, refusal or transport problem is INCOMPLETE. Verdicts are memoized by the pack hash, so a rerun gives the same answer.

### Mechanism

Precondition: the request factory builds the canonical Invocation `df-dry-judge judge --repo <root> --base <BaseCommit> --head <HeadCommit> --policy-rev <protected base> --artifact-dir <host temp guid> --credential-file <host path> --config <host config path>`. Inputs are read from the git object DB by SHA only (git cat-file --batch, or a pinned in-process reader). They are content-addressed, so working-tree state does not matter, and the runner's before/after identity check still covers the rest.

1. CHANGE SET. `git diff --no-ext-diff --no-textconv -U0 -M50% --diff-filter=AMR <base> <head> -- "*.cs"`. Keep production paths only. The default exclusions are tests/**, **/*Tests*/**, *.g.cs, *.Designer.cs, **/Migrations/**, obj/ and bin/, and the protected-base policy can extend the list. Parse the hunks into added line ranges at head.

2. UNITS. Parse each changed head file with Roslyn (Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp, pinned). Map added ranges to the smallest enclosing member: a method, constructor, accessor, local function, or a lambda with 3 or more statements. A member becomes a "unit" when it has at least 5 added lines or at least 30% of its lines are added, and it has at least 6 non-blank lines and 50 tokens. Patches with zero units return a raw PASS early (or NOT_APPLICABLE when the policy allows it), and no model is called.

3. NORMALIZATION AND FINGERPRINTS. Each unit gets two token streams. The strict stream drops trivia and comments. The blind stream also replaces every identifier with $ID and every literal with $LIT, while keeping keywords, operators and invoked BCL member names. From these come a blind whole-unit SHA-256 and MOSS-style winnowing fingerprints over the blind stream (k=12 tokens, window w=8).

4. BASE INDEX. The same extraction runs over every production .cs member at the base tree. The result is an inverted index from fingerprint to (path, member, line range, base blob oid). It is cached on disk, keyed by base tree id plus extractor version. The base tree is immutable, so on a warm cache this step is just a lookup. Other units added in the same patch are also indexed, which catches duplication inside the patch.

5. CANDIDATES. A unit gets a candidate when:
   (a) blind hash equal (type-1/2), or
   (b) winnow containment |F_unit ∩ F_cand| / |F_unit| >= 0.50 with at least 50 shared tokens (type-3), or
   (c) Engram retrieval (type-3/4 recall only).
   Engram retrieval runs per unit: `search_code(query = signature + doc summary + top-20 identifier bag, repo, kind="method", path_glob="src/**", max_results=8)` plus `search_symbols` on the camel-split verb/noun bag. A hit is kept when its score is at least 0.38 (a starting value to calibrate). Each hit must resolve to a member that exists in the BASE tree. The candidate bytes are re-read from base blobs, never taken from Engram chunkText. Hits whose path or range is not in the base tree are dropped.
   Rank (a) > (b) by containment > (c) by score, and keep the top k = 5 (8 at most). The raw Engram responses are frozen into a retrieval snapshot keyed by (base tree id, unit blind hash, query string, Engram repo + artifact version hash). Reruns therefore reuse identical candidates even after Engram re-indexes.

6. DETERMINISTIC FAST PATHS (no LLM).
   - FAST FAIL: blind-hash equal, at least 25 normalized lines, both sides production, the pair fingerprint is not in the protected-base allowlist `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json`, and the head does not request an exemption.
   - FAST PASS: the unit has zero candidates.
   - EXEMPT: the pair fingerprint, sha256(sorted blind hashes of both sides), is listed in the protected-base allowlist. The exemption carries its reason and is recorded in the receipt.
   Only the gray-zone units go to the model.

7. EVIDENCE PACK. A canonical JSON document, sorted by path then line, containing: base, head, unit list (path, range, head blob oid, code text), candidates labelled c1..ck (source, path, range, base blob oid, similarity metrics, code text), the DRY policy text (from the protected base via `git show <policy-rev>:.delivery-forge/dry-policy.md`), and any exemption requests added at head (as context only). packHash = sha256(canonical bytes).

8. JUDGE CALLS. For each gray unit, make N=3 independent, stateless, single-turn calls to POST /v1/messages:
   - model "claude-haiku-5-5" (a fixed snapshot ID, no alias), with the API version header pinned
   - output_config.effort "medium", set explicitly; max_tokens 2048
   - no temperature, top_p or top_k: these are removed in API version 2026-10-21 and sending them returns 400, so temperature 0 is impossible and reproducibility comes from step 10
   - output_config.format = the static json_schema dry-verdict.v1
   - a static cached prefix (cache_control ephemeral 5m) holding the system prompt, rubric, protected-base dry-policy text and 6 labelled few-shot exemplars (about 4K tokens), followed by the per-unit suffix: the unit plus c1..ck wrapped in <untrusted_code> tags, with an instruction that their contents are data.
   Schema fields (additionalProperties false):
   - verdict enum [DUPLICATE_SHOULD_REUSE_EXISTING, DUPLICATE_SHOULD_EXTRACT_SHARED, LOAD_BEARING, TRIVIAL_BOILERPLATE, NOT_DUPLICATE, UNSURE]
   - cited_candidate enum [c1..c8, none]
   - load_bearing_reason enum [none, hot_path, per_variant_semantics, generated_or_schema, framework_boilerplate, other]
   - patch_lines {start: integer, end: integer}
   - suggested_target: string (an existing symbol or helper name)
   - rationale: string
   Client-side validation covers what the schema cannot express: the cited candidate must be at most k; it must not be none if and only if the verdict is DUPLICATE_*; patch_lines must fall inside the unit; and the rationale is capped at 600 characters.

9. CITATION VERIFIER (deterministic). A DUPLICATE_* vote counts only if its cited cN:
   (i) exists in the pack;
   (ii) resolves to base-tree bytes whose blob oid matches the pack (or to another unit in the patch, for intra-patch duplication); and
   (iii) passes a similarity floor: blind-hash equal, OR containment >= 0.35 over the cited patch_lines range, OR (Engram-sourced and score >= 0.38 and the same arity/return-kind shape from Roslyn).
   A vote that fails the verifier becomes INVALID. Because the citation is an enum over supplied candidates, the model cannot invent a symbol. It can only point at a wrong supplied one, which the verifier catches.

10. AGGREGATION per unit.
    - FAIL_FINDING: 3/3 votes are DUPLICATE_* with a verified citation of the same cN (REUSE versus EXTRACT may differ).
    - CLEAN: 3/3 votes are in {LOAD_BEARING, TRIVIAL_BOILERPLATE, NOT_DUPLICATE}.
    - INCOMPLETE: anything else, including any UNSURE, INVALID, refusal or max_tokens stop.
    - Optional escalation (configurable, off by default): on a split, run the unit 3 times on claude-sonnet-5-5 and apply the same rule. Both vote sets are recorded. Expect little gain, because decision-model and LLM errors are correlated (arXiv 2609.29769).
    The verdict cache stores, under key sha256(packHash || unit id || configuration identity), the request bytes hash, raw response bytes, resolved model id and usage. On a hit, no API call is made.

11. GATE OUTCOME, emitted by the launcher on stdout as df-dry-verdict-v1 JSON:
    - FAIL if at least 1 unit is FAIL_FINDING or FAST FAIL. Each finding lists the patch location, the cited existing symbol and suggested_target, and incomplete units become Limitations. Rationale: the author must refactor regardless, and FAIL is backed by a concrete, verified finding.
    - PASS only if every unit is CLEAN, FAST PASS or EXEMPT, with counts reconciled.
    - INCOMPLETE otherwise.

### Plugin contract fit

Class DryJudgeEvaluationPlugin : IDeterministicEvaluationPlugin, with Id "delivery-forge.dry-judge", Version "1.0.0", ExpectedTool "df-dry-judge" and CompatibilityAdapter EvidenceAdapterKind.External. It must be explicitly selected through EvaluationPluginSelection, because Resolve throws for External plugins without a selection.

- REGISTRATION: TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistration(GateId "dry", Plugin, ExpectedToolVersion "1.0.0", ExpectedFormatVersion "df-dry-verdict-v1", ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity = the reviewed launcher closure hash).
- CAPABILITY: a CapabilityProbe with Source TrustedExplicit, ExplicitExecutable = the absolute path of the self-contained single-file launcher, VersionArguments ["--version","--json"] returning {schemaVersion, toolVersion:"1.0.0", formatVersion:"df-dry-verdict-v1"}, and RequiredVersionPrefix "1.0.0". This works with ValidateCapabilityBinding: there is a real on-disk executable, ExecutableIdentity is the sha256 of its bytes, and Invocation.FileName equals Capability.ExecutablePath.
- DetectInputs(candidateInputs, ignoredInputs): returns every candidate path matching the production C# globs (src/**/*.cs minus the exclusions in step 1), excluding ignored paths, sorted ordinally. This is the search universe. The contract only hands DetectInputs path lists, so the diff-specific scope cannot be expressed here. It goes into the pack hash instead (see determinism).
- ValidateBeforeExecution(request): checks that the Invocation is exactly the canonical template. FileName must equal Capability.ExecutablePath. --base and --head must equal request.ExpectedRepository.BaseCommit and HeadCommit. --policy-rev must equal the protected policy source revision. --credential-file and --artifact-dir must be absolute and outside the repo. The Environment must be the cleared allow-list (SystemRoot, WINDIR, TMP, TEMP, HOME or USERPROFILE). StandardInput must be null. Any violation returns NormalizedEvidence(Incomplete, ...).
- CaptureBeforeAsync(request, ct): runs within the gate deadline. Computes the configuration identity and runtime closure identity (see determinism) and the expected unit-count preimage: changed production .cs paths and added-line count from git diff --numstat against the SHAs. Hashes the launcher bytes. Validates the closure against the reviewed pin. Stores everything in EvaluationPluginSnapshot(State = DryJudgeSnapshot). On a closure mismatch or unreadable config it throws InvalidOperationException, which the runner maps to ERROR for non-anti-slop plugins. Note: that mapping is hard-coded per plugin type (GateRunner :255-265), so INCOMPLETE would need the same generalization as anti-slop.
- GetConfigurationIdentity / GetRuntimeClosureIdentity(request, snapshot): return the snapshot values, or the literal "unavailable" when the snapshot is null (pre-snapshot Bind), mirroring anti-slop. GetExecutableIdentity returns the snapshot's launcher hash.
- EXECUTION: the host ICommandExecutor runs the launcher. All model, Engram and git work happens inside that process. GateRunner has no in-process execute hook, and doing network calls inside CaptureBeforeAsync would skip the runner's bounded-capture, timeout and quiescence handling.
- Normalize(request, execution, snapshot):
  - Parses stdout as df-dry-verdict-v1. Stdout is bounded at 64 MiB for non-anti-slop plugins; the launcher emits a summary under 256 KiB.
  - Reconciles: changedFiles and addedLines must equal the snapshot's numstat values; units = fastFail + fastPass + exempt + judged; per judged unit, votes.Length == N; every cited path/range must belong to the frozen base tree or the patch.
  - Exit code contract: 0 = clean, 1 = findings, 2 = incomplete, anything else = ERROR handling.
  - Builds new NormalizedEvidence(GateOutcome.Fail, reason, Fixture:false, ProductionCapable:true, Limitations) for findings; NormalizedEvidence.Pass(reason, false) for clean; NormalizedEvidence.Incomplete(...) for every gap.
  - The runner already turns TimedOut, Cancelled, a null ExitCode, !OwnedProcessQuiescent or an exceeded capture bound into INCOMPLETE before the plugin verdict matters.
- ValidateAfterExecution(request, snapshot): re-hashes the launcher and config files. Any change returns Incomplete. This is best-effort only, as in anti-slop: a same-UID host cannot make the closure immutable.
- AppendBoundArtifacts(request, snapshot, gateDirectory, artifactHashes): copies the following from the launcher's artifact dir into gateDirectory with FileMode.CreateNew and records "sha256:<hex>" for each:
  - dry-evidence-pack.json
  - dry-retrieval-snapshot.json (raw Engram rows)
  - dry-judge-transcript.jsonl (per-call request hash, raw response bytes, resolved model, usage, cacheHit)
  - dry-verdict.json
  - dry-binding.json (gateId, base/head/tree, plugin id/version, configurationIdentity, runtimeClosureIdentity, packHash, model, N, findings with citations)
  The plugin writes its own binding sidecar, because GateRunner.AppendAntiSlopBindingArtifact is hard-keyed to "dmmulroy.anti-slop". All exceptions are wrapped as IOException, because the runner only catches IOException and UnauthorizedAccessException there.
- AUTHORITY REALITY: under current code a PASS from an externally registered plugin is downgraded to a final INCOMPLETE with PluginRawOutcome "PASS" (GateRunner :396-403, HasTrustedLegacyV1Authority). FAIL passes through unchanged. So out of the box this is a blocking-FAIL / advisory-PASS gate. For PASS to be authoritative, the plugin must become a code-owned built-in with a TrustedGatePluginRegistry.BuiltIns binding.
- CONTRACT GAPS and adaptation: the contract gives no base tree, diff or blobs, and frozen materialization is anti-slop-only (`is not AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` at GateRunner :861). Phase 1 therefore has the launcher read immutable git objects by SHA itself. Phase 2 proposal: add IChangeScopedEvaluationPlugin plus a RepositoryChangeSet (ChangedPaths, AddedRanges, BaseObjectIds, HeadObjectIds) produced by a generalized GitRepositoryIdentityReader, and replace the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` type checks with a capability interface. The runner can then materialize head and base blobs and own the input-set identity.
- COMPOSITION: AdvisoryDryJudgeComposition, mirroring AdvisoryAntiSlopComposition: build the executor, the GitRepositoryIdentityReader, a TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistry with the dry registration, the capability via ToolCapabilityDetector (not hand-built), and DryJudgeRequestFactory.Create, then call runner.RunAsync.

### Determinism and receipts

PINNED:
- the launcher binary: self-contained net10 single-file; its sha256 is the ExecutableIdentity
- the launcher closure: publish dir, Roslyn assembly versions, git reader
- the model id "claude-haiku-5-5": a fixed snapshot with no alias, retirement not before 2027-10-07; the escalation model "claude-sonnet-5-5" is likewise fixed
- the anthropic API version header; effort "medium"; max_tokens; N=3
- the prompt template, schema and few-shot bytes
- the thresholds JSON (unit min 6 lines/50 tokens, winnow k=12/w=8, containment 0.50/0.35, Engram floor 0.38, k=5, fast-fail 25 lines)
- the Engram endpoint and repo name

There is no temperature or seed: API version 2026-10-21 rejects sampling params with a 400. So "deterministic" here means memoized and reconcilable, not regenerable. That is stated in the receipt Limitations.

CONFIGURATION IDENTITY = sha256 over, in fixed order with \0 separators:
- plugin id and version
- model id, escalation model or "none", API version, effort, max_tokens, N, unanimity rule id
- sha256 of prompts/dry-judge-system.v1.md, schema/dry-verdict.v1.json and fewshot/dry-examples.v1.jsonl
- thresholds JSON bytes
- extractor version and the Roslyn version string
- the protected-base blob oids of .delivery-forge/dry-policy.md and dry-allow.json (via git ls-tree <policy-rev>)
- the Engram URL, repo and mode (required or optional)
- environment allow-list keys and values (secret excluded)
- the credential-file PATH only, never its contents

The API key and the Engram bearer token live in a host-owned 0600 file outside the repo. They are never put in Environment, Arguments or StandardInput, all of which the receipt envelope records verbatim.

RUNTIME CLOSURE IDENTITY = sha256 over the launcher publish directory bytes, sorted path-wise. It is compared to the reviewed pin (ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity). A mismatch throws in CaptureBeforeAsync.

INPUT-SET IDENTITY: the runner's default, sha256 of the sorted path list from DetectInputs. That is weak: paths only, no blob ids. The binding sidecar and the stdout verdict therefore also carry packHash = sha256(canonical evidence pack). The pack includes base and head SHAs, every unit's head blob oid and range, every candidate's base blob oid and range, and the retrieval-snapshot hash. packHash is the real "what was judged" identity.

RERUN REPRODUCTION:
- The base index is cached by base tree id, and the retrieval snapshot by (base tree id, unit hash, query, Engram version). The same base..head gives the same pack.
- The verdict cache is keyed by sha256(packHash, unit id, configuration identity) and returns the stored raw responses with no API call. The same base..head plus the same config gives a byte-identical outcome; the receipt records cacheHit=true and the original response hashes.
- Any change to the code, the candidates, the policy, the prompt, the schema or the model changes the key and forces a fresh judgment.
- The transcript artifact lets an auditor re-run aggregation and citation verification offline from the stored responses. Those two steps are pure functions.

BECOMES INCOMPLETE:
- a non-canonical invocation, a closure or launcher hash mismatch, an unreadable protected policy
- the config file changing between capture and post-validation
- reconciliation failure (changedFiles, addedLines or unit counts mismatch, or votes != N)
- any unit with a split vote, UNSURE, an INVALID citation, stop_reason refusal or max_tokens, or a schema-validation failure
- HTTP 429/529/5xx after bounded retries (3 tries with exponential backoff, capped by the remaining gate deadline), or a transport error
- Engram unavailable while engramMode=required (with engramMode=optional this is a Limitation, "semantic recall unavailable")
- Roslyn parse diagnostics in a changed file, which mean the unit boundaries are unreliable
- a base-tree candidate that fails to resolve
- a timeout, cancellation, non-quiescent process or exceeded capture bound (runner-mapped)
- a final PASS without built-in authority (runner-downgraded, raw PASS retained)

### Engram usage

Engram is used for recall only and never as the source of the bytes judged.

1. Per gray-zone unit, the launcher calls `search_code(query = method signature + XML doc summary + top-20 camel-split identifier bag, repo = <repo>, kind = "method", path_glob = "src/**", max_results = 8)`. It also calls `search_symbols(query = verb+noun bag such as "Parse ContentRange header", repo, kind = "method")`, which finds an existing helper the patch should have called (the "re-implemented an existing helper" case that token tools miss).
2. Hits with score >= 0.38 (search_code, vector source; to be calibrated, since the observed range was 0.28-0.42 and quality is weak) or that pass the repo-scoped search_symbols floors are resolved to symbol ids. Their path and line range are checked against the BASE tree. The candidate code text is re-read from base git blobs. Engram's chunkText (truncated, and from the default-branch index) is never sent to the model as evidence.
3. Optionally, `change_impact(repo, changed_files, view="review")` is called once per gate. Its convention exemplars and hub list are added to the static prompt context as "known shared helpers in the touched communities". The call is cheap, bounded and pack-hashed.

Engram is reached through its HTTP MCP endpoint as JSON-RPC tools/call from the launcher, with the bearer token in the credential file. Raw responses are frozen into dry-retrieval-snapshot.json and cached per (base tree id, unit hash, query, Engram artifact version from list_artifacts). Reruns therefore do not drift when Engram re-indexes.

Limitations:
- Engram indexes the default branch, so code added at base after the last index is invisible to semantic recall. The local Roslyn base index covers it for type-1/2/3.
- The patch head is never indexed, so intra-patch duplication is handled locally.
- Engram is optional in config. Without it, type-4 recall drops to near zero, recorded as a Limitation.

### Load-bearing duplication handling

There are four layers, and none of them is a candidate-controlled bypass.

1. DETERMINISTIC SCOPE. Tests, generated code, migrations and Designer files are out of scope by default. Units under 6 lines or 50 tokens are never candidates. This removes most DI registrations, constructor assignment blocks and one-line guards.

2. PROTECTED-BASE POLICY. `.delivery-forge/dry-policy.md` is read via `git show <protected policy rev>:...`, the same pattern as anti-slop-ignore through ProtectedGitPolicyResolver, so a patch cannot weaken it for itself. It states the repo's DRY conventions in prose, for example: per-VCS bulk indexers (src/**/Vcs/*BulkIndexer.cs) may duplicate control flow when the semantics differ per provider; Span-based hot paths in tokenizers may be hand-inlined; tree-sitter extractors share helpers via ExtractorBase. It goes into the cached prompt prefix. The schema's load_bearing_reason enum (hot_path, per_variant_semantics, generated_or_schema, framework_boilerplate) gives the model a typed way to say "duplicated on purpose".

3. FINGERPRINT ALLOWLIST. `.delivery-forge/dry-allow.json`, also from the protected base, holds entries of {pairFingerprint = sha256(sorted blind hashes of both members), reason, owner, addedIn}. A matching pair is EXEMPT without a model call, and the receipt records it. Editing either side changes the blind hash, so the exemption expires automatically and the pair is judged again. An exemption cannot silently cover new copies.

4. NEW LOAD-BEARING DUPLICATION IN THE SAME PATCH. The allowlist comes from the base, so a PR cannot exempt itself. Instead, an allowlist entry added at head is treated as an exemption REQUEST. Its reason is shown to the model as context, and the deterministic fast-fail is turned off for that pair. The pair passes only if all 3 votes are LOAD_BEARING or TRIVIAL. Once merged, the entry becomes a base-tree exemption for later patches. In-code comments are passed as untrusted data and never auto-pass.

Bias is deliberately toward not failing. FAIL needs unanimous 3/3 votes plus a deterministically verified citation, so an ambiguous load-bearing case lands at INCOMPLETE, a visible "needs a human or an allowlist entry" state, rather than FAIL.

### Cost and latency

For a typical 300-line C# patch across about 6 files:

- UNITS: about 10-20 touched members. After the size floor and the Roslyn/winnowing pre-filter, typically 0-3 go to FAST PASS or FAST FAIL with no candidates, and about 3-8 gray-zone units reach the model.
- TOKENS: the static cached prefix (system prompt, rubric, dry-policy, 6 few-shots) is about 4-5K tokens. The per-unit suffix (unit plus 5 candidates) is about 3-6K tokens, so a call is about 8-10K input and about 400-900 output including adaptive thinking.
- HAIKU 5.5 ($0.10/M input, $0.50/M output; cache read $0.01/M; prompts stay under 100K so the 5x tier is never hit):
  - 6 units x 3 votes = 18 calls; about 18 x 5K uncached input = 90K = $0.009, plus 18 x 5K cached = $0.001, plus about 14K output = $0.007
  - about $0.02 per run, about $0.05 worst case (15 gray units); a cache hit on a rerun costs $0
- SONNET 5.5 ($2/$10): about 20x, so about $0.35-0.9 per run; the escalation-only mode adds about $0.1 when splits happen
- ENGRAM: 2 queries per gray unit (search_code + search_symbols), 0.5-2 s each, run in parallel
- LATENCY (wall clock, inside the gate deadline):
  - cold base index (parse plus fingerprint about 150k LOC of production C#): about 20-40 s, measured once per base tree id and then cached; warm, under 2 s
  - git diff, cat-file and unit extraction: 1-3 s
  - Engram: about 3-6 s with parallelism 8
  - judge calls: Haiku about 2-5 s each, parallelism 6, so about 5-10 s
  - total about 15-25 s warm, about 45-70 s cold; with Sonnet, about 30-60 s warm
- TIMEOUT: set the gate Timeout to 180 s so the retry and backoff budget fits.

These are back-of-envelope figures from list pricing (fetched 2026-10-28), not measured.

### Precision and recall expectation

TYPE-1 (exact): about 100% recall at member granularity for units of 6 or more lines (blind hash plus winnowing). Copies of 25 or more lines are deterministic fast-FAILs, which removes model variance where it matters most.

TYPE-2 (renamed identifiers or literals): high recall. The blind token stream normalizes identifiers and literals, which jscpd 4.2.5 and PMD CPD for C# do not. Precision depends on the model correctly calling short renamed clones (mapping code, DTO copy constructors) TRIVIAL_BOILERPLATE. Literature has GPT-4-class type-2 recall at about 0.86, but there is no Claude or C# data.

TYPE-3 (gapped, near-miss): moderate recall. Winnowing containment >= 0.50 catches roughly 50%+ of token overlap with edits. Heavily restructured copies fall below the floor, and Engram is the only fallback. LLMs are reported strong at confirming type-3 (about 0.85-0.97 for GPT-4-class on BigCloneBench (BCB) VST3/ST3), so precision on gray-zone pairs should be good.

TYPE-4 (semantic) and "should have called the existing helper": low recall. It depends entirely on Engram search_code/search_symbols surfacing the helper, and the probe showed weak ranking. Published LLM type-4 recall is poor (0.07-0.23 BCB WT3/T4), and cross-domain embeddings generalize badly. Expect most semantic re-implementations to be missed. Those that are surfaced and unanimously confirmed will be precise.

OVER-FLAG RISKS:
- repetitive C# scaffolding (attribute stacks on controllers, Refit interfaces, EF configurations, per-table Store CRUD); the cause is that high-similarity non-clones get labelled clones (documented LLM bias)
- per-VCS sibling logic, when the policy prose does not name the family

Mitigations: unanimity, the policy prefix and the allowlist. The expected effect is that over-flags turn into INCOMPLETE rather than FAIL.

MISSES:
- sub-6-line fragments and pattern-level repetition spread across members
- cross-repo duplication (out of scope)
- duplication against code added on the default branch after Engram's last index, for type-4 only
- anything whose candidate never makes the top k

TARGET before enabling blocking: FAIL precision >= 0.90 and INCOMPLETE rate <= 10% of runs on a labelled set. Proposed set: engram-csharp's about 40 "Watched, not filed" pairs, filed DryUp issues as positives, and 40+ curated boilerplate and per-VCS negatives, all replayed as synthetic patches.

### Failure modes

- Prompt injection: patch code or comments steer votes toward NOT_DUPLICATE, giving a false PASS. Bounded because PASS is advisory under current authority (final INCOMPLETE, raw PASS), exact clones of 25+ lines fail deterministically without the model, and the enum schema blocks free-form output. Injection-driven false FAILs are blocked by the citation verifier.
- Correlated model error: all 3 Haiku votes agree on a wrong DUPLICATE verdict for a high-similarity non-clone, giving a false FAIL that blocks a merge. Unanimity does not protect against systematic bias. The protected-base allowlist is the escape hatch, but it needs a follow-up or a two-step PR.
- INCOMPLETE storms: noisy splits on borderline units, 429/529 rate limiting in CI (Priority Tier is unavailable on Haiku 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5), or Engram outages make the gate block often for reasons unrelated to DRY. That erodes trust and invites the policy to disable the gate.
- Authority mismatch: current GateRunner downgrades every external-plugin PASS to final INCOMPLETE. If the merge policy treats INCOMPLETE as blocking, every clean patch blocks unless the plugin becomes a code-owned built-in with legacy-v1 authority, or the composition treats raw-PASS INCOMPLETE as advisory.
- Model retirement or silent drift: claude-haiku-5-5 retires no earlier than 2027-10-07. Snapshot IDs are fixed, but serving-stack nondeterminism means a cache-miss rerun of an unchanged pack can produce a different verdict, which the receipt can only expose, not prevent.
- Retrieval misses: Engram's default-branch index lags, search_code ranking is weak, and k=5 truncation drops the true helper, so the gate passes (or reaches FAST PASS) on genuinely non-DRY code. This is a silent false negative with no INCOMPLETE signal.
- Roslyn unit mapping is wrong for partial classes, source generators, top-level statements or #if regions. Parse diagnostics map to INCOMPLETE, but clean-parsing yet semantically odd code (for example, preprocessor-variant duplicates) can be mis-scoped.
- Secret leakage: if an operator puts the API key in Environment or Arguments instead of the credential file, it is written verbatim into the receipt envelope. ValidateBeforeExecution must reject any non-allow-listed env key.
- Data governance: proprietary engram-csharp source is sent to the Anthropic API, and the org's ZDR or retention status for Haiku 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 was not verified.
- Cache poisoning or tampering: the verdict cache dir is host-owned, but the same-UID host can edit it. Cached responses are re-hashed against the transcript artifact, yet nothing proves the cached bytes came from the API. The cache is evidence, not authority.

### Implementation sketch

EVIDENCE COMPONENT (paths relative to components/evidence/DeliveryForge.Evidence/; one type per file per HoT style):
- Adapters/DryJudgeEvaluationPlugin.cs
- Adapters/DryJudgeSnapshot.cs
- Adapters/DryJudgeRequestFactory.cs (canonical CommandInvocation; arg template constant)
- Adapters/DryJudgeVerdictReport.cs and DryJudgeUnitVerdict.cs (stdout DTOs, df-dry-verdict-v1)
- Adapters/DryJudgeVerdictReportParser.cs
- Adapters/DryJudgeIdentity.cs (configuration and closure hashing)
- AdvisoryDryJudgeComposition.cs
- tests in DeterministicEvaluationPluginTests style: a stub launcher fixture that emits canned verdicts covering every INCOMPLETE path and reconciliation mismatches

LAUNCHER: tools/DeliveryForge.DryJudge/, a net10 console app published self-contained single-file for linux-x64 and win-x64:
- Program.cs (verbs --version --json, judge)
- GitObjectReader.cs (git cat-file --batch by SHA; git path and bytes hashed into config identity)
- UnifiedDiffParser.cs
- RoslynUnitExtractor.cs (Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp pinned to an exact 4.x/5.x version and reviewed)
- BlindTokenNormalizer.cs
- WinnowingFingerprinter.cs
- BaseTreeCloneIndex.cs and BaseTreeCloneIndexCache.cs (keyed by base tree id)
- EngramMcpClient.cs (streamable-HTTP JSON-RPC tools/call)
- EngramCandidateSource.cs
- RetrievalSnapshotCache.cs
- EvidencePackBuilder.cs (canonical JSON, ordinal sort)
- AnthropicJudgeClient.cs (raw HttpClient to /v1/messages with pinned version header, output_config.format json_schema, cache_control on the prefix; no sampling params; an SDK adds closure surface)
- VerdictSchemaValidator.cs
- CitationVerifier.cs
- VoteAggregator.cs
- VerdictCache.cs
- ArtifactWriter.cs
- resources: prompts/dry-judge-system.v1.md, schema/dry-verdict.v1.json, fewshot/dry-examples.v1.jsonl, thresholds.v1.json

TARGET REPO: .delivery-forge/dry-policy.md and .delivery-forge/dry-allow.json in engram-csharp.

PHASE 2 contract work (optional, recommended):
- IChangeScopedEvaluationPlugin and RepositoryChangeSet
- generalize the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks in GateRunner (:115, :181/:861, :260, :273, :285, :296, :381, :499, :521, :565, :645) to capability interfaces
- catch all exceptions from AppendBoundArtifacts as INCOMPLETE

EXTERNAL PINS: Haiku 5.5 "claude-haiku-5-5" (escalation "claude-sonnet-5-5"); the Anthropic API version header; Roslyn exact package version; .NET 10 runtime embedded in the single-file publish; the Engram endpoint https://external-engram-dev.hot.co.nz/mcp; no jscpd dependency (Roslyn blind hashing supersedes it for C#; jscpd 4.2.5 stays the weekly repo-wide sweep).

EFFORT: launcher about 8-10 dev-days; plugin, request factory, parser and composition about 3-4 days; labelled eval set plus calibration of the 0.38, 0.50 and 0.35 floors and the prompt about 4-5 days; docs/quality-gates.md section about 1 day; phase 2 runner generalization about 5 days. Total about 3-4 weeks for one engineer.

### Novelty claim

The tier is the owner's #3 (cheap model). What goes beyond "ask Haiku if it is DRY":

1. Closed-set citations. A static schema enum (c1..c8 | none) is bound per call to a deterministically retrieved candidate list. The model cannot name a symbol that was not retrieved, and a deterministic verifier then re-checks the cited candidate's base-blob identity and similarity floor. "Cite or it is not a FAIL" becomes a structural property, not a prompt request.
2. A deterministic fast-FAIL lane for blind-hash clones of 25 or more lines, and a fast-PASS lane for units with no candidates. The model only sees the gray zone, which cuts cost and removes model variance from the cases most likely to block.
3. A self-expiring pair-fingerprint allowlist read from the protected base. Exemptions cannot be self-granted and die when either side changes. Head-added entries become exemption requests that still need unanimous LOAD_BEARING votes.
4. Two-level memoization. Retrieval snapshots are keyed by base tree id, and verdicts by the evidence-pack hash plus configuration identity. Reruns are byte-identical even though temperature 0 is impossible on these models and Engram re-indexes. Replayable transcripts let auditors re-derive aggregation offline.
5. Unanimity-or-INCOMPLETE rather than majority. Escalation defaults to off, based on the published finding that cheap-judge errors are correlated.
6. An honest adaptation of PR #52's contract: phase 1 reads immutable git objects by SHA inside a hashed launcher, phase 2 proposes a change-scoped plugin interface, and the plan accepts that PASS stays advisory without built-in authority.

### Open questions

- Does INCOMPLETE block merges in the target policy? If it does, every clean patch blocks under current authority rules, so the plugin must become a code-owned built-in with a TrustedGatePluginRegistry.BuiltIns legacy-v1 binding, or the composition must treat raw-PASS INCOMPLETE as advisory. Which does the owner want?
- Can Engram be reached headlessly from CI? Today auth is an interactive Entra PKCE flow with client ce5d95e9-...; is there a client-credentials or service-principal path? If not, should engramMode default to optional, accepting near-zero type-4 recall?
- Is sending engram-csharp source to the Anthropic API acceptable, and is the org's ZDR or retention status for claude-haiku-5-5 and claude-sonnet-5-5 confirmed?
- Should a FAIL with at least one concrete verified finding win over other units being INCOMPLETE (proposed), or should INCOMPLETE take precedence as in anti-slop's diagnostics-first ordering?
- Calibration of the floors: Engram score 0.38, containment 0.50 to nominate and 0.35 to verify citations, fast-fail at 25 lines, unit minimum of 6 lines and 50 tokens. These need the labelled eval set. Is the owner willing to label about 100 pairs from DryUpScan history and the 'Watched, not filed' list?
- Is phase 2 (IChangeScopedEvaluationPlugin plus RepositoryChangeSet, removing the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks from GateRunner) acceptable scope, or must phase 1 ship without touching GateRunner?
- Does Engram expose a stable index version or indexed-commit id usable as the retrieval-snapshot key? list_artifacts version hashes are a guess, and brief_repo may or may not report the indexed commit.
- Should exemption requests added at head require an extra human signal (for example a CODEOWNERS-reviewed path), or is unanimous LOAD_BEARING from the model sufficient for the same-PR case?

### Judge verdicts

**contract-fit (5/10).** The proposal maps onto the plugin interface method by method and handles most failure paths well: unanimity-or-INCOMPLETE, a citation verifier, memoized verdicts, and a protected-base allowlist. The contract gaps it names are real. GateRunner hard-codes `is Adapters.AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` at lines 115, 260, 273, 285, 296, 381, 785 and 861. It also catches only IOException and UnauthorizedAccessException, plus InvalidOperationException on some paths. Four fit problems remain.

1. The change-scoped diff is not expressible in the contract. DetectInputs receives path lists only, and the proposal itself says the diff scope goes into the pack hash. The input-set identity is therefore paths only. The runner never snapshots or freezes the base or head blobs, which anti-slop gets through frozen materialization. So "frozen inputs" are enforced by the launcher, not by the runner.

2. A PASS from an externally registered plugin is downgraded to a final INCOMPLETE with raw outcome PASS. Out of the box this is a blocking-FAIL, advisory-PASS gate. The proposal discloses this, but it means the default design cannot produce an authoritative PASS.

3. The verdict is not reproducible by the contract's own standard. There is no temperature or seed. A cache miss can give a different verdict, and the verdict cache sits in a same-UID directory that the proposal admits is not authenticated. Memoization by pack hash substitutes for determinism. It records the outcome but does not make the outcome reproducible. Receipt discipline treats receipts as evidence, so this is tolerable only as an advisory gate.

4. Bypass surface exists. Head-added allowlist entries and in-code comments reach the model as context. A false PASS needs 3 of 3 votes in the clean set, and a candidate-crafted pack can steer all 3, because the votes are correlated and the prompt-injection mitigation is only "advisory PASS". That is weaker than the anti-slop bar of a deterministic verdict from a tool the candidate cannot influence. The candidate also controls which units exist, through its choice of how to split members.

Other gaps: the launcher reaches Engram and Anthropic over the network inside the executor. Runtime closure identity omits the model's served weights, the Engram index state and the cache contents. Engram retrieval is frozen into a snapshot keyed by an unverified artifact version, which the proposal lists as an open question. A FAIL that wins over other INCOMPLETE units departs from the diagnostics-first ordering in anti-slop.

Strongest weakness: The contract has no notion of a change set. PASS from an external plugin is downgraded to INCOMPLETE, and the verdict is an LLM vote that cannot be regenerated. What actually gets checked is a pack hash the plugin builds for itself, not runner-owned frozen inputs. Only a blocking-FAIL advisory gate fits cleanly today. A trustworthy PASS needs both GateRunner changes and a built-in registration.

Suggested fix: Ship phase 2 first: add a change-scoped plugin capability and a RepositoryChangeSet so the runner owns base and head blob materialization and the input-set identity. Replace the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks with capability interfaces, and map all exceptions to INCOMPLETE. Make the deterministic lane (blind-hash and winnowing clones) the only source of authoritative PASS or FAIL. Make the LLM lane only able to downgrade a deterministic candidate to INCOMPLETE or advisory, never to PASS. Drop head-supplied exemption context from the model input. Authenticate the verdict cache, for example with an HMAC or by storing it inside the receipt. Verify Engram's index version before relying on it as a snapshot key.

**accuracy (5/10).** Judged only on precision/recall for real duplication in engram-csharp. Strengths: Roslyn blind-token hashing plus winnowing at member granularity catches the core issue-238 shapes. Byte-identical wrappers across 5 files and manually renamed helpers are type-1/2 and will be found. Function-for-function duplicated private static helpers are also found as long as each is at least 6 lines and 50 tokens. Deterministic fast-FAIL at 25+ lines is a strong, low-variance precision anchor. Unanimous 3/3 votes plus citation verification, and a bias toward INCOMPLETE over FAIL, protect against over-flagging. Gaming is partly resisted: identifier and literal renaming is neutralized, and statement reordering degrades winnowing only moderately.

Real weaknesses on recall:
1. Unit floor (6 lines/50 tokens, 5 added lines or 30%) leaves the known 11-28 line "Watched, not filed" clones in range, but the 85%-identical-with-mechanical-differences methods are the hard case. Blind-stream containment >= 0.50 with k=12 is plausible, but mechanical differences (different client type, different provider call) are spread through the method. The token-12 gram shatters, so the 0.50 candidate threshold can miss exactly the issue-238 near-miss shape. Engram is the only fallback, and the proposal itself says its ranking is weak (0.28-0.42 observed).
2. Splitting a method into two sub-6-line or sub-50-token members evades the gate entirely. Extracting the duplicated body into a private helper in the same file is also a trivial evasion, since it is only judged against base and intra-patch units. Reordering statements and inserting no-op statements lower containment. There is no pattern-level or sub-member clone detection. Top-k=5 truncation with a mixed ranking can drop the true sibling when 5 near-identical sibling indexers all rank similarly. Truncation is silent.
3. The "5 sibling files" shape specifically is a family clone: a new 6th sibling will have 5 candidates. That fits k=5 barely, but across hundreds of base members, siblings rank high, so this is likely fine. Hot-path false negatives are a different issue.

Real weaknesses on precision:
1. Per-table Stores, Refit-style interfaces, EF configs and Store CRUD are high-similarity, structurally identical, and legitimately repetitive. Under blind normalization they hash identically, and a 25+ line blind-hash match fast-FAILs with no model call. Blind hashing erases identifiers, so two Stores' 25-line CRUD methods are "byte-identical" in blind form by design. This is the single largest false-FAIL risk, and it bypasses the LLM guardrail entirely. Only the protected allowlist or the policy prose (which the fast path does not consult) saves them. Tests are excluded, which helps, but per-VCS sibling logic is exactly where the 25-line fast-fail fires, and it is the case the policy prose is meant to cover, so the prose is ignored on that path.
2. Unanimity only guards against random variance, not the systematic bias the proposal itself notes. Three Haiku votes on the same pack are highly correlated, so "3/3" overstates confidence. Combined with the citation verifier's weak floor (containment >= 0.35, or Engram score >= 0.38 plus arity match), a plausible-looking wrong citation can pass.
3. Records, DI registration and test arrange blocks are mostly under the size floor, excluded by path, or fall to TRIVIAL_BOILERPLATE; this is fine. Generated code is excluded by filename only, so [GeneratedCode]-attributed or source-generator output in other files is not.

Net: good type-1/2 coverage and a conservative FAIL posture, but the deterministic fast-FAIL lane over-flags per-table and per-VCS boilerplate (blind hash by design), near-miss type-3 recall is moderate with trivial evasions (split method, extract-to-private-helper, reorder), and semantic recall depends on weak Engram retrieval. Unanimity gives false confidence under correlated errors.

Strongest weakness: The deterministic FAST FAIL on blind-hash-equal pairs of 25+ lines bypasses the model and the policy prose. Because identifiers and literals are erased, structurally identical per-table Store CRUD, per-VCS sibling logic, and Refit/EF scaffolding hash identically. They would hard-FAIL unless already in the protected-base allowlist, so the main false-positive control is skipped exactly where this repo has the most legitimate repetition. Separately, type-3 recall (85%-identical methods with scattered mechanical differences) relies on k=12 winnowing and weak Engram retrieval.

Suggested fix: Route blind-hash fast-FAIL candidates through the policy/family check before failing: only fast-FAIL when the pair is not within a policy-declared family (path globs like Vcs/*BulkIndexer, *Store, Extractors) and the identifiers differ by only a small renamed set, otherwise send it to the model lane. Lower the winnow gram to about k=6-8 and use a similarity-of-structure (AST subtree/Zhang-Shasha or normalized Levenshtein on blind stream) measure for 80-95% near-miss candidates. Add sibling-family expansion (all members in the same family glob get included as candidates instead of top-5), and detect evasions by also fingerprinting the whole touched file's member set so splitting or extracting a helper still matches. Then calibrate on the 40 'Watched, not filed' pairs plus negatives before enabling blocking.

**cost-ops (6/10).** Operating cost per run is excellent: about $0.02 for Haiku, memoized reruns cost $0, and the deterministic fast paths skip the model for the clearest cases. The design is also careful about caching, since the base index is keyed by tree id and the verdicts by pack hash. It scores 6 and not higher because of three operability problems.

1. Cost order. The owner ranks deterministic below decision model below cheap LLM. This is tier 3, and a large share of its machinery (Roslyn winnowing, blind hashing, fast-fail) is already a deterministic check. That layer would be tier 1 and almost free, yet here it is only a front end to an LLM.

2. Implementation and maintenance effort. The estimate is 3-4 weeks for one engineer and 8-10 dev-days for the launcher alone. That is the heaviest of any plausible option and carries a lot of surface: Roslyn closure pins, a git reader, an Engram MCP client, an Anthropic client, a verdict cache, and a Phase 2 rewrite of GateRunner.

3. External dependencies and flakiness. The gate needs the Anthropic API, a key, rate limits (429/529 in CI), Engram with interactive Entra PKCE auth and no confirmed headless path, and a retrieval index of default-branch code that lags. Every one of these failures lands as INCOMPLETE, and that INCOMPLETE storm risk is real. Unanimity of 3 votes turns any disagreement into INCOMPLETE, which can make the gate block often for reasons unrelated to DRY.

Two further issues. Under current authority rules, PASS is downgraded to INCOMPLETE, so the gate does not work out of the box unless INCOMPLETE is advisory. The latency figures are unmeasured; the cold base index of 20-40 s is plausible, but 45-70 s cold under a 180 s timeout leaves thin margin. On the positive side, model pinning is easy (a fixed snapshot ID, retirement not before 2027-10-07), and the stubbed-launcher test strategy is sound.

Strongest weakness: It is a tier-3 LLM design that takes 3-4 weeks to build and depends on the Anthropic API, Engram auth and index freshness, and the GateRunner changes in Phase 2. Many routine failure modes surface as INCOMPLETE, so the gate is likely to block often for non-DRY reasons. The deterministic Roslyn/winnowing layer, which is the cheap and reliable part, is not offered as a standalone first tier.

Suggested fix: Ship the deterministic Roslyn blind-hash and winnowing stage on its own as the first plugin (tier 1, no network, no key, no Engram). It would fast-FAIL type-1/2 clones of 25 lines or more, and everything else would be PASS or an advisory Limitation. Make the LLM judge an optional second stage, off by default and configurable, with Engram in optional mode and a single vote with verified citation instead of 3. Treat Phase 2 as separate work. Measure cold and warm latency on the real 150k LOC repo before committing to the timeout.

### Adversarial refutation

Not in the top five; not refuted.


## A.novel-proof-of-search: Proof-of-Removability DRY gate (Roslyn fingerprints + refactor-feasibility probe + host search ledger)

Panel mean 5 (contract-fit 5, accuracy 5, cost-ops 5). Tier: novel.

**One line.** This gate does not judge whether a patch is "not DRY". It fails only when the host proves the duplication can be removed: it finds clone pairs with a deterministic Roslyn fingerprint search over the frozen base and head trees, then mechanically unifies each candidate pair in a scratch copy, compiles it and runs tests. If that passes, the gate FAILs and attaches the unifying diff. Pairs that cannot be unified must carry a hash-bound exemption from the protected-base registry. A host-owned Engram proxy also records what the agent saw while searching, so "the agent saw the existing helper and duplicated it anyway" becomes a recorded fact.

### Mechanism

Inputs: the base and head commit SHAs from GateRequest.ExpectedRepository, the repo's .git object DB, and two files read at the BASE commit (git show base:path, same pattern as ProtectedGitPolicyResolver): .delivery-forge/dry-policy.json and .delivery-forge/dry-exemptions.json. There is also an optional per-run Engram ledger snapshot supplied by the host.

1. Freeze the trees. The tool DeliveryForge.DryProbe reads base and head through `git -c core.quotepath=off --no-replace-objects cat-file --batch` with GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1, re-hashing each blob in memory (same idea as VerifyGitBlobIdentity). It gets the patch hunks from `git diff -U0 --no-ext-diff --no-textconv -z base head -- '*.cs'`. The worktree is never read. Commits are content-addressed, so the inputs are frozen by construction.

2. Extract units. Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp 5.0.0 does a syntax-only parse with no MSBuild and no analyzers. A unit is a method, constructor, accessor, local function or lambda body, or any block, with at least 6 statements AND at least 50 normalized tokens (about the engram-csharp jscpd floor of 70 raw tokens / 10 lines). Excluded:
   - paths matching the policy's nonProduction globs (tests/**, **/*.g.cs, **/obj/**, Migrations/**);
   - "boilerplate units" whose statements are all ArgumentNullException guards, simple member-assignments, or ILogger calls.
   A changed .cs file with syntax errors means INCOMPLETE.

3. Fingerprint each unit three ways:
   - (a) T1: SHA-256 of the token stream without trivia.
   - (b) T2: SHA-256 after alpha-renaming parameters and locals to positional placeholders and abstracting literals to STR/NUM/BOOL/NULL.
   - (c) T3: a multiset of per-statement T2 hashes (Merkle, bottom-up) plus a 64-permutation MinHash over 5-gram token shingles.
   The base-tree index is cached under the key sha256(baseTreeId + toolVersion + policyBytes) in a host-owned cache directory. A cache entry is re-verified by recount before use.

4. Select subjects and pairs.
   - A subject is a head unit whose symbol key (namespace.type.member(paramTypes)) is absent from base, or that has 50% or more added lines in the hunks.
   - Candidates for each subject come from: T1/T2 bucket hits; LSH (16 bands x 4 rows) hits that also pass Dice(statement multisets) >= 0.85, token-LCS ratio >= 0.80 and size ratio in [0.75, 1.33]; and the same checks run head-vs-head inside the patch.
   - A pair is PREEXISTING, and dropped (ratchet, never punishes touching an old clone), if the same (T2 left, T2 right) pair already existed in the base index.
   - A pair is EXEMPT if dry-exemptions.json at base has an entry bound to both sides' (path, symbolKey, T2 hash). If either body changes, the exemption auto-expires.

5. Run the refactor-feasibility probe on non-exempt pairs. Budget: at most 3 pairs and 360 s, ordered ledger-seen first, then T1 > T2 > T3, then size descending.
   - The probe materializes the verified head tree into a host temp directory outside the repo and restores offline: `dotnet restore --locked-mode` against a host-owned NUGET_PACKAGES cache with no remote sources. A missing package means INCOMPLETE.
   - It builds the affected project plus the test projects that reference it through the ProjectReference graph, and runs those tests to get a BASELINE.
   - A red baseline means INCOMPLETE ("cannot prove anything on a red tree").
   - Then it tries two strategies:
     - S1 Reuse: the subject is T1/T2-equivalent to an existing accessible member B (same return and parameter types modulo names; B static, or on the same containing type). The subject body is rewritten to delegate to B, or its call sites are retargeted to B and it is deleted.
     - S2 Extract: Plotkin anti-unification of the two syntax trees into a template. Limits: at most 3 holes; holes must be expression-level (literal, identifier, simple member access) or at most 1 generic type parameter with no constraints required. The template becomes a private static helper on the common containing type, or an internal static class in the deepest common directory of the same project. Both sites are replaced with calls.
   - The edit is kept only if its "DRY dividend" (net normalized-token reduction, i.e. removed duplicate tokens minus helper and call-site tokens) is at least 40.
   - Then `dotnet build --no-restore -warnaserror-` and `dotnet test --no-build` on the same test set.
   - Probe status:
     - UNIFIED: builds, and every test that was green at baseline stays green. Pair is PROVEN_REMOVABLE.
     - DIVERGENT: a test that is green at baseline fails on the unified variant, reproduced twice. This is evidence of behavioral difference, and the pair is cleared.
     - UNSUPPORTED: transform shape not supported, or dividend < 40.
     - BUDGET: probe budget ran out.

6. Ledger (optional policy requireSearchReceipt, default off). For each new public/internal non-trivial symbol, the ledger must contain an Engram search_code / search_symbols / search_text / search_group call whose query either contains the symbol name or has Jaccard >= 0.25 on identifier subtokens against (name + parameter types + top-5 body identifiers). Separately, a subject that is T1/T2 of any symbol id the ledger shows was RETURNED to the agent is tagged SEEN_AND_DUPLICATED. That tag raises probe priority and is named in the FAIL reason.

7. Verdict (tool exit 0 = clean, 1 = findings, anything else = INCOMPLETE):
   - FAIL: any non-preexisting, non-exempt pair that is PROVEN_REMOVABLE. The reason lists pair ids, and stdout carries the unified diff for each.
   - INCOMPLETE:
     - any non-exempt T1/T2 pair that is UNSUPPORTED or BUDGET;
     - a T3 pair with similarity >= 0.95 that is UNSUPPORTED (pairs below 0.95 are an advisory limitation by default; policy knob unprovableNearMiss);
     - count mismatch;
     - syntax diagnostics;
     - a red baseline;
     - a ledger obligation that is unmet while required.
   - PASS: all subject units are accounted for, counts reconcile, and every above-threshold pair is EXEMPT, PREEXISTING or DIVERGENT.
   - NOT_APPLICABLE: zero subject units above the floor, and only when policy AllowedNotApplicable lists the gate.

### Plugin contract fit

Class DryUnificationEvaluationPlugin : IDeterministicEvaluationPlugin:
- Id "delivery-forge.dry-unification", Version "1.0.0", ExpectedTool "delivery-forge-dryprobe".
- CompatibilityAdapter = EvidenceAdapterKind.External, so it must be explicitly selected through EvaluationPluginSelection (Resolve throws otherwise).
- Registered by trusted host code through TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistration(GateId "dry", Plugin, ExpectedToolVersion "1.0.0", ExpectedFormatVersion "dryprobe-json-v1", ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity <reviewed sha>), or as a built-in line plus a TrustedGatePluginBinding in TrustedGatePluginRegistry.BuiltIns if it should ever mint authority.

Members:
- DetectInputs(candidateInputs, ignoredInputs): returns the tracked *.cs paths that are not ignored and not under nonProduction globs. Note that ValidateAutomaticScopeAsync is hard-gated to `plugin is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` (GateRunner.cs:861), so today these are only the receipt's declared inputs and default ComputeInputSetIdentity. The real frozen identity, sha256 over (base tree, head tree, policy bytes, exemption bytes, ledger bytes), is written to the sidecar.
- ValidateBeforeExecution(request): returns ERROR/INCOMPLETE unless all of these hold:
  - Capability.Version == "1.0.0" and FormatVersion == "dryprobe-json-v1";
  - Invocation.Arguments exactly equals the canonical vector built by DryUnificationRequestFactory (`analyze --git-dir <abs> --base <ExpectedRepository.BaseCommit> --head <ExpectedRepository.HeadCommit> --policy-blob <oid> --exemptions-blob <oid> --ledger <host path|none> --probe-budget-seconds 360 --max-probes 3 --format dryprobe-json-v1`);
  - Invocation.InheritEnvironment == false with the allow-list SystemRoot, WINDIR, TMP, TEMP, DOTNET_ROOT (host-pinned SDK), NUGET_PACKAGES (host offline cache), DOTNET_CLI_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT=1, DOTNET_NOLOGO=1, DOTNET_SKIP_FIRST_TIME_EXPERIENCE=1, GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1, GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1. No secrets are needed: Engram is not called at gate time.
- CaptureBeforeAsync(request, ct): reads the base-pinned dry-policy.json and dry-exemptions.json blobs via git show and verifies their oids equal the argument oids. It hashes the ledger snapshot file, the DryProbe publish directory, the pinned dotnet SDK directory and the NUGET_PACKAGES manifest, and checks the offline cache and SDK version against the candidate global.json. A mismatch throws InvalidOperationException, which becomes INCOMPLETE (rule (7)). Result: EvaluationPluginSnapshot(State: DryCaptureState{PolicySha, ExemptionsSha, LedgerSha, ToolClosureSha, SdkSha, ExecutableSha}).
- GetConfigurationIdentity(request, snapshot): sha256 over policy + exemptions + ledger + thresholds + env allow-list. Returns "unavailable" when snapshot is null, same as anti-slop.
- GetRuntimeClosureIdentity(request, snapshot): sha256 over the tool publish directory, the Microsoft.CodeAnalysis*.dll bytes and the pinned SDK directory. It is compared to the reviewed constant, as ValidateReviewedPinsAsync does.
- GetExecutableIdentity: the snapshot sha of DryProbe(.exe), which satisfies ValidateCapabilityBinding's FileName == Capability.ExecutablePath plus hash check (GateRunner.cs:766-805). The tool is a real self-contained single-file executable, so no runner change is needed for executable-less tools.
- Execution: the host ICommandExecutor runs the tool. Stdout is the dryprobe-json-v1 report, well under the 64 MiB non-anti-slop capture cap.
- Normalize(request, CommandResult, snapshot):
  - Parse the JSON. Require report.base/head == ExpectedRepository, schemaVersion "1", and counts.subjectUnitsParsed == counts.subjectUnitsDeclared.
  - Every pair must have a disposition in {preexisting, exempt, unified, divergent, unsupported, budget}, and diagnostics must be empty.
  - Exit code must be 0 or 1, and a finding with exit 0 (or the reverse) means INCOMPLETE.
  - FAIL = new NormalizedEvidence(GateOutcome.Fail, "<n> duplicate(s) provably removable: <pairIds>", false, true, limitations), built with the constructor because there is no Fail factory.
  - PASS = NormalizedEvidence.Pass(...) (ProductionCapable false).
  - N/A is built with NotApplicableRationale "no C# production units above floor".
- ValidateAfterExecution: re-verifies that the tool's reported base/head and blob counts match the snapshot.
- AppendBoundArtifacts(request, snapshot, gateDirectory, artifactHashes): it gets no CommandResult, so the unification diffs stay inside stdout.txt, which the runner already hashes. It writes dry-binding.json with FileMode.CreateNew containing: schemaVersion 1.0.0; kind dry-unification-binding; base/head/tree; policy/exemptions/ledger shas; tool and SDK identities; inputSetIdentity. It records "dry-binding.json" -> "sha256:<hex>". Only IOException and UnauthorizedAccessException escape to the runner's handler, so everything else must be caught internally.
- Outcome surfacing: runner precedence passes FAIL through unchanged. PASS is downgraded to final INCOMPLETE "advisory" (GateRunner.cs:396-403), because an externally registered plugin has no legacy-v1 authority and future-v2 is disabled. This gate therefore BLOCKS correctly on FAIL today and becomes authoritative on PASS only when authority work lands.
- Optional runner generalization: replace the `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks (GateRunner.cs:115, 861, 499) with an IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin / IBoundSidecarPlugin interface, so the runner-owned sidecar and frozen-scope pipeline can serve this plugin. The plugin does not strictly need it.

### Determinism and receipts

Pinned (reviewed constants in the plugin, like anti-slop's ReviewedPackageSha256):
- DryProbe executable bytes and publish directory;
- Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp 5.0.0 (exact, packages.lock.json);
- the dotnet SDK used by the probe, through a host DOTNET_ROOT. The SDK must match the candidate global.json, else INCOMPLETE;
- the NUGET_PACKAGES offline cache manifest;
- the thresholds compiled into the tool version and echoed in the report.

Hashed identities:
- InputSetIdentity (sidecar): sha256(baseTreeId, headTreeId, policyBlobOid, exemptionsBlobOid, ledgerSha or "none").
- ConfigurationIdentity: policy + exemptions + ledger + env allow-list + thresholds.
- RuntimeClosureIdentity: tool + Roslyn + SDK.
- ExecutableIdentity: tool bytes.

The static phase (extraction, fingerprints, pair selection, preexisting ratchet, exemption match, ledger check) is a pure function of those, so a rerun reproduces it bit-for-bit. The base-index cache is keyed by tree id and re-verified, never trusted blind.

The probe phase runs dotnet build/test on candidate code, so it inherits test flakiness. Mitigations:
- the baseline must be green in the same run;
- DIVERGENT requires the failure to reproduce twice;
- UNIFIED requires a green build and green tests and is otherwise re-runnable from the attached diff (anyone can `git apply` it on head and rebuild).

Engram is NOT called at gate time. The ledger is a frozen host-recorded file, so live index drift cannot change a verdict.

INCOMPLETE, never PASS, in these cases:
- a syntax error in a changed .cs file;
- count mismatch or a report base/head mismatch;
- tool exit not 0 or 1, or a finding/exit disagreement;
- timeout, cancel, or capture over the limit;
- red baseline;
- missing offline package or SDK mismatch;
- a non-exempt T1/T2 pair that is UNSUPPORTED or BUDGET;
- a T3 pair at >= 0.95 that is UNSUPPORTED;
- the ledger is required but absent, its sha differs, or a symbol lacks a search receipt;
- exemption or policy blob oids do not match the base;
- the post-run source identity changed (that one is ERROR, per runner).

The receipt is append-only evidence, not authority. A FAIL receipt carries the exact unification patch in stdout.txt plus dry-binding.json.

### Engram usage

Three bounded uses, none on the verdict path at gate time:

(1) Proof-of-search ledger. A new host component, EngramLedgerProxy in DeliveryForge.Execution/Host, is a streamable-HTTP MCP pass-through. AgentAdapterProfile writes it into the worker's MCP config as "engram". The host, not the agent, holds the Entra token. Each call (tool, args, response sha256, returned symbolIds/paths, timestamp, and Engram index state if exposed) is appended to the RunStore as kind "engram-call". At gate time it is exported as a sealed ledger snapshot whose sha is bound into the identities. From it the gate derives:
- per-new-symbol search receipts (policy requireSearchReceipt);
- the SEEN_AND_DUPLICATED tag (the agent was shown symbol X and wrote a T1/T2 copy of it anyway).
The host re-reads candidate bodies from the frozen base tree by path, never from get_symbol: get_symbol fetches from the VCS default branch, which may not equal the base.

(2) Cross-repo advisory recall. The tool's optional pre-pass (run by the composition, not the gate, and recorded into the ledger) calls search_group / search_code corpus-wide with signature + top identifiers. T1/T2 matches in another repo that the head already references as a package (contract_impact) appear as advisory Limitations only, never FAIL, because they are not frozen.

(3) Probe ordering hint. structural_impact(symbol_id, include_reachable_tests=true) on the existing side can order which test projects run first inside the budget. The authoritative test set is still the ProjectReference graph from the head tree, so a stale index can only change speed, not the verdict.

Within-repo duplicate detection deliberately does NOT depend on Engram. The base-tree Roslyn index is complete and frozen, while Engram indexes the default branch and cannot see the patch head.

### Load-bearing duplication handling

Six layers, none of them a bypass the worker can apply in its own patch:

(1) Size and boilerplate floor: at least 6 statements and at least 50 normalized tokens. Units made only of guard clauses, simple assignments or logger calls are never subjects.

(2) Ratchet: pairs that already existed at base are excluded, so touching an existing sibling family (for example the 5 VCS bulk indexers) does not fail. Only NEW duplication fails.

(3) Proof requirement: FAIL needs the duplication to be mechanically unifiable, with a DRY dividend of at least 40 net tokens, while staying green. Deliberately per-VCS logic that differs in statements, types or behavior is UNSUPPORTED (type-3 below 0.95 is advisory by default) or DIVERGENT (tests go red when unified), so it does not fail.

(4) Protected exemption registry: .delivery-forge/dry-exemptions.json is read at the BASE commit. Each entry is {id, left{path, symbolKey, t2Hash}, right{...}, reason enum [hot-path, per-vendor-variant, generated-shape, framework-boilerplate, isolation-boundary], workItem, reviewedBy}. The worker cannot add an exemption in the same patch; this follows the docs rule "Workers cannot ... broaden exemptions". An exemption needs a separately reviewed change merged first. It is bound to body hashes, so it expires automatically when either copy changes, and stale exemptions cannot accumulate.

(5) Protected policy families: dry-policy.json at base can declare sibling families (for example glob src/**/Indexing/*BulkIndexer.cs, mode allow-structural-variant) that downgrade T3 pairs inside the family to advisory. T1 copies are still allowed to fail.

(6) Hot-path markers like [MethodImpl(AggressiveInlining)] or a "// perf" comment are surfaced in the report as hints but never honoured alone, because they are worker-controlled. They only matter once they are in the registry.

### Cost and latency

$0 model cost. No LLM or decision-model calls; the Engram proxy adds about 0 cost.

For a typical 300-line C# patch against engram-csharp (~150k LOC):
- Static phase: about 4-8 s cold to parse and fingerprint the base tree, under 1 s on a warm cache keyed by tree id. Subject extraction and LSH pairing are tens of ms. When no pair passes the filters (expected for most patches), the total is about 10-20 s including process start and git cat-file.
- Probe phase, only when a candidate pair survives:
  - offline restore check: about 5-15 s;
  - baseline incremental build of the affected project plus referencing test projects: about 30-90 s (unmeasured; needs measuring on engram-csharp);
  - baseline tests: 10-120 s depending on the project;
  - each pair: about 0.1 s transform plus 20-60 s rebuild plus the test run.
  - The cap is 3 pairs and 360 s, so the worst case is about 6-7 minutes and the typical candidate case is about 1.5-3 minutes.

Disk: one temp head materialization (about the repo size) per run, deleted afterwards. Compute is the same order as the existing DotNet test gate.

### Precision and recall expectation

- Type-1 (exact): about 100% recall within the repo for units above the floor. FAIL precision is high because a passing build and tests prove removability.
- Type-2 (renamed identifiers or literals): high recall via alpha-renaming and literal abstraction. S1/S2 should unify most of them, so FAIL precision is high.
- Type-3 (gapped):
  - Candidate recall is moderate. Dice >= 0.85 on statement hashes means roughly 15% of statements can differ.
  - Proof power is low: S2 only abstracts expression-level holes, so pairs with inserted or deleted statements end up UNSUPPORTED. They become advisory below 0.95 and INCOMPLETE at or above 0.95.
  - This is the main recall gap. It is deliberately conservative: it under-fails rather than over-fails.
- Type-4 (semantic): not detected. Cross-repo semantic nominations from Engram are advisory only.

Expected over-flagging:
- Mapping/DTO-conversion and test-like setup code inside production that unifies cleanly. The 40-token dividend and boilerplate filter reduce this but do not eliminate it.
- Unifications that compile and pass tests but are architecturally wrong. Examples: a helper placed across a layering boundary, or coupling two modules that should stay independent. Since the 300-line patch is graded against itself plus the base, a reviewer may still disagree. The exemption registry is the escape hatch.

Missed:
- Clones split across differently-shaped methods.
- Clones below 6 statements (the owner's "Watched, not filed" 11-28 line pairs are mostly above this floor, so they should be caught if newly introduced).
- Duplication of logic that lives in non-C# files (SQL, YAML, tree-sitter queries).
- Untested code: a unified variant of untested code passes trivially. The gate still FAILs then, which is correct since there is no behavior to diverge, but DIVERGENT clearing is impossible.

Validation plan: replay engram-csharp history. Revert merged DryUpScan-filed fixes, and use the ~40 watched pairs plus seeded synthetic T1/T2/T3 clones as the labeled eval set.

### Failure modes

- Probe executes candidate code (MSBuild targets, source generators, tests), the same trust level as the existing DotNet gate. A hostile candidate can sabotage builds to force UNSUPPORTED, or craft a failing test to fake DIVERGENT. Sabotage yields INCOMPLETE, never PASS, for T1/T2. A faked DIVERGENT on a T1/T2 pair could clear it, so policy may require T1 pairs never to clear by divergence alone (open question).
- Flaky tests: a red baseline yields INCOMPLETE (blocks merge for unrelated reasons). Flakes during the unified run could mis-mark DIVERGENT; mitigated by requiring reproduction twice, which costs time.
- Offline restore gaps: a new package in the patch that is not in the host NUGET_PACKAGES cache makes every probe INCOMPLETE until the cache is refreshed by host ops.
- SDK mismatch: a candidate global.json requiring an SDK the host has not pinned yields INCOMPLETE.
- Anti-unification placement heuristics (deepest common directory, internal static class) may produce a compiling but stylistically wrong refactor. The gate still FAILs. This is acceptable because the diff is offered as proof, not as the mandated fix, but agents may apply it blindly.
- Unification that is semantically valid but architecturally bad (crossing a project or layer boundary) only compiles if the reference already exists. When it compiles, the FAIL may be argued; the escape route is a reviewed exemption, adding friction.
- Ledger proxy can be bypassed if the worker sandbox has its own route to Engram or another search tool (not enforceable on a dev Windows box without network isolation). The receipt obligation then measures only proxied calls. Hence it defaults to off, and SEEN_AND_DUPLICATED is an aggravating tag, not the basis of FAIL.
- Search-receipt obligation is gameable with junk queries that match symbol names. It proves the search happened, not good faith.
- Base-index cache corruption or tool-version skew: mitigated by keying on tree id plus tool version and re-counting, but a cache bug would affect every run until the tool version bumps.
- Runtime: large solutions with slow incremental builds can blow the 360 s probe budget; BUDGET means INCOMPLETE, which blocks merges until the budget is tuned.
- AppendBoundArtifacts exceptions other than IOException and UnauthorizedAccessException propagate through the runner, so the plugin must catch everything itself.
- Final PASS is reported as advisory INCOMPLETE under current authority rules (GateRunner.cs:396-403), so the gate can block but cannot yet positively authorize.

### Implementation sketch

New host tool: tools/DeliveryForge.DryProbe (net10.0, self-contained single-file publish for win-x64 and linux-x64, reviewed sha pinned in the plugin; Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp 5.0.0 exact in packages.lock.json; no MSBuildWorkspace in the static phase). One type per file:
- GitObjectReader: cat-file --batch with --no-replace-objects and blob re-hash.
- PatchHunkReader: diff -U0 -z.
- SyntaxUnitExtractor, BoilerplateClassifier, UnitNormalizer: alpha-rename and literal abstraction.
- UnitFingerprint: T1/T2/statement-multiset/MinHash64.
- BaseIndexCache: keyed by tree id + tool version.
- CandidatePairFinder: buckets + LSH 16x4 + Dice/LCS verification.
- PreexistingPairRatchet.
- ExemptionRegistry and DryPolicy: JSON schema v1, read by blob oid.
- SearchLedgerVerifier.
- ReuseRewriter (S1).
- AntiUnifier and HelperExtractor (S2).
- DryDividendCalculator.
- ProbeWorkspace: head materialization, dotnet restore --locked-mode offline, build/test via ProjectReference graph, baseline + rerun.
- DryProbeReportWriter: dryprobe-json-v1; `--version` prints `{schemaVersion, toolVersion}` for CapabilityDetector.

Evidence component:
- Adapters/DryUnificationEvaluationPlugin.cs
- Adapters/DryUnificationRequestFactory.cs: canonical args and env allow-list.
- Adapters/DryProbeReport.cs and DryProbeReportParser.cs
- Adapters/DryCaptureState.cs
- AdvisoryDryComposition.cs: mirrors AdvisoryAntiSlopComposition: ProcessCommandExecutor, GitRepositoryIdentityReader, TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistry, ToolCapability via ToolCapabilityDetector with CapabilityProbe(Tool "delivery-forge-dryprobe", Source TrustedExplicit, RequiredVersionPrefix "1.0.", DocumentedFormatVersion "dryprobe-json-v1").
- Optional: IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin / IBoundSidecarPlugin to replace the anti-slop type checks in GateRunner.

Execution component:
- Host/EngramLedgerProxy.cs: MCP streamable-HTTP pass-through that appends "engram-call" records to RunStore.
- Host/IEngramSearchLedgerReader.cs and EngramSearchLedgerReader.cs: export a sealed ledger snapshot per run id.
- An AgentAdapterProfile change to inject the proxy URL.

Contracts: a TrustedGatePluginBinding for gate "dry" if it is promoted to a built-in.

Protected repo files in engram-csharp: .delivery-forge/dry-policy.json and dry-exemptions.json (seeded from the current "Watched, not filed" list as preexisting).

Tests: the StubEvaluationPlugin pattern plus fixtures for T1/T2/T3/boilerplate/preexisting/exempt/divergent/red-baseline/budget/syntax-error/ledger-missing; normalization tests for every INCOMPLETE branch; a replay eval on engram-csharp history.

Effort, about 6-8 dev-weeks total:
- static phase: 1.5-2 weeks;
- S1+S2 probe: 2-3 weeks;
- plugin/factory/composition/tests: 1 week;
- ledger proxy: 1 week;
- eval set and threshold tuning: 0.5-1 week.

### Novelty claim

The owner's three options all ask a judge (a tool score, a decision model or an LLM) whether code "looks duplicated". This design changes the question to one with an objective answer: can the duplication be removed mechanically without breaking the build or tests?

(1) A FAIL is a proof, not an opinion. The receipt carries a unification diff that compiles and keeps tests green, and anyone can replay it. The same diff is the fix, so "fail until DRY" comes with an actionable remedy.

(2) Load-bearing duplication is handled by evidence rather than taste:
- behavioral divergence (tests go red when unified);
- non-unifiable shape;
- a hash-bound, protected-base exemption that expires when either copy changes and that the worker cannot self-grant.

(3) Host-mediated proof-of-search turns the agent's Engram session into an unforgeable ledger. This enables a new class of evidence: SEEN_AND_DUPLICATED, meaning the agent was shown the equivalent and copied it anyway. It also enables an optional per-symbol search obligation.

(4) A DRY dividend threshold (net normalized-token reduction >= 40) measures whether extraction is worth doing, instead of a raw clone-size cut-off.
