(5) A base-vs-head ratchet over clone-pair fingerprints means only NEW duplication fails. This turns engram-csharp's weekly repo sweep into a per-patch gate without inheriting the ~40-pair backlog.

### Open questions

- Does Engram expose an indexed-commit SHA or generation per repo (list_repos / brief_repo) that the ledger proxy can record as 'index state'? Not verified.
- Where should the per-run ledger enter the gate? GateRequest has no run id. Options: construct the plugin per run with an injected ledger path (proposed), or add a RunId / HostInputs field to GateRequest (a contract change).
- Should T1 pairs ever be clearable by DIVERGENT (test-observed behavior difference), given that candidate tests are candidate-controlled? A stricter policy is T1 = exempt-or-fail only.
- Exemption authoring flow: must an exemption land in a separate reviewed PR first (proposed, strict), or may an in-patch exemption be accepted when bound to the exact-patch review receipt (base/head/tree/patch hash)?
- Default for UNSUPPORTED type-3 pairs below 0.95: advisory (proposed) or INCOMPLETE? This is the main precision/recall dial and needs the engram-csharp replay eval.
- Is a 360 s probe budget acceptable as a blocking gate cost for engram-csharp, or should the probe run only in a second, slower gate tier while the static phase gives a fast INCOMPLETE?
- Should the host offline NuGet cache and the pinned SDK be part of the reviewed runtime closure constant (strict, but churns on every SDK patch) or a separately-attested input?
- Does the team want the gate to emit the unification diff to the coding agent automatically (closing the loop) or only attach it as evidence?
- Is network isolation of the worker sandbox available, so the Engram proxy cannot be bypassed? Without it the search-receipt obligation should stay advisory.

### Judge verdicts

**contract-fit (5/10).** Interface mapping is mostly sound. The interface does have DetectInputs, ValidateBeforeExecution, ValidateAfterExecution, GetConfigurationIdentity, two GetRuntimeClosureIdentity overloads, GetExecutableIdentity, CaptureBeforeAsync and Normalize, and the proposal uses them correctly. The base-commit policy and exemption reads, the registry-selected plugin, and the "UNSUPPORTED, BUDGET, syntax error or red baseline = INCOMPLETE" rules all fit the INCOMPLETE-over-PASS discipline. I confirmed that GateRunner.cs has many `plugin is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` special cases (lines 115, 260, 273, 285, 296, 381, 785, 861). The proposal admits that the runner-owned frozen-scope and sidecar pipeline is anti-slop only. That means its real frozen-input identity (base tree, head tree, policy, exemptions, ledger) lives in a plugin-written sidecar rather than the runner's input-set identity, and so is not runner-verified. The weaknesses are these. (1) The probe runs candidate-controlled restore, build and test, which is the main bypass. A candidate can fake DIVERGENT with a failing test and clear a pair, and the proposal leaves T1-by-divergence as an open question. Pairs that produce no unification, for example by breaking the build, land in INCOMPLETE rather than PASS, but an attacker-chosen DIVERGENT is a real route to PASS. (2) The runtime-closure identity covers the tool, Roslyn and SDK, but the NuGet cache and the builds on a mutable host are not reproducible. Verdicts depend on test flakiness, timing and a budget, so the same inputs may not give the same verdict. The plugin can only capture the tool and SDK identities, not that dynamic state. (3) The ledger is a host-recorded file and its enforcement is off by default. (4) Because PASS is downgraded to advisory INCOMPLETE under current authority rules, the gate can block on FAIL but cannot authorize, so it only partly fits the contract today. (5) Any unhandled exception thrown inside AppendBoundArtifacts bypasses the plugin's own handling.

Strongest weakness: The PASS path depends on candidate-controlled build and test execution (DIVERGENT clears a pair). A hostile candidate can fake DIVERGENT, and the runtime closure identity does not cover that dynamic behavior, so the verdict is not reproducible from the pinned identities. In addition, the runner-owned frozen-input and sidecar pipeline is hard-wired to AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin, so this plugin's input-set identity is plugin-asserted rather than runner-verified.

Suggested fix: Split the gate into two tiers. The first is a pure static Roslyn tier that is a fully deterministic function of frozen inputs, and it feeds the plugin contract. It should FAIL on exact T1/T2 clones that are non-exempt and non-preexisting, with no dependence on candidate execution. The second is a separate, sandboxed advisory probe tier whose results only add FAIL evidence and can never produce PASS or clear a pair, so a candidate-controlled DIVERGENT result cannot count as clearance. Alongside this, generalize the runner's `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin` checks into an IFrozenTreeEvaluationPlugin interface so the runner computes and verifies the input-set identity, and make the ledger either fully optional or an input bound into the runner identity, never plugin-asserted only.

**accuracy (5/10).** Precision is the design's strength. FAIL needs a mechanically proven unification (compiles, baseline-green tests stay green, dividend >= 40 tokens), so false FAILs on record declarations, DI registration, generated code and trivial boilerplate are unlikely. Those are excluded by the 6-statement/50-token floor, the boilerplate classifier, nonProduction globs and the base-pinned exemption registry. The ratchet stops pre-existing sibling families from failing, and T1/T2 recall is high.

Recall on the issue-238 shapes is weaker.
- Byte-identical wrapper methods across 5 files: caught by T1 if each wrapper has at least 6 statements and 50 tokens. Thin wrappers often fall below the floor and are missed.
- 85%-identical methods with mechanical differences: this is the type-3 gap. They survive Dice >= 0.85 only if differing statements are few. S2 allows at most 3 expression-level holes and no statement insertion or deletion, so mechanical differences such as different types, different calls or an extra statement come back UNSUPPORTED. Below 0.95 similarity that is advisory (a pass), so the gate silently under-fails on the main target shape.
- Private static helpers duplicated function-for-function: S1/S2 can unify them, but each private helper is probably small and may fall under the 50-token floor.
- A copy-pasted helper manually renamed: T2 alpha-renaming only covers parameters and locals. Renamed methods, fields, private members and called helpers still hash differently, so the clone falls to T3.

Further weaknesses:
- The ~40 known watched pairs are 11-28 lines, many near the floor. Proof-of-unification must also compile in a repo with per-VCS types and tests.
- Per-table Stores often differ in SQL strings, types or table names. Those become holes or type-3 pairs, again UNSUPPORTED.
- The DIVERGENT clearing path is gameable. A candidate-authored test can fail when unified and so clear a T2 pair. Tests may also not exist for the code, in which case it is not cleared and the gate FAILs, which is right.
- Splitting a method into sub-6-statement units evades the floor. It is only partly covered by the "any block" unit rule.
- Reordering statements defeats the LCS check but not the statement multiset.
- UNSUPPORTED/BUDGET on T1/T2 pairs gives INCOMPLETE, which blocks merges for tooling reasons, with a 360 s budget and a 3-pair cap. A noisy patch that exceeds 3 pairs yields BUDGET.
- Semantic (type-4) duplication is not detected.

Overall it is high precision and low-to-moderate recall on exactly the type-3 shapes the owner cares most about.

Strongest weakness: The proof-of-removability requirement (S2 allows at most 3 expression-level holes and no statement insertion or deletion) means the 85%-identical-with-mechanical-differences methods and manually renamed helpers become UNSUPPORTED. Below 0.95 that is advisory, so the gate passes on the core type-3 duplication it was meant to catch.

Suggested fix: Split detection from proof. Make high-confidence T3 pairs (Dice >= 0.85 AND LCS >= 0.80) a FAIL with a registry-exempt escape hatch, instead of advisory when unifiable by a heuristic. Add an S3 strategy covering statement-level gaps (template method or delegate/lambda parameter, extra-statement hook). Extend T2 normalization to alpha-rename private methods, fields and local helper names so renamed copies match as T2. Lower or make adaptive the 6-statement/50-token floor for wrappers and private static helpers that recur across 3 or more sibling files (count-based trigger like DryUpScan's). Make the divergence clear for T1 pairs require exemption rather than test failure.

**cost-ops (5/10).** Zero model cost and no network or Engram dependence on the verdict path fit the owner's top preference. The static phase is credible: Roslyn fingerprints cached by tree id, about 10-20 s when no pair survives. The cost lens still finds real problems.

1. The probe phase is not cheap or cold-start friendly. It needs an offline restore, a baseline build of the affected project and its referencing test projects, baseline tests, then per-pair rebuild and test runs. That is about 1.5-3 min typical and 6-7 min worst case. The proposal admits these timings are unmeasured. A blocking per-patch gate at that latency is heavy. It also repeats the existing DotNet test gate's compute, so there is no sharing.

2. Operability burden is high. It needs a host-owned offline NuGet cache that must be refreshed whenever a patch adds a package, otherwise everything goes INCOMPLETE. It needs a pinned SDK matching the candidate's global.json. It needs a self-contained single-file publish for each OS with its sha pinned in the plugin. SDK or Roslyn patches churn the reviewed closure constant. Roslyn 5.0.0 pinning is a further update burden.

3. Many paths end in INCOMPLETE. These include a red baseline, flaky tests, budget exhaustion, an SDK mismatch, a missing package, and any non-exempt T1/T2 pair that is UNSUPPORTED. INCOMPLETE blocks the merge, so every false INCOMPLETE is an operator ticket. Flake handling (reproduce twice) adds more time. This is a high maintenance load.

4. The effort estimate of 6-8 dev-weeks is large for an advisory plugin. S2 anti-unification with placement heuristics is research-grade code. The ledger proxy (MCP pass-through, Entra token, RunStore changes, AgentAdapterProfile change) is a separate project and is off by default. Anti-slop-style runner generalization is optional but likely needed.

5. The Engram ledger and the cross-repo recall are optional extras that raise cost for little gate value. They are bypassable without network isolation.

6. A cheaper phased path exists: ship the static phase alone, with fingerprint plus ratchet, as an advisory plugin in about 2 weeks. The probe is bolted onto it.

The design is deterministic, but the heavy build-and-test dependency makes it the most expensive and fragile of the deterministic options.

Strongest weakness: The probe phase makes a blocking gate depend on offline restore, baseline build and tests, and per-pair rebuild and tests (1.5-7 min, unmeasured), plus a host-pinned SDK and NuGet cache. Many ordinary conditions (flaky tests, a new package, an SDK bump, budget exhaustion) turn into INCOMPLETE merge blockers, and 6-8 dev-weeks of build effort come with them.

Suggested fix: Split into tiers. Ship phase 1, the static Roslyn T1/T2 fingerprint search with the base-vs-head ratchet and a protected exemption registry, as the fast advisory plugin (about 10-20 s, 2-3 weeks of effort). Make the build-and-test probe a separate, optional, non-blocking second-tier gate that only attaches a proposed unification diff and never produces INCOMPLETE for tooling gaps. Measure real restore, build and test times on engram-csharp before committing to a budget. Drop the Engram ledger proxy and the cross-repo recall until the static gate proves value. Treat UNSUPPORTED and BUDGET outcomes as advisory rather than INCOMPLETE until the precision data is in.

### Adversarial refutation

Not in the top five; not refuted.

# Appendix B: completeness critique

A final Claude Opus 5.5 critic reviewed all judged designs and refutations.

## Gaps no design addressed

- Fix-loop contract is undefined. 'FAILs until DRY' needs a retry protocol: a retry cap, an escalation path to a human override recorded in the receipt, and a guarantee the gate converges, so that fixing finding A cannot surface new finding B indefinitely. No design specifies this.
- Remediation payload has no agreed shape. Designs mention file:line for both sides, but none defines a machine-readable finding schema for the agent: the existing symbol to call, a suggested extraction target and project/namespace, and the clone class with all members. None reconciles that payload with the oracle-leak concern raised in the refutations, i.e. how much detail to expose versus how many reruns to allow.
- No design accounts for the refactor PR itself. Extracting a helper from 3 of 5 siblings creates a new helper that matches the 2 remaining siblings, so the issue-238 DRY-up PRs would FAIL their own gate. Only one refutation salvage proposes net clone-mass delta accounting. No design adopts it as the primary verdict.
- Reinvention of shared HoT libraries or NuGet packages is ignored: HouseOfTravelLibrary, SerilogExtensions, Polly, BCL helpers such as hand-rolled retry, string or guard helpers. Only dry-recall has an optional search_group step. Every other design compares against the same repo only.
- No design integrates with the weekly sweep. None reuses Engram.DryUpScan (JscpdReportParser, ThresholdEvaluator, SiblingDeduper, PathFilters) as the single source of threshold and scope truth. None says whether the ~40-pair Watched list seeds the allowlist or ratchet. None says whether gate WATCH output feeds the sweep, or guarantees that a gate PASS means the sweep will not later file an issue for the same code. Gate and sweep thresholds and normalizers will drift apart.
- TFVC repos are not covered. Every design assumes git base/head SHAs. HoT TFVC work arrives via git-tfs bridges (tfs/default as base), shelvesets and /checkin-copy-only. None defines the base for a shelveset, how protected-base config is read without a protected git ref, or how Engram's TFVC indexing relates to that base.
- Language scope is C# only. delivery-forge is multi-language and the anti-slop precedent is JS/TS. Duplication in SQL text, .sql files, tree-sitter .scm queries, YAML pipelines, bicep and Svelte goes unexamined. Per-table Stores are mostly SQL strings, which every L1/H2 normalizer collapses to a single LIT.
- Test code policy is just a path exclusion, and the patch controls paths. No design has a tiered policy: lower-severity test-vs-test duplication, test code re-implementing production helpers, or duplicated fixture builders. Test-path classification needs to come from protected-base project metadata (IsTestProject in the csproj), not from directory names.
- Generated code is decided by patch-authored signals: headers, *.g.cs names, [GeneratedCode] attributes. No design uses a base-protected generator registry. Source-generator output that never appears in the tree, and partial classes that split a type across a generated and a hand-written file, are not handled.
- Cross-PR and temporal effects are ignored. Two concurrent PRs can each add the same helper and each pass, then duplicate on merge. There is no merge-queue re-evaluation against the updated base, no cumulative creep accounting across stacked PRs, and no defined behavior when the base moves after a rebase.
- No shared evaluation harness. Each design calibrates ad hoc, mostly against the same jscpd-found Watched pairs. There is no common labelled corpus with held-out positives, hard negatives (per-VCS providers, DTO and record families, DI wiring, EF config, per-table Stores) and an adversarial set (rename, split, pad, reorder, loop-to-LINQ). Without one, the 4 options cannot be compared on recall and FPR.
- Rollout and authority are not planned. External-plugin PASS is downgraded to INCOMPLETE and FAIL is advisory. No design says what actually consumes FAIL as blocking (the harness retry loop? a PR check?), and none plans a shadow or advisory period with measured override rates before enforcement.
- Very large and mechanical patches have no policy: bulk renames, formatter passes, namespace moves, API signature sweeps across sibling families. Most designs either FAIL them through the sibling rule or go INCOMPLETE on budget.
- Exemption governance is reinvented in every design: dry-allow.json, families, ledgers, dry-exemptions.json, each with different keying. No common registry format exists with span and content binding, expiry, CODEOWNERS enforcement and a human-readable excerpt. No design closes the two-PR self-grant path, where an agent first merges an allowlist entry and then the duplicate.
- Cost and latency budgets are unmeasured and not compared on a common basis (per-patch p50/p95, base-index cache reuse across PRs, model spend per PR). The preference order of deterministic, then decision model, then LLM depends on these numbers.

## Missing option ideas

- Sweep-as-gate clone-mass delta: run the existing pinned jscpd@4.2.5 plus Engram.DryUpScan pipeline (same config, PathFilters and ThresholdEvaluator as the weekly workflow) on the frozen base tree and the frozen head tree, scoped to the changed files plus their clone partners. Compare the resulting clone-group sets by total duplicated-token mass per group. FAIL when the patch increases mass, or creates a group that crosses DryUpScan's filing thresholds. PASS when mass is flat or falls, so partial refactors, moves and signature sweeps pass by construction. This reuses already-reviewed tooling, guarantees gate/sweep consistency, and turns the Watched list into the natural baseline. Base-side results can be cached per base commit, so per-PR cost is roughly one incremental head scan.
- Diff self-similarity lockstep detector plus a co-change history prior. Instead of comparing the patch to the tree, compare the patch's hunks to each other: normalize each added hunk (identifier-blind tokens) and flag pairs or sets of hunks in different files with high similarity. This is the 'same edit applied to N siblings' pattern (QA F-05/06/07/11, #974), the strongest signal that a shared abstraction is missing. Mine git log for files that are repeatedly co-edited with similar hunks to compute a 'shotgun-surgery debt' prior, which raises severity when a patch extends a known lockstep family. It is deterministic, cheap (diff-sized input, no tree index) and catches intra-patch duplication that tree-vs-tree ratchets miss.
- Behavioral fingerprinting for semantic (type-4) duplicates. For each new or changed pure-ish method (static, no I/O, parameters of primitive, string or collection types, detected with Roslyn), select signature-compatible existing candidates through the Engram search_symbols / search_code shortlist. Run both methods in a sandboxed, network-less process on a fixed-seed generated input set (FsCheck-style, a few hundred cases including edge values). Identical output vectors, or identical exception types, mean a semantic duplicate. FAIL with a 'call existing X instead' finding. The result is deterministic given the seed and pinned runtime, it catches rename, loop-to-LINQ and rewrite evasions that defeat every textual matcher, and it needs no LLM. Its scope is limited to the small-helper class (string, guard, mapping and parse utilities), which is exactly where agents most often reinvent existing code.

## Cross-cutting risks

- Contract prerequisite in common: every design needs the same GateRunner generalization. That means removing the anti-slop-only branches (GateRunner.cs:115/260/273/285/296/381/785/861), adding base/diff/change-set fields to RepositoryEvaluationScope, base-blob materialization, and runner-minted input identities. Until that exists, external-plugin PASS is downgraded to INCOMPLETE and FAIL is advisory. Build it once as shared infrastructure, not per option.
- The gate rewards gaming over fixing. Fixed line, token and statement floors plus explicit file:line findings give agents an oracle. Rename, split into sub-floor helpers, pad, reorder, or reflow until the finding disappears. The result is obfuscated duplication with a green receipt, which is worse than no gate.
- Patch-controlled exemptions recur across designs: path globs, *.g.cs names, auto-generated headers, [GeneratedCode], *Tests* directories, #if branches outside the pinned symbol set, hot-path attributes, and dry-intentional comments. Any scope or exemption decision must be derived from protected-base data only.
- Allowlist and family chicken-and-egg: legitimate new siblings (a 6th extractor, a new per-table Store) cannot pass without a prior merged exemption PR. That pushes owners toward broad globs and rubber-stamped ledger PRs, and opens a two-PR self-grant path.
- Partial-refactor false positives: pair-based or class-size attribution punishes incremental DRY-up (a new helper still matches the remaining siblings) and API sweeps across existing families. Without net mass-delta accounting the gate discourages the very cleanup issue 238 wants.
- Recurring normalization bug: per-unit positional alpha-renaming used as the fingerprint stream makes tokens context-dependent. Winnowing and type-3 recall collapse for sub-blocks and inserted statements. This was refuted in 3 separate designs. Fingerprint over an identifier-blind stream, and alpha-rename only during pair verification.
- The size floors (5-10 statements, 40-70 tokens, 8-10 lines) systematically miss short byte-identical wrappers and small private helpers. The FAIL tiers (80 LOC, 25 statements, 3 files) push the dominant 11-28 line, 2-instance shape into WATCH, so most designs do not meet 'fails until DRY' on issue-238 shapes.
- Calibration is circular. Thresholds are tuned on the same 41 jscpd-found Watched pairs, with no held-out set, no hard negatives and no adversarial set, so FPR and recall on renamed or type-3 code are effectively unmeasured for every option.
- Syntax-only Roslyn has no semantic model. It cannot tell locals from fields from types, resolve overloads or partial classes, or see inactive preprocessor branches. That limits both accuracy and the 'call existing X' remediation for every Roslyn-based option.
- Engram dependency risks: it indexes only the default branch (head unseen, staleness and index lag), headless CI auth is unsolved, and results are non-reproducible, so its output must be frozen into a manifest. An empty or poor manifest silently lowers recall while still passing reconciliation.
- Model stages: no seed and no temperature (removed in API 2026-10-21), and verdict caches keyed by exact text, so a cosmetic edit re-rolls the verdict (pass@k shopping). Identifiers, string literals, paths and rationale fields carry prompt injection, and comment-stripping does not remove them. Jev is early access with no SLA or retirement guarantee.
- INCOMPLETE fatigue: provider outages, budget overflow, parse errors, stale manifests, red baselines and unresolved votes all map to INCOMPLETE. Under external-plugin semantics that is indistinguishable from a clean pass, so the gate either blocks often for non-DRY reasons or carries no signal.
- Maintenance load: pinned Roslyn, jscpd, Node, zstd and model versions mean every bump forces re-pinning closure hashes and re-calibrating, and none of the cross-OS byte-identity claims (zstd, tokenizers) have been verified.

## C.contract

### Facts

(8) Bind again with snapshot :267 -> ConfigurationIdentity then written into request :272. (9) anti-slop only: ValidateCapabilityBinding with snapshot :273. (10) execution of request.Invocation by the host ICommandExecutor :285-312 (bounded capture). (11) WriteArtifactsAsync writes stdout.txt/stderr.txt :323 (impl :680-698). (12) plugin.AppendBoundArtifacts :331 (IOException/Unauthorized only caught -> artifactBindingError -> INCOMPLETE). (13) repo identity re-read :340-352; sourceChanged = before!=after or dirty. (14) plugin.Normalize :357 (any thrown exception -> ERROR 'Evidence normalizer failed' :363-366). (15) pluginRawOutcome = normalized.Outcome :369. (16) plugin.ValidateAfterExecution :370. (17) Integrity override chain :371-412. (18) final Bind with status Executed/NotApplicable and PluginRawOutcome text :436-441, persisted :447.
- GATERUNNER HAS NO PLUGIN-EXECUTE HOOK. The only execution path is the host _executor running request.Invocation (a CommandInvocation: FileName, Arguments, WorkingDirectory, Environment, InheritEnvironment, StandardInput; EvidenceModels.cs:105-111) at GateRunner.cs:285-312. The plugin sees only CommandResult (ExitCode, StandardOutput, StandardError, TimedOut, Cancelled, StartedAt, CompletedAt, OwnedProcessQuiescent; EvidenceModels.cs:113-121). A plugin whose work happens in-process (e.g. a model call) must do it inside CaptureBeforeAsync or Normalize, or be wrapped as a local executable invoked via Invocation. Also ValidateCapabilityBinding (:742-806) REQUIRES request.Capability.Supported, absolute ExecutablePath, ExecutableIdentity, ProbeWorkingDirectory == trusted root, request.Invocation.FileName == Capability.ExecutablePath (:783-789 region, mismatch -> ERROR 'invocation executable is not the detected capability executable'), and ExecutablePath bytes hash == ExecutableIdentity (or plugin.GetExecutableIdentity snapshot) :784-805. So every plugin must have a real local executable on disk to hash; there is no executable-less/API tool mode.
- BASE vs HEAD: the plugin contract gives NO base tree, NO diff, and NO file contents. GateRequest (EvidenceModels.cs:141-152) carries ExpectedRepository = RepositoryIdentity(BaseCommit, HeadCommit, TreeId, IsClean) (:103) which are commit/tree SHAs only; plus Invocation, Scope (string label), Policy, Capability, ConfigurationIdentity, Timeout, OutputDirectory, Plugin (EvaluationPluginSelection: Id, Enabled, Inputs, EnableEffectRules; DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs:16-20). Plugin inputs are a flat list of repository-relative path strings (NormalizeInputs :244-260, rejects rooted, '.'/'..', option-like first segment). DetectInputs(candidateInputs, ignoredInputs) takes only path lists (:55). RepositoryEvaluationScope record (EvidenceModels.cs:212-220): TrackedFiles (all head paths), IgnoredFiles, InputSetIdentity, SuppressionViolations, InvalidInputs, RetiredIgnoredFiles, MaterializedRoot, MaterializedObjectIds (path->blob oid) -- head-tree only. IRepositoryEvaluationScopeReader.ReadEvaluationScopeAsync(expectedHeadCommit, timeout, ct) (:222-228) takes only the head commit; the reader already knows protectedBaseCommit via its constructor (GitRepositoryIdentityReader.cs:6-9).
- WHERE BASE IS ACTUALLY USED TODAY: only inside GitRepositoryIdentityReader (anti-slop-specific). ReadEvaluationScopeAsync (GitRepositoryIdentityReader.cs:71-176) runs `git ls-tree -r -z <head>` :87, `git diff --name-only -z <base> <head>` :108-111 (changed paths), `git ls-tree -r -z <base>` :119, then ReadAndMaterializeBatchedAsync (:239+) reads head blobs AND base blobs for changed paths via `git cat-file --batch` (:414) and uses base only for suppression-directive comparison (headProfile vs baseProfile). Only head blobs of included JS-family, non-ignored, mode 100644/100755 entries are written to the private temp root (MaterializeFrozenObjectAsync); base blobs are scanned in memory, never materialized. Plugin interface is never handed base bytes or the diff. Consequence: a DRY/diff-aware plugin needs a NEW contract surface (scope reader exposing changed-path set + added/changed hunks and base blobs), because RepositoryEvaluationScope has no changed-files or hunk field.
- FROZEN INPUT MECHANICS (anti-slop only, GateRunner.cs:854-907): scope validation is hard-gated by `plugin is not AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin -> return new(null,null,null,null)` (:861-862); other plugins get NO frozen scope, NO materialization, NO input-set identity from the scope reader. If _repository is not IRepositoryEvaluationScopeReader -> INCOMPLETE :863-865. Steps: ReadEvaluationScopeAsync(head) :868; expected = NormalizeInputs(plugin.DetectInputs(scope.TrackedFiles, scope.IgnoredFiles)) :870-871; requested = NormalizeInputs(request.Plugin.Inputs) :872; InvalidInputs non-empty -> INCOMPLETE :873; expected != requested -> INCOMPLETE :877-880; SuppressionViolations -> INCOMPLETE :881; zero inputs -> NOT_APPLICABLE only if policy AllowedNotApplicable contains gate else INCOMPLETE :885-898. On success the runner replaces request.Invocation.WorkingDirectory with scope.MaterializedRoot :194-198 and disposes (deletes) it via FrozenMaterializationLease :199, :928-931. After execution, GitRepositoryIdentityReader.VerifyMaterializedInputs re-hashes materialized files to expected blob ids :414-428; mismatch -> INCOMPLETE. Docs (docs/quality-gates.md:46) describe: git cat-file --batch-check/--batch, in-memory git object hash verification (VerifyGitBlobIdentity), bytes written to host-owned root outside the repo, read-only, removed after receipt persistence, bounded counts/sizes (MaximumFrozenInputBytes 512 MiB, MaximumFrozenObjectBytes 64 MiB, MaximumBatchPayloadBytes 16 MiB; GitRepositoryIdentityReader.cs:14-19), all phases share one finite gate deadline.
- INPUT-SET IDENTITY: (a) default/legacy: DeterministicEvaluationPluginRegistry.ComputeInputSetIdentity(inputs) = sha256 of UTF-8 of inputs joined by '\n' plus trailing '\n' (DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs:262-266); legacy inputs inferred from Invocation.Arguments ending .csproj/.sln/.slnx/.cs plus 'scope:<Scope>' (:268-282). (b) frozen-tree (anti-slop): scope.InputSetIdentity from GitRepositoryIdentityReader.cs:153-176: sha256 over, for each JS-family tree entry sorted by path: path\0 objectId\0 mode\0 ignoredFlag(1 byte)\0 ... then 'retired-ignore' entries; this is the 'complete tracked-head identity'. The runner overwrites pluginBinding.InputSetIdentity with it (GateRunner.cs:192-193, 443-444). It covers path/blob oid/mode/ignore decision, NOT the base commit or diff.
- CONFIGURATION IDENTITY (anti-slop): AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.ComputeConfigurationIdentityAsync (Adapters/AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs:479-560+) = incremental SHA-256 over bundle files package.json, package-lock.json, oxlint.config.ts, run-oxlint-batches.mjs (+oxlint.effect.config.ts if effect rules), directories vendor/anti-slop, node_modules/oxlint, node_modules/@oxlint/plugins, all @oxlint/binding-* dirs, the entry-point path check (must be node_modules/oxlint/bin/oxlint), batch runner path check, manifest bytes ('inputs/manifest.json') when batched (and manifest inputs must equal frozen inputs), runtime/node-path, runtime/node-bytes, environment/inherit and every environment key/value in ordinal order. Computed in CaptureBeforeAsync (:112-145) and compared to request.ConfigurationIdentity unless that is 'unavailable' (:134-138) -> InvalidOperationException -> INCOMPLETE. Returned from GetConfigurationIdentity only when the snapshot exists, else the literal 'unavailable' (:204-209). Default for other plugins = request.ConfigurationIdentity (DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs:67-69).
- RUNTIME CLOSURE IDENTITY (anti-slop): ComputeRuntimeClosureIdentityAsync (Adapters/AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs:446-477) = SHA-256 over package.json, package-lock.json, oxlint.config.ts, run-oxlint-batches.mjs (+effect config), vendor/anti-slop dir, node_modules/@oxlint/plugins dir, node_modules/oxlint dir, exactly ONE @oxlint/binding-* dir (else throws), and Node executable bytes ('runtime/node-bytes'). Then ValidateReviewedPinsAsync (:400-444) compares file digests to hard-coded constants (ReviewedPackageSha256 etc., :14-22) and the closure to ReviewedLinuxX64GnuRuntimeClosureSha256 / ...EffectRuntimeClosureSha256 (:19-22); mismatch -> InvalidOperationException -> INCOMPLETE. Closure cache ceiling 256 MiB (:9). Computed only when `_enforceReviewedPins && bundleRoot && nodePath` registered, else it falls back to request.ConfigurationIdentity (:123-124). Docs state the same-UID host cannot make the installed closure immutable, so there is NO post-snapshot mutation detection (docs/quality-gates.md:50). Default for other plugins = request.ConfigurationIdentity (DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs:71).
- EXECUTABLE IDENTITY: ToolCapability.ExecutableIdentity (EvidenceModels.cs:62) = 'sha256:<hex>' of the executable file bytes, computed by ToolCapabilityDetector.HashExecutable (CapabilityDetector.cs:44) and re-hashed after probes to detect change (:70-73). Anti-slop returns the snapshot's hash of request.Invocation.FileName (Node) via GetExecutableIdentity (AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs:134-137 capture, :211-216 getter); runner compares it to Capability.ExecutableIdentity (GateRunner.cs:784-791) both pre-execution and post-execution (:375).
- VERSION PINNING / CAPABILITY: ExpectedTool is a string compared ordinally to request.Capability.Tool (GateRunner.cs:747-749; mismatch -> ERROR). ToolCapability is only creatable by the detector via internal FromDetection (EvidenceModels.cs:80-92) so DetectionVerified is true (:77); Capability.Supported false -> INCOMPLETE (:756-760). CapabilityProbe (CapabilityDetector.cs:14-24): Tool, Source (TrustedExplicit|Path|RepositoryToolManifest), ExplicitExecutable, RepositoryRoot, VersionArguments, HelpArguments, RequiredVersionPrefix, DocumentedFormatVersion, Fixture. DetectAsync (:35-83) runs version probe then help probe (each 30s), ParseVersion (JSON schemaVersion/toolVersion when documented format is JSON; else regex :156-179) and VersionMatches(prefix) (:181). RepositoryToolManifest source is always Unsupported (:38-42). Pinning is also done by the plugin itself: anti-slop requires Capability.Version == '1.78.0' and FormatVersion == 'oxlint-json-v1' (AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs:164-168). Registration pins: TrustedGatePluginBinding(GateId, PluginId, PluginVersion, Tool, ExpectedToolVersion?, ExpectedFormatVersion?, ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity?, LegacyV1Authority=false) (contracts/DeliveryForge.Contracts/Validation/TrustedGatePluginRegistry.cs:6-14). Registry asserts plugin Id/Version/ExpectedTool equal the binding (DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs:225-242). LegacyV1CapabilityMatches only compares Tool and ExpectedFormatVersion (GateRunner.cs:971-978), not version.
- REGISTRATION: Registry constructor requires non-blank Id, Version, ExpectedTool and unique Id else EvaluationPluginException (DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs:113-120). External plugins register via TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistration(GateId, Plugin, ExpectedToolVersion?, ExpectedFormatVersion?, ExpectedRuntimeClosureIdentity?) -> TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistry (TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistry.cs:6-47). Plugins other than the built-ins must use CompatibilityAdapter = EvidenceAdapterKind.External (as anti-slop does, AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs:80) and be explicitly selected via EvaluationPluginSelection (Resolve :154). Selection of inputs happens via DeterministicEvaluationPluginRegistry.SelectApplicable(configurations, candidateInputs, ignoredInputs) (:164-200) which throws if DetectInputs returns a path outside the candidate set or in ignored set (:189-191). Policy can select registered ids but cannot register (docs/quality-gates.md:20,52).
- OUTCOME MAPPING by the runner (GateRunner.cs:371-412, precedence top to bottom, first match wins; GateOutcome enum EvidenceModels.cs:13-20: Pass, Fail, Incomplete, Error, NotApplicable): identity read failed -> INCOMPLETE; sourceChanged (base/head/tree/clean differs before vs after) -> ERROR; capability binding failure post-run -> that failure (ERROR or INCOMPLETE); plugin.ValidateAfterExecution non-null -> that; !OwnedProcessQuiescent -> INCOMPLETE; captureBoundExceeded (stream limit 1 MiB for anti-slop, 64 MiB others; GateRunner.cs:16-17) -> INCOMPLETE; TimedOut -> INCOMPLETE; Cancelled -> INCOMPLETE; ExitCode null -> INCOMPLETE; plugin PASS but ExitCode != 0 -> ERROR; plugin PASS but (!Policy.AuthorityVerified || gate not in Policy.RequiredGates || Capability.Fixture || !HasTrustedLegacyV1Authority || !normalized.ProductionCapable) -> downgraded to INCOMPLETE ('advisory') :396-403; NOT_APPLICABLE without rationale/policy allowance/etc -> INCOMPLETE :404-412; frozen-input re-hash failure -> INCOMPLETE :414-427; PublishAttemptDirectory failure downgrades PASS/N/A to INCOMPLETE :445-446. FAIL from the plugin is passed through unchanged (no downgrade rule applies to Fail). The un-downgraded plugin verdict is retained as Plugin.PluginRawOutcome (EvaluationPluginReceiptMetadata EvaluationPluginReceiptMetadata :27-36; set at GateRunner.cs:441). Plugin cannot produce a final authoritative PASS unless built-in legacy-v1 authority holds (HasTrustedLegacyV1Authority GateRunner.cs:955-969: requires _usesBuiltInExecutionRegistry, ReferenceEquals authority registry == TrustedGatePluginRegistry.BuiltIns, and binding.LegacyV1Authority). Docs: externally registered plugins and caller-injected registries cannot mint legacy-v1 authority (docs/quality-gates.md:64); future-v2 authority is disabled (:66); so any new DRY plugin's PASS will currently surface as INCOMPLETE final outcome with raw outcome PASS, while FAIL surfaces as FAIL.
- HOW ANTI-SLOP NORMALIZES (Adapters/AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs:238-259 and NormalizeReport :261-... ): zero inputs / unsupported input -> INCOMPLETE; unparsable JSON or unsupported shape -> INCOMPLETE; number_of_files != analyzed inputs or number_of_rules != allowed rule count -> INCOMPLETE; exit code not 0/1 -> INCOMPLETE; any parser/tool diagnostic, finding outside frozen inputs, or unexpected rule id -> INCOMPLETE; findings > 0 -> FAIL (ProductionCapable true); clean and exit 0 -> PASS (ProductionCapable=false unless exact trusted policy binding exists, :246-249); clean but exit != 0 -> INCOMPLETE. Design principle: PASS requires positive proof of completeness (counts reconcile), FAIL requires a concrete finding, everything else is INCOMPLETE.
- NormalizedEvidence record (EvidenceModels.cs:22-38): (GateOutcome Outcome, string Reason, bool Fixture, bool ProductionCapable, IReadOnlyList<string> Limitations, string? NotApplicableRationale=null). Factory helpers only for Pass(reason, fixture) (sets ProductionCapable=false with limitation 'Tool outcome is not production authority.'), Incomplete(...), Error(...). Fail and NotApplicable must be built with the constructor.
- AppendBoundArtifacts: contract is `void AppendBoundArtifacts(request, snapshot, gateDirectory, IDictionary<string,string> artifactHashes)` (DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs:90-94), called after stdout.txt/stderr.txt are written (GateRunner.cs:323-331). Implementer writes files into gateDirectory and records name -> 'sha256:<hex>' in artifactHashes. Anti-slop's implementation is an EMPTY body (AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs:279-285); anti-slop's sidecar is written by the runner itself: AppendAntiSlopBindingArtifact (GateRunner.cs:582-637) writes 'anti-slop-binding.json' (name const GateRunner.cs:15) with schemaVersion 1.0.0, kind 'anti-slop-evaluation-binding', gateId, policyIdentity, finalOutcome, ProductionCapable, authority ('production-capable'|'advisory'), repository{baseCommit, headCommit, treeId}, plugin{id, version, enabled, status, configurationIdentity, runtimeClosureIdentity, inputSetIdentity, inputs, pluginRawOutcome, effectRulesEnabled}, tool{name, version, formatVersion, executableIdentity}, logicalWorkingDirectory '<host-owned-frozen-inputs>', scope; the call is hard-keyed by plugin.Id == 'dmmulroy.anti-slop' (GateRunner.cs:499-502) so a new plugin gets NO equivalent sidecar unless GateRunner is extended or the plugin writes its own via AppendBoundArtifacts (exceptions other than IOException/UnauthorizedAccessException from AppendBoundArtifacts are not caught there and would propagate). Files are written with FileMode.CreateNew; artifactHashes entries land in the receipt's artifact map (GateRunResult.ArtifactHashes, EvidenceModels.cs:169). The sealed legacy adapters' implementation delegates to EvidenceAdapterDispatcher.AppendBoundArtifacts (DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs:314-324).
- RECEIPT SHAPE: GateRunResult (EvidenceModels.cs:154-175) = Outcome, Reason, Fixture, ProductionCapable, Limitations, SourceChanged, Invocation, Execution, Before, After (RepositoryIdentity), PolicyIdentity, CapabilityIdentity, ConfigurationIdentity, Scope, ArtifactHashes, ReceiptPath, NotApplicableRationale, plus init props Plugin (EvaluationPluginReceiptMetadata: Id, Version, Enabled, Status Selected|Disabled|Executed|NotApplicable, ConfigurationIdentity, RuntimeClosureIdentity, Inputs, InputSetIdentity, PluginRawOutcome) and TrustedReceiptProvenance. Canonical v1 receipt does not include plugin metadata in its command envelope (docs/quality-gates.md:64); BuildLegacyV1CommandEnvelope (GateRunner.cs:556-580) hashes FileName, Arguments, WorkingDirectory, Environment, Scope, ExecutableIdentity, Capability tool/version/format/executable/fixture/probe dir, PolicySourceRevision, PolicyContentIdentity, ConfigurationIdentity. The v2 envelope BuildFutureV2CommandEnvelope (:639-675) adds plugin id/version/enabled/status/configIdentity/runtimeClosureIdentity/inputs/inputSetIdentity/pluginRawOutcome. Receipts are append-only evidence, not authority (docs/quality-gates.md:62,64).
- CAPABILITY OF EXISTING COMPOSITION TO DRIVE A NEW PLUGIN: AdvisoryAntiSlopComposition.RunAsync (AdvisoryAntiSlopComposition.cs:14-52, core :88-140) builds ProcessCommandExecutor, GitRepositoryIdentityReader(executor, repoRoot, protectedBaseCommit) (:29), registry via CreateBuiltIns(bundleRoot, nodePath) (:30), a ToolCapability via ToolCapability.FromDetection(...) hand-built from the Node path hash (:31-41, note: bypasses ToolCapabilityDetector), a GateRunner, then SelectApplicable -> AntiSlopRequestFactory.Create -> runner.RunAsync per selection; disabled selection and zero-input selection do not execute and fabricate no receipts (:107-117). The policy is synthesized as advisory (CreateAdvisoryPolicy :142-165, PolicyAuthorityKind.ProtectedGitBase). CreateBuiltIns is hard-wired to the four built-ins and the anti-slop bundle (DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs:125-143); a new plugin needs either an added built-in line + authority binding in TrustedGatePluginRegistry.BuiltIns (AssertMatchesAuthorityRegistry :141 would otherwise throw drift) or a TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistry (TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistry.cs:16-47), the latter never legacy-v1-authoritative.
- DETERMINISM / CLOSURE BREAKS FOR A NETWORK MODEL API TOOL (derived from the code above): (1) GateRunner requires an absolute on-disk ExecutablePath whose sha256 equals Capability.ExecutableIdentity, and Invocation.FileName equals it (GateRunner.cs:766-805); an API has no executable bytes, so the plugin must be a local launcher/client binary or the runner must be extended with a non-executable tool kind. The launcher hash would pin only the client, NOT the remote model weights/version. (2) RuntimeClosureIdentity is by default = ConfigurationIdentity (DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs:71) and for anti-slop is a byte hash of an installed file closure; for an API model there is no byte closure: the best available identities are model id string, pinned dated snapshot/version, prompt template bytes, rubric bytes, sampling params (temperature, seed), endpoint host; none is content-verifiable by the receipt consumer, and providers may silently change served weights or alias targets. (3) Sealing: the Environment must be a cleared allow-list for anti-slop (AllowedEnvironment SystemRoot/WINDIR/TMP/TEMP, AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs:33-35,171-175); API credentials need an env var or stdin, so the plugin must explicitly allow-list that single secret and exclude it from the receipt (ConfigurationIdentity hashes every env key/value at AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs:~556-560 -> would leak or make the identity secret-dependent unless excluded). Also Invocation (including Arguments and StandardInput) is written verbatim into the receipt (BuildLegacyV1CommandEnvelope GateRunner.cs:556-580), so prompts with code/secrets would be embedded or must be hashed. (4) Network egress: the process executor (ProcessCommandExecutor) provides tree-kill/timeout/bounded capture but nothing in the contract sandboxes or attests network access; a network call breaks hermetic/replayable execution and TimedOut/transport errors must map to INCOMPLETE (GateRunner.cs:381-388) never PASS or FAIL. (5) Non-determinism: LLM/decision-model outputs are only reproducible if the plugin forces determinism (temp 0, seed, version pin) and still cannot prove it; two runs on identical frozen inputs may differ, violating the 'same inputs => same outcome' property the receipt implies. Only a plugin that stores the raw request/response as bound artifacts (AppendBoundArtifacts, hashes into artifactHashes) gives replay evidence, and the runner reads only stdout/stderr (1 MiB cap for anti-slop at GateRunner.cs:16 vs 64 MiB for others :17) so a large model transcript must be written via AppendBoundArtifacts. (6) Frozen-input materialization and ValidateAutomaticScopeAsync are anti-slop-only (GateRunner.cs:861-862); a new plugin gets no frozen blob tree unless that branch is generalized (e.g. introduce an interface on the plugin instead of `is AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin`; the same type check recurs at :115, :260, :273, :285, :296, :381, :499, :521, :565, :645). (7) Receipts carry ProductionCapable=false for non-built-in plugins, so PASS from a model plugin is always reported advisory/INCOMPLETE under current code (GateRunner.cs:396-403).
- REQUIRED-GATE / AUTHORITY BINDING: if Policy.AuthorityVerified and gate in Policy.RequiredGates, a trusted binding must exist whose PluginId/Version/Tool equal the selected plugin and Capability.Tool, otherwise INCOMPLETE/ERROR (GateRunner.cs:807-827). Protected policy source revision must equal frozen base commit and repo root (GateRunner.cs:159-170). Gate claim: per-gate lease file prevents concurrent runs; each execution gets a fresh '<gateId>.attempt-<guid>' directory (GateRunner.cs:88-93; docs/quality-gates.md:70).
- COMPOSITE FILES FOR HELPERS A DESIGNER MAY REUSE: JavaScriptSuppressionScanner.cs (suppression-directive scanner used only by anti-slop scope), AntiSlopManifestPolicy.cs/AntiSlopRequestFactory.cs (canonical request + content-addressed manifest creation, Adapters/AntiSlopRequestFactory.cs:10-60+), BoundedCaptureStream/BoundedProcessCapture (bounded process IO), GateClaimLease.cs, ProtectedGitPolicyResolver.cs (reads protected-base files, e.g. .delivery-forge/anti-slop-ignore via `git show <base>:<path>`, GitRepositoryIdentityReader.cs:646), TrustedEvaluationPluginRegistry.cs. The documented minimal stub plugin is StubEvaluationPlugin in DeterministicEvaluationPluginTests (docs/quality-gates.md:54).

### Caveats

- Skimmed AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs lines 1-260 plus closure/config functions (400-560); did not read the remaining ~315 lines (AntiSlopManifestPolicy ceilings, ClosureReadCache, AppendClosureDirectoryAsync detail) nor GateRunner persistence helpers (PersistAsync internals, PublishAttemptDirectory) beyond signatures.
- Environment hashing in ComputeConfigurationIdentityAsync is cited at roughly :556-560 (viewed lines 520-560, truncated at the foreach over Environment); exact end-of-function line not read.
- GateRunner.cs line numbers cited from two sed views; the 'LegacyV1CapabilityMatches' tail at :971-978 and the artifact-binding block at :331 were read directly. Line ranges for the ownedExecutor branches are :285-312.
- Statement that externally registered plugin PASS surfaces as final INCOMPLETE is derived from the code chain at GateRunner.cs:396-403 plus HasTrustedLegacyV1Authority (:955-969); not verified by running tests.
- Did not read EvidencePolicy.cs/ResolvedEvidencePolicy, TrustedGatePluginRegistry.BuiltIns contents (which gate ids are bound), or any test files; the claim that anti-slop has no legacy-v1 authority comes from docs/quality-gates.md:64.
- Item 'network model API' analysis is inference from the contract, not existing code; no model-API plugin exists in the repo.
- Docs line 20 says plugin config yields INCOMPLETE if a required gate is disabled; verified in code at GateRunner.cs:150-157.

## C.clone-tools

### Facts

- JSCPD 4.2.5 (the pinned TS line) JSON: reporter writes {statistics, duplicates[]} to <output>/jscpd-report.json. Each duplicates entry: format, lines, tokens, fragment, firstFile{name,start,end,startLoc{line,column},endLoc,blame}, secondFile{same}. Source: unpkg @jscpd/finder@4.2.5 dist/index.js (JsonReporter.generateJson/cloneFound).
- CRITICAL jscpd 4.2.5 caveat: JSON 'fragment' is ONE string, taken from duplicationA only. FragmentsHook sets fragment on both duplicationA and duplicationB internally (substring of file by range), but JsonReporter emits only duplicationA.fragment. The B-side text must be re-read from disk by name + line range (startLoc/endLoc) - trivial since both trees are checked out.
- jscpd 4.2.5 IClone (unpkg @jscpd/core@4.2.5): format, isNew?, foundDate?, duplicationA/B {sourceId, start, end (ITokenLocation), range [charStart,charEnd], fragment?, blame?}. isNew is only used for console '*' marker; nothing in finder sets it, so no built-in baseline/new-clone logic in 4.2.5.
- jscpd 4.x CLI options (unpkg jscpd@4.2.5 README): --pattern/-p (default **/*), --mode/-m strict|mild|weak (default mild), --threshold/-t (percentage, exit nonzero if exceeded; repo-wide not per-patch), --blame/-b (git author/date), --ignore/-i globs, --min-lines/-l (5), --min-tokens/-k (50), --store (leveldb optional), --store-path, --reporters (console,json,xml,csv,markdown,html,sarif,ai,...), --skipLocal (only report clones across different folders; needs >1 path), --formats-exts, --gitignore. No tree-vs-tree compare, no baseline in 4.x README. Statistics block has newClones/newDuplicatedLines fields but README does not document how they are computed.
- jscpd detection is token-based (Rabin-Karp over tokens from Prism tokenizers); --mode controls which token classes are skipped (strict keeps almost everything; mild skips newlines/empty/comments-ish; weak also skips more). Effective type-1 and type-2-lite (whitespace/comment); identifier-renamed type-2 is NOT normalized in 4.x modes (identifiers kept). Type-3 only via small gaps not supported. [inference from docs: modes are token-class skips]
- jscpd v5.x (github.com/kucherenko/jscpd main page now describes a Rust rewrite, MIT, 220+/224 formats): adds --baseline <file> + --update-baseline, --fail-on-new-clones[=N], --baseline-from-ref <git ref> (checks out base ref tree into temp detached worktree, scans with same options, treats clones absent from base as new; needs 2 scans; fails on shallow clones missing ref), --compare (pair functions of two folders by semantic similarity, experimental), --similarity (function-level, C# listed), --semantic (C# listed), --skip-local, --follow-symlinks, --ignore-identifiers/--ignore-literals/--ignore-annotations, --blame with console-full. This is exactly the per-patch gate primitive, but is a major-version jump from pinned 4.2.5 and a different implementation (the README in main shows 5.3.0 in BibTeX; release notes 5.1.0 first mention baseline). JSON report in v5: mentions kind/similarity/method fields; sample not verified.
- PMD CPD (PMD 7.x docs): C# supported along with Java, C/C++, Go, Kotlin, Ruby, Swift, etc. Report formats: text, xml, csv, csv_with_linecount_per_file, vs, markdown. Exit codes: 0 none, 4 duplications found (unless --no-fail-on-violation), 5 recoverable error, 2 usage, 1 exception. C#-specific option: --ignore-usings. --ignore-literals/--ignore-identifiers are NOT listed for C# (listed for Java and C++) so C# detection is effectively type-1 plus whitespace/comments. --minimum-tokens required. No diff-scope or baseline; file selection via PMD shared --dir/--file-list style options (file-list not confirmed on CPD page). License: BSD-style (PMD is BSD; license page not fetched - unverified in this run). Token-based (Karp-Rabin).
- JetBrains dupFinder (ReSharper Command Line Tools, also .NET tool run via 'jb dupfinder'; InspectCode is separate): free cross-platform, C# and VB.NET only. Options --discard-cost, --discard-fields/--discard-literals/--discard-local-vars/--discard-types (when true, fragments differing only in those are not reported - i.e. type-2 style structural matching on the R# PSI tree), --exclude, --show-text (embeds fragment text), --normalize-types, --debug, --output. XML output: Statistics (CodeBaseCost, TotalFragmentsCost, TotalDuplicatesCost), Duplicates/Duplicate@Cost with Fragment elements holding file, offset range, line range, and Text if --show-text. Closest to a true C#-aware deterministic detector (Roslyn-free, R# PSI), gives a cost score for ranking. Licensing terms not stated on the page. No diff/baseline mode; you filter fragments by changed line ranges yourself. 2021.2 docs; TeamCity 2026.1 still ships Duplicates Finder runner (so tool still maintained).
- SonarQube/SonarCloud: duplication = >=100 successive duplicated tokens spread over >=10 lines (non-Java; Java is >=10 statements); indentation and string literals ignored (type-1/light type-2). Metrics: new_duplicated_lines, new_duplicated_lines_density, new_duplicated_blocks. Detection runs over the WHOLE codebase and then the ones touching new code are reported as new-code duplications (Sonar staff, community.sonarsource.com thread 6947/76576). Conditions on duplication are not computed when <20 lines of new code in SonarCloud (can be disabled in SonarQube). Reported C# false positives on repetitive attribute/using boilerplate in controllers. Needs a server and scanner (not pin-and-run-offline friendly; LGPL community edition + proprietary tiers).
- Simian (Similarity Analyser): proprietary/commercial (FreeBSD port license 'SIMIAN', JPackage 'Proprietary'), line-based, C# among supported languages, last packaged version 2.5.10 (FreeBSD ports, 2024-12-31). Line-based hashing type-1/lightly type-2 (ignore options). Not recommended: closed, licensing friction, no diff scope.
- NiCad (Cordy/Roy, Queen's University): TXL-based, supports C, C#, Java, Python, WSDL at function/block granularity; detects type-1, type-2 (blind renaming), type-3 near-miss with configurable threshold (e.g. 0.3 dissimilarity) via pretty-printing, normalization and LCS comparison. Needs the TXL runtime; license on official page not confirmed in this run (research/academic - verify). Strong for type-3 but heavy toolchain, slow on 150k LOC compared to token hashing, no diff mode (you would run on base+head trees and diff clone classes).
- CCFinderSW (Osaka University): token-based, ANTLR grammar-driven multilingual tokenizer (grammars-v4, 150+ grammars); detects type-1 and type-2 only, NOT type-3. C# not explicitly listed in the retrieved sources (plausible via ANTLR C# grammar). License not found in this run.
- Deckard (UC Davis, ICSE 2007): tree vectors + LSH, type-2/3-ish; shipped generators for C, Java, PHP; no C# generator found; SUTD/Deckard fork last release v1.2.1 (2011), copyright to 2018. Effectively unmaintained; reject.
- Roslyn analyzers on NuGet: no credible general-purpose clone detector found. NuGet search surfaced 'Detects Duplicate Code Shapes' (ajbarga 2.0.0, collamore 1.6.4/1.9.1) and 'Detects Duplicate Code' (bcoll, 2022) with no docs retrieved; XPathDuplicateAnalyzer and MBSD.Analysers.RedundantCode are narrow (duplicate XPath / redundant constructs). Verdict: write own Roslyn syntax-tree normalizer if C#-aware hashing wanted (hash method bodies after identifier/literal normalization) - none off-the-shelf validated.
- Tree-sitter hashing tools: fossil-mcp (Rust, Apache-2.0 OR MIT, v0.1.7 on crates.io; C# among ~15 languages; Merkle/bottom-up subtree hashing: type-1 exact, type-2 normalized identifiers/literals, type-3 structural similarity default threshold 0.8; output text/JSON/SARIF; CLI or MCP server). treepeat (Apache-2.0, pip CLI, LSH over tree-sitter AST, rulesets none/default/loose, console+SARIF, self-described proof of concept, languages python/js/ts/go/rust/java/kotlin/php/etc - NO C#). Both young; neither has diff-scope mode; both are self-built-ish building blocks. Tree-sitter C# grammar exists, so a bespoke ~300 LOC method-level Merkle hasher is feasible in .NET (engram-csharp already has tree-sitter extractors).
- Embedding-based local clone detectors: 'Jonathangadeaharder/semantic-clone-detection' (GitHub, GraphCodeBERT embeddings + FAISS ANN + ONNX INT8, tree-sitter multi-language incl. C#; type-4; page not fetchable this run - claims unverified, license/maturity unknown). Literature: UniXcoder/GraphCodeBERT/CodeBERT dominate; arXiv 2506.10995 (small code models for clone detection), 2510.15480 (selecting/combining LLMs for scalable clone detection), 2510.04143 (semantic clones of unseen functionality). Key warning: a UniXcoder tuned on BigCloneBench gets 0.940 F1 same-language but 0.269 F1 cross-language (CLCDSA) - benchmark generalization is poor, so precision on a private C# repo must be self-validated; embeddings are for candidate recall only, not final fail.
- Diff-scoping approach that works with ANY pair-reporting token detector (jscpd 4.2.5, PMD CPD, dupFinder): run over base+head tree (or head tree) once, then keep only clone pairs where at least one side's line range overlaps an added/changed hunk of the patch (from git diff -U0), and (for ratcheting) drop pairs whose identical pair existed in a base-tree run. No tool in this set natively does 'changed-hunks vs whole tree' except Sonar (whole-codebase detect then filter to new code) and jscpd v5 (--baseline-from-ref).
- Speed on ~150k LOC (no authoritative benchmark retrieved; engram-csharp already runs jscpd 4.2.5 weekly over src/ in GitHub Actions - treat as minutes-scale at worst; token hashing tools (jscpd/CPD/dupFinder) are linear-ish; NiCad/Deckard heavier). Needs measurement: unverified.

### Caveats

- Several pages could not be fetched (kucherenko/jscpd 4.x README on GitHub 404, PMD license page, NiCad official page, semantic-clone-detection repo, jscpd v5.1.0 release notes timed out); a few facts come from search summaries rather than primary pages and are flagged as unverified.
- The jscpd repo main page now documents a v5 (apparently Rust) rewrite; baseline/compare/--similarity features are v5-only and NOT in pinned 4.2.5. Upgrading changes behavior and token/clone counts; engram-csharp DryUpScan parser targets 4.x JSON shape (statistics + duplicates + firstFile/secondFile).
- jscpd 4.2.5 JSON emits fragment text for side A only; side B needs a re-read from disk. Whether the 4.2.5 CLI always registers FragmentsHook for the json reporter was not directly confirmed, though the JsonReporter output code reads duplicationA.fragment.
- jscpd 4.x identifier-renamed (type-2) detection is not confirmed; modes only change which token classes are skipped. PMD CPD for C# lacks --ignore-identifiers/--ignore-literals per the 7.25.0 docs, so type-2 on C# is not available there.
- Licenses not verified for PMD (believed BSD-style), NiCad/TXL, CCFinderSW, dupFinder (JetBrains EULA), and semantic-clone-detection repo. Speed numbers for 150k LOC were not found in any source.
- No evidence any listed tool detects type-4 (semantic) clones deterministically; only embedding/LLM approaches target type-4, with documented poor cross-domain generalization.
- WebSearch summaries were used for several claims (Simian license, Deckard activity, CCFinderSW, NiCad languages); treat as secondary sources.

## C.engram

### Facts

- Schemas loaded via ToolSearch for all 12 engram tools. Only input params and tool descriptions were available; no output schemas were published, so return shapes below come from descriptions plus the one probe.
- search_code: params query (req), repo (optional; omit = corpus-wide), max_results (default tier budget by repo file count: Tiny 4, Small 6, Medium 10, Large 12, XL 15; corpus-wide 10; 0 = max 75), component, domain, kind (function/class/method/constant/type/interface/route), language, path_glob, modified_since (requires DB indexer). Hybrid structural + semantic. Repo-scoped by default; corpus-wide needs DB vector indexer. Rows carry repo field.
- search_code probe: query 'store commit files for repository', repo Nexus. Returned 6 rows, all Nexus. Row fields: repo, symbolId (chunk_<n> for non-symbol chunks), filePath, score (float, observed 0.31 to 0.40), source ('vector'), chunkText, symbolResult (null on all rows), matchedTokens (string array of query tokens hit), truncated (bool). Observed chunkText ran about 1000+ chars with truncated=true on long chunks, not the ~400 chars the description states. Top hits were README.md, tools/Inject-PluginHistory.ps1, Clear-BuildOutput.ps1, nuget.config, .gitignore, .gitattributes: no true match for the query. Scores appear to be weak signal here.
- search_code: no parameter accepts raw code text to embed; query is a string only. No embedding field appeared in the probe row. Whether raw embeddings are exposed anywhere else is not stated by any schema.
- search_symbols: params query (req), repo (optional), max_results (same tier defaults; corpus-wide fixed 20), kind and file_pattern (repo-scoped only; error if repo omitted). Each row has repo and source discriminator 'weighted' or 'fts'; the two score scales are not comparable. Repo-scoped floors: absolute score >= 15 and relative >= 0.4 x top score.
- search_text: params query (literal substring, LIKE wildcards escaped), repo optional, max_results (corpus default 50). Corpus-wide query needs at least 3 literal characters and DB indexer.
- get_symbol(repo, symbol_id) and get_symbols(repo, symbol_ids as comma-separated string): return source body fetched on demand from VCS REST API. get_symbols is best-effort and returns { symbols, missingSymbolIds }.
- get_file_outline(repo, file_path): repo-relative or indexed $/ form. get_symbol source comes from VCS, so no server checkout is needed.
- change_impact(repo, changed_files, view): changed_files is comma or newline separated repo-relative paths, capped at 1000 with explicit truncation flag. view omitted = 'impact'; view='review' = bounded grounding pack (callers, prior reviews, linked work items, contract crossings, convention exemplars). Operates on indexed (default-branch) data.
- find_related_code(repo, filePath, maxHops 1-5, as_of optional): co-commit and work-item relationships. Historical as_of is hop-1 co-commit only. Empty result for files older than historyFloor is a window boundary, not proof of no relation.
- structural_impact(repo, symbol_id, max_depth 1-5 default 3, include_reachable_tests default false, max_response_chars default 40000): upstream callers and riskScore. Errors if symbol_id not in index.
- detect_communities(repo): runs Louvain clustering on call graph, persists results, advances generation. Takes only repo.
- get_artifact(repo, type, key): types module_briefing, symbol_context, scope_pack, contract_map (keys like HTTP, Nexus), architecture_map (key 'directories'), unreferenced_symbol_report (key 'symbols'), clone_report (key 'all'), semantic_drift_report (key 'latest'), semantic_anomaly_report (key 'all'). Zero-LLM, precompiled. list_artifacts(repo, type optional) returns per-key version hashes.
- Repo scoping: most search and read tools take repo (name). Corpus-wide variants exist only for search_code, search_symbols, search_text, and only with DB-backed indexer. Engram indexes default branch, so an unmerged PR head is not searchable.

### Caveats

- Return schemas were not published for most tools; only descriptions were available. Claims about returned fields other than the probe are from descriptions, not verified.
- Not verified: whether any tool returns raw embedding vectors (probe row had none, but absence in one row is weak evidence). Not verified: similarity score scale per source beyond observed 0.28-0.42 range on one query.
- Probe result quality is poor for the query (no direct store/commit match in Nexus top 6), so search_code ranking quality is unproven from this single probe.
- Scope note: beyond the one requested probe, I also ran one total-recall memory_search, per standing user rule to search memories at conversation start. Its hits (Engram pipeline, HOTBrain-v2 gate decisions) were not relevant to the schema question.
- Did not read the PR 52 files (DeterministicEvaluationPlugins.cs, EvidenceModels.cs, quality-gates.md, AntiSlopEvaluationPlugin.cs, AdvisoryAntiSlopComposition.cs) or the engram-csharp #238 issue; these were in the computed task but not in this step's assignment of schema and probe only.

### Sources

- ToolSearch select of mcp__engram__search_code, search_symbols, search_text, get_symbol, get_symbols, change_impact, find_related_code, get_file_outline, structural_impact, detect_communities, get_artifact, list_artifacts (schemas and descriptions)
- mcp__engram__search_code probe: query 'store commit files for repository', repo 'Nexus', 6 rows returned
- mcp__total-recall__memory_search (context check, 4 hits, not used for facts above)

## C.jev

### Facts

- JEV BASICS: Jev = TypeSafe AI's first 'System One' decision-only model (typed outputs, never prose). Early access since 2026-09-15 (one source says 09-14), waitlist/console key. Company: SF lab, founders Diogo Almeida (RLHF co-inventor), Erik Gafni, Sasha Sheng; $40M seed led by DCVC (secondary: nocode.mba/The Register). https://typesafe.ai/ ; https://www.infoq.com/news/2026/10/typesafe-ai-jev-released/ ; https://www.rezolve.ai/blog/what-is-jev-typesafe-ai-s-decision-model
- API (primary docs): POST https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone, header Authorization: Bearer <key> (key from https://console.typesafe.ai/), JSON body {state, model, questions}. 'state' = text OR JSON (concepts/state.md). questions = object keyed by name; each has type (choice|score|noul), instructions (string), criteria (choice: object label->description; score: ordered array of rubric level descriptions; noul: none). Multiple, mixed-type questions per request; each evaluated in parallel and isolated against same state (adding questions barely changes latency). https://docs.typesafe.ai/introduction/quickstart.md ; https://docs.typesafe.ai/introduction.md
- ALSO a 4th primitive 'Box' (image bounding box) exists; images supported in Jev API per docs index (docs list Box/Images pages) but models.md says Jev 1.13/1.14 input is Text only (docs inconsistency, unverified). https://docs.typesafe.ai/models.md
- RESPONSE SHAPES (quickstart example, values 'can change between releases'): response = {model:'jev-1.13.0' (resolved version), answers:{name:{...}}, usage:{input_tokens, output_tokens}}. Choice -> {type:'choice', choice:'technical', confidence:1.0, probabilities:{label:p,...}}. Score -> {type:'score', score:0.67 (normalized expectation over levels), confidence:0.5, legend:{'0':..,'1':..}, probabilities:{'0':0.33,'1':0.67,'2':0.0}}. Noul -> {type:'noul', noul:0.99} (single 0..1 probability, no confidence field). https://docs.typesafe.ai/introduction/quickstart.md ; https://docs.typesafe.ai/introduction.md
- CALIBRATION: trained with 'Reinforcement Learning for Calibrated Decisions' (RLCD); docs push confidence-gated routing (act above threshold, escalate below) https://docs.typesafe.ai/patterns/confidence-routing.md. Independent calibration evidence is thin (secondary commentary); Armin Ronacher (via InfoQ): design 'delegates the hallucination problem' to the user to pick a threshold. Marketing 'zero hallucinations' claim is unverified; InfoQ/HN: cannot emit invalid type but can emit wrong valid value.
- PRICING: $0.042 per 1M input tokens ($42/Btok), output tokens free; long-context costs same per token; no separate pricing page (docs/models.md is authoritative). https://docs.typesafe.ai/models.md ; https://docs.litellm.ai/blog/typesafe_jev. Typical per-call cost for a few hundred lines of code (~5-10k tokens) is ~$0.0002-0.0004 (my arithmetic, not vendor-stated). Whether pricing is subsidized/temporary: TypeSafe FAQ question present, answer not retrieved (unverified).
- LATENCY: vendor quotes 70-500 ms end to end; vendor homepage example 0.114 s vs 8.566 s for LLM ($0.000081 vs $0.01388). Third party AIML API measured 140 ms server-side, 0.84 s end-to-end (secondary, via web search summary https://aimlapi.com/blog/what-is-jev). OpenChamber tweet analysis: median 76 ms, p75 270 ms (via InfoQ). Vendor headline 193.6x faster / 444.6x cheaper is vendor-run on workflows built by TypeSafe's own team (Towards AI critique: reference probabilities are average of two frontier models).
- CONTEXT: Jev 1.14 (jev-1.14.0, released 2026-10-14) and 1.13 (jev-1.13.0): 64k tokens per request, 32k per question (state + one question), text-only. Header 'TypeSafe-Danger-Zone-Long-Context: true' raises to 262k but slower and less accurate. No cap on question count within limits. Body limit 32 MiB, UTF-8 JSON, max nesting 64, gzip/zstd. Errors: 400 max_tokens_exceeded, 401, 413, 415, 422, 429, 529. Rate limits per org (tokens+requests/sec), values only in console. https://docs.typesafe.ai/models.md ; https://docs.typesafe.ai/api/errors.md. So a few hundred lines of code plus candidate matches (~10-20k tokens) FITS, but accuracy degrades with large noisy state.
- VERSIONING/PINNING: aliases jev-latest and jev-preview currently -> jev-1.14.0 and move on new releases; versioned IDs (jev-1.13.0) accepted and recommended when confidence thresholds are tuned; response reports resolved model. No deprecation/retirement policy documented (unverified how long old versions stay served). Same weights for all accounts, no fine-tuning on customer data; customer data not used for training; English best, CJK weaker. https://docs.typesafe.ai/models.md
- DETERMINISM: NOT documented. No temperature/seed parameter in API docs. Self-consistency cookbook (https://docs.typesafe.ai/cookbooks/consistency_noul_cookbook.md) reports low run-to-run variation (mean per-question prob std 0.0102, 'covered' answer ranging 0.43-0.53) but every request included a changing uid so identical-request determinism is NOT established; notes LLMs vary even at temp 0. Docs say it leans toward the first choice option (position bias; reorder options to test consistency). => treat as non-deterministic-but-stable; delivery-forge must record model id + inputs + raw response in the receipt and quantize/threshold with margin, not rely on replay equality.
- KNOWN WEAKNESSES (vendor's own jaggedness page, jev-1.13): accuracy falls as unrelated content grows (distractors) -> filter in code first; cannot count reliably (lines, occurrences); not a calculator; bad at comparing numerics/dates; literal reading; multi-hop/double negatives hurt; adversarial/embedded instructions in state can steer answers (relevant: candidate patch code/comments are untrusted input, prompt-injection surface); contradictory criteria confuse; first-option bias; not for generation (extract candidates with regex/generative model and let it pick). https://docs.typesafe.ai/model-jaggedness/jev-1.13.md. Directly relevant design implication: feed pre-selected candidate clone pairs (from deterministic tool/Engram) and ask per-pair Noul/Choice questions, rather than dumping whole diff.
- SDKs: official Python (pip typesafe-sdk, 3.10+) and JavaScript/TypeScript (@typesafe-ai/sdk, Node 20+); curl/HTTP from any language. NO official .NET/C# SDK found. OpenAPI spec at https://api.typesafe.ai/openapi.json (can generate C# client via NSwag/Kiota; unverified generation quality). https://docs.typesafe.ai/sdk.md ; https://docs.typesafe.ai/introduction/quickstart.md. Gateways: LiteLLM pass-through (POST /typesafe/v1/systemone, since v1.103.0-rc) https://docs.litellm.ai/blog/typesafe_jev ; Vercel AI Gateway, Netlify, LangChain TypeSafeClassifier (via InfoQ). A Python 'System One adapter' lets other models run the same schema for benchmarking (via InfoQ). Five Elixir clients appeared (InfoQ).
- BENCHMARKS/CRITICISM: Independent paper Rao & Callison-Burch, 'Decision Models vs. LLMs as Rubric Judges: Cheaper, Faster, and Wrong in the Same Places', arXiv 2609.29769 (abstract page read): compares Jev and Perplexity's Decider vs three flash-tier LLM judges on 9 panels/7 benchmarks; LLM judges cost 16-357x more and 5-350x slower; each decision model significantly trailed LLM judges in at most 6 of 27 comparisons; >85% of flash-LLM verdicts on a decision model's most confident errors repeat the error (errors correlated, so LLM-as-fallback gives little: cascades gained <=2.7 points even with oracle thresholds). https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29769 . Implication: a Jev->Haiku cascade adds little accuracy; use Haiku as cost-capped tiebreak only for audit, not for 'second opinion' accuracy.
- OTHER CRITICISM: HN comment: speed comparison misleading, can't do general generation; early-access user: out-of-distribution behavior differs from LLM; research-credit dispute (Nandakishor Mukkunnoth claims prior published probability-estimation work; unproven) and architecture is secret (Towards AI https://pub.towardsai.net/jev-might-be-a-bigger-deal-than-it-looks-34d6981f8e76 , remio.ai https://www.remio.ai/post/typesafe-jev-research-dispute-grows-as-decision-models-flood-the-ai-market). Practitioner results: Vercel engineer safety classifier 5-18x faster than replaced LLM; Bryo AI: Gemini slightly more accurate on email classification but 10-20x costlier; Jack & Jill case study 88% screening cost reduction (all via InfoQ/typesafe.ai, vendor-adjacent).
- IBM MIXTURE OF EXPERTS: searched twice; found NO coverage of an IBM Mixture of Experts podcast episode on Jev (UNVERIFIED / not found). Do not cite it.
- ALT 1 - Microsoft-Decision-1 (Microsoft Foundry + OpenRouter, released 2026-10-09 per rezolve/search summary): same Choice/Score/Noul primitives, text-only, post-trained from Qwen3.5-9B, 32K context, one listing $0.042/M input output free; 'model' field = Foundry deployment name; vendor-reported accuracy over 36 blind benchmarks and low decision-flip rate under perturbation (vendor-run); Microsoft excludes sole-model use for consequential decisions about people; base model will be rebased so calibration may shift across versions. Sources (secondary): https://itnext.io/microsoft-decision-1-foundry-deep-dive-5a1c1f93a0b7 ; https://www.computerworld.com/?p=4234294 ; https://www.productbuilder.net/models/microsoft-decision-1. Pricing/context from a single listing, unverified vs Microsoft docs. Azure-hosted deployment may suit HoT's Azure estate and .NET (Azure SDK) - inference, unverified.
- ALT 2 - Perplexity pplx-decider-v1-27b (open weights, Apache 2.0, Hugging Face perplexity-ai/..., Qwen-based 27B fine-tune, ~262k context, Decisions API opened 2026-10-01; v1.1 exists per weaker sources). Text/JSON/images in; yes/no probabilities, choices, rubric scores out. Self-hostable => version-pinned weights, can be made reproducible (fixed weights + greedy/fixed kernels on a pinned GPU stack; reproducibility still not guaranteed across hardware - my note). Provider-reported 85.71% vs 84.51% for Jev 1.13.0 on 7,210-row panel; prompts/label mappings unpublished, no independent reproduction. Pricing in secondary sources conflicts ($0.04 vs $0.02/M). https://www.besthub.dev/articles/perplexity-s-pplx-decider-v1-1-27b-open-weight-decision-model-beats-jev-runs-locally-d08e6d61587a ; https://aiweekly.co/alerts/perplexity-open-sources-27b-decider-edges-jev-on-11-test-panel . Covered also in arXiv 2609.29769 as 'open reproduction'.
- ALT 3 - Claude Haiku/Sonnet-class with structured output constrained to an enum/JSON schema (e.g. {verdict: ENUM[duplicate, load_bearing, trivial, distinct], extract_target}) plus logprob-free confidence via N-sample agreement or an explicit 'p' field. Not researched via web this session (general knowledge; verify current Haiku 5.5 pricing/structured-output support via claude-api docs). Pros: handles semantic clones and can justify; cons: not deterministic even at temp 0 (per Jev cookbook claim about LLMs), costlier (paper: 16-357x), errors correlate with decision models.
- ALT 4 - no web research, design-level options (my suggestions, unverified in practice): (a) logistic regression / gradient-boosted classifier over deterministic clone features (token-clone length, normalized-AST similarity, sibling-file count, path class: prod vs test, hot-path tag, is-added-vs-existing, Engram search_code similarity score), trained on labeled history of engram-csharp's DryUpScan 'filed' vs 'watched' vs reviewer-dismissed decisions and ~40 'Watched, not filed' pairs; fully deterministic, runs in-process in .NET (ML.NET), versionable by hash of model file; (b) small fine-tuned code-embedding cross-encoder or open classifier (e.g. CodeBERT/UniXcoder-class) fed pair of snippets, self-hosted ONNX in .NET; deterministic given pinned weights/runtime. Both fit the 'IDeterministicEvaluationPlugin + frozen model identity' receipt pattern.

### Caveats

- Most web findings come via search-engine summaries and WebFetch small-model summaries, not direct reading of full pages; primary Jev docs (docs.typesafe.ai) were fetched directly and are the most reliable. Secondary blogs (rezolve, nocode.mba, eesel, aimlapi, itnext, besthub, aiweekly) are lower confidence.
- Determinism of Jev for identical requests is undocumented; do not assume replayable outputs. The cookbook's low variance test was confounded by a changing uid field.
- Early access: access gated by waitlist/console; rate limits only visible in console; no documented SLA, deprecation policy, or data-residency (relevant for HoT/NZ code). Whether proprietary source code is acceptable to send to a startup API is a security/policy question not researched.
- Jev docs inconsistency: models.md says text-only while docs index lists Images/Box primitives; irrelevant for code but note.
- The Microsoft-Decision-1 and Perplexity Decider details (dates, prices, context, benchmarks) come from secondary/aggregator sources and conflict in places (Perplexity price $0.04 vs $0.02; v1 vs v1.1); verify on primary pages (Hugging Face model card, Microsoft Foundry docs) before design commitment.
- I did not retrieve the arXiv paper body (PDF was binary; only abstract page summarized), so per-benchmark numbers and whether any panel resembles code-duplication judgments are unknown. No benchmark found for Jev on code-clone/DRY tasks specifically: expect to need our own labeled eval set.
- IBM Mixture of Experts episode mention not found anywhere; marked unverified.
- Haiku 5.5 / Sonnet 5.5 pricing and structured-output specifics were not researched here; alt 3 and alt 4 are design suggestions, not sourced facts.
- TypeSafe FAQ answers (subsidized pricing? deterministic? where does it struggle?) were present as questions on the homepage but answers were not returned by the fetch.
