## C.llm-judge

### Facts

- PRICING (Claude API, per MTok, fetched 2026-10-28): Haiku 5.5 (claude-haiku-5-5) $0.10 in / $0.50 out for prompts <=100K tokens; $0.50 / $2.50 for prompts >100K (per-request, cache reads count toward the 100K). Sonnet 5.5 (claude-sonnet-5-5) $2 in / $10 out, flat across the full 1M context. Haiku 4.5 is $1/$5 (cached skill table, not re-fetched). Source: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing
- PROMPT CACHING: Haiku 5.5 writes $0.125 (5m) / $0.20 (1h), reads $0.01/MTok. Sonnet 5.5 writes $2.50 (5m) / $4 (1h), reads $0.10/MTok (0.05x base). Minimum cacheable prompt 512 tokens on both. Default TTL 5 min, refreshed on each hit. Docs state caching has no effect on output: response is identical to uncached. Sources: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing , https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching
- BATCH API: 50% off input and output, stacks with caching. Haiku 5.5 batch $0.05 in / $0.25 out (<=100K prompts); Sonnet 5.5 batch $1 / $5. Async, so only suitable for non-blocking gates (nightly/weekly sweep, not a blocking PR check). Source: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing
- BACK-OF-ENVELOPE COST (my arithmetic, not from docs): a 20K-token judge prompt (diff + top-k Engram candidate snippets) with ~1K output tokens incl. thinking is about $0.0025 on Haiku 5.5 and about $0.05 on Sonnet 5.5 uncached; with a cached 10K-token static prefix and batch, roughly half or less. Note newer tokenizer yields ~30% more tokens than Haiku 4.5 for the same text, so count with count_tokens on the target model.
- MODEL SPECS: Haiku 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 both have 1M context, 128K max output, 'reliable knowledge cutoff' Jun 2026. Model IDs are pinned snapshots with no date suffix and no alias ('claude-haiku-5-5 is a fixed model ID'). Retirement not sooner than 2027-10-07 (Haiku 5.5) and 2027-09-28 (Sonnet 5.5) on Anthropic-operated platforms. Default effort: Haiku 5.5 medium, Sonnet 5.5 high. Adaptive thinking on by default for both; thinking tokens count toward max_tokens and are billed as output. Sources: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/overview , https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/haiku-5-5/whats-new-haiku-5-5
- STRUCTURED OUTPUT: output_config.format = {type: json_schema, schema} constrains output to the schema; GA, no beta header, supported on Haiku 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 (and Opus 5.5, Fable 5.1). Schema limits: additionalProperties:false required; enum, const, anyOf/allOf, internal $ref ok; NO numeric min/max, string length, or minItems >1 constraints (validate those client-side); recursive schemas unsupported. Exceptions to guarantee: stop_reason refusal, stop_reason max_tokens, enum casing may differ. Grammar cached 24h; first request with a new schema has extra latency. Docs explicitly guarantee structure, not content/repeatability. Source: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/structured-outputs
- TEMPERATURE-0 IS NOT AVAILABLE: Haiku 5.5 migration guide says 'omit temperature, top_p, and top_k'. API version 2026-10-21 (released 2026-10-21, current) removes temperature/top_p/top_k: a request sending any of them returns 400 on every model. Sonnet 5.5 also rejects non-default sampling values per the claude-api skill reference. So there is no Anthropic-side determinism knob for these models; reproducibility must be engineered around the call. Sources: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/versioning , https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/haiku-5-5/migration-guide
- FORCED TOOL USE DIFFERS: Sonnet 5.5 returns 400 on tool_choice any/tool (use output_config.format or strict tool use + auto). Haiku 5.5 still accepts forced tool_choice but the response then has no thinking block. Either way, json_schema structured outputs is the cleaner path for a verdict. Sources: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/sonnet-5-5/whats-new-sonnet-5-5 , https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/haiku-5-5/migration-guide
- ASSISTANT PREFILL returns 400 on Haiku 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5; use structured outputs instead. Safety classifiers can return stop_reason 'refusal' on both models; Haiku 5.5 has no server-side fallback. A gate must map refusal/max_tokens to INCOMPLETE, consistent with the delivery-forge rule that uncertain/truncated = INCOMPLETE, never PASS. Sources: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/haiku-5-5/migration-guide , https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/sonnet-5-5/whats-new-sonnet-5-5
- THINKING-BLOCK CONSTRAINTS (only matter if multi-turn): both models bind thinking blocks to the model/account, and edited earlier turns invalidate them (400 on accounts created on/after 2026-08-31). A single-shot stateless judge call avoids all of this. Source: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/sonnet-5-5/whats-new-sonnet-5-5
- INFERENCE NONDETERMINISM IS GENERAL: Thinking Machines (Horace He, Sept 2025) argues most endpoint nondeterminism at temp 0 comes from kernels not being batch-invariant, and server load makes the batch composition random per request; fix needs batch-invariant kernels (~tens of % overhead per secondary reports), which a hosted-API consumer cannot control. Secondary coverage: https://simonwillison.net/2025/Sep/11/defeating-nondeterminism
- EMPIRICAL STABILITY OF LLM CLONE VERDICTS: Zhu et al. 2025 (5 models, 4 prompts) found temperature had little effect (F1 moved <0.032 across settings) while prompt wording mattered far more (GPT-4o-mini F1 swing 0.49 across prompts; one prompt gave only 0.376). GPT-4o and GPT-4o-mini repeat-run consistency >90%; Llama 3.1 8B changed >=20% of answers on resubmission, up to 80% inconsistency on one prompt. Implication: prompt is a first-class versioned artifact; hash it. Source: https://arxiv.org/html/2511.01176v1
- CLONE-DETECTION ACCURACY, LLM vs type: Zhang & Saber 2024 (arXiv 2407.02402), recall only (no negatives in datasets), BigCloneBench GPT-3.5 / GPT-4: T1 1.00/1.00; T2 0.56/0.86; VST3 0.52/0.94; ST3 0.44/0.97; MT3 0.23/0.87; WT3/T4 0.07/0.23. On GPTCloneBench MT3 0.69/0.84, WT3/T4 0.58/0.77. Both models worst on Type-4; GPT-3.5 produced 280 and GPT-4 232 false positives on the BCB WT3/T4 set; models showed higher recall on LLM-generated than human-written clones (possible self-bias). Source: https://arxiv.org/html/2407.02402v1
- DATASET SENSITIVITY: Zhu et al. 2025 (arXiv 2511.01176): o3-mini F1 0.943 on CodeNet-random Java pairs but mean F1 dropped by 0.52 on BigCloneBench-random; GPT-4o dropped 0.42, GPT-4o-mini 0.26, Mistral 0.26. Recall near zero for GPT-4o/mini on BCB pairs with token similarity in [0,0.2). High-similarity non-clones were more likely to be labeled clones (over-flagging). Type-2 (renamed) clones were sometimes missed when the prompt emphasized functional equivalence. Models: o3-mini, GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini, Llama 3.1 8B, Mistral 7B (no Claude). The paper reports no head-to-head vs token-based tools. Source: https://arxiv.org/html/2511.01176v1
- SEMANTIC-CLONE (T4) WEAKNESS AND EXECUTION FIX: HyClone (arXiv 2508.01357, Python, PyFuncEquivDataset 751 pairs) says direct LLM judgments are biased by syntactic differences, giving high false-negative rates on semantic clones: GPT-4o-mini baseline recall 0.0615 (precision 0.53, F1 0.11); Deepseek-v3 recall 0.58, Qwen3-235b recall 0.68. LLM screening plus LLM-generated test inputs and output comparison lifted recall to 0.81-0.85 and F1 to 0.62-0.64, but precision dipped (0.49-0.51); more test cases tended to add false positives. When challenged ('are you sure?') LLM verdicts degrade: Deepseek-v3 multi-turn challenged precision fell to 0.10 with 75.6% flip rate. Implication: do not allow multi-turn re-asking; single stateless verdict. Source: https://arxiv.org/html/2508.01357v1
- TOKEN-BASED TOOLS ARE STILL COMPETITIVE: Alam et al., ICSME 2025 (arXiv 2509.25754) evaluated nine classical clone detectors on GPTCloneBench plus BigCloneBench/SemanticCloneBench and conclude classical tools 'retain considerable effectiveness' on AI-generated clones, with normalization the major factor, and recommend a hybrid classical+AI approach. Per a secondary review, token-based CloneWorks is good on MT3 but Type-4 recall is low (about 20% in Java), while fine-tuned encoders (CodeBERT) reach about 94.5% T4 recall on BCB; that last number is a fine-tuned encoder, not a prompted LLM. Sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25754 , https://www.themoonlight.io/review/are-classical-clone-detectors-good-enough-for-the-ai-era
- REFACTOR-ORIENTED STUDY: 'Can LLMs Identify and Refactor Code Clones' (Stevens Institute record) on 20 JHotDraw cases vs JDeodorant: GPT-4o near-perfect on Type-1, about 51.2% on Type-2 and about 85.6% on Type-3; struggles with Type-2/3; refactoring quality reportedly around 84%. Models also covered include Gemini 2.5 Pro, GPT-5, DeepSeek-R1, Claude Sonnet 3.7, Claude Opus 4.1 (per record; per-model numbers not retrieved). Source: https://researchwith.stevens.edu/en/publications/can-large-language-models-identify-and-refactor-code-clones-an-em/
- SURVEY SUPPORT: systematic mapping of 28 LLM clone-detection papers says LLMs can help on complex Type-3/4 clones but Type-4 remains challenging; Java used in 83% of studies and Python in 50% (C# is essentially unstudied, relevant to engram-csharp); prompt sensitivity is a recurring concern. Source: https://researchwith.stevens.edu/en/publications/a-systematic-mapping-study-on-the-research-landscape-of-llm-based/ (and survey https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.01191)
- DESIGN IMPLICATIONS (synthesis, not sourced claims): (a) the strongest evidence supports LLMs as a re-ranker/verifier over candidates found by deterministic or embedding retrieval (tier 1 + Engram search_code), not as a from-scratch whole-repo detector, because recall on low-similarity T4 pairs is poor and false positives rise on high-similarity non-clones; (b) the LLM question should be 'is this specific candidate pair a duplicate that SHOULD be shared (extract) vs load-bearing/boilerplate', with a closed-enum verdict + cited file:line evidence; (c) Haiku 5.5 is cheap enough (~$0.0025 per 20K-token call) that an N=3 vote is still cheaper than one Sonnet 5.5 call; (d) no Claude-model clone-detection benchmark was found, so a gate needs its own labeled eval set built from engram-csharp's existing 'Watched, not filed' ~40 clone pairs plus known-OK boilerplate.

### Caveats

- REPRODUCIBILITY RECIPE below is my synthesis from the docs facts, not an Anthropic-published recommendation. Anthropic offers no seed or temperature=0 guarantee for these models (sampling params removed in API version 2026-10-21), so 'deterministic' here means 'reproducible by memoization', not 'regenerates identically'. Recommended: (1) pin the exact model id (IDs are fixed snapshots; Haiku 5.5 has no alias) and record it in the receipt; (2) canonicalize and hash model id + system prompt + JSON schema + effort setting + the exact input bytes (patch hunks and candidate snippets sorted by path/line), and cache the verdict keyed by that hash so a re-run on the same tree returns the stored verdict (this fits delivery-forge's frozen-input / input-set-identity pattern); (3) set output_config.effort explicitly (Haiku 5.5 default is medium, Sonnet 5.5 default is high) and record it; (4) single stateless call, no multi-turn challenge (HyClone shows flip rates up to ~76% under challenge); (5) schema with closed enums (e.g. DRY | NOT_DRY | LOAD_BEARING_OK | UNSURE) plus evidence fields, validate numeric/length constraints client-side since the schema cannot express them; (6) treat refusal, max_tokens, schema failure, or UNSURE as INCOMPLETE; (7) optionally run N=3 independent calls and require unanimity for FAIL (and map any disagreement to INCOMPLETE) rather than simple majority, because the gate's cost of a false FAIL (blocking a merge) is higher than a missed duplicate; N-way voting for self-consistency is from Wang et al. 2022 (arXiv 2203.11171, cited from memory, not fetched this session); (8) escalate only borderline cases from Haiku 5.5 to Sonnet 5.5 and record both verdicts; (9) re-baseline against the labeled eval set on any model/prompt change, since prompt choice moved F1 by up to 0.49 in published work.
- Evidence gap: I found no published evaluation of any Claude model (Haiku 4.5/5.5, Sonnet 4.x/5.x) on type-3/type-4 clone detection, and no evaluation on C#. Published numbers are on GPT-3.5/4/4o/o3-mini, Llama, Mistral, Deepseek, Qwen, mostly Java/Python and BigCloneBench-style data. Do not transfer them to Claude without a local eval.
- Published studies measure pair classification ('is A a clone of B'), not the gate's real task ('should this patch have reused existing code or extracted a helper'). No paper found measures false positives on boilerplate/getters/framework scaffolding or on deliberately-duplicated hot paths; the over-flagging claim is supported only indirectly (high-similarity non-clones labeled as clones; 232-280 false positives on BCB WT3/T4). Load-bearing duplication detection is an unstudied judgment call and will rely on prompt rubric plus Engram context.
- BigCloneBench labels are noisy and Zhu et al. note competitive-programming ground-truth issues; numbers should be read as directional. Zhang & Saber report recall only (positives only), so their results cannot show precision. Zhu et al. used temperature 0.3 on OpenAI/open models, not Claude; o3-mini does not accept temperature, matching the Claude direction of travel.
- Some secondary sources only: the CodeBERT 94.5% and CloneWorks ~20% T4 recall figures come from a third-party review summary (themoonlight.io) of the ICSME paper, and the Stevens JHotDraw percentages come from a publication record abstract; I did not read those primary papers in full. The Thinking Machines claim is cited via secondary coverage (simonwillison.net link); the original is believed to be https://thinkingmachines.ai/blog/defeating-nondeterminism-in-llm-inference/ but I did not fetch it. The ~34% determinism overhead figure is from a vendor blog and unverified.
- Pricing and capability facts were read from platform.claude.com (docs.anthropic.com 301-redirects there) on 2026-10-28 and can change; Haiku 5.5 is the only current model with length-tiered pricing (>100K-token prompts cost 5x), so keep judge prompts under 100K tokens (candidate pairs, not whole files). Priority Tier is not supported on Haiku 5.5 or Sonnet 5.5, so rate-limit/capacity risk in CI is not purchasable away; plan for 429 retry mapping to INCOMPLETE. Claude API rate limits for these models were not researched.
- Jev (TypeSafe AI) and Luna/Terra were not researched in this task; scope was cheap-Claude-as-judge plus clone-detection evidence only.
- Zero Data Retention: structured outputs is ZDR-eligible excluding Covered Models; Haiku 5.5 / Sonnet 5.5 ZDR status for this org was not checked. If engram-csharp code is sent to the API, confirm org data-retention terms.

# Appendix D: method

- Workflow: 48 agents, 0 errors, about 4.77M subagent tokens, 24 minutes wall clock.
- Phases: Scout (5) -> Propose (8, Opus 5.5 high) -> Judge (24, Sonnet 5.5, 3 lenses per design) -> Refute (10, Sonnet 5.5 high, 2 lenses per top-5 design) -> Critique (1, Opus 5.5 high).
- Angles assigned: det-token-clone, det-ast-structural, engram-semantic, jev-decision, cheap-llm-judge, novel-information-theoretic, novel-proof-of-search, hybrid-cascade.
- Synthesis and the top-4 selection in the main body are by Claude Fable 5.1.
- The option 2 prototype scripts (exp1-3, replay, ratchet, trained; python-zstandard 0.25.0) were run by the proposer on engram-csharp @ adf2abc and left in a local temp directory on the author's machine; they are not in this repository.
